# BRAIN.md — Askwell build state

> Mutable. Updated at the end of every session.
> If this file is stale, the next session starts from confusion.

---

## Current phase

**M1 — It answers from my documents. Complete, 37 of 37.** M0 complete, 21 of 21.

Askwell answers from a user's own documents, end to end, for the first time: add a folder, files are recorded and deduplicated by content hash, six formats extract with an OCR fallback, chunks embed, hybrid retrieval reranks, the prompt composes with the C7 boundary intact, and the answer streams in with citations that open the cited page.

**M2 — it says when it doesn't know — is complete, 15 of 15.** `0.3.0`. That milestone is where C5 stops being a system prompt and becomes a measured number: the abstention subset with its pass bar of 0.90, and the rule that it is never improved by lowering the retrieval threshold. **Since `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053` a below-threshold retrieval abstains before composition ever runs**, not after the model decides to: `askwell.ask._run_generation` compares every reranked candidate's score, via the new `askwell.retrieve.candidate_score` (a sigmoid over the cross-encoder logit when reranking ran, the real dense/lexical score otherwise — the logit alone is unbounded and not comparable to a `[0, 1]` threshold), against `Settings.retrieval_score_threshold` (`0.65`, unmeasured, unchanged) before ever calling `compose()`. The threshold in force and every candidate's score, including the near-miss, are stored on `messages.trace` rather than recomputed later, and `audit_interactions` gained `abstained`/`threshold` so an abstention is queryable directly. A source-scoped question against a source still indexing gets its own trace reason (`source_indexing`, via `askwell.ingest.coverage`) rather than a generic "nothing matched". **Since `M2-ABSTAIN-BE-054` an abstained turn has real copy, not a placeholder**: `askwell.agent.abstain.compose_abstention` builds the three-part message (state, prove, next action) as a pure template — no second model call — from real passage/document/database counts (`askwell.ask._abstain_reason` now queries them with the same join and `WHERE` `askwell.retrieve`'s own searches use) and the nearest scored candidate's own heading (or filename, when it has none). `empty_corpus` and `source_indexing` get their own wording rather than the below-threshold sentence with counts zeroed out. `prompts/abstention.v1.md` states C5's standing rule; `test_abstain.py` asserts it survives. `messages.content` and the audit record's `answer` now carry the composed text — streaming it to the browser as a `token` event is `M2-ABSTAIN-FE-055`'s job, not built here. **Since `M2-ABSTAIN-FE-055` the Ask screen renders that composed message as its own state**, not a blank answer: `isAbstained` (`web/lib/ask.ts`) reads the one signal available client-side — `completed`, no answer text, a non-null `reason` — since the server never streams the composition as `token` events (there is nothing to stream token by token; the whole message is already known synchronously). `AbstentionState` (`ask-screen.tsx`) renders it in `--ink`/`--muted`, full measure, never `--alarm`, with an **Add a source** action beneath it that retains the question (`fillComposer`, the same pattern `context-rail.tsx`'s "Ask about this source" already uses) — the region `M6.5-WEB-FE-186`'s escalation offer later adds siblings to, never rendered above the abstention statement (C10). The provenance margin gained its own abstention-specific empty copy. A past abstained turn already collapsed with no source count (`M2-ABSTAIN-BE-054`'s `source_count: null`); this ticket is what makes an *expanded* one show the state instead of nothing. No interactive browser walkthrough was run — this environment has no browser tool — verified instead by `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (lint, typecheck, 171 tests, build, contrast, offline) and unit tests on `isAbstained`. **Since `M2-ABSTAIN-OBS-056` the abstention rate is computable locally, on demand, from what `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053` already stored**: `askwell.observability.abstention_rate` counts the `abstained` flag on the most recent `window` (default 500) `ask_asked` rows in `audit_interactions` and reports how many it actually covered, bounded rather than scanning the whole log. It reads no other column — not the stored threshold, not the candidate scores, and never `Settings.retrieval_score_threshold` — because the rate this ticket exists to make queryable is "how many past turns actually abstained," not "how would today's configuration have scored them"; the two other stored values (`M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053`) already make that distinction inspectable turn by turn. No dashboard and no wiring anywhere yet — the function is the whole surface, matching the ticket's own out-of-scope line. **Since `M2-PARTIAL-BE-057` a compound question answers the part retrieval actually covers and names the rest, rather than smoothing over it or, worse, blurring it into a full abstention**: `askwell.ask._run_generation` now composes every non-abstained turn with the new `askwell.agent.partial.compose_partial` in place of `askwell.agent.compose.compose` — a fresh, versioned prompt (`prompts/partial_answer.v1.md`) rather than a branch bolted onto the ordinary one, matching the ticket's own "distinct composition path" line. The model answers, with citations, exactly what the retrieved content supports, and names anything it does not as its own line, `Not covered: <the specific aspect>.`; `askwell.agent.partial.split_partial_answer` reads that convention back out of the composed text the same way `segment_claims` already reads citation markers back out, and sets `partial_coverage`/`uncovered_aspects` on both `messages.trace` (turn level and the `compose` step) and the `audit_interactions` payload — no migration, both stores already carry flexible `jsonb`. There is no separate Python-side detector deciding when to use the partial path: since aspect decomposition is prompt-driven by the ticket's own Assumptions line, detection and composition are one model call, and a fully-covered question composes and is marked exactly as it would have before this ticket. The abstain/partial boundary cannot blur structurally — `compose_partial` only ever runs once `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053`'s own threshold check has already decided the turn is not abstaining, so "every aspect uncovered" stays the abstention branch by construction. **Found while verifying against the real stack, not fixed here:** the shipped `Qwen3.5-4B-Q4_K_M.gguf` is a reasoning model, and nothing anywhere in the pipeline strips its `<think>` block before `turn.text` is built — live-verified, the model rehearsed the `Not covered:` line three times while drafting inside `<think>`, so `uncovered_aspects` came back with three duplicates for one real gap. This is a pre-existing hole in the whole generation-consumption path (`segment_claims`'s citation markers have the identical exposure and predate this ticket), not something this ticket introduced or is scoped to fix — filed as issue #220. **Since `M2-PARTIAL-BE-059` retrieved passages that genuinely disagree on the same asked fact are presented as a conflict, never silently resolved to one of them**: `askwell.agent.conflict.compose_conflict` is now the single composition call site's prompt (`prompts/conflicting_sources.v1.md`, a superset of `partial_answer.v1.md`'s own content plus a conflict section and a memory-resolution section), replacing `compose_partial` there the same way `compose_partial` replaced `compose` the ticket before it — `compose_partial`/`partial_answer.v1.md` are left in place, tested, unused at the call site, matching what already happened to `compose`/`answer_composition.v1.md`. When two or more passages give a materially different value for the same fact, the model presents both as ordinary cited claims and marks the presentation with a fixed line, `Conflicting sources on <the fact>:`, which `split_conflict_answer` reads back the same way `split_partial_answer` reads `Not covered:` — the two conventions coexist in one composed text since a conflict and an uncovered aspect are independent. `messages.trace` and `audit_interactions` both gained `conflict_detected`/`conflict_topic`. Supersession needed no new code: `askwell.retrieve` already excludes a superseded document from every candidate query, so two candidates reaching this module are, by construction, both current. A `memory_fact` parameter on `compose_conflict` is the M3 hook the ticket's scope names — inert, nothing calls it with a value yet. **Deferred, filed as issue #223:** the edge case asking that a low-confidence-OCR side of a conflict note that in its presentation is prompt-only — `Candidate` carries no OCR-confidence signal today, so the model can only note this if the passage text itself says so; threading `document_pages.ocr_confidence` through `retrieve.py` was outside this ticket's stated touchpoints. Detection quality is unmeasured — the conflicting-source eval subset (0.75 bar) landed later as `M2-EVAL-TEST-066`. **Since `M2-PARTIAL-FE-058` the partial and conflicting-sources states render, not just compose:** a new `AnsweredContent` (`ask-screen.tsx`) reads `web/lib/answer-annotations.ts`'s `parseAnswerAnnotations` — a client-side mirror of `split_partial_answer`/`split_conflict_answer` reading the same fixed `Not covered:`/`Conflicting sources on ...:` lines back out of `turn.answer`, the pattern `lib/claims.ts` already uses for citation ordinals — and layers `UncoveredBlock` (a `--rule-strong`-edged, `--muted` block, visually distinct from the answered prose above it) and `ConflictBanner`/`ResolveOffer` on top of `AnswerProse`'s now-cleaned text, never replacing it. The resolve offer is a client-only choice among the turn's own cited documents, stated plainly as not saved until M3 supplies `askwell.agent.conflict`'s inert `memory_fact` hook a real value. Conflict cards (margin and inline) gained a document date and, when superseded since the answer streamed, that label too — `GET /documents/{id}` gained `added_at` for this, the only date source that exists (no ingestion-metadata or filename-derived date is extracted anywhere yet, so the ticket's own fallback rule is what always fires). **This ticket's first attempt was rejected by its own audit** (issue #226: cards rendered in citation-stream — i.e. model-preference — order, which `ask.md` §5's own Validation Rule forbids) and parked rather than merged; this pass carries that attempt's code forward and fixes exactly that gap with `web/lib/document-dates.ts`'s new `useDocumentDates`/`sortByDateAndSupersession`, computed once for the whole list before render rather than per card (a per-card fetch cannot see its siblings' dates, which is why the first attempt could not have sorted correctly no matter how it rendered). Full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-29. **Issue #225 (superseded-edge-case only reachable on a re-viewed past turn) is closed as filed, left as built** — `askwell.agent.conflict` already excludes a superseded document from every live candidate query, so building a synthetic path to exercise "conflict + superseded" together live would be speculative code for a scenario retrieval already prevents by design. **Since `M2-FAIL-FE-060` the product degrades to search rather than to nothing when the assistant is down**: `GET /search` (`askwell.retrieve.register_search`, `askwell.retrieve.search`) runs the same dense-plus-lexical, RRF-fused pipeline `retrieve()` uses, except it catches the query-embedding call's own `InferenceUnavailable`/`InferenceFailed` and degrades to lexical search alone rather than failing — `retrieve()` itself stays unguarded there on purpose, since an ordinary question should still fail loudly when the assistant is absent. The response carries `keyword_only` so the caller knows which happened. On Ask, a new panel (`DegradedSearch`, `ask-screen.tsx`) appears above the composer whenever `/assistant` reports unavailable, offering exactly that keyword search with results linking to the source at its page. Two of the ticket's other edge cases were already true and only verified here, not built: `askwell.assistant._EXPLANATIONS` already gives `STARTING`/`CRASHED` distinct headlines from every other unavailable cause (`M0-MODEL-BE-020`), and `useStatus`'s existing poll already recovers the interface with no reload. **Deferred, filed as issue #227:** the ticket's ingestion bullet — embedding queues indefinitely rather than failing while the assistant is down — contradicts the deliberate, tested `M1-INDEX-ING-032` decision that every stage failure is bounded by `MAX_ATTEMPTS` then needs a manual retry (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28; `states-and-edge-cases.md` §3's "Embedding job failed after retries" row). Left unimplemented rather than silently reversing that decision or breaking the test that encodes it (`test_a_failure_exhausted_after_retries_is_visible_and_the_retry_works`); needs a product decision, not a guess. **Since `M2-DELETE-BE-061` deletion is a tombstone, not a delete**: `askwell.sources.delete_document`/`delete_source` clear a document's chunks' `content` and `embedding` in one statement, set `deleted_at`/`deleted_reason`, and leave the row so an old citation still resolves to "deleted on `<date>`" instead of breaking — `superseded_by` stays reserved for versions, never reused for this. Deleting a source cascades the same tombstone to every live document under it (including mid-import, so nothing is left half-indexed) and deletes its `schema_notes` outright, while general memory is untouched — a different table this function never reaches. Both routes (`DELETE /documents/{id}?reason=`, `DELETE /sources/{id}`) are decisions records, so C6 holds. Two things this ticket found already built rather than needing building: retrieval already excludes tombstoned material (`deleted_at IS NULL AND superseded_by IS NULL`, present since `M1-ADD-BE-023`/`M1-INDEX-ING-032`) and the database already refuses a cleared chunk keeping its embedding (`ck_chunks_cleared_content_has_no_embedding`, `M0-DATA-DB-014`). What this ticket did add beyond the two routes: `askwell.ingest`'s `_park`/`_finish`/`_fail` and the `indexing` write in `process` now all guard `deleted_at IS NULL`, closing a race where a job already claimed when a deletion runs could write a tombstoned document's status back to `ready`/`attention`/`queued` after the fact. **Since `M2-DELETE-FE-062` deletion has a confirmation and the deleted state renders everywhere a citation can land**: the library's own `DeleteControl` (`web/components/library/library-screen.tsx`) confirms inline against a source, naming it and stating the three facts `docs/ux/library.md` §4 requires, then calls `DELETE /sources/{id}`. Getting deleted sources to *stay* listed needed two backend changes this ticket's own dependencies did not already cover: `sources.deleted_at` did not exist (migration `5f3a7c1e9d42`, `delete_source` now stamps it), and `askwell.ingest.coverage`'s snapshot query was actively filtering `status <> 'deleted'` out of what the library reads — both fixed rather than stubbed, and recorded as a decision since it changes what every other reader of that snapshot sees. `LibraryFilters.showDeleted` hides deleted rows by default. The provenance margin's cards (`ProvenanceMargin`/`InlineSourceCards`) now live-check each cited document with `GET /documents/{id}` and render a greyed, unclickable "Deleted on `<date>`" card in place of the passage when it answers deleted — a card is composed once from the trace and never learns of a later deletion on its own, so this is the thing that closes that gap. The viewer (`DocumentViewer`) renders the same fact via `DeletedSourceNotice`, and subscribes to the same `subscribeIngest` stream the library already polls so a document open in one tab updates to the deleted state within one poll interval of being deleted from another, rather than showing stale content — the ticket's own edge case. **Found and fixed in the same change, issue [#231](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/231):** `GET /documents/{id}` 404'd a tombstoned document identically to a bad id, which is exactly what the citation card and the viewer both needed to tell apart — `document_metadata` now resolves the tombstone (`deleted`/`deleted_at`/`deleted_reason`) before the availability checks that assume a live document; `GET /documents/{id}/file` is unchanged and still 404s, since the bytes really are gone. This ticket's frontend checks (compose service has no source bind mount, unlike `scripts/dev.sh`) needed an API image rebuild to verify against the real stack; browser-level rendering was not visually checked — no browser tool in this session — verified instead through the API contracts each surface reads plus `scripts/dev.sh web-check`'s type-checked build and pure-function tests. **Since `M2-EVAL-TEST-063` the eval harness itself exists**, offline, ahead of any real suite: `eval/bench.py` (new top-level `eval/`, `docs/build-plan.md`'s own placement) loads a suite from `eval/suites/*.json`, runs each task through `askwell.inference.client.InferenceClient.generate` — the same Unix-socket path the product uses, so C1 needs no separate enforcement here — a fixed three times (`RUNS_PER_TASK` is a constant, not a flag, so a suite cannot be run once and reported as three), and reports mean and worst-of-3 per category, or a strict pass/fail for a `pass_bar == 1.0` suite so "0.97 on ten tasks" never reads as nearly fine. A model-unavailable `InferenceUnavailable` aborts the whole run with a named reason and writes no results file, rather than reporting zeros as a score; a per-run `InferenceFailed` (timeout, malformed output) is recorded on that run with its reason and the task continues. Results carry the model actually loaded (read from the supervisor's own `state.json`, never a name in code), the profile, and every prompt file's version (scanned from `api/src/askwell/agent/prompts/*.vN.md`) — `AGENTS.md`'s audit line. New `scripts/dev.sh eval` mounts `.run/` at `/run/askwell` with `--network=none`, the one addition to `in_image`'s volumes; it runs from `/app/api`, not `/app`, because `Settings`'s `.env` read from `/app` would otherwise pick up the real repo-root `.env`'s raw `POSTGRES_*` variables and refuse to start. `eval/suites/smoke.v1.json` is a two-task fixture proving the harness, not one of the eight real categories (`M2-EVAL-TEST-064` onward) — those and the CI gate (`M2-EVAL-DEPLOY-067`) are explicitly out of scope. 28 new tests in `eval/tests/` (own `eval/pyproject.toml`, since `eval/` is outside `api/pyproject.toml`'s `testpaths`; run via `scripts/dev.sh run pytest /app/eval/tests`, not yet wired into `scripts/dev.sh check`), all against a fake `InferenceClient` — no model needed for the harness's own correctness. Verified against the real stack: `scripts/dev.sh eval --suite smoke.v1` against the stopped supervisor failed clearly (`model unavailable: The assistant is stopped.`, no file written); started `scripts/dev.sh inference` natively and re-ran — `Qwen3.5-4B-Q4_K_M.gguf`, mean 1.00, worst-of-3 1.00, both tasks, ~2m20s for six generations, result JSON written and inspected. `scripts/dev.sh check` (529 passed, 1 skipped) stayed clean throughout. **Since `M2-EVAL-DEPLOY-067` the eval gate runs in CI, closing M2**: `.github/workflows/eval.yml` triggers on a push or PR touching a prompt file, `retrieve.py`, `config.py`, a suite file, or any of the three suite-mode modules, plus manual dispatch, and runs on `[self-hosted, askwell]` — the maintainer's own machine, per `AGENTS.md` §1's "one user, one machine", since the 165-task gate needs a loaded model and minutes of runtime a hosted runner does not comfortably give. It brings up the maintainer's own persistent Compose stack (not a throwaway one — reuses `.env` and volumes), self-heals the generation model from `models_catalog.py`'s registry-verified spec if missing, but fails clearly rather than guessing at a download for the embedding/reranker weights, which have no automated fetch source yet (filed as issue [#244](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/244), carried forward from the parked first attempt's now-corrected reference — that attempt cited issue #241, which had since become an unrelated BRAIN.md-staleness issue). It starts `scripts/dev.sh inference` natively if not already `ready` (timing out at 5 minutes with the supervisor's own `model_missing` state surfaced distinctly), then runs `grounded_qa.v1`, `abstention.v1`, `conflicting_sources.v1` through `scripts/dev.sh eval`, publishing each suite's summary to the job's step summary and the full JSON results as a 90-day artifact. A suite's exit code distinguishes an infrastructure failure (a `HarnessError` — model unavailable, corpus fixture broken) from one that ran to completion and scored below its `docs/build-plan.md` bar, by checking for `bench.py`'s own "written to eval/results" line in stdout — more robust than matching one error string, since any future `HarnessError` message would otherwise misclassify silently. The whole 30-minute job times out loudly rather than leaving a PR's checks queued forever if the runner is offline — the ticket's own named edge case. **Not built here, requires a manual one-time repo-settings step outside any file's reach:** naming this workflow's `eval` job as a required branch-protection status check on `main`, which is what actually blocks a merge with no recorded run — `AGENTS.md` §4's rule is enforced by GitHub's own required-check UI once that's set, not by custom logic in the workflow. `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-29, has the full reasoning, including why the first attempt (`origin/parked/m2-eval-deploy-067-attempt-1`) was parked rather than merged: its diff carried forward stale reverts of `M2-PARTIAL-FE-058` work it was branched before, not a defect in the workflow content itself, which this pass keeps essentially unchanged. `M2` is now 15 of 15, complete — `0.2.51` → `0.3.0` per `AGENTS.md` §7 (a completed 0.x milestone is a `MINOR` bump). `scripts/dev.sh check` (529 passed, 1 skipped) stayed clean; no code path changed, only CI configuration and docs, so no new tests were needed beyond what `M2-EVAL-TEST-063`–`066` already cover.

The repository is no longer documentation only. `api/` exists: an image, manifests, the application, and a test suite across forty-odd modules (the exact count is what `scripts/dev.sh test` prints — a number written down here goes stale within a ticket). The API starts, refuses bad configuration by name, and serves `GET /health` reporting five components separately. `podman compose up -d` brings up four services, the database carries the full v1 schema, and the interface loads at `http://127.0.0.1:8000`. `web/` builds to static assets and the API serves them — the `web` container is gone from the topology. Material can be handed to Askwell, refused by name, and — since `M1-ADD-BE-023` — recorded as sources and documents with content-hash duplicate detection. Since `M1-ADD-ING-025` those records go onto a durable queue that a worker drains in the background, with per-file progress streamed to the browser and a queue that survives a restart. Since `M1-EXTRACT-ING-026` a text-layer PDF is actually read: page text, page count and per-page usability land in Postgres. Since `M1-EXTRACT-ING-027` six more formats read for real — `.docx`, `.pptx`, `.xlsx`, `.txt`, `.md`, `.html` — each with its own page-equivalent anchor (`documents.anchor_kind`, `document_pages.anchor_label`). Since `M1-EXTRACT-ING-028` a scanned PDF with no text layer is read too — OCR with orientation detection runs inline inside `extract`, per page. Since `M1-EXTRACT-ING-029` that OCR pass also measures its own confidence, per page and per document, and a low-confidence document flags its source `needs attention` with a specific reason rather than indexing silently. Since `M1-EXTRACT-VAL-030` a document Askwell cannot even open is classified before any parser sees its bytes — missing from disk, unreadable, corrupt, or password-protected each read distinctly by file name — and a password-protected PDF prompts for its password (`POST /ingest/documents/{id}/password`) rather than exhausting its retries silently; the password is carried for one attempt only, in the job queue, never in Postgres or a log line. Since `M1-INDEX-ING-031` extracted text is actually chunked — a table never splits from its header, a heading is carried as metadata on every chunk beneath it, a heading-free run breaks at sentence boundaries, a slide is never merged with another slide. Since `M1-INDEX-ING-032` a chunked document is actually embedded — `embed` sends every un-embedded chunk to the native inference process in bounded batches, retries a failing batch with backoff, and a document reaches `ready` only once every one of its chunks has a vector. **The pipeline is complete for the first time**: `extract`, `chunk` and `embed` are all real, and a clean document now runs start to finish to `ready` with nothing named as still missing. Since `M1-ASK-RET-035` a chunked, embedded corpus is actually searchable — `askwell.retrieve.retrieve` runs dense and lexical search and fuses them with Reciprocal Rank Fusion. Since `M1-ASK-RET-036` that fused order is no longer the last word: the top candidates go through a cross-encoder rerank pass, with the reranked order used, both score sets retained, and graceful degradation to fusion order — stated on the result, not silent — if the reranker is unavailable. Since `M1-ASK-BE-037` the prompt a question would be answered with exists for the first time, as a versioned file (`api/src/askwell/agent/prompts/answer_composition.v1.md`) rather than inline text — `askwell.agent.compose.compose` delimits retrieved candidates in `<retrieved-content>` blocks and flags, without blocking, any candidate whose content matches a heuristic instruction-like pattern. **Since `M1-ASK-API-038` a question gets an answer, over HTTP, for the first time.** `POST /ask` calls `retrieve()` then `compose()` against a real question for the first time and streams the model's own tokens back over server-sent events, with named step labels ahead of the first token, citations resolved from the model's own `[index]` references into the real `citations` table, a stop signal, and a reconnect (`GET /ask/{message_id}/stream`) that continues a turn already running rather than restarting it. Generation is a background task independent of the HTTP request that started it — closing the tab does not stop it, and the answer is saved either way. `InferenceClient` gained `stream_generate`, generation as a token stream instead of one round trip. **Since `M1-CITE-BE-042` a citation is tied to a claim, not just an index**: `askwell.agent.claims.segment_claims` reads the answer as sentences, and only a sentence carrying a citation marker becomes a claim — a claim citing two passages produces two citation rows sharing one `claim_ordinal`, and `citations.quoted_span` is populated for the first time, `None` when the claim's own words are not found verbatim in the chunk rather than the citation being dropped. Since `M1-ASK-OBS-041` every question and answer writes one `audit_interactions` row in the same transaction as the answer, and an audit write failure now fails the turn visibly rather than leaving it `running` forever. Since `M1-VIEW-FE-046` a citation opens the actual document at the actual cited page, highlighted, rather than only naming it in the margin. Since `M1-VIEW-FE-047` that viewer covers every format the corpus actually has: Word, PowerPoint, plain text, Markdown and HTML open as converted text at the cited heading (or a stated "no headings here" note plus the chunk's own position, when there is none), a spreadsheet opens as a hand-windowed virtual table scrolled to and highlighting the cited row, and a scanned PDF page shows its own OCR'd text alongside the rendered page image — flagged when confidence is low, stated plainly when nothing was read at all. **Since `M1-CITE-TEST-045` the counter-metric C4 needs can finally be queried, not just claimed**: `askwell.agent.citation_check.check_citations` re-segments every stored assistant answer's own text independently of however its citation rows were written, and reports the percentage with every claim covered plus the exact quoted text of any claim that is not — the gap the `M1-CITE-BE-042` session itself flagged ("did every marked claim get a citation row" is not "did any factual claim go uncited"). **Since `M1-VIEW-FE-048` the viewer no longer strands anyone**: a context rail names which answer and claim sent someone there, "back to answer" lands on the exact claim (not the top of the conversation), and next/previous stepping walks every passage the answer cited, across documents, absent rather than disabled when there is only one. A superseded document now says so, with a link to the current version — resolving issue #141, open since `M1-INDEX-BE-034`. **Since `M1-VIEW-BE-049` a renamed or moved file is told apart from a deleted one**: `askwell.documents._availability` (open time) and a new `askwell.ingest.sweep_missing` (a `worker.py` cron, every `ASKWELL_MISSING_CHECK_SECONDS`) both check a document's recorded path against disk, and — only once `roots.source_availability` confirms the document's own root is reachable — set `documents.missing_since` rather than anything that could be mistaken for deletion. A whole root being unmounted reports as `root_unavailable`, never as its documents individually missing. `POST /documents/{id}/relocate` repairs the path once the candidate's sha256 matches; a mismatch is refused, named, with a pointer at adding the file as a new one instead. The viewer renders both states for the first time (`MovedFileNotice`, `RootUnavailableNotice`), replacing the generic "no longer at its recorded path" message the two previously shared. **Since `M1-CONV-BE-177` a turn carries a stored summary and source count for the first time**: `askwell.agent.summarize.summarize_turn` produces both, once, at composition time, from exactly what the turn itself produced — the answer's first sentence and the distinct documents its own citation rows named — and `askwell.ask` writes them into `messages.summary`/`messages.source_count` in the same transaction as the answer, never recomputed on read. An abstained turn (no citation rows) gets `source_count = NULL`, never `0` — the distinction `docs/ux/conversation.md` §5 asks for by name.

(The last sentence of this paragraph used to say the Compose stack, the schema and inference did not exist. All three do; it was left behind by three tickets in a row and is removed rather than corrected.)

Since `M1-ASK-FE-039` a question can be typed and watched, for the first time: the Ask screen (`web/components/ask/ask-screen.tsx`) has a composer (`Enter` submits, `Shift+Enter` newlines), named retrieval steps ahead of the first token, and streaming answer text, driven by `POST /ask` over SSE. `web/components/ask/ask-state.tsx`'s `AskProvider` holds the conversation above the router — the same placement `AddProvider` uses — so a completed answer survives navigating away and back, and a question asked mid-answer queues rather than interleaving. **Since `M1-CITE-FE-043` the Ask screen cites visibly, for the first time:** `web/components/ask/provenance-margin.tsx`'s `ProvenanceMargin` replaces `Shell`'s static placeholder in the permanent right rail, rendering one card per cited chunk — filename, page or anchor, the retrieved passage — as `citation` events arrive, grouped so two claims naming the same passage produce one card with two leaders rather than two cards. `web/lib/claims.ts`'s `segmentClaims` mirrors `askwell.agent.claims.segment_claims` client-side so a `claim_ordinal` names the same rendered sentence on both sides without either telling the other; `web/components/ask/leader.tsx` is a small DOM-node registry (not React state — a claim span and its card are siblings under `ShellFrame`, not parent and child) that draws a `--rule-strong` hairline between them, polling briefly while the turn streams to track reflow and falling back to resize/scroll recompute once it settles. The `citation` SSE event gained `filename`, `anchor_kind`, `heading` and `passage` (`api/src/askwell/ask.py`, `askwell.retrieve.Candidate`) — a join the ticket's own Touchpoints line called for that no endpoint existed to serve; reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28. Since `M1-ASK-FE-039a` the composer also carries its mic control, disabled with its reason (`aria-disabled`, not `disabled`, so it stays reachable and a screen reader announces why) — no audio work of any kind, `voice.md` §2's geometry reserved now so `M6-VUI-FE-132` changes state rather than layout. **Since `M1-ASK-BE-040` a turn no longer depends on this process staying alive to be honest about its own state**: `POST /ask` writes the assistant `messages` row as `running` before the background task even starts, `askwell.ask.reconcile_interrupted` fails every row still `running` at the next startup (nothing survives a restart in the in-memory `_turns` registry, so a `running` row a fresh process finds can only be the last one's), and `Settings.generation_max_concurrent` bounds how many turns actually retrieve-and-generate at once so several abandoned tabs cannot pile the machine up.

**Since `M1-CONV-FE-178` past turns collapse for the first time**: `web/components/ask/ask-screen.tsx`'s `TurnRow` renders exactly one turn full (`LiveTurn` — the pre-existing rendering, margin and all), any turn waiting behind a streaming answer as a small `QueuedTurn` placeholder, and every other turn collapsed to its question (CSS-truncated to one line), its stored summary, and a `SourceCountBadge`. The badge spends `--provenance` only on an answered turn's count — a filled dot plus the number — and renders a visibly different shape (an outlined, slashed circle, `--muted`) for a turn that abstained or failed, so the distinction survives greyscale (`design-system.md` §8). `TurnDivider` groups turns by calendar day. Two new pure functions in `lib/ask.ts` carry the logic that would otherwise live only in JSX: `liveTurnId` (the running turn if one exists, else the most recently asked — so a question queued behind a streaming answer never steals live status from it) and `dividerLabel` ("earlier today" / "yesterday" / a date, comparing two turns' timestamps against now).

**M3 — it learns my material — complete, 20 of 20.** `0.4.0`. `M3-RAISE-BE-068` built the four ambiguity triggers (`askwell.clarify`: abbreviations, unreadable scans, ambiguous document identity, contradictions between sources), each raised only when all three tests hold (cannot determine, material, user plausibly knows) and otherwise inferred as a low-confidence `memory` row or dropped outright when there is nothing safe to guess. `raise_candidates` runs once per source, from `askwell.ingest.refresh_source`, once nothing is left outstanding. **Since `M3-RAISE-BE-069` those candidates are ranked and capped at 5 per source**, user-adjustable (`get_clarification_cap`/`set_clarification_cap`, `settings` table, default 5, every change a decisions record — never silent). Ranking order: `contradiction`, then `document_identity` (not in `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §8's own list — a default, reasoned and logged in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-30), then `abbreviation` by corpus frequency, then `unreadable_scan` weighted by document size; date-format and unguessable-column triggers are M4's. Ties break deterministically by subject. `clarifications.rank` is now written; a passing candidate ranked below the cap gets a generic low-confidence memory inference naming its own rank and the cap — never the candidate's own guessed answer, since nothing about *that* is safe to invent. **Not built, filed as issue [#249](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/249):** the ticket's own edge case of a late-arriving high-ranking candidate displacing an already-raised, unanswered one from an earlier ingestion batch — `raise_candidates` still scans a source exactly once. **Since `M3-RAISE-BE-070` memory and schema notes are checked before any candidate becomes a question**: `askwell.clarify._known_facts` looks up every candidate's subject in `memory`, then `schema_notes`, both filtered to `superseded_by IS NULL` — a current fact of any confidence (including an unreviewed low-confidence inference) suppresses the question entirely, and the fact applies rather than being re-asked. This runs once, across all four triggers' candidates together, in place of the abbreviation trigger's own former inline memory pre-filter (`M3-RAISE-BE-068`), which did the same lookup silently; every suppression is now a `clarification_suppressed` decisions record naming the fact applied and counted on `RaiseResult.suppressed`. Matching is exact only, never fuzzy, per the ticket's own Assumption — a near-match subject must not falsely suppress a real question. The same abbreviation across a second, third and fourth source is asked once because the lookup is global (`memory` carries no `source_id`); a skipped or dismissed question is not re-raised for its own source because `raise_candidates`'s existing "does this source already have any clarification row" guard (`M3-RAISE-BE-068`) already covered that case before this ticket. `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-13, has the reasoning for centralising rather than teaching each trigger its own dedup. **`M3-REVIEW-BE-072a`** exposed the queue over HTTP: `GET /clarifications` (grouped, server-side), `POST .../answer`, `POST .../skip`. **`M3-REVIEW-FE-072`** is the first screen reading it — `/clarifications` as a single grouped list with a rail badge and a post-ingestion prompt; answering and skipping are not wired up yet (`-073`/`-074`).

**Since `M3-STORE-BE-076` `memory` and `schema_notes` have a dedicated write-side module**: `askwell.memory`'s `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note` discard an inference outright when an active user-origin fact already covers the same subject/position, and retire any active inference for that subject/position the moment a user-origin write arrives; `correct_memory_fact`/`correct_schema_note` supersede an active user-origin fact with a new value without ever updating a row in place, so a superseded value stays readable; `get_active_memory_facts`/`get_active_schema_notes` apply the retrieval-time precedence (user-origin before inferred, newer before older within each) as an ordering, not a filter. `memory` gained a nullable `source_id` (migration `2ae457a0587a`) so a general fact can say which source taught it and keep saying so, labelled as from a deleted source, after that source is soft-deleted. Rebuilt fresh rather than rebasing the parked `PR #254` branch as issue #276 recommended — `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17, has the reasoning; the module itself ported over cleanly since nothing since `PR #254` touched it. Folds in issue #258's fix (a direct `superseded_by` assertion on the schema-notes reverse-supersede path). **`clarify.py`/`review.py` still write through their own inline `INSERT`s, not this module** — unchanged from before this ticket, filed as its own follow-up (#282) rather than rewired here, since that touches enough files (`AGENTS.md` §4's ">3 files, agree first" line) to be a separate change. Unblocks #262 (`M3-STORE-OBS-077`), #263 (`M3-APPLY-RET-078`), #264 (`M3-APPLY-ING-080`) — all three commented, none started.

**Since `M3-STORE-OBS-077` the two remaining decisions-record shapes exist**: `askwell.memory.delete_memory_fact`/`delete_schema_note` (`memory_deleted`/`schema_note_deleted`) and `askwell.review.dismiss_group`/`undo_answer` (`clarification_dismissed`, one per item; `clarification_answer_undone`, additive — never removes or rewrites `clarification_answered`). `POST /sources/{id}/clarifications/dismiss` and `POST /clarifications/{id}/undo` are live alongside the existing `answer`/`skip` routes. #262 and #263 both closed/re-owned this session (`0fbe5447` had already merged `076`, unblocking both). #288 filed and deferred: deleting a correction resurrects the value it superseded, via `076`'s own `superseded_by` FK — not reachable until `M3-MEM-FE-084`.

**M4 — it answers from my data — and M5 — it handles harder questions — are both complete.** M4 (`docs/backlog/M4-it-answers-from-my-data.md`) closed with `M4-RESULT-FE-111`. M5 (`docs/backlog/M5-it-handles-harder-questions.md`), 14 of 14, closed with this session's `M5-TRACE-FE-123`: the last of the seven trace states (`docs/ux/trace.md` §5) — most of the surface (rotation, tool ceiling, normal, abstention) was already built by earlier `TRACE` tickets, so this one's own new work was the failed-mid-answer and partial states plus the online-backend readiness for M8. A question needing both a document lookup and a database query now answers correctly in one turn, with a readable trace of how it happened, in every state that turn can land in. **M6 — *I can speak to it*** (`docs/backlog/M6-i-can-speak-to-it.md`), 12 of 13, underway: `M6-AUDIO-DEPLOY-125` (the voice container), `M6-AUDIO-API-126` (the WebSocket audio transport), `M6-STT-BE-127` (English transcription), `M6-STT-BE-128` (turn detection), `M6-TTS-BE-130` (sentence-streamed speech), `M6-TTS-BE-131` (falling back to text when synthesis is unavailable), `M6-VUI-FE-128a` (real mic capture, the voice socket client, the composer's base states), `M6-VUI-FE-134` (the past-latency-budget indicator), `M6-PERF-TEST-136` (the latency measurement harness), `M6-VUI-FE-132` (the live level meter and elapsed time), `M6-VUI-FE-133` (the stop control) and `M6-VUI-FE-135` (permission denied, non-English, abstention spoken in full, this session) are done. The voice channel now produces a real transcript with a confidence measure, reports non-English speech rather than mis-transcribing it, stores both on `messages`/`audit_interactions`, closes a turn on its own once it hears a pause after speech, actually answers by driving `askwell.ask`'s own generation and speaking sentence by sentence, never fails a turn just because the voice model could not be loaded, the composer itself has a real mic control wired to all of it rather than a disabled stub, a turn taking longer than usual says so, only once it actually has, the actual first-audio latency can be measured per stage rather than felt, someone speaking into it can see it hearing them and is told plainly if their mic is silent instead, a running answer can always be stopped with no barge-in, and — since this session — the mic control disables itself with an explanation before it is ever pressed on a browser that already denied it, non-English speech ends the turn with a stated reason instead of a poor transcript, and an abstention (like any other answer that finishes normally) stays shown in full rather than reverting to the idle prompt the instant the turn completes. See the `0.5.12` entry below for the full detail. **The `0.5.11` entry's own "Next" line undercounted M6 as done at 129 alone** — 128a through 136 sum to 13 named tickets, and 135 was simply not mentioned there; left as found rather than silently corrected, same as the `M3-REVIEW-FE-075` staleness this file already flags elsewhere. **`M6-STT-FE-129` (showing the transcript and confirming before answering on low confidence) landed this session — M6 is now complete, 13 of 13.** Its own real dependency turned out to be backend, not frontend: issue #468, filed while scoping the ticket, found that `askwell.voice_stt.build_stt_driver` called `on_transcript` unconditionally with no pause point and no message type for a client correction — resolved by a genuine hold-for-confirmation gate (`VoiceTurn.request_confirmation`/`resolve_confirmation`, `confirm`/`edit` control messages, a confidence threshold and a walked-away timeout), not a client-side stub over an eager answer. See the `0.5.13` entry below and `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-21, for the full detail. Whisper/Kokoro weights still are not present in this environment, so no voice ticket's own cold-start walkthrough has yet exercised a real pass-path turn — this ticket's confirm/edit/timeout paths were verified by driving the driver and the channel directly instead, plus a live (unmocked) smoke test of `/voice/ws` against the rebuilt `api` image.

**M7 — pilot hardening — underway, 18 tickets landed** (`M7-PROBE-DEPLOY-137`, `M7-PROBE-FE-138`, `M7-LOG-BE-153`, `M7-SET-FE-148`, `M7-SEC-BE-151`, `M7-SET-FE-147`, `M7-SEC-BE-152`, `M7-OPS-DEPLOY-154a`, `M7-LOG-BE-155`, `M7-BACKUP-BE-157`, `M7-UPDATE-BE-161`, `M7-LOG-BE-154`, `M7-LOG-FE-156`, `M7-SET-BE-145a`, `M7-BACKUP-BE-158`, `M7-PERF-TEST-167`, `M7-PACK-DEPLOY-139`, `M7-PACK-DEPLOY-140`). **`M7-SEC-BE-152` (chunk content encryption) landed between the `0.6.5` and `0.6.6` entries below with no `CHANGELOG.md` entry of its own** — found while updating this line for `M7-LOG-BE-155`, left as found rather than silently backfilled; its full reasoning is in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-21 ("chunk content is encrypted; the search index and the embedding are not"). `M7-PROBE-DEPLOY-137`: a real host-side hardware probe (`deploy/probe/askwell-probe`), replacing the M1 in-container reading (`askwell.hardware.probe`, which stays as the fallback for a machine the real probe has never run on) as the thing that actually decides a profile. See the `0.6.0` entry below and `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-21, for the full detail. **M6 completing deferred its own `MINOR` bump to the next ticket to land (per the `0.5.13` line above) — this ticket is that next ticket, so it carries the `0.5.13` → `0.6.0` bump rather than a `PATCH`.** **`M7-PACK-DEPLOY-139` is done too, `0.7.7`** — the Linux installer (`deploy/linux/install.sh`/`lib.sh`/`uninstall.sh`), rebuilt from the earlier closed PR #560 with its two fixes (issue #503's `sudo` check, the `askwell-shell` filename) carried forward and a new fix for issue #584 (real generated database passwords instead of the checked-in `change-me*` placeholders). Verified end to end against this checkout with a stand-in shell binary — fresh install, upgrade-preserves-`.env`, uninstall-keeps-data, `--purge-data` removes it. See `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-23, and `docs/manual-tests/M7-PACK-DEPLOY-139.md`. Issue #559 (no release pipeline produces the real shell binary or an installable bundle) is unaffected and stays open, re-owned — this installer will consume that artefact the moment such a pipeline exists, with no changes to this script. **`M7-PACK-DEPLOY-140` is done too, `0.7.8`** — the Windows installer, same shape, with virtualisation checked before Podman (WSL2 is Windows' only path to running containers) and every copied file verified post-copy so a quarantined binary is named rather than silently missing. No Windows machine or VM on this build host to run the real walkthrough against — issue #590, filed the same way #559 tracks Linux's identical gap. See `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-23, and `docs/manual-tests/M7-PACK-DEPLOY-140.md`. **`M7-PACK-DEPLOY-141` is done too, `0.7.9`** — the macOS installer, third and last of the three, with Podman's own Mac-only VM ("the Podman machine") created and started rather than assumed present, and the machine's default home-directory-only mount window named (informationally, not enforced — the same `not_mounted` design `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-27, already accepted for every platform) rather than silently letting a folder outside it fail invisibly later. The ticket text's own signing/notarisation premise (`M7-TAURI-DEPLOY-184` "wired in here") is stale against the settled 2026-08-26 unsigned-distribution decision; this installer never checks or claims a signature, matching Windows' own treatment of "unsigned" as settled fact. A smaller gap surfaced while checking this ticket's own "explained" scope line against the tree: the application-level permission explanation (`folders.tsx`, `docs/ux/add-source.md` §7) genuinely works, from `M7-TAURI-FE-182`; only `Info.plist`'s `NSDesktopFolderUsageDescription`-style strings, which would customise macOS's own system dialog text, are missing — filed as issue #593, not absorbed into this ticket since authoring that content is `M7-TAURI-FE-182`'s scope. No Mac or Mac VM on this build host — issue #592, same shape as #590/#559. See `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-23, and `docs/manual-tests/M7-PACK-DEPLOY-141.md`. **Next: the remaining M7 tickets** (`M7-OFFLINE-DEPLOY-144`, `M7-TAURI-DEPLOY-183`/`184`/`184a`) or issue #559/#592/#593's follow-through.

**`M7-PROBE-FE-138`** — `0.6.1`. `GET /setup`'s hardware profile now prefers the real host probe over the M1 interim reading whenever `askwell-probe` has run, and carries a new `probe_failed` flag distinct from `floor_met`: the welcome screen states a genuine below-floor reading (concrete per-GB numbers, unchanged) and a detection failure (the `standard` fallback, named as a fallback) as two different sentences rather than one vague warning. Settings gained a hardware-profile section (`web/components/settings/hardware-profile.tsx`, `web/lib/probe.ts`) with a manual override — `POST /probe/override` (`api/src/askwell/probe.py`) writes the same `hardware.profile` setting the probe itself writes, states the consequence before every change, records `profile_overridden` as a hash-chained decision, and never refuses a profile the machine did not measure into. A genuine reprobe still overwrites a stale override the next time it runs. See `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-21, for the full detail. Verified end-to-end against the running stack (`GET /setup`, `GET /probe`, `POST /probe/override` with a bad tier rejected at 400, the decision visible in `audit_decisions` via `scripts/dev.sh psql`) rather than only unit-tested — the API image needed a rebuild (`scripts/dev.sh build-api`) to pick up the change, which is worth remembering next session since the compose file bakes `api/src` into the image rather than mounting it live.

**`M7-LOG-BE-153` is now done too, `0.6.2`** — log storage budget with staged degradation (`docs/audit-log.md` §3). New `api/src/askwell/log_budget.py` measures `audit_interactions` (`pg_total_relation_size`) plus the trace ring buffer against an effective budget — `min(configured cap, 5% of current free disk)`, recomputed on every call rather than snapshotted at install, so a disk filling from outside Askwell tightens the effective budget the same way lowering the setting would (the ticket's own edge case). Three stages: `ok`, `notice` at 80% (surfaced by `GET /log-budget` for `M7-SET-FE-148`, not built yet, to render as the persistent dismissible notice), and `hard_limit` at or over, which the three add-source routes (`POST /sources`, `/sources/dump`, `/sources/connection`) now enforce with a 507 and a stated reason — checked *after* each route's own validation, so a malformed request (bad folder, blank host, unsupported engine) still refuses on those terms alone without ever touching the database, matching what `test_sources_api.py` already asserted before this ticket. Asking a question never calls this check at all, which is the actual mechanism behind "asking keeps working". A budget change (`POST /log-budget`) is a hash-chained decisions record, the same read/write-pair shape `askwell.retrieve`'s threshold setting already established; a stage transition is logged, not recorded as a decision. "Only a decisions-store write failure fails an action" and "traces never fail an action" needed no new code — both are `M0-DATA-OBS-015`'s existing generic behaviour, exercised again here rather than duplicated. Free disk is read from `Settings.trace_dir`'s filesystem — the API and database run in separate containers with no shared mount this module could inspect, and the trace directory is the fairest proxy available; `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning. Verified against a real Postgres, including an HTTP-level test that a refused batch commits zero rows (`api/tests/test_log_budget.py`); not yet exercised against the actual running compose stack's own Postgres, whose migration state on this machine is currently stale for reasons unrelated to this ticket (`alembic_version` points at a revision no longer in history — a pre-existing dev-environment artifact, not touched or caused by this change). **Next: `M7-LOG-BE-154`** (interaction retention window and prune) or `M7-SET-FE-148` (the settings storage screen that is this ticket's own first real consumer) — either is unblocked; `M7-TAURI-DEPLOY-181` (the desktop shell) remains available in parallel, dependencies already satisfied.

**`M7-SET-FE-148` is done too, `0.6.3`** — the settings storage section (`docs/ux/settings.md` §5, `web/components/settings/storage.tsx`). Its own Dependencies named only `M7-LOG-BE-153` and `M1-LIB-FE-050`, both complete, but its Scope asked for two things whose backends `M7-LOG-BE-153`'s own note above already named as separate, unbuilt tickets — `M7-LOG-BE-154` (retention/prune) and `M7-LOG-BE-155` (export). Rather than halting the whole ticket over that gap, the three fully-backed displays were built for real and the two that are not got an honest, visible statement instead of a fake stub: **new** `askwell.sources.storage_by_source` (`GET /sources/storage`) answers per-source index size — chunk content bytes plus `chunk_count × embedding_dimensions × 4` (one float4 lane per embedding dimension), approximate by design and stated as such; `None` ("unknown"), never `0`, for a `connection`/`dump` source (queried in place, never chunked) or one still `queued`/`indexing`. **Issue #484 is closed**: `Usage` gains `configured_bytes` alongside `budget_bytes`, so the settings screen can say "you set X, but 5% of free disk caps it at Y" instead of a number that silently stops moving. The retention window gets a real value and a real decisions record (`log_budget.get_retention_months`/`set_retention_months`, `GET`/`POST /log-budget/retention`, same shape `set_budget` established) with explicit copy that nothing is pruned yet — `M7-LOG-BE-154` will read this same setting rather than invent its own. Export and prune renders as a disabled entry point stating it is not built, the same pattern `settings.md` §3 already uses for Online AI ahead of Phase 7. The at-the-limit statement ("ingestion stops first, asking keeps working") is static and always shown. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including a new issue (#486) filed for the actual cause of `M7-LOG-BE-153`'s own unexercised-against-the-stack note: `compose.yaml` mounts nothing at `/var/lib/askwell`, so `GET /log-budget` 500s against this machine's running containers until that volume exists — worked around by hand (not persisted) to verify the storage section's budget display end-to-end. Verified: `pytest -m requires_db` (18 tests in `test_log_budget.py`, 4 new in `test_storage.py`, all passing against a real Postgres), `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (lint, typecheck, unit tests, build, token hygiene, contrast, offline check — all pass), and a live round trip against the running compose stack for all three new/changed endpoints (`GET`/`POST /log-budget`, `GET`/`POST /log-budget/retention`, `GET /sources/storage`) after rebuilding the API image and the web bundle. **Next: `M7-LOG-BE-154` or `M7-LOG-BE-155`** — either now has its first real settings-screen consumer; `M7-TAURI-DEPLOY-181` remains available in parallel.

**`M7-SET-FE-147` is done too, `0.6.5`** — the settings screen's privacy and security section (`docs/ux/settings.md` §4, `web/components/settings/privacy-security.tsx`). Three displays: the passphrase control (`web/components/settings/passphrase.tsx`), which is exactly the "own ticket" `0.6.4`'s own Next line said `M7-SEC-BE-151`'s frontend needed — set/change/remove, the no-recovery warning stated next to the button rather than in a dialog, strength feedback from the server's own heuristic (`checkStrength`, never reimplemented client-side); network activity (`web/components/settings/network-activity.tsx`, new `web/lib/network.ts`), reading `GET /network`'s live `permitted`/`refused` counts straight from the egress proxy and rendering "unavailable" rather than a false zero when the counters cannot be read; and connected databases (`web/components/settings/connections.tsx`, `M4-CONN-FE-096`), moved into this section from its previous standalone placement on the settings page and demoted from its own `h2` to an `h3` to nest correctly. No new "read-only" field was needed on a connection — `M4-CONN-SEC-097`'s write probe already refuses a write-capable credential before a source is ever created, so every connected database is read-only by construction, which is what the component already said. **There is no web-search setting anywhere on this screen** (`docs/ux/web-search.md` §5) and no control here pre-authorises egress of any kind. Verified against the real running stack after rebuilding both images: `GET /network` returned real, non-fabricated proxy data (`refused: 2`, both against `huggingface.co:443`, `permitted: 0`), `GET /settings/passphrase` returned `{"enabled": false, "locked": false}`, and the rendered `/settings` HTML contains all three new headings under "Privacy and security". `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (lint, typecheck, token hygiene, contrast, offline/C1 check) and `pnpm test` (358 tests) both clean. **Next**: `M7-SEC-BE-152` (corpus encryption) remains the natural passphrase follow-on; issue #504 (the worker process never unlocks) is still unblocked-but-unclaimed.

**M6.5 — it can look outside — complete, 11 of 11** (`docs/backlog/M6.5-it-can-look-outside.md`). `M6.5-WEB-BE-185` builds the provider interface and its fixture implementation; `M6.5-WEB-SEC-187` builds the proxy grant that authorises it; `M6.5-WEB-BE-188` builds the fetch caps, the `<web-content>` delimiter, and the corpus-invariance guarantee; `M6.5-WEB-BE-189` builds the `web_citations` record shape; `M6.5-WEB-FE-190` builds the web results region; `M6.5-WEB-FE-186` builds the escalation offer and its first live caller into `escalate_web_search`; `M6.5-WEB-FE-191` wires a live turn's own generated web answer and mixed-source claims; `M6.5-WEB-FE-192` finishes the offer's remaining states — searching, nothing found, unavailable, closed; `M6.5-EVAL-TEST-194` adds the quality gate's eighth category; `M6.5-WEB-BE-195` is the real provider — see the `0.6.10` through `0.6.19` entries below. **`M6.5-WEB-OBS-193` (this session) closes the milestone**: `askwell.websearch.web_search_trace_steps` appends the escalation's own steps — acceptance, search, one `web_search_fetch` per kept result (flagged with the same `flag_injection_text` heuristic a document candidate already goes through), one `web_search_drop` per dropped result — onto `messages.trace` after the turn's own `abstain` step, through the same fail-open `TraceRing`-then-`messages.trace` write `askwell.ask._run_generation` already uses. New `WebSearchDrop`/`WebSearchOutcome.dropped` so a result the provider returned but never kept (today, always "no usable passage" — `askwell.webfetch.fetch_pages`'s own caps stay unwired, issue #551) is never silently absent. Issue #549 (the declined-offer edge case) resolved as working-as-intended, backlog doc corrected in the same change. Full reasoning, including why "fetched" means what the provider returned rather than a separately fetched page, in `docs/decisions.md`, this date. 11 new tests across `test_websearch.py`/`test_websearch_api.py`. `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (987 unmarked, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (782 passed) both clean; not exercised against the machine's own long-running compose stack, whose Postgres is still on the pre-existing stale migration revision `M7-BACKUP-BE-157`'s entry first flagged (`db upgrade head` fails with `Can't locate revision identified by 'a3c9f5e1d2b7'`), unrelated to this ticket. **Next milestone: M7 — pilot hardening — continues**, 11 tickets landed so far (see the entry above); `M8` (online AI credits) has not started.

## Last completed

**`0.7.9` — `M7-PACK-DEPLOY-141`**: the macOS installer (`deploy/macos/install.sh`/`lib.sh`/`uninstall.sh`), mirroring `M7-PACK-DEPLOY-139`/`140`'s shape with macOS's own two problems named. `check_runtime` creates and starts a Podman machine (`podman machine init`/`start`) when neither exists, since Podman has no native macOS container runtime — detected via `podman machine list --noheading`'s table output rather than `--format`'s Go-template field names, which could not be verified against a real running instance. This was run for real against this build host's own Podman (Linux hosts can create a Podman machine too, even though Linux's own installer never uses one): `init`/`start` correctly exercised the detection functions before failing on a host-specific missing `gvproxy` binary, unrelated to the installer's own correctness. `check_roots_mount` names, informationally, that the machine only sees `$HOME` by default and an external volume needs the machine recreated with `--volume` — the macOS-specific cause for the `not_mounted` state `docs/decisions.md` (2026-08-27) already accepted for every platform, not a new enforcement mechanism. The ticket text's own signing/notarisation premise is stale against the settled 2026-08-26 unsigned-distribution decision; `install.sh` never checks or claims a signature. 36 unit tests (`deploy/macos/install.test.sh`, run directly under this Linux build host's bash — no macOS-only syscalls in anything under test) pass. No real macOS walkthrough was run (issue #592, same shape as #590/#559). `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including a small, corrected-mid-session overstatement about `M7-TAURI-FE-182`'s macOS permission-explanation UI (it works; only `Info.plist`'s system-dialog wording is missing, issue #593).

**`0.7.8` — `M7-PACK-DEPLOY-140`**: the Windows installer (`deploy/windows/install.ps1`/`lib.ps1`/`uninstall.ps1`), mirroring `M7-PACK-DEPLOY-139`'s Linux shape with Windows' own problems named. Checks virtualisation (parsed from `systeminfo`'s "Hyper-V Requirements" text, not a guessed CIM property name) before Podman, since WSL2 is the only way Podman runs containers on Windows and a missing firmware setting is refused by name with what to enable — Askwell cannot flip it itself. Refuses before copying on insufficient disk space, an old Podman, or a computed install path past Windows' 260-character `MAX_PATH`; refuses to attempt a Podman install without administrator rights rather than silently failing partway. Every copied file is verified with a post-copy `Test-Path`, naming antivirus quarantine as the likely cause for the native inference binary and the unsigned shell specifically when one goes missing. Writes a Start-menu entry and a Startup-folder shortcut (session start), an `HKCU` uninstall registry entry, and the same `change-me*`-replacing `.env` fix as issue #584 (`Set-AskwellEnvPasswords`, Windows' side of that fix). Registered-root path handling across the WSL2 boundary — a changed drive letter reporting the root `unavailable`, a too-long path failing per file — is existing `M1-ADD-ING-021` logic (`roots.py`, `extract_common.py`), exercised rather than rebuilt; `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §3 now names both Windows triggers explicitly. 33 unit tests (`deploy/windows/install.test.ps1`, run via `pwsh` on this Linux build host) pass, and all three scripts parse with no syntax errors — but **no real Windows walkthrough was run**: this build host has neither a Windows machine nor a Windows VM, so `Test-AskwellRuntime` (shells to `systeminfo`/`winget`), the real file copies, and `Register-AskwellShortcuts` (a Windows-only `WScript.Shell` COM object) were never exercised end to end. Issue #590 tracks that gap the same way #559 already tracks Linux's; #559 itself (no CI job builds `askwell-shell.exe`) is unchanged and now named as blocking Windows too. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why `systeminfo` text parsing was chosen over a `Get-ComputerInfo` property this session could not verify against a real Windows machine.

**`0.7.6` — `M7-TAURI-FE-182`**: native file dialogs for root registration, relocating a moved document, and the add-source screen's browse alternative — rebuilt from scratch this session, since the branch this ticket had previously been built on had been reset to `main` and none of that code (nor the tests a prior closing comment claimed) survived. `web/src-tauri/src/main.rs` gains five commands: `pick_folder`/`pick_file`/`pick_files` (native dialogs) and `list_dir`/`read_head` (scoped filesystem reads), granted to the shell's one origin in `capabilities/default.json` (`remote.urls` now `http://127.0.0.1:*` only, dropping an unused `localhost` entry). `list_dir`/`read_head` refuse any path outside `AllowedRoots` — the set of paths a dialog has actually returned this session — fixing issue #580 (an earlier attempt had no such check at all). `path_string()` refuses a genuinely non-UTF-8 path at the dialog itself rather than mangling it with `to_string_lossy()`, resolving issue #499's finding the same way. `web/lib/native.ts` wraps the bridge, talking to `globalThis.__TAURI_INTERNALS__` directly rather than `@tauri-apps/api` (that package's `invoke()` hardcodes `window`, which this repo's jsdom-free `node --test` runner does not have — see `docs/decisions.md`). `web/lib/selection.ts`'s new `fromNativeFolder`/`fromNativeFiles` reuse `add-source.ts`'s existing `flatten`/`MAX_FILES` walk, so a native folder pick is counted and capped exactly like a browser drop. Wired into `components/settings/folders.tsx`, `components/documents/viewer-shared.tsx`'s `MovedFileNotice`, and `components/add/add-screen.tsx`'s `FilesRoute` — each keeps its typed-path browser fallback. `docs/ux/add-source.md` §7 gained the macOS permission-prompt copy the ticket's own edge case names, rendered verbatim in `folders.tsx`. 24 new tests (`native.test.ts`, `selection.test.ts`), all passing alongside the existing 411; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint .` both clean (0 errors). **Not run end-to-end** — no system webview libraries and no `sudo` to install them on this host (issue #497, reaffirmed this session, still open), so `cargo check`/`cargo test` cannot execute; confidence instead comes from `rustc --edition 2021 --emit=metadata`, which parses the whole file with zero syntax errors, only expected unresolved-crate ones. Issues #579 and #580 closed with real fixes; #497 and #498 (Windows/macOS path translation, still unverified) re-affirmed and left open — both need hardware this session does not have. **Next:** whoever next has a working Rust toolchain with the system webview libraries should run `docs/manual-tests/M7-TAURI-FE-182.md` end to end and close #497 for real; `M7-TAURI-DEPLOY-183` (process supervision) remains available in parallel.

**`0.7.5` — `M7-PERF-TEST-167`**: measure the answer-path performance budgets (`docs/ux/ask.md` §7) on a realistic corpus, per profile. New `eval/fixtures/generate_perf_corpus.py` (a synthetic multi-page-PDF corpus sized for latency measurement — default 80 documents, each carrying one invented checkable fact, larger and purpose-built rather than the `grounded_qa.v1` fixture corpus reused past its own size) and `eval/answer_latency.py`, which measures the four budgets — first-step label, first token, full-answer p50/p95 — as this script's own wall clock against `POST /ask`'s real SSE stream (deliberately unlike `eval/voice_latency.py`, which reads the server's own stage timings exclusively; the budgets here are phrased "of submit"/"within Ns", which means what a person watching the screen experiences, not a component's internal duration), plus a per-stage `retrieve`/`compose` breakdown read from the server's own `GET /ask/{id}/trace` (`message_id` taken straight off the `done` event's own payload — present on every event already). Ingestion throughput is measured through the real `POST /roots`/`POST /sources` flow against `GET /ingest`'s own queue estimate, the same estimate a real user sees (`askwell.ingest._estimate`). `scripts/dev.sh answer-latency` runs it against the live stack. Exits non-zero with no results file written when nothing could be honestly measured, and a `done` event is never treated as success on its own — `status`/`reason` are read and a `failed` turn is a measured failure, not a silent pass — both built in from this script's first commit rather than retrofitted, a direct fix for issue #572 (filed against a since-discarded, uncommitted earlier draft of this same harness that shipped both bugs).

**Found and fixed a live blocker while building this**: `inference-bridge` in `compose.yaml` was crash-looping (`RestartCount` in the tens of thousands) from the identical `ConfigurationError` issue #439 already named for `egress-proxy` — `Settings.sandbox_database_url`/`sandbox_owner_password`/`sandbox_readonly_password` required unconditionally, none set on that service's block. With no working inference socket there was no real generation to measure at all; fixed the same way as `egress-proxy`/`voice` (three `:?`-guarded vars added), verified live (`RestartCount=0`, `/health` reports `inference: reachable`). `egress-proxy` itself was already fixed on this branch (found clean, closed issue #492 as resolved — the uncommitted workaround it described was gone).

**Not measured end-to-end against a live stack this session.** Running the finished, tested harness for real hit two genuine environment blockers on this specific shared dev machine, neither a harness bug: (1) content encryption is enabled and locked (`GET /settings/passphrase` → `{"enabled": true, "locked": true}`) with a passphrase this session does not hold, from an earlier, unrelated session — every one of the 40 freshly generated documents failed ingestion with `Locked: A passphrase is set.`; (2) with `--skip-ingest` against the 19 sources already on this machine (accumulated by other tickets' own manual testing), a real `POST /ask` composed a real prompt that the native inference process refused outright — `request (14232 tokens) exceeds the available context size (8192 tokens)` — a genuine finding (retrieval-composed context exceeding the configured window once a corpus accumulates), not a hang or a timeout. Both are recorded as issue #576, with a recommendation to use a disposable environment for future perf runs so unrelated tickets' state stops contaminating this measurement. **A still-valid prior measurement exists on this same machine and profile, from before it accumulated that state**: `eval/results/answer-latency-standard-20260921T101607Z.json` (2026-09-21, a since-discarded harness version, real numbers, not reproduced by the code now in the repo) — `standard` profile, 40-document corpus, cold full-answer p50 27.6s / p95 101.6s, warm p50 26.4s / p95 100.9s (**both miss** the 20s/60s budgets), first-step label median 9.4ms (well inside the 400ms budget), dominant stage `retrieve` on every miss, first-token never scored in that run either (the exact gap issue #572 names — this session's rewrite fixes the SSE parsing bug that likely caused it, timing `step`/`token` inline as each event arrives rather than after a buffered parse, but that fix was not re-verified against a real scored `first_token` this session, since no clean run completed). `docs/success-metrics.md` §2 updated to cite these numbers rather than stay `*assumed*`.

Full reasoning for the wall-clock-vs-server-timing split in `docs/decisions.md`, this date. 12 new pure tests (`eval/tests/test_answer_latency.py` — SSE event-kind pairing, percentile summarization, stage attribution, budget verdict; no running stack, matching `eval/tests/test_voice_latency.py`'s own split), run via `python -m pytest eval/tests/test_answer_latency.py -c eval/pyproject.toml` since `eval/` has no test entry in `scripts/dev.sh` or CI, same precedent as every other `eval/tests/*` module. `scripts/dev.sh run ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean on both new files (`eval/` is outside `mypy`'s `files = ["src"]` scope, same as every other `eval/*.py`). Issues #491 (harness never run) and #572 (the two bugs) are resolved by this ticket's own account above rather than closed outright — #572's fixes are real and unit-tested but not yet proven against a real scored `first_token`, and #491's "run it for real" is only half-satisfied (the harness ran for real and correctly refused to claim a measurement it did not have); leaving both open with this comment rather than closing on partial evidence. #576 (this session's own environment blockers) filed fresh. **Next**: get the passphrase unlocked or the shared machine reset (issue #576), then re-run `scripts/dev.sh answer-latency` for a clean, current-code measurement — that is the direct, only remaining step to close #491/#572 for good.

**`0.7.4` — `M7-BACKUP-BE-158`**: restore, with the re-embed cost stated at the moment it matters (`docs/ux/settings.md` §6/§9). `askwell.restore` reads a `askwell.backup` artefact back onto a machine — `POST /restore/inspect` states version, chunk count and re-embed cost before anyone commits; `POST /restore` refuses a newer artefact by name (`VersionRefused`), refuses to merge into existing corpus/memory data unless `replace_existing` is set (`ExistingDataPresent`), and refuses a passphrase-protected backup with no passphrase (`PassphraseRequired`). **Two architecture gaps surfaced only while building this, both closed inline rather than left as guesses**: (1) `askwell_app` — what the API/worker processes connect as — has no DDL grant at all, so restore cannot run `alembic upgrade head` itself (as the ticket's own AC wording implies); `_check_schema_current` instead refuses plainly, naming `scripts/dev.sh db upgrade head`, resolving issue #570 as "requires current" rather than "applies migrations." (2) `askwell_app` has no `TRUNCATE`/`DELETE`/`UPDATE` grant on `audit_decisions`/`audit_interactions` (C6) — `replace_existing` clears every other table with `DELETE` (no `TRUNCATE` grant anywhere either) but never touches the audit tables, which are always restored by appending; `enqueue` writes no decisions record (only `run_job`, at the end, after the restored chain) because writing one at intent time gave a clean machine's `audit_decisions` a second hash-chain root and broke the ticket's own "confirm the chain is intact" golden path. **Passphrase-protected backups needed a new `askwell.backup` capability to be restorable at all**: `install_secret_wrapped`, the install secret encrypted with a key derived from the passphrase alone (`crypto.derive_key(b"", passphrase)`), carried in the manifest — without it, a backup's content, encrypted under `derive_key(install_secret, passphrase)`, is permanently undecryptable on any machine with a different (freshly generated) install secret. `askwell.backup.enqueue` gained a required-when-protected `backup_passphrase` parameter (backward-incompatible with the shape `M7-BACKUP-BE-157` shipped one session earlier — necessary, since a protected backup with no wrapped secret could never have been restored regardless of when this was found). Resume is per-table, not per-row: each table restores in its own transaction, `tables_done` gates a retry so a crash never re-attempts a table already committed — the fix for issue #571 (previously: any `replace_existing=False` retry after a crash mid-restore raised a unique-violation on the first row and could never finish). Re-embedding resumes for free via `WHERE embedding IS NULL`. `Source` ORM model gained `deleted_at`/`last_healthy_at` (issue #569 — real columns on the live table, invisible to `Base.metadata` before this). New `RestoreJob` (`api/src/askwell/db/models.py`), migration `e8b3f61a92d4` (`restore_jobs`, `backup_jobs.install_secret_wrapped`); new `restore_job` arq task wired into `worker.startup` (a passphrase-protected resumed job returns to `queued` but does not self-redispatch — the passphrase was never persisted). Full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, this date. 22 new tests (`api/tests/test_restore.py`, `requires_db`, including a genuine two-install-secret round trip and a crash-simulated per-table resume), 2 `test_backup.py` tests updated/added for the new required passphrase. Verified: `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (819 passed, full suite), `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (lint/format/typecheck/993 unmarked clean), and against the machine's own rebuilt compose stack — `scripts/dev.sh db upgrade head` applied cleanly to `e8b3f61a92d4` (the stale-migration drift every recent entry above flagged is gone), the worker restarted with no `worker_resume_deferred` and `restore_job` registered among its 15 functions, and `POST /backup`/`POST /restore/inspect`/`GET /restore/{id}` all returned the expected real HTTP responses (422/404) against the live session-cookied API. Issues #570, #571, #569 resolved and closed by this ticket; #518 (unmeasured re-embed constant) re-owned — still open, still correctly out of this ticket's scope per its own original recommendation, commented rather than silently left. **Next**: `M7-BACKUP-TEST-159` (tested restore, every release) is unblocked. Frontend for backup/restore in settings has not been started.

**`0.7.2` — `M7-LOG-BE-154`**: interaction retention window and prune (`docs/audit-log.md` §8). New `askwell.log_prune`: `enqueue` reads `askwell.log_budget.get_retention_months` (already a real setting since `M7-SET-FE-148`, previously unread by anything), computes a cutoff with calendar-month subtraction (stdlib only, no `dateutil` dependency), and refuses in two ways — `PruneNotExported` unless a completed, unfiltered (`since IS NULL`) `export_jobs` row already covers the cutoff, and `PruneRequiresConfirmation` if the window would remove ≥90% of what exists, unless `acknowledged_nearly_everything` is set — mirroring `askwell.log_export.ExportNotAcknowledged`'s "state it once, then allow it" shape. Neither refusal fires when nothing is prunable at all, so a fresh install or a window that keeps everything is never told to go export first. The delete (`DELETE FROM audit_interactions WHERE occurred_at < cutoff`) and the `interactions_pruned` decisions record naming the range and `boundary_hash` (the last deleted record's own hash, which is also the `prev_hash` every surviving record still chains to) are one transaction — same job-table-plus-`arq` shape as `askwell.log_export`/`askwell.backup` (`prune_jobs`, `PruneJob`, `resume`/`dispatch`/`run_job`, `POST`/`GET /log-prune`), and a worker killed mid-run leaves the chain exactly as it was, so a resumed retry is not distinguishable from running once. **`askwell.audit.verify` gained `prune_boundaries`** (issue #516, filed against this exact gap while `M7-LOG-BE-155` was being built): a chain that does not start at `GENESIS` is checked against every `interactions_pruned` boundary before being reported as `MISSING_GENESIS`, and verifies intact with an explanatory note when one matches — `askwell-verify` (`audit.main`) now reads these boundaries itself, so the command needed no new flag. Decisions and memory are never touched — enforced by construction, every statement in `run_job` names `audit_interactions` explicitly. Full reasoning, including the rejected re-anchor-genesis-with-a-marker-record alternative, in `docs/decisions.md`, this date.
