New `PruneJob` (`api/src/askwell/db/models.py`), migration `f2c7d4e1a683`. 19 new tests (`api/tests/test_log_prune.py`) plus 3 new `verify`/`prune_boundaries` tests in `api/tests/test_audit_chain.py`; `test_models.py`'s documented-table list updated for `prune_jobs`. Verified: `pytest -m requires_db` (full suite, 804 passed, including a genuinely crash-simulated resume test and a real hash-chain round trip through `verify`), `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (993 unmarked passed, 1 skipped, lint/format/typecheck all clean), and the app imports cleanly with `/log-prune`/`/log-prune/{job_id}` registered against a rebuilt `api` image (`scripts/dev.sh build-api`). **Not exercised against this machine's own long-running compose stack** — its Postgres is still on the pre-existing stale migration revision `M7-BACKUP-BE-157`'s entry first flagged (`db upgrade head` fails with `Can't locate revision identified by 'a3c9f5e1d2b7'`), unrelated to this ticket and not touched here. Issue #516 is resolved by this ticket, commented and closed. Issue #487 (the settings storage "log over budget" state not prominent) is unrelated to this ticket's own backend scope — re-owned with a comment pointing at `M7-SET-FE-148`/a future frontend ticket, not resolved here. **Next**: the frontend ticket that wires Export and prune's settings-screen entry point to the real `/log-export`/`/log-prune` endpoints is the natural follow-on — not yet filed as a numbered ticket in the backlog docs this session touched.

**`0.7.3` — `M7-LOG-FE-156`**: the settings-screen verifier `askwell.audit`'s own docstring flagged as missing ("The settings surface for this arrives in M7. Until then it is a command."). New `askwell.log_verify.run` calls `askwell.audit.verify` for both stores in one request, records a `log_verification_run` decision naming either store's break where one exists, and is exposed as `GET /log-verify` — synchronous, deliberately: unlike export/backup/prune, a read that commits nothing has no state a crash would leave worth resuming, so this is not a fourth `arq` job table. `VerificationResult` gained `broken_at` (the broken record's own `occurred_at`) so a break names both the record and its date, which is also now in `askwell-verify`'s own `__str__`. New `web/components/settings/verify-log.tsx`: a "Verify the log" action with an elapsed-time counter and a Stop button (`AbortController`, honest for a read — nothing was written to undo), and a plain per-store report that never uses the word "immutable" and always states that Askwell never rewrites history, so a break means something outside it changed the file. A chain starting after a legitimate prune (`M7-LOG-BE-154`) reports intact with the boundary explained. Full reasoning for the synchronous-not-a-job-table choice, including what would have to change to reverse it, in `docs/decisions.md`, this date. 21 new tests (`api/tests/test_log_verify.py`, `requires_db`, unit-level plus one HTTP round trip). Verified: `pytest -m requires_db` (full suite, 809 passed), `scripts/dev.sh check` (993 passed, 1 skipped, lint/format/typecheck clean), `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (full frontend gate, including the offline/C1 check — clean after fixing a `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint error the first draft's elapsed-timer effect triggered). **Exercised against the real, rebuilt, running compose stack** (this machine's Postgres migration state is current again — the staleness the previous two entries flagged is no longer present, unremarked cause, not investigated further here): `GET /log-verify` over HTTP with a real session cookie, cross-checked against `askwell-verify` run inside the `api` container — both reported the identical break, same record id and date, in this machine's own `audit_decisions` chain; the rebuilt web bundle was confirmed to contain and serve the new component. **That break is real and pre-existing, not caused by this ticket** — filed as issue #565 rather than silently worked around, since the honest description until someone confirms its cause is "broken," not "known test artifact," even though the likely cause is an earlier session's own manual verification walkthrough (several entries above describe altering a record by hand to prove `verify` catches it). No browser click-through was performed — no interactive browser tool was available in this session; the curl-plus-bundle-grep check above and the full `web-check`/`check` gates are what stands in for it, stated plainly rather than claimed as more than it is. **Next**: `M7-TAURI-DEPLOY-181` (the desktop shell) remains available; the settings-screen wiring for export/prune noted above is still unclaimed.

**`0.7.1` — `M7-SET-BE-145a`**: a user-supplied model, and which model answered — the settings section `M7-SET-FE-146` will build and the persistent marker `M7-SET-FE-146a` will build both now have a real backend, and the false dependency cycle between those two tickets is corrected (this ticket, then `146`, then `146a`). New `askwell.model_select`: `validate_model_file` checks a candidate path is absolute, readable and starts with the GGUF magic bytes — rejected at selection with the reason, never at the next question — and is never described as "validated" the way a shipped model is, since that is C9's registry check and this is a file the user placed themselves. `select_user_model` swaps it against the host inference supervisor over a new file-signal seam in the same run directory `state.json`/the inference socket already live in (`model-swap-request.json`/`model-swap-result.json`, the same shape `askwell.model_download`'s `fetch-request.json`/`fetch-progress.json` already established), holding every permit of `askwell.ask.generation_semaphore` for the swap's duration so a turn already answering finishes on the model it started with and a new question queues behind the swap. `deploy/inference/askwell-inference`'s `Supervisor` gained `swap_model`: it terminates the running `llama-server` child and lets its own `supervise()` loop bring the new one up — the same path a crash already takes, but not counted as one (`consecutive` untouched) — and on a failed load, restores the previous model and names why in the result the API is waiting on, never leaving the role down for a swap that did not work. Persisted in `settings` (`model.user_model_path`, and `model.active_source` — set only by this module, at the moment a swap actually succeeds or fails, never guessed from the loaded file's name per the ticket's own Validation Rule; full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, this date) and reapplied automatically at startup (`reapply_user_model`, backgrounded so a five-minute cold-boot load on a light profile does not make every restart wait twice). New `messages.model_identity` column (migration `a1c2e5f6b3d4`, `NOT NULL` with a server default distinguishing "predates this column" from "no model was loaded"), stamped at `POST /ask`'s own pending-row insert and at `voice_tts._insert_pending_answer`, both at the same insert `askwell.ask.reconcile_interrupted` already depends on existing before generation starts. New `GET /model`/`POST /model/select`. A model larger than the probed hardware (`askwell.probe`, falling back to `askwell.hardware.probe()` when no real probe has run) is accepted with the consequence stated in `SwapOutcome.size_warning`, never silently refused. 19 new tests: `api/tests/test_model_select.py` (`requires_db` — validation, a successful swap's persistence and decisions record, a failed swap leaving the setting untouched, the four `active_model_identity` readings, reapply-on-restart success and failure) and `api/tests/test_model_swap_host.py` (the supervisor script loaded and exercised directly, `start_once`/`wait_until_ready`/`drain` faked so nothing spawns a real `llama-server` — the guard rails on `swap_model`, a successful swap driven through a real `supervise()` loop, a failed one restoring the previous model, and the watcher's own file-signal round trip). `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` both clean (997 unmarked, 1 skipped; 815 passed). **Not verified against the running compose stack**: this environment has no real GGUF model or GPU, and the stack's own Postgres is still on the pre-existing stale migration revision noted below, unrelated to this ticket — the manual walkthrough (select a second model file, ask a question, restart, confirm both answers record which model produced them) needs real hardware. **Next**: `M7-SET-FE-146` (the settings screen that calls `GET /model`/`POST /model/select`), then `M7-SET-FE-146a` (the persistent per-answer marker) — in that order, as this ticket's own Context section requires.

**`0.6.19` — `M6.5-WEB-BE-195`**: the real provider behind `M6.5-WEB-BE-185`'s interface. New `askwell.websearch.DDGSWebSearchProvider` — `ddgs` (MIT, keyless), pinned to its single `duckduckgo` backend rather than `"auto"` so the egress grant (`M6.5-WEB-SEC-187`) still only ever needs one destination; that backend's search URL, `https://html.duckduckgo.com/html/`, turned out to already be `Settings.web_search_destination_host`'s value, so the grant needed no change at all. `ddgs` is synchronous, so `search` runs it via `asyncio.to_thread`; the proxy is passed to `ddgs.DDGS(proxy=...)` explicitly (built from `Settings.egress_proxy_host`/`_port`) rather than relying on `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY`, which `ddgs` never reads itself — confirmed by reading `ddgs` 9.16.0's own source, not assumed. `_PROVIDERS`' factory signature changed from `Callable[[], WebSearchProvider]` to `Callable[[Settings], WebSearchProvider]` to hand the new provider its settings; `FixtureWebSearchProvider`'s factory and one test double in `test_websearch_api.py` (`_patch_results`) both needed their lambda arity updated to match — caught by `scripts/dev.sh test-db`, not by the unmarked suite, since the break was in an HTTP-level test.

**Passages are `ddgs`'s own search snippets, not fetched page content.** `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-08-26 entry for `M6.5-WEB-BE-188` had planned for this ticket to call `askwell.webfetch.fetch_pages` on the result URLs and widen the egress grant around that fetch too. It does not: `ddgs`'s `"auto"` mode (and, one level further out, the actual result URLs a search returns) both need a destination list an exact-match, one-string-per-turn grant cannot express, and widening that grant is a real change to C1's sole enforcement point that does not belong in a 3–4 hour backend ticket. Pinning to `duckduckgo` kept the single-destination design correct for the *search* call; fetching result pages is deferred to issue #551, with the redesign options laid out there. Full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-22 (superseding the `188` entry's plan on this one point).

**Also resolved, all named in this ticket's own "definition of done":** issue #545 (compose.yaml never passed the four `ASKWELL_WEB_SEARCH_*` vars through — fixed, `${VAR:-default}` pattern matching every other setting in that block) and issue #529 (backlog docs' stale `M6.5-WEB-BLOCKED-195` references — `docs/backlog/README.md` and `docs/backlog/M6.5-it-can-look-outside.md` corrected; the historical entries in `docs/decisions.md` itself are left as written, since that log is append-only and accurate to when they were written). Issue #548 (a hardcoded `fixture` default in `compose.yaml`) turned out not to exist on `main` — its only trace was an unmerged, explicitly-marked "not accepted" WIP commit — so there was nothing to fix; commented and closed as not reproducible. Issue #538 (fetch attempts only `structlog`-logged, not audited) is unaffected by this ticket, since nothing here calls `fetch_pages` — re-owned, unresolved, tracked in #551 alongside the fetch-pages wiring it depends on.

**Verified against the real running stack**, not just unit tests: rebuilt the api image, set `ASKWELL_WEB_SEARCH_PROVIDER=ddgs` in `.env`, and called `escalate_web_search` directly inside the running `api` container. A real question returned ten real Wikipedia results with real domains, titles, URLs and retrieval timestamps; the grant opened and closed around the call (visible in the container's own logs and in `audit_interactions`). Calling the provider's `search()` directly, bypassing the grant, was refused by the proxy with `403` — confirms the destination stays closed outside an authorised turn. Stopping the `egress-proxy` container and repeating the escalation produced `status="unavailable"` with a DNS-resolution reason, not a crash, and it is still on the audit record — the "cable unplugged" acceptance criterion. `scripts/dev.sh check` and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` both clean after the fix above (983 + 802 passed across the two runs, matching this ticket's own scope).

**Next**: `M6.5-WEB-OBS-193` (trace flagging for fetched web content) is the milestone's last unbuilt ticket. Issue #551 (fetching real page content behind a web result) is the natural, non-blocking follow-on if snippet-only answers turn out to be a real quality problem in practice.

**`0.6.18` — `M6.5-EVAL-TEST-194`**: the quality gate's eighth category, web escalation discipline — 10 tasks, pass bar **1.00, no exceptions** (`docs/build-plan.md`; gate total now 165 tasks across eight categories). New `eval/web_escalation.py` (`mode: "web_escalation"`, wired into `eval/suite.py`, `eval/bench.py`, `.github/workflows/eval.yml`'s suite loop) drives a real `askwell.ask` turn per task against the fixture corpus, same as `eval.abstain`, over five scenarios a new `Task.web_escalation_setup` field selects: `plain` (a near-miss or half-covered corpus — both take the same below-threshold path), `repeat` (the same question twice in one conversation), `after_accept` (a second, different question in a conversation where an earlier question's escalation was already accepted — `websearch.escalate_web_search` is called once here, explicitly, to set up that prior state, never as the automatic turn under test), `after_stop` (a question in a conversation whose previous turn is on the record as `stopped`), and `retrieval_error` (`askwell.ask.retrieve` monkeypatched to raise for one call). **The primary, pass/fail assertion is the egress proxy's own `permitted` counter** (`askwell.network.read_activity`), read before and after every run — a nonzero delta fails the run outright, before the turn's own text is even considered, because `FixtureWebSearchProvider` never touches a real socket either way and a text-only check cannot tell "never escalated" from "escalated to a fixture that happened not to reach a network." Full reasoning — why five scenarios, why the proxy counter and not the answer text, why `M7-QA-TEST-168`'s "register in the release checklist" dependency is deferred (that ticket doesn't exist yet) — in `docs/decisions.md`, this date.

Scoring itself is a pure function, `web_escalation_run_score`, unit-tested without a database: a nonzero proxy delta fails regardless of what the scenario observed; a zero delta with broken discipline (an un-abstained answer, a turn that didn't fail cleanly on a broken retrieval path) still fails; only a zero delta with preserved discipline passes. 6 new tests in `eval/tests/test_web_escalation.py`, plus suite-shape assertions (10 tasks, pass_bar 1.0, strict, all five setups covered, an unknown `web_escalation_setup` rejected at load).

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (977 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged — this ticket adds no code under `api/src/`). `eval`'s own suite (`scripts/dev.sh run pytest /app/eval/tests -c /app/eval/pyproject.toml`) — the new file's 6 tests pass in isolation and alongside `test_suite.py`/`test_abstain.py` (36 passed); the five `test_runner.py` errors (`sandbox_database_url`/`sandbox_owner_password`/`sandbox_readonly_password` missing) are pre-existing in this environment — confirmed identical on `main` before this change via `git stash` — and unrelated to this ticket. **Not verified**: a live run of `eval/bench.py --suite web_escalation.v1` against a running local model, stack, and egress proxy — this environment has neither the native inference process nor the proxy's Redis counters reachable, the same gap named in every prior eval-suite ticket's own BRAIN entry; the suite's own testing notes (cold start, disconnect the network, run the suite, read the proxy's permitted count, deliberately add an automatic-fetch-on-thin-retrieval regression and confirm the suite catches it, revert) are written for the self-hosted runner or a maintainer's own machine to carry out. **Next**: nothing named by this ticket blocks; `M6.5-WEB-BE-195` or `M6.5-WEB-OBS-193` remain open in the milestone.

**`0.6.17` — `M6.5-WEB-FE-192`**: the escalation offer's remaining states — searching, nothing found, unavailable, and the escalation closing (`docs/ux/web-search.md` §4, `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §1/§7.1, C10). `EscalationOffer` (`ask-screen.tsx`) now tracks `phase` (`"idle"`/`"sending"`/`"settled"`) and the raw `outcome` rather than a single `webStatus` string, and three new pure functions in `web/lib/web-search.ts` turn that into what renders: `webSearchDisplayStatus` collapses them into `"idle"`/`"sending"`/`"answered"`/`"nothing_found"`/`"unavailable"` — critically, a settled `"ok"` outcome with `answerText === null` (every fetched result dropped by `M6.5-WEB-BE-188`'s caps) reads as `"nothing_found"`, never `"answered"`, which is this ticket's own edge case ("reads as nothing usable came back, not as an answer"); `webSearchStatusMessage` carries the fixed copy, including `docs/web-search.md` §6's `"I can't reach the web right now."` verbatim for `unavailable`, distinct from `"Nothing found on the web either."` for `nothing_found`; `webSearchShowsClosedNote` is true only once a search has actually settled, so the closing note never appears after an ordinary turn or while one is still in flight — "only turns that escalated get one."

`escalateWebSearch` gained an optional `signal` parameter. `EscalationOffer` now shows a `Stop` control beside the named-progress line while `"sending"`, wired to an `AbortController`: aborting disconnects the fetch, which is what lets `askwell.websearch`'s cancelled-coroutine path (`M6.5-WEB-SEC-187`, already built to handle exactly this) close the egress grant, and the offer reverts to `"idle"` rather than reporting `"unavailable"` for a search the user chose to stop — `AbortError` is caught and handled separately from a real provider failure. `CollapsedTurn` gained a `WebMarker`: `--inferred`, never `--provenance`, a dashed ring rather than `SourceCountBadge`'s filled dot so the two remain distinguishable even in greyscale (`design-system.md` §8) — shown whenever `turn.webCitations.length > 0`, satisfying `conversation.md` §5's "keeps its web marker when collapsed." Confirmed with a live agent (`Explore`) that no conversation-history/reload endpoint exists yet (issue #156) — every `AskTurn`, including `webCitations`, is in-memory for the current session only, so this marker persisting past a full page reload is out of reach until that endpoint exists; not attempted here as it is outside this ticket's own frontend scope.

12 new tests in `lib/web-search.test.ts` (all unmarked, pure): the five `webSearchDisplayStatus` branches including the caps-dropped-everything case, the two fixed-copy assertions, `webSearchShowsClosedNote`'s five branches, `webSearchOptionCost`'s five-way distinctness, and one confirming `escalateWebSearch` actually forwards its `signal` to `fetch`. `scripts/dev.sh web-check`-equivalent (lint, typecheck, 387 unmarked tests, build, token hygiene, contrast, offline/C1 scan) all clean.

**Not verified against a live escalation on the real running stack** — the sandbox this session ran in has no working native inference (`ConnectError` on every `/ask`), so no turn can reach a real abstention to escalate from at all; confirmed instead that `GET /settings/web-search` correctly reports `available: true` against the real API once `ASKWELL_WEB_SEARCH_PROVIDER=fixture` actually reaches the container (see below) — a real end-to-end walkthrough of searching/nothing-found/unavailable is deferred to whoever next has a working inference model. **Filed issue #545**: while trying that verification, found `compose.yaml`'s `api` service never passes `ASKWELL_WEB_SEARCH_PROVIDER`/`_DESTINATION_HOST`/`_DESTINATION_PORT`/`_GRANT_TTL_SECONDS` through at all — every `.env` setting for this feature has been a no-op since `M6.5-WEB-BE-185`, independent of anything this ticket touches. Verified locally by temporarily adding the missing `environment:` line and reverting before committing (not part of this diff).

**`0.6.16` — `M6.5-WEB-FE-191`**: mixed answers — each claim points at its own kind of source (`docs/ux/web-search.md` §3, `docs/web-search.md` §4, C10). `POST /ask/{message_id}/escalate/web` now generates the escalation's own answer once `escalate_web_search` returns something usable, rather than reporting only a status and a count — new `askwell.websearch.compose_and_generate_web_answer` and `askwell.agent.compose.compose_web_answer`/`delimit_web_results`. A **second, narrower generation call**, not a re-run of the turn's own: it reuses the existing `answer_composition.v1.md` system prompt and its `<web-content>` section (written by `M6.5-WEB-BE-188`, never previously called) with no `<retrieved-content>` at all, because the original turn's document candidates were never persisted past that request. Claim ordinals inside that call are local (1-based); the endpoint offsets them by `len(segment_claims(<the turn's current stored content>))` before writing `web_citations` and returning them, and appends the generated text to `messages.content` so a second escalation on the same turn continues the same ordinal space rather than colliding with the first. Full reasoning, including why this was not folded into `_run_generation`, in `docs/decisions.md`, this date.

New on the frontend: `AskTurn.webAnswer`/`webCitations` (`ask-state.tsx`), deliberately separate from `answer` so an abstention's own text is never displaced once a web answer exists beside it — `isAbstained` is unaffected. `WebResultsRegion` (`M6.5-WEB-FE-190`, built but never called until now) is wired in under both `AbstentionState` and a partial answer's `AnsweredContent`, via a new shared `WebAnswerBlock`. `AnswerProse`/`ClaimSpan` gained an `ordinalOffset` and a second, independent hover-pairing path — new `web/components/ask/web-pairing.tsx` (`useLiveWebPairs`/`useWebRaised`), deliberately its own file sharing no implementation with `provenance-margin.tsx`, per `web-result.tsx`'s own standing rule. A web claim raises only its own result card and a document claim raises only its own margin card, true by construction (a claim ordinal only ever appears in one of the two pairing lists) rather than by a check either hook makes — `ClaimSpan`'s `raised` is `useRaised(key) || useWebRaised(key)` with both hooks called unconditionally (an earlier draft short-circuited the second call with `||` directly on the hook invocations and ESLint's `rules-of-hooks` caught it). A web result supporting an already-documented claim still never joins the margin — it has no code path into `turn.citations` at all. `web/lib/web-citations.ts` gained `WebResult.claimOrdinals` and `applyWebCitation` (groups by URL, mirroring `applyCitation`). `web/lib/web-search.ts`'s `escalateWebSearch` now returns `answerText`/`citations` alongside the existing status fields; its docstring's stale claim that rendering results was `M6.5-WEB-FE-192`'s job is corrected.

New tests: 7 in `api/tests/test_compose.py` (`delimit_web_results`, `compose_web_answer`), 4 in `api/tests/test_websearch.py` (`compose_and_generate_web_answer` — text and local citations, ordinals stay local not offset, an out-of-range index is skipped not raised, degrades to `None` on `InferenceUnavailable`), 2 new `requires_db` tests in `api/tests/test_websearch_api.py` (an abstained turn with results gets a generated answer, `messages.content` and `web_citations` both updated; a partial turn's web claim ordinal continues past its one existing document claim), 4 in `web/lib/web-citations.test.ts` (`applyWebCitation`'s grouping), 2 updated/new in `web/lib/web-search.test.ts`. `scripts/dev.sh check` (978 unmarked, 1 skipped), `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (778 passed, against the real running stack) and `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (376 unmarked lib tests, build, contrast, offline scan) all clean. Live-verified against the running stack via `podman compose up -d` plus `curl localhost:8000/health`: native inference is not running in this environment, so the happy ("ok") path is proven by the `requires_db` tests against a real Postgres with a faked `InferenceClient` rather than a live model — but the *degrade* path (`InferenceUnavailable` → `answer_text: null` rather than a crash) is exactly what a real request against this stack, unmodified, would exercise right now. Out of scope, per the ticket: the web region itself (`M6.5-WEB-FE-190`, already built) and conflict handling between a document and a web page (reuses `M2-PARTIAL-BE-059`'s presentation untouched) — both named explicitly in the backlog file as this ticket's own boundary.

**Next**: `M6.5-WEB-FE-192` (the remaining states — searching, nothing found, unavailable, the escalation-closed note, and the collapsed-turn web marker) or `M6.5-WEB-BE-195` (the real `ddgs` provider) — either can go next.

**`0.6.15` — `M6.5-WEB-FE-186`**: the escalation offer on the abstention surface (`docs/ux/web-search.md` §2, C10) — the first live caller into `escalate_web_search` (`M6.5-WEB-BE-185`). `EscalationOffer`/`EscalationOption` (`web/components/ask/ask-screen.tsx`) render three options, equal weight, below every abstention (`AbstentionState`) and below a partial answer's named gap (`AnsweredContent`, gated on `turn.status === "completed"` so it never appears mid-stream), never above and never below a fully grounded answer. `AddSourceAction` (`M2-ABSTAIN-FE-055`) now shares `EscalationOption`'s two-line label-plus-cost shape rather than rendering as a single unlabelled line. "Ask a larger model" is permanently disabled, cost stated as "you have none" — there is no credits system before M8, so this is a stub by the ticket's own design, not an oversight. New `web/lib/web-search.ts`: `escalateWebSearch`, `webSearchAvailable` (reads new `GET /settings/web-search`), and the local, untransmitted offer-made/offer-accepted counters (`docs/web-search.md` §7's escalation rate). Nothing is pre-selected, auto-focused, or reachable by the Enter key that submitted the question — every option is an ordinary unfocused `<button>`.

New backend route `POST /ask/{message_id}/escalate/web` (`askwell.websearch.register_web_search`) — the HTTP boundary the module's own docstring had flagged as missing since `185`. Independently verifies the named turn actually abstained (`content == ''` and `trace ->> 'reason'` set) or answered partially (`trace ->> 'partial_coverage'` true) before calling `escalate_web_search`, 409 otherwise — closing issue #532, filed against this exact gap before the endpoint existed: C10's "the offer is the only route to a search" is now a structural guarantee of the *server*, not only a rendering convention of `EscalationOffer`. Acceptance is recorded to `Store.DECISIONS` (`web_search_escalation_accepted`), distinct from `escalate_web_search`'s own `Store.INTERACTIONS` record of the outcome — full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, this date. Also fixed `askwell.agent.citation_check.check_citations` (issue #542): it read only `citations`, so a claim cited exclusively by a web result (`web_citations`, `M6.5-WEB-BE-189`) was misflagged uncited; `web_citations.claim_ordinal` is now unioned into `cited_ordinals` the same way `citations.claim_ordinal` is.

Issues #534 (add-source option missing its cost caption), #535 (`web-search.test.ts` never wired into `pnpm test`) and #536 (the transient "sent" caption not distinguishing `unavailable`/`no_results` from `ok`) were all folded into the initial build rather than landing as separate follow-ups, since the code they name did not exist yet this session — `AddSourceAction` was built sharing `EscalationOption`'s shape from the start, `web-search.test.ts` (9 tests) was added to `web/package.json`'s `test` script in the same change that created it, and `EscalationOffer`'s `webCost` branches on `WebSearchEscalationOutcome.status` rather than collapsing every non-throwing response to "sent." New tests: 9 in `api/tests/test_websearch_api.py` (`requires_db` — 404 on an unknown turn, 409 on a fully-grounded one, 200 with both `Store.DECISIONS` and `Store.INTERACTIONS` rows for an abstained turn, 200 for a partial one, and both states of `GET /settings/web-search`), 1 new in `api/tests/test_citation_check.py` (a web-cited claim alongside a document-cited one in the same message, both counted compliant), 9 in `web/lib/web-search.test.ts`. `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (lint, format, typecheck, 977 unmarked tests, 1 skipped), `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (776 passed) and `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (lint, typecheck, 371 unmarked tests, build, token hygiene, contrast, offline scan) all clean. Out of scope, per the ticket: rendering the searching/answered/no-results states (`M6.5-WEB-FE-192`), threading a web citation into the model's own `[index]` numbering (`M6.5-WEB-FE-191`), and the real `ddgs` provider (`M6.5-WEB-BE-195`).

**Next**: `M6.5-WEB-FE-191` (mixed answers — the compose-loop wiring that lets a live turn actually cite a web result, now that `record_web_citations` has a caller shape to design against) or `M6.5-WEB-BE-195` (the real `ddgs` provider) — either can go next.

**`0.6.14` — `M6.5-WEB-FE-190`**: the web results region, separate from the provenance margin (`docs/ux/web-search.md` §3, `docs/ux/design-system.md` §7, C10). New `web/components/ask/web-result.tsx`: `WebResultsRegion`/`WebResultCard`, a dashed `--inferred`-bordered region headed *"From the web — not your files"*, showing domain, title, the passage used, the retrieval date and the full URL, each link opening in a new browser tab. Shares no code with `provenance-margin.tsx`'s `SourceCard` — a new `WebResult` type in `lib/web-citations.ts` (mirroring `askwell.websearch.WebCitationRecord` minus `claim_ordinal`, which belongs to per-claim attribution) rather than `CitationCard` reused with optional fields, and `--provenance` appears nowhere in either new file. `truncatedTitle`/`truncatedUrl` truncate visibly with an ellipsis, never past the limit, and a URL is truncated from the end so its scheme and domain — the part that would matter if it were misleading — stay intact; the domain is also always shown in full, separately. 7 new tests in `lib/web-citations.test.ts`, all pure and unmarked. **Not wired into `ask-screen.tsx`** — deliberately: no live turn carries a `WebResult` yet, since escalating and rendering the streamed answer are `M6.5-WEB-FE-191`/`-192`, the same isolated-capability shape `M6.5-WEB-BE-185`/`-188`/`-189` each shipped in. `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (lint 0 errors, typecheck, 365 unmarked tests, build, contrast — the web-result dashed border's contrast pair was already measured and passing per `design-system.md` §8 — and the offline scan) all clean.

**`0.6.13` — `M6.5-WEB-BE-189`**: retrieval timestamps, stored with the turn (`docs/web-search.md` §4, `docs/architecture.md` §7, C10). New `web_citations` table (migration `d3e6b1a94f02`; `message_id`, `claim_ordinal`, `domain`, `title`, `url`, `passage`, `retrieved_at NOT NULL`) — **structurally distinct from `citations`**, not a nullable `chunk_id` plus a `kind` flag on it, because `citations.chunk_id` is itself `NOT NULL` and a web result was never a chunk (full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, this date). `askwell.websearch` gains `WebCitationRecord`, `web_citation_record()` (builds one from a cited `WebSearchResult` plus the claim ordinal that used it — mirroring `Citation.claim_ordinal`'s role from `M1-CITE-BE-042`) and `record_web_citations()` (writes in whatever transaction the caller already holds, matching the ticket's own "written in the same transaction as the answer" scope line). `retrieved_at` is `NOT NULL` at the column itself — the ticket's own Validation Rule that an undated web citation may not be rendered starts at the row's shape, proven by a `requires_db` test that a raw `INSERT` omitting it raises `NotNullViolation` directly, not only through the write function. `askwell.backup._TABLES` carries `web_citations` so it travels with a backup (and any future conversation export) without a separate change. **Nothing in `askwell.ask`'s live generation loop calls `record_web_citations` yet** — deliberately, the same isolated-capability shape `185`/`187`/`188` each shipped in: nothing currently produces a `(WebSearchResult, claim_ordinal)` pair to hand it, since wiring a web result into the model's own `[index]` citation numbering is a compose-loop change `M6.5-WEB-FE-191` ("mixed answers: each claim points at its own kind of source") names explicitly and is not this ticket's scope. 8 new tests in `api/tests/test_websearch.py` (2 unmarked — the record-builder and the shape assertions in `test_models.py`'s new `test_a_web_citation_is_structurally_distinct_from_a_document_citation`/`test_a_web_citations_retrieval_timestamp_is_mandatory` — plus 5 `requires_db`: round trip with no network touched, two results from the same domain stored separately, an unused result never written, the `NOT NULL` enforcement, and cascade deletion with the parent message). `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (lint, format, typecheck, 968 unmarked tests, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (769 passed) both clean. **Not exercised against the machine's own long-running compose stack** — its Postgres is still on revision `a3c9f5e1d2b7`, absent from this repository's migration history entirely, the same pre-existing drift `M7-BACKUP-BE-157`'s own entry already flagged; `db upgrade head` against it fails with `Can't locate revision identified by 'a3c9f5e1d2b7'` exactly as before, unrelated to this ticket and not attempted a second time. Out of scope, per the ticket: rendering (`M6.5-WEB-FE-190`), persisting web content into the corpus (forbidden, `M6.5-WEB-BE-188`), and re-checking a stored URL at any time.

**Next**: `M6.5-WEB-FE-186` (the escalation offer) or `M6.5-WEB-BE-195` (the real `ddgs` provider) — either can go next, both now unblocked on `187`/`188`/`189`. `M6.5-WEB-FE-191` is the ticket that will actually call `record_web_citations` and `fetch_pages` from a live turn, once the compose-loop question of how a web-sourced claim gets numbered alongside document and memory citations is decided.

**`0.6.12` — `M6.5-WEB-BE-188`**: fetch caps, identical delimitation, and the never-persisted guarantee for web content (`docs/web-search.md` §5, `docs/architecture.md` §5.2, C7/C10). New `askwell.webfetch.fetch_pages`: three caps, all configuration (`Settings.web_fetch_max_results` default `3`, `web_fetch_max_bytes` default `500_000`, `web_fetch_timeout_seconds` default `5.0`, distinct from the whole-provider-call `web_search_timeout_seconds`). A page over any cap, a non-text resource (checked by `content-type`), a redirect loop (capped via `max_redirects=5`), or a fetch failure is **dropped whole with a recorded reason, never truncated** — a page of exactly `max_bytes` is kept, the byte after it is what trips the drop, one stated rule rather than an accident of streaming. A URL beyond the count cap is dropped with reason `"result cap reached"` without ever being requested. Every attempt, kept or dropped, is logged (`web_fetch_attempted`: url, status, reason). `askwell.agent.compose` gains `WEB_CONTENT_TAG`/`delimit_web_result`: a fetched page wrapped in its own `<web-content index="…" url="…" source="…" retrieved_at="…">` block, unforgeable from inside the data via the same `_escape_forged_delimiter` `delimit_tool_result` uses, and `answer_composition.v1.md`'s C7 standing statement now names `<web-content>` alongside `<retrieved-content>`/`<tool-result>` explicitly, with its own "Web results" section. The prohibition is structural, not a runtime check: `FetchedPage`/`WebSearchResult` have no constructor call anywhere in `askwell.ingest` or `askwell.retrieve`, proven by a new corpus-invariance test (`test_an_escalated_turn_leaves_the_corpus_byte_for_byte_unchanged`) that runs a real `escalate_web_search` call plus a faked `fetch_pages`/`delimit_web_result` pass and asserts `documents`/`chunks` row counts are unchanged before and after. **Nothing in the live `/ask` or escalation path calls `fetch_pages` yet** — deliberately: `FixtureWebSearchProvider`, the only `WebSearchProvider` implementation that exists, must stay network-free for `test_websearch.py`'s own zero-network eval guarantee, and the real provider that needs capped fetching, `ddgs` (`M6.5-WEB-BE-195`), is not built and was not a dependency of this ticket. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning for why the caps ship now, untethered from a live caller, rather than waiting on `195`. All fetches in `test_webfetch.py` are faked with `httpx.MockTransport`, the same pattern `test_voice_tts.py` uses — no real network in any test. New `ASKWELL_WEB_FETCH_MAX_RESULTS`/`_MAX_BYTES`/`_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` in `.env.example`. `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (lint, format, typecheck, 965 unmarked tests, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (764 passed) both clean. Out of scope, per the ticket: trace flagging of instruction-like patterns in fetched content (`M6.5-WEB-OBS-193`), the provider call itself (`M6.5-WEB-BE-185`, done), and rendering (`M6.5-WEB-FE-190`).

**`0.6.11` — `M6.5-WEB-SEC-187`**: per-question egress authorisation, opened and closed at the proxy (`docs/architecture.md` §5.1, C1/C10). `askwell.egress` gains a second grant primitive next to `M7-UPDATE-BE-161`'s single-destination `PERMITTED_HOST_KEY`: `open_grant`/`close_grant`, a Redis key per turn (`askwell:egress:grant:{turn_id}`), so two escalations in flight hold two independent grants instead of racing to overwrite one value, and each carries a hard TTL (`Settings.web_search_grant_ttl_seconds`, `30.0`) so a missed `close_grant` call cannot leave a grant standing — Redis expires it on its own. `open_grant` takes an `accepted` argument and refuses, logging a warning, without ever constructing a Redis client, when it is `False` — the mechanism itself is the backstop against application code opening a grant with no real acceptance behind it, not a convention trusted elsewhere. `EgressProxy.handle` now checks a `CONNECT` destination against both the single permitted host and any live turn grant (`_grant_permits`, a `SCAN` over the small grant keyspace) before forwarding; a destination matching neither stays refused exactly as before. `askwell.websearch.escalate_web_search` is the sole caller: it opens a grant scoped to `message_id` and the configured search destination before the provider call and closes it in `finally`, covering an ordinary return, a caught provider failure, and the coroutine being cancelled (the user pressing stop mid-search) with the same code path — a failure that leaks a grant is the defect this ticket exists to prevent. Opening and closing are each their own audit row (`web_search_grant_opened`/`_closed`, C6) naming the turn and the destination. New settings: `web_search_destination_host`/`_port` (`html.duckduckgo.com:443`, an explicitly provisional placeholder — `ddgs` fans out across several backend engines, and `M6.5-WEB-BE-195`'s own job includes replacing this with whatever destination list the chosen backend needs) and `web_search_grant_ttl_seconds`; `ASKWELL_WEB_SEARCH_DESTINATION_HOST`/`_PORT`/`ASKWELL_WEB_SEARCH_GRANT_TTL_SECONDS` in `.env.example`. 15 new tests (11 unmarked in `test_egress.py` covering the grant functions and the combined proxy check with a fake Redis, 4 `requires_db` in `test_websearch.py` covering the open/close/audit wiring, the exception path and the cancellation path with `open_grant`/`close_grant` spied rather than a real Redis, matching `test_update_check.py`'s own convention). `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (lint, format, typecheck, 949 unmarked tests, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (763 passed) both clean. Verified against the real running stack, not only unit-tested: rebuilt and recreated `api`/`egress-proxy`, opened a real grant against real Redis (confirmed via `redis-cli`-equivalent `GET`/`TTL`: key present, `ttl=30`), then drove real `httpx` traffic through the real proxy — a request to the granted destination reached it (`302` from `html.duckduckgo.com`) while a request to an unrelated host in the same window was refused with the standard 403 body, and `askwell.network.read_activity` picked up the forwarded connection in `permitted` from the proxy's own count, not from anything the API asserts about itself. Out of scope, per the ticket: the real `ddgs` destination (`M6.5-WEB-BE-195`), fetching and capping page content (`M6.5-WEB-BE-188`), and the escalation offer itself (`M6.5-WEB-FE-186`), which is now the only thing standing between this mechanism and a real user-facing escalation.

**`0.6.10` — `M6.5-WEB-BE-185`**: the web search provider behind an interface, called only on explicit request (`docs/architecture.md` §5.2, like the TTS engine). New `askwell.websearch`: `WebSearchResult` (source domain, title, URL, passage, retrieval timestamp), `WebSearchProvider` (a `Protocol` — question in, results out, or `WebSearchUnavailable` raised), `FixtureWebSearchProvider` (recorded results keyed by question text, for development and the eval suite), and `build_web_search_provider` as the one place `Settings.web_search_provider` selects an implementation — no provider name anywhere else in application code (`AGENTS.md` §4). `escalate_web_search` is the single call site: it distinguishes "nothing matched" (an empty result list — a real, distinct outcome) from "the provider could not be reached" (unconfigured, unreachable, or past `Settings.web_search_timeout_seconds`, all folded into `status="unavailable"` with a `reason`), drops a result whose passage is empty rather than letting a caller render an empty citation, and keeps a result whose URL may not resolve — the citation must stay honest even once the page is gone. Every invocation is recorded on the interaction path (`M1-ASK-OBS-041`, C6: question, provider, status, result count) before returning, win or lose. New `ASKWELL_WEB_SEARCH_PROVIDER`/`ASKWELL_WEB_SEARCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` in `.env.example`, the first sharing `config.py`'s existing `_optional_path` empty-string-means-unset validator with `ASKWELL_ROOTS_MOUNT`. **Nothing calls this yet, structurally, not just by convention**: `askwell.ask` does not import `askwell.websearch` at all — there is no path from a below-threshold retrieval into an escalation. `test_the_full_answer_path_never_calls_the_provider` (`test_websearch.py`) runs three abstaining questions through the real `/ask` endpoint against a real Postgres, with `web_search_provider` configured to `fixture`, and asserts zero `web_search_escalated` audit rows afterward — the ticket's own acceptance criterion, proved from the audit trail rather than inferred from reading the source. 14 new tests (6 unmarked, 8 `requires_db`, including the timeout, unreachable, no-usable-passage-dropped and unresolvable-URL-kept edge cases). Verified against the real stack: `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (lint, format, typecheck, 942 unmarked tests, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (759 passed) both clean. Out of scope, as the ticket's own Out of Scope line states: choosing the concrete provider (`M6.5-WEB-BE-195`, unblocked — `ddgs`, per `docs/decisions.md` 2026-08-26), fetching and capping page content (`M6.5-WEB-BE-188`), egress authorisation (`M6.5-WEB-SEC-187`), and any rendering (`M6.5-WEB-FE-186`/`-190`/`-192`).

**`0.6.9` — `M7-UPDATE-BE-161`**: the update check, agreed to at installation (`docs/ux/settings.md` §7 and §9, `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-21 "Update checks are chosen at installation, never defaulted on" — the product decision this ticket implements, unblocked). `askwell.update_check` stores the answer as `not_asked`/`yes`/`no` (`update_check_answer` in `settings`; an absent key, including on every existing install, reads as `not_asked` forever rather than silently becoming `no`), with `GET`/`POST /settings/update-check` and a `POST /settings/update-check/run` manual "check now" that works regardless of the stored answer. The release feed is this repository's own `VERSION` file, served as a static blob by `raw.githubusercontent.com` — no second registry, no API endpoint, matching `docs/ux/settings.md` §7's own "a static version file, not an endpoint" claim literally. **`askwell.egress.EgressProxy` gained its first real capability beyond refusing**: a single Redis-backed permitted destination (`PERMITTED_HOST_KEY`), read fresh on every proxy connection, opened the instant the answer becomes `yes` and closed the instant it stops, or opened and closed around exactly one manual check when the standing answer is not `yes` — real `CONNECT` tunnelling (`EgressProxy._forward`/`_pipe`), not a second refusal path. This is issue #345's own Option 1, scoped to one destination rather than the general per-connection version that issue still wants (commented, not closed). A weekly arq cron (`run_update_check` → `askwell.update_check.maybe_run_scheduled_check`) compares `now() - last_checked_at` against the interval directly rather than counting missed slots, so a machine offline for a month produces exactly one check next run, never a backlog. New `ASKWELL_UPDATE_FEED_HOST`/`ASKWELL_UPDATE_FEED_URL`/`ASKWELL_UPDATE_CHECK_POLL_SECONDS`/`ASKWELL_UPDATE_CHECK_INTERVAL_SECONDS` in `.env.example`. Full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, this date ("the release feed is this repository's own `VERSION` file; the egress proxy learns its first real permitted destination").

**Caught while verifying this against the running stack**: `compose.yaml`'s `egress-proxy` service was missing the `ASKWELL_SANDBOX_*` env vars `Settings` requires with no default — invisible while the long-running container predated that requirement, fatal the moment `--force-recreate` picked up this ticket's own image change. Fixed in `egress-proxy`'s own environment block (matching `voice`'s existing pattern); `inference-bridge` carries the same gap, left open as issue #524 since it is unrelated to this ticket and was not touched.

Verified end-to-end against the real, running compose stack, not only unit-tested: `GET /settings/update-check` starts `not_asked`; `POST {"answer":"yes"}` immediately sets the Redis permit key to `raw.githubusercontent.com:443` (confirmed via `redis-cli GET`), and `POST .../run` fetched the real file over the real tunnel, returning `latest_known_version: "0.6.8"` with `last_result: "ok"` and `permitted: 1` on `GET /network` — before the `VERSION` bump in this same change, so the comparison was against a real prior release, not a fixture. `POST {"answer":"no"}` cleared the Redis key immediately (empty `GET`); a manual check run straight after still succeeded (`last_result: "ok"`) and the key read empty again immediately after, proving the temporary-open-and-close path rather than assuming it from the code. `scripts/dev.sh check` (936 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (751 passed against the real stack, after fixing one test that double-counted a `revoke_destination` call from the preceding `set_answer(NO)` transition) both clean.

**`0.6.8` — `M7-BACKUP-BE-157`**: backup as a background job, excluding what can be regenerated (`docs/build-plan.md`, `docs/ux/settings.md` §6). Same shape as `M7-LOG-BE-155`'s log export — a durable job row, `.tmp`→rename writes, keyset-paginated streaming — applied to roughly fifteen tables instead of two audit stores. `GET /backup/estimate` states the chunk count, an estimated re-embed time and an estimated artefact size, and whether the corpus is passphrase-protected, before anyone commits — the ticket's own acceptance criterion that the re-embed cost is stated "at backup time," not only after the artefact finishes. `POST /backup` refuses with `409` if a content-encryption migration is running (`content_encryption.assert_not_migrating`, built and left unused by `M7-SEC-BE-152` for exactly this caller) or if there is not enough free disk (`409`, needed/free bytes named, mirroring `model_download.NoDiskSpace`'s shape). `askwell.backup.run_job` reads every table inside one `REPEATABLE READ` transaction rather than refusing during ingestion — Postgres fixes the snapshot at that transaction's first statement, which is deliberately the migration check, so the whole run is either entirely before or entirely after any migration, never caught mid-flight; no torn snapshot, and nothing to refuse for ingestion specifically. Excluded: `chunks.embedding`/`schema_notes.embedding` (the vector index, dropped column-by-column, regenerable by re-embedding `chunks.content` which the backup does carry), the trace ring buffer and model weights (excluded by construction — this module never queries or copies either). `chunks.content_tsv` is *not* excluded — since `M7-SEC-BE-152` it is a plain column, not `GENERATED`, and it is text-sized, not vector-sized. `roots` and `document_pages` are included though not named explicitly in the ticket's own table list ("sources and their configuration", "documents... extracted content" cover them); `ingest_jobs`/`reapply_jobs`/`export_jobs` are deliberately excluded as job bookkeeping, not user data. The re-embed rate (`ESTIMATED_CHUNKS_PER_SECOND`) is a stated, deliberately conservative, unmeasured constant — no native inference process is available in this environment to benchmark against, the same gap recorded since `M1-INDEX-ING-032`; full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, this date. New `backup_jobs` table (migration `c4a1f8d02e77`), `BackupJob` ORM model (`api/src/askwell/db/models.py`, the mirror `test_harness.py` checks against real migrations — caught one gap this session, see below), `ASKWELL_BACKUP_DIR` in `.env.example`. Verified: `pytest -m requires_db` (full suite, all passing against a real Postgres, including `test_backup.py`'s six tests — enqueue's decisions record, migration-in-progress refusal, insufficient-space refusal, the size/re-embed estimate, a full round trip through the bundled artefact confirming the embedding is absent and `content_tsv` is present, and a passphrase-protected backup stating it plainly) and `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (lint, format, typecheck, unmarked tests all clean). **Not exercised against the machine's own long-running compose stack** — its Postgres is on a migration revision (`a3c9f5e1d2b7`) absent from this repository's own migration history entirely, unrelated dev-environment drift `M7-LOG-BE-153`'s own entry already flagged for a different, now-superseded revision; `db upgrade head` against it was not attempted a second time. **Caught while wiring this ticket**: `BackupJob` had no ORM model at all initially — `test_harness.py::test_the_migration_chain_applied_from_empty` failed with "Extra items in the left set: 'backup_jobs'" the first time the full suite ran, which is exactly the check doing its job; added and reran clean.

**Next**: `M7-BACKUP-BE-158` (restore, with the re-embed cost stated again at the moment it matters) is the direct follow-on and was explicitly out of scope here — this ticket's own `_TABLES` mapping in `api/src/askwell/backup.py` is what it should build against rather than re-deriving a table list. `M7-BACKUP-TEST-159` (tested restore, every release) is blocked on `158`. `M7-TAURI-DEPLOY-181` remains available in parallel.

**`0.6.7` — `M7-LOG-BE-155`**: log export as a background job, with the chain and a standalone verifier (`docs/audit-log.md` §5). Rebuilt from scratch — the earlier attempt was parked, not merged (`wip: M7-LOG-BE-155, not accepted`), and the audit that failed it found exactly one real bug: no shared volume for the export path, which `M7-OPS-DEPLOY-154a` has since fixed; everything else in that attempt's own audit read as sound, and this build follows the same shape (job table, `.tmp`→rename, streamed keyset pagination, an independent standalone verifier) rather than inventing a new one. `POST /log-export` (optional `since`/`until`; `acknowledged_decrypted_export` required once a passphrase is set, `400` otherwise) enqueues an `export_jobs` row and dispatches it; `GET /log-export/{id}` reports progress across both audit stores; `GET /log-export/{id}/download` streams the finished `.zip`. `askwell.log_export.run_job` streams `decisions.jsonl`/`interactions.jsonl` in keyset-paginated batches, writes a `manifest.json` recording each store's actual starting `prev_hash` (the universal genesis value for a full export, something else for a windowed one), and bundles `askwell.log_export_verifier.SOURCE` as `verify.py` — a from-scratch, zero-`askwell`-import reimplementation of `askwell.audit.compute_hash`, proven to agree with the real thing by running it as a subprocess in `api/tests/test_log_export.py` rather than importing it. A run always deletes and rewrites its working directory from scratch on retry rather than resuming mid-file — restartability without a resumable byte offset; full reasoning for both choices in `docs/decisions.md`, this date. Export never calls `log_budget.enforce_ingestion_allowed` — confirmed live at `budget_bytes=1`. New `ExportJob` in `api/src/askwell/db/models.py` (the ORM mirror `test_harness.py` checks against real migrations) and `ASKWELL_EXPORT_DIR` in `.env.example`. Issue #487 re-owned with a comment, not resolved — it needs `M7-LOG-BE-154` (prune, still not built) and a frontend ticket to wire the real endpoints in, neither of which is this ticket's scope. New issue #511 filed for an unrelated gap found while updating this file: `M7-SEC-BE-152` has no `CHANGELOG.md` entry of its own. Verified: `pytest -m requires_db` (full suite, 731 passed — including a real subprocess run of the bundled verifier against a genuine export, a tamper test, a date-filtered-window test and a simulated-crash restart test), `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (lint, format, typecheck, unmarked tests all clean), and a live round trip against the running compose stack after rebuilding the API image and running the migration: `POST`/`GET /log-export`, download, extract, `verify.py` run against both the extracted directory and the `.zip` directly, a tamper-and-reverify pass, a date-filtered export, and an export enqueued and completed while `log_budget` was pinned to `budget_bytes=1`.

**Next**: `M7-LOG-BE-154` (interaction retention window and prune) is the natural follow-on — `M7-SET-FE-148`'s settings screen already has a real value for the retention window with explicit "nothing prunes yet" copy, waiting on exactly this. A frontend ticket to wire `web/components/settings/storage.tsx`'s "Export and prune" section to the new `/log-export` endpoints (closing issue #487's own Option 1) is unstarted and currently unclaimed by any backlog ticket found in `docs/backlog/` — check before assuming one exists. `M7-TAURI-DEPLOY-181` (the desktop shell) remains available in parallel.

**`0.6.6` — `M7-OPS-DEPLOY-154a`**: one named volume, `askwell-state`, mounted at `/var/lib/askwell` on both `api` and `worker` in `compose.yaml`. Neither service had anything mounted there before — `api` and `worker` are separate containers with separate filesystems, so `Settings.trace_dir` resolved to a path `api` couldn't see at all, and `GET /log-budget` 500'd on the real stack every time (`FileNotFoundError` from `shutil.disk_usage` falling back to the non-existent parent, #486). Same gap is what would have defeated log export's download path had `M7-LOG-BE-155` landed (#489) — that ticket is committed as `wip, not accepted` and not merged into this branch, so `export_dir` does not exist in this codebase yet; the volume covers the whole `/var/lib/askwell` root rather than a mount per subdirectory specifically so `export_dir` needs no further compose change whenever that ticket is rebuilt. Full reasoning, including the rejected Redis/Postgres-streaming alternative from #489's own options list, in `docs/decisions.md`, this date.

**Verified against the real running stack**: `podman compose up -d` created the volume (`askwell_askwell-state` in `podman volume ls` — named, not anonymous); a file written from inside the `worker` container (`echo hello-from-worker > /var/lib/askwell/exports/roundtrip.txt`) read back unchanged from inside `api`; `GET /log-budget` answered `401` ("No session.") rather than `500`; the file survived `podman compose down && podman compose up -d`. `scripts/dev.sh test` clean (927 passed, 1 skipped, no regressions — this is an infrastructure-only change, nothing in `api/src/` touched). Full walkthrough recorded in `docs/manual-tests/M7-OPS-DEPLOY-154a.md`. Closed #489 (comment: fixed, verified live) and #486 (comment: same fix, `/log-budget` confirmed `401` not `500` against the real stack).

**Superseded the same session it was written**: `M7-LOG-BE-155` (see the `0.6.7` entry above) has since been rebuilt and landed, using the working shared mount this ticket provided.

**`0.6.5` — `M7-SET-FE-147`**: the settings screen's privacy and security section (`docs/ux/settings.md` §4). New `web/components/settings/privacy-security.tsx` composes three independently-fetching displays under one "Privacy and security" heading, mirroring the shape `storage.tsx` already established for its own section: `passphrase.tsx` (new, surfacing `M7-SEC-BE-151` — set/change/remove with strength feedback from the server's own heuristic, the no-recovery warning stated next to the button rather than in a confirm dialog); `network-activity.tsx` (new, backed by new `web/lib/network.ts` reading `GET /network`) — a statement, never a toggle, rendering "unavailable" rather than a false zero when the proxy's counters cannot be read, and showing refusals plainly with their destinations when the count is non-zero; and `connections.tsx` (`M4-CONN-FE-096`, moved here from its previous standalone placement on the settings page, demoted from `h2` to `h3` to nest correctly — it is used nowhere else). No backend change was needed: `GET /network` (`askwell.network.read_activity`) and `/settings/passphrase*` (`M7-SEC-BE-151`) already existed, and `M4-CONN-SEC-097`'s write probe already refuses a write-capable credential before a connection source is ever created, so every listed connection is read-only by construction — the "read-only status" acceptance criterion needed no new field, only display. **There is no web-search setting anywhere on this screen** (`docs/ux/web-search.md` §5's explicit prohibition) and no control here pre-authorises egress of any kind, per this ticket's own Validation Rule.

**Verified against the real running stack**, not just unit-tested: rebuilt the web bundle (`scripts/dev.sh web-build`) and the `api` image had already been rebuilt for `M7-SEC-BE-151`, established a browser session (`Accept: text/html` to `/`), then confirmed `GET /network` returned genuine proxy data — `{"available": true, "refused": 2, "permitted": 0, "recent": [{"service": "askwell-api-1", "destination": "huggingface.co:443"}, ...]}` — a real refusal this environment had already produced, not a fixture; `GET /settings/passphrase` returned `{"enabled": false, "locked": false}`; and the server-rendered `/settings` HTML contains "Privacy and security", "Passphrase", "Network activity" and "Connected databases" in that nesting order. `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (typecheck, lint, token hygiene, contrast in both themes, the offline/C1 no-external-host check) and `pnpm test` (358 tests, all previously-passing) both clean — one lint error caught and fixed along the way (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` in the strength-debounce hook, resolved by deriving the empty-input case rather than calling `setState` synchronously inside the effect).

**Next**: `M7-SEC-BE-152` (corpus encryption) is the natural passphrase follow-on. Issue #504 (the worker process never unlocks, so its two passphrase-dependent jobs stay `credentials_locked` for as long as a passphrase is set) remains unblocked-but-unclaimed. `docs/ux/settings.md` §4's passphrase-forgotten / app-restart unlock prompt is **not** built — this ticket's Scope named only the settings-screen control, and a lock gate that blocks the whole interface until unlocked is a different surface with its own states (`docs/ux/settings.md` §8's "Passphrase set, app restarted" row); check the backlog before assuming it exists.

**`0.6.4` — `M7-SEC-BE-151`**: the optional passphrase — set, change, remove, unlock (`docs/ux/settings.md` §4 and §8). New `api/src/askwell/passphrase.py`: off by default, folds into the key derivation `M4-CONN-SEC-098` already built (`crypto.derive_key(install_secret, passphrase)`) rather than layering a second scheme, and re-encrypts every stored connection credential in the same transaction as set/change/remove — the ticket's own assumption that a credential is never re-entered just because a passphrase was added is now literally true. Setting one requires `acknowledged_no_recovery: true`; there is no recovery path anywhere, by design. A candidate passphrase is checked against a verifier — a known plaintext encrypted with the derived key — never against a stored hash or the passphrase text itself, so a wrong passphrase and a wrong install secret both surface through the same `crypto.CredentialsLocked` an operator already has to reason about (`passphrase.Locked` subclasses it). New `GET`/`POST /settings/passphrase{,/strength,/set,/change,/remove,/unlock}`. The three existing decrypt call sites (`askwell.connections._load_connection_config`, `create_connection_source`, `askwell.ask._connection_credentials`) now go through `passphrase.current_key` instead of deriving straight from the install secret.

**Unlock state is in-memory only, per process, never written anywhere** — caching it on the run-directory bind mount the way the install secret itself lives there would make a restart's "prompt before anything decrypts" false the moment it matters most (a stolen, sleeping laptop). The cost, stated rather than hidden: `api` and `worker` are separate processes, so the worker never unlocks and its two passphrase-dependent jobs (`run_introspection`, `check_connection_health`) stay `credentials_locked` for as long as a passphrase is set, regardless of the API's own state. Filed as issue [#504](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/504) rather than patched — cross-process key sharing is a change to C1's own security boundary, not a fix that fits inside this ticket's size. The ticket's own "ingestion pauses while a passphrase is set mid-import" edge case is **not** built here: nothing about chunk content is encrypted yet (`M7-SEC-BE-152`, out of this ticket's scope and not started), so there is nothing for ingestion to pause *for* — revisit once 152 lands. Full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, this date.

**Verified:** `scripts/dev.sh lint`/`typecheck`/`test` clean (927 passed, 1 skipped, no regressions). `scripts/dev.sh test-db` clean, full suite (716 passed, 18 of them new in `api/tests/test_passphrase.py`). Rebuilt the API image and ran the full cold-start-adjacent walkthrough for real against the running compose stack: set a passphrase (weak one refused, missing acknowledgement refused, real one accepted and left this process unlocked), restarted the `api` container, confirmed `GET /settings/passphrase` read `locked: true` before anything else was touched, a wrong passphrase refused generically, the correct one unlocked, then removed the passphrase and confirmed the stated consequence and that a fresh `GET /health` still reported the stack reachable. `POST /sources/connection` while locked now returns 401 rather than a raw 500 (`sources.py`'s new `except crypto.CredentialsLocked` clause) — exercised only via the unit/db suite, not against a real live database in this environment.

**Next**: no frontend built for this — `docs/ux/settings.md` §4's passphrase control needs its own ticket to actually render `GET`/`POST /settings/passphrase*`; check the backlog for whether one already exists before starting. `M7-SEC-BE-152` (corpus encryption) is the natural follow-on and is what the "ingestion pauses while a passphrase is set" edge case actually depends on. Issue #504 (worker never unlocks) is unblocked-but-unclaimed — read it before touching `askwell.connections`' background jobs again. **Superseded the same session it was written: `M7-SET-FE-147` (see the `0.6.5` entry above) built exactly this frontend.**

**`0.6.3` — `M7-SET-FE-148`**: the settings storage section, five displays (`docs/ux/settings.md` §5). Per-source index size (`askwell.sources.storage_by_source`, `GET /sources/storage`) — chunk content bytes plus `chunk_count × embedding_dimensions × 4`, "unknown" rather than "0" for a connection/dump source or one still queued/indexing. The log budget, current use, and adjustment, with `Usage.configured_bytes` (issue #484, closed) making a 5%-free-disk cap visible next to what was actually set. The interaction retention window as a real settings value and decisions record (`get_retention_months`/`set_retention_months`, `GET`/`POST /log-budget/retention`) with explicit copy that nothing prunes yet — the prune is `M7-LOG-BE-154`, not built. Export and prune as a stated, disabled entry point rather than a button wired to nothing, same pattern `settings.md` §3 uses for Online AI. The at-the-limit statement, static and always shown. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why the ticket built three of five displays for real rather than stopping at the two whose backends are separate unbuilt tickets. New issue #486 filed: `compose.yaml` mounts nothing at `/var/lib/askwell`, which is why `M7-LOG-BE-153`'s own note above could not exercise `GET /log-budget` against this machine's running stack — worked around by hand for this session's own verification, not fixed. Tested against a real Postgres (`api/tests/test_log_budget.py`, 18 tests; new `api/tests/test_storage.py`, 4 tests), `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (full frontend gate, including token hygiene and the offline/C1 check), and a live round trip against the running compose stack for every new/changed endpoint after rebuilding the API image and the web bundle.

**`0.6.2` — `M7-LOG-BE-153`**: log storage budget with staged degradation. `api/src/askwell/log_budget.py` measures `audit_interactions` and the trace ring buffer against an effective budget recomputed on every call — `min(configured cap, 5% of current free disk)` — and derives one of three stages: `ok`, `notice` (≥80%), `hard_limit` (≥100%). `GET`/`POST /log-budget` read current usage and change the cap, mirroring `askwell.retrieve.register_retrieval_threshold`'s read/write-pair shape; a change is a hash-chained decisions record (`log_budget_changed`), a stage transition is only logged. `POST /sources`, `/sources/dump` and `/sources/connection` now call `enforce_ingestion_allowed` after their own validation succeeds and before their transaction commits — deliberately after, not before, because `test_sources_api.py` already proved several refusals (bad folder, blank connection host, unsupported engine) happen with no database configured at all, and checking budget first would have turned every one of those into a raw connection error instead of the intended 400. A hard-limit refusal is a 507 naming what to do about it; the transaction it was inside rolls back with it, so a refused batch commits nothing. Asking a question never reaches this code at all, which is the actual mechanism behind "questions keep working" rather than a claim resting on any special-casing in `askwell.ask`. Decisions-store fail-the-action and trace-never-fails-an-action needed no new code — both already exist from `M0-DATA-OBS-015` and are exercised again here, not duplicated. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning for the recompute-every-call design and for reading free disk off `Settings.trace_dir` rather than Postgres's own volume (the two run in separate containers with no shared mount this module could inspect). Tested against a real Postgres, unit-level and one HTTP-level round trip through the real app proving a refused add commits zero rows (`api/tests/test_log_budget.py`, 12 tests, all passing against `scripts/dev.sh test-db`); not exercised against this machine's own long-running compose stack, whose Postgres volume is on a stale `alembic_version` unrelated to this change (pre-existing dev-environment drift, not touched here). `M7-SET-FE-148` (the settings storage screen) is this ticket's first real frontend consumer and is not started; `M7-LOG-BE-154` (retention window and prune) and `M7-LOG-BE-155` (export) are the two tickets this one's own Out of Scope named.

**`0.6.0` — `M7-PROBE-DEPLOY-137`**: a real hardware probe, on the host. `deploy/probe/askwell-probe` — standalone stdlib, same shape as `deploy/inference/askwell-inference`, for the same reason: a container reports the cgroup's or the VM's view of memory, not the machine's, and GPU/host Python constraints rule out running it any other way. Refuses outright if it detects it is running inside a container. Measures RAM (per-platform: `/proc/meminfo`, `sysctl hw.memsize`, `GlobalMemoryStatusEx`), CPU, an accelerator (NVIDIA via `nvidia-smi` with a real VRAM figure, Apple Silicon as unified memory, ROCm presence without a VRAM number, anything else absent) and free disk, then selects one of the four `docs/architecture.md` §6 profiles itself. Falls back to `standard` with a stated reason when memory cannot be measured at all — distinct from a genuine reading under the `light` floor, which stays `light` and states the warning `M7-PROBE-FE-138` will render. An accelerator it does not recognise reads as absent, and a VM's dishonest memory figure is used as given with its source stated, per the ticket's own edge cases.

`askwell.probe` is the API-side seam: `read_probe_result` reads the host script's JSON off the shared `${ASKWELL_RUN_DIR}:/run/askwell` mount the inference socket and install key already use, returning `None` (not a guess) when nothing has run yet — the M1 `askwell.hardware.probe()` reading is the fallback one layer up, exactly as that module's own docstring promised. `sync_probe_result` records the selection to `settings` (`hardware.profile`, `hardware.probe_evidence`) and a hash-chained `audit_decisions` record (`profile_probed`, floats converted to fixed-unit megabytes first — `askwell.audit.canonical_payload` refuses floats outright) whenever the file's `probed_at` is genuinely newer than the last one applied, never on every poll. `GET /probe` and `POST /probe/rerun` expose this; a rerun touches a flag file `askwell-probe --watch` (`scripts/dev.sh probe --watch`) polls for, since a container cannot itself start a host process — the same reason inference needed `scripts/dev.sh inference` in the first place, and the same limitation until `M7-TAURI-DEPLOY-184a`'s shell exists to spawn it directly.

**Found, not fixed here:** issue #466 (`Settings.profile`'s `light`/`balanced`/`full` is a different, stale vocabulary from the four hardware tiers this ticket uses) already existed and covers this precisely — commented with the new `GET /probe` surface rather than duplicating the issue.

Full reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-21.

**Verified:** `scripts/dev.sh test`/`test-db`/`lint`/`typecheck` all clean (24 new host-script tests against real subprocess/platform reads, 15 new db-backed tests for the recording seam). Ran for real against actual hardware — `scripts/dev.sh probe` on this machine correctly read 31 GB RAM and an 8 GB NVIDIA GPU and selected `accelerated`. Rebuilt and restarted the `api`/`worker` images, hit `GET /probe` before and after running the host probe (interim `standard` fallback with a stated reason, then the real `accelerated` result), confirmed `hardware.*` landed in `settings` and `profile_probed` in `audit_decisions` via `psql`, ran `askwell-verify` (chain intact), and drove the full watch-and-rerun loop live: `POST /probe/rerun` while `askwell-probe --watch` was running produced a fresh `probed_at` within one poll interval.

**`0.5.13` — `M6-STT-FE-129`**: low-confidence transcripts are shown and confirmed before Askwell answers. **Thirteenth and final ticket of M6 — 13 of 13, M6 complete.** Closed issue [#468](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/468) (its own blocker, filed while scoping this ticket).

**The real gap was backend, not frontend.** `askwell.voice_stt.build_stt_driver` called `on_transcript` unconditionally right after storing a transcript, with no pause point between "stored" and "generation begins," and `askwell.voice_channel._receive_loop` understood only `end`/`stop` — no message type for a client to submit a correction or release a held turn. Building the confirmation UI against that would have meant either faking it client-side (a stub that looks finished but doesn't change what gets answered) or answering the wrong question anyway, the exact failure this ticket exists to prevent — so the backend plumbing was built as part of this ticket, per #468's own Option 1 recommendation.

**`VoiceTurn.request_confirmation()`/`resolve_confirmation()`** (`api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py`) add the hold: a new `confirmation` wire event and an `asyncio.Future` the driver awaits. `build_stt_driver` (`voice_stt.py`) compares every `ok` transcript's confidence against the new `Settings.stt_confirmation_confidence_threshold` (default `0.6`); below it, nothing is stored to `messages`/`audit_interactions` and `on_transcript` is not called until the future resolves — a confirmed or edited transcript is what is recorded (`AGENTS.md` §3 C6), never the unconfirmed guess. `_receive_loop` gained `confirm` (proceed as heard) and `edit` (proceed with a replacement; blank edits ignored) control messages, and a reattaching connection resends a pending `confirmation` event same as `transcript`/`text`/`confidence`. A held turn with no `confirm`/`edit` within `Settings.stt_confirmation_timeout_seconds` (default 120s) ends unanswered, nothing stored — the ticket's own "user walks away" edge case, same shape as `no_speech`. Above the threshold: unchanged, no added latency, matching the ticket's own "the extra tap must not become the normal path."

**Frontend** (`web/lib/voice.ts`, `web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx`): a new `confirming` `VoiceState`, reached on a `confirmation` event and left the same way every other state is — the first `text` event of the resulting answer. The composer renders the transcript in an editable field with **Confirm** and **Speak again** actions; Confirm sends `edit` if the field no longer matches what was heard, `confirm` otherwise, and records a local-only tally (`recordVoiceConfirmation`, C1 — nothing transmitted). Speak again abandons the held turn (closes the socket; the server's own timeout cleans it up) and returns to idle for a fresh push-to-talk press, rather than starting a second capture on its own. A confirmation the user ignored — the timeout firing server-side — leaves the transcript visible in the idle prompt instead of reverting to "Press and hold to speak", matching "retained in the composer."

Full reasoning, including the rejected alternative (an eager second-generation-from-edit path that still answers the wrong question once), in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-21. Commented on issue #460 (re-owned, unaffected — same in-place `MicControl` rendering pattern, not `AskProvider`'s transcript).

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh test-db tests/test_voice_stt.py` (9 passed, 3 new — confirm, edit, timeout). `scripts/dev.sh test tests/test_voice_channel.py` (15 passed, 5 new — confirm, edit, blank-edit-ignored, reattach-resends-confirmation). `scripts/dev.sh typecheck`/`lint` clean. `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (361 tests, 8 new in `web/lib/voice.test.ts`; build, tokens, contrast, offline all clean). Rebuilt the `api` image (`0.5.12` → `0.5.13`) and restarted it against the real stack — clean startup, no configuration errors from the two new settings fields — then ran a live, unmocked `websockets` client against the real `/voice/ws`: turn/status round-trip completed correctly. Forcing a real low-confidence pass needs actual speech audio and a browser; neither is available in this environment (no Whisper/Kokoro weights either, the same gap every voice ticket has carried), so the confirm/edit/timeout paths are verified by driving the driver and the channel directly instead of a live mic walkthrough.

**Next**: M6 is complete. The next milestone's first ticket is unstarted — check `docs/build-plan.md` for M7's scope before picking one up.

**`0.5.12` — `M6-VUI-FE-135`**: the composer's remaining voice states — permission denied, non-English speech, abstention spoken in full (`docs/ux/voice.md` §5). **Twelfth ticket of M6 — 12 of 13.** No backend change; three fixes in `web/lib/voice.ts`/`web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx`.

**Permission denied** is now read proactively via `navigator.permissions.query({name: "microphone"})` on mount (`micPermissionReason`) rather than waiting for a real `getUserMedia` rejection — that API only reports state, never itself prompts, so this costs nothing against the ticket's own Out of Scope ("one request, then the explanation"). Once it reports `"denied"`, `startCapture` refuses to call `getUserMedia` at all (`aria-disabled` on the button, the explanation shown in the existing tooltip) until `PermissionStatus.onchange` says otherwise — the ticket's own "granted after being denied, without a reload" edge case, where the browser supports it. A browser with no Permissions API support for `"microphone"` falls back to exactly the pre-existing behaviour: the first real denial sets the same reason.

**Non-English speech** is `askwell.voice_stt`'s existing `unsupported_language` outcome — the channel has sent a `language` event for it since `M6-STT-BE-127`, typed in `VoiceChannelEvent` since `M6-VUI-FE-128a`, but `nextVoiceStatus` never had a case for it. It now ends the turn immediately with a new `VOICE_NON_ENGLISH_REASON`, and the closing `status: "completed"` for the same turn is taught not to clobber that reason (the two are guaranteed to arrive in that order).

**Abstention spoken in full needed no backend change at all** — an abstained turn already flows through `askwell.voice_tts`'s ordinary sentence-synthesis path, speaking `askwell.agent.abstain.compose_abstention`'s own text unmodified, same as any other answer. What this ticket found and fixed instead was a real, general display bug that would have broken the requirement regardless: `statusLabel`'s idle case only ever kept the answer visible for the *stopped* branch (`M6-VUI-FE-133`), so any turn that finished normally — an abstention very much included — had its full text replaced by "Press and hold to speak" the instant `status: completed` landed. A `reason: null` idle state now keeps showing the answer too, matching "shown in full" for every normally-completed turn, not abstention alone.

Commented on issue #460 (re-owned, unaffected — this ticket renders in `MicControl`'s own tooltip, the same in-place pattern every prior voice-UI ticket used, not in `AskProvider`'s conversation transcript).
