**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (351 tests total, 12 new in `web/lib/voice.test.ts`; lint, typecheck, production build, token hygiene, contrast, offline check all clean). `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (891 passed, 1 skipped — backend untouched). Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack: the served bundle contains the new "Askwell handles English in this version" copy; a real `websockets` client against the real `/voice/ws` hit the same known wall every prior voice ticket's own walkthrough has (`TranscriptionUnavailable` — no Whisper weights in this environment), so the `language` event and the abstention-spoken-in-full path could not be exercised against a real model pass this session either. No browser tool was available, so the proactive-permission-check and the visual "answer stays shown" behaviour are unverified beyond `web/lib/voice.test.ts`'s pure-function coverage and reading the render wiring directly.

**Next**: `M6-STT-FE-129` (low-confidence confirmation) is the last open M6 ticket — once it lands, M6 is complete and takes the next `MINOR` bump (`0.6.0`, `AGENTS.md` §7).

**`0.5.11` — `M6-VUI-FE-133`**: a stop control, and deliberately no barge-in. **Eleventh ticket of M6 — 11 of 13.** Closed issue [#463](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/463) (the missing test for "speaking over the answer does nothing"); filed issue [#471](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/471) (the voice channel's wire protocol has no event letting a client tell a stopped-during-generation answer apart from a stopped-during-synthesis-only one, so the composer's post-stop label deliberately never claims "partial" even though the underlying `messages` row is marked correctly).

A "Stop" button renders in the composer whenever an answer is generating or being spoken (`canStop`, true only in the `answering` state, `web/lib/voice.ts`) and is the only way out of a running turn. Pressing it sends `{"type": "stop"}` over the voice socket — mirrored server-side onto `_Turn.stop_requested`, unchanged since `M6-TTS-BE-130` — and, locally and immediately, closes the playback `AudioContext` to discard whatever audio is queued or already playing rather than waiting for the sentence in progress to finish. `startCapture`'s existing "speaking over the answer does nothing" guard now shares one pure predicate pair (`canStartCapture`/`canStop`) with the new stop guard, closing #463: that guard existed correctly since `M6-VUI-FE-128a` but had no automated test until it moved out of component code (which touches `getUserMedia`/`WebSocket`/`AudioContext` and has no `.test.tsx` harness) into `web/lib/voice.ts`, already `node:test`-covered without a browser. Backend untouched — `askwell.ask`'s own `status == "stopped"` check inside `_run_generation`'s token loop, and `askwell.voice_tts._speak_answer`'s mirroring of `stop_requested` onto it, already did exactly what this ticket's Validation Rule needs since `M6-TTS-BE-130` landed.

**The post-stop label says only "Stopped.", never "partial"** — see #471 above and `docs/decisions.md`, this date, for the full reasoning: the voice channel's `status` WS event carries `VoiceTurn.status` (`"completed"`/`"failed"`), never `askwell.ask`'s own `"stopped"` vs `"completed"` distinction, so a client here genuinely cannot tell the two edge cases apart from the wire alone. Inventing a client-side guess was rejected as exactly the kind of fabricated signal `AGENTS.md` §4 rules out — the `messages`/`audit_interactions` record is correct regardless of what this label says.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (345 tests in `web/lib/voice.test.ts`, 8 new). `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (891 passed, 1 skipped — backend untouched). Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack: the served bundle contains the new `stop_pressed` code; a real `websockets` client against the real `/voice/ws` sent audio then an explicit `{"type": "stop"}` frame and received `turn` then `status: failed` from the real `TranscriptionUnavailable` path (no Whisper weights in this environment) — the `stop` frame is parsed and handled cleanly, the same known wall every voice ticket's own walkthrough has hit before generation is reached. No browser tool was available this session, so the actual click/hold interaction in a real browser is unverified beyond `web/lib/voice.test.ts`'s pure-function coverage and this transport-level exercise. Full detail in `docs/manual-tests/M6-VUI-FE-133.md`.

**Next (superseded by the `0.5.12` entry above — this undercounted M6, see that entry's own note)**: `M6-VUI-FE-135` (permission denied, non-English, abstention spoken in full) was still open at this point, in addition to `M6-STT-FE-129`.

**`0.5.10` — `M6-VUI-FE-132`**: the live level meter and elapsed-time indicator in the composer's mic control. **Tenth ticket of M6 — 10 of 13.** Closed issue [#454](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/454) (its blocker — no frontend voice plumbing — was resolved by `M6-VUI-FE-128a`, which had landed since #454 was filed; re-verified and closed here rather than left stale).

The level is `rmsLevel` of the same capture buffer `onaudioprocess` already downsamples and sends — one more pass over a buffer that already exists, updated via `transform: scaleX()` on every buffer (~11/s) rather than `width`, to stay off the layout thread. A muted system mic still delivers real, on-schedule callbacks at or near zero, so `micAppearsSilent` watches how long it has been since a buffer crossed `MIC_LEVEL_SILENCE_THRESHOLD` and swaps the tooltip to "Your microphone appears silent" once that exceeds 1.5s, rather than continuing to claim it is listening — the ticket's own stated edge case. Elapsed time is a plain wall-clock diff from capture start on its own interval, independent of audio arriving, so it counts through silence rather than resetting. All three (`rmsLevel`, `micAppearsSilent`, `formatElapsed`) are pure functions in `web/lib/voice.ts`, driven from `MicControl`'s existing event handlers (`onopen`, `stopCapture`, `handleConnectionLost`, `teardownCapture`) rather than a `status`-keyed effect, after the first attempt tripped `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` (`AGENTS.md` §5's own read-only-checks gate).

**Ticket scope asked for a hands-free toggle alongside push-to-talk; not built.** Issue [#455](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/455) had already resolved this exact contradiction against `docs/ux/voice.md` §7 ("Settled: push-to-talk is the only mode in v1") before this pickup — re-confirmed rather than re-litigated.

**The ticket's own acceptance criterion "the transcript and answer render in the conversation exactly as for a typed question" is not met by this ticket.** That is issue #460's scope (`AskProvider`'s `turns` still does not receive a voice turn) — a far larger change than this ticket's own granularity ("one control, two indicators") — not something to fold in silently. Commented on #460 noting `M6-VUI-FE-132` as a second dependent, alongside `M6-STT-FE-129`.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (357 tests, 18 new in `web/lib/voice.test.ts`; typecheck; lint; build; `check-tokens`; `contrast`; `check-offline`). `scripts/dev.sh web-build` succeeds. **No live mic/voice-channel walkthrough** — no browser tool available in this session, and no Whisper/Kokoro weights present, the same gap every voice ticket so far has carried forward — so the meter/silence/elapsed behaviour is verified by unit tests against the pure functions plus a clean production build, not end to end against a real turn.

**`0.5.9` — `M6-PERF-TEST-136`**: measure voice latency against the profile budgets. **Ninth ticket of M6 — 9 of 13.**

`askwell.voice_channel.VoiceTurn` gained `stage_timings`/`mark()` (first-write-wins) and six checkpoints are now marked across the real voice path: `speech_ended` (`_receive_loop`, every path that closes `audio_in` — VAD-triggered pause, `end`, `stop`), `transcription_done` (`voice_stt`, after `/transcribe` returns), `retrieval_started`/`generation_started` (`voice_tts._drain_events`, read off `askwell.ask`'s own `step` events rather than re-measured independently), and `first_sentence_ready`/`first_audio_ready` (`voice_tts._speak_sentence`, around the first `/synthesize` call). The new pure `stage_breakdown_ms` turns these into five named millisecond intervals — `transcription_ms`, `retrieval_ms`, `generation_ms`, `synthesis_ms`, and `first_audio_ms` (the ticket's own headline metric, end of speech to first audio) — with the four attributable stages summing exactly to the fifth, so a miss is always attributable. `_speak_answer` emits the breakdown as one `timing` WebSocket event right before the turn's closing audio sentinel.

`eval/voice_latency.py` (new, `scripts/dev.sh voice-latency --fixture <path> --profile <tier>`) drives `/voice/ws` repeatedly with one fixed audio input the way a real turn arrives — audio frames, then an explicit `end`, the moment a user actually stops speaking rather than a VAD guess about it — reads the server's own `timing` event per turn (never reconstructs a duration from this script's own frame-arrival timestamps, which would fold the harness's own network jitter into numbers meant to describe the voice path), discards the first turn as warm-up (reported separately), and reports median/worst-case per stage against the budget (3.5s `accelerated`, 8s everywhere else — `web/lib/voice.ts`'s own mapping, kept consistent rather than inventing a third number for `workstation`). Twenty turns by default, a JSON report recording profile/model/date written per run.

**`--profile` is a required operator argument, not read from `GET /health`.** `Settings.profile` (`light`/`balanced`/`full`) is a different, non-overlapping enum from the hardware tier the budget table is keyed on (`light`/`standard`/`accelerated`/`workstation`) — `M6-VUI-FE-134` hit this same mismatch already and reads `GET /setup`'s `probe.tier` instead of `/health`'s `profile` for the identical reason. This ticket goes one step further and calls `askwell.hardware.probe()` directly (valid under "one user, one machine" — the harness and the stack it measures share a machine by construction), checks it against the requested `--profile`, and refuses to measure on a mismatch — the ticket's own "a machine that cannot run the accelerated profile is reported as not measured, never a pass" edge case. What this does not solve — confirming the *server's own* configuration agrees with the claimed tier — is filed as issue [#466](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/466) rather than guessed around.

**No audio fixture ships with the repository, deliberately.** A usable fixture has to be real, recognisable speech — Whisper reports `no_speech` on silence or a synthetic tone, which never reaches generation or synthesis — and this environment can neither fetch one (C1) nor synthesize one (Kokoro's weights aren't present here either, the same gap every voice ticket's own walkthrough has already recorded). `--fixture` is therefore required; record one on the machine this actually runs against.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (891 passed, 1 skipped — 4 new in `api/tests/test_voice_timing.py`). `scripts/dev.sh test-db` clean (1 new in `test_voice_tts.py`, asserting the full six-mark ordering, the five-key breakdown, and the `timing` event landing last). `eval/tests/test_voice_latency.py` (9 new, pure — budget selection, median/worst summarization, dominant-stage attribution, fixture loading; run via `python -m pytest eval/tests/test_voice_latency.py -c eval/pyproject.toml`, since `eval/` has no test entry in `scripts/dev.sh` or CI, same as every other `eval/tests/*` module today). Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack: `scripts/dev.sh voice-latency --fixture <16kHz mono PCM16 wav> --profile standard --turns 2` connected to the real `/voice/ws`, streamed audio, sent `end`, and received `status: failed` from the real `TranscriptionUnavailable` path (`api` logs: "No Whisper small (CTranslate2) file at /models/whisper-small/model.bin") — the harness correctly reported `NOT MEASURED — every turn failed`, exit code 2, with a JSON report recording `profile: standard`, `model: null` (no inference process running either, honestly reported rather than guessed) and the date. **The full pass-path — a real transcript reaching generation and synthesis, actual millisecond numbers against the budget — is unverified in this environment**, the same known gap every voice ticket so far has carried forward; this is the first real exercise of `M6-PERF-TEST-136`'s own harness against real hardware and real models, and it remains outstanding.

**Next**: run this harness for real, on hardware with Whisper/Kokoro weights present, on whichever profiles are available — that is what actually answers the ticket's own open question about containerised versus native transcription (`docs/architecture.md` §2.1's "revisit if the `standard` profile misses its budget"). `M6-STT-FE-129` (low-confidence confirmation) and `M6-VUI-FE-132` (the level meter, blocked on [#454](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/454)) remained the other open M6 tickets at the time — see the `0.5.10` entry above: `-132` is now done, `#454` closed.

**`0.5.8` — `M6-VUI-FE-134`**: the past-latency-budget indicator, only once the budget is genuinely passed. **Eighth ticket of M6 — 8 of 13.** Unblocked issue [#457](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/457), closed with this ticket.

`MicControl` starts a timer at the real end of speech — `stopCapture`'s own `listening → transcribing` transition, the button release, matching what the user experiences as "I'm done talking" rather than turn start. Nothing shows on a healthy turn. The timer, and any indicator already showing, is cleared the instant the turn's own end arrives first: the first synthesized audio chunk actually reaching `playAudioChunk` ("audio begins", `voice.md` §5's own wording), the turn completing, the turn failing (its failure message replaces the indicator per `voice.md` §5), or the connection dropping — so a turn that passes budget and finishes a moment later never flickers on and off. The indicator fades in via a CSS opacity transition (`.ask-mic-latency` in `globals.css`) rather than snapping.

**Caught before landing**: the ticket's budget is keyed on the hardware tier `askwell.hardware.probe` returns (`light`/`standard`/`accelerated`/`workstation`), which is a completely different axis from `/health`'s own `profile` field — the deployment `Profile` enum (`light`/`balanced`/`full`, `askwell.config.Profile`) that selects models. The first pass read `/health` via `useStatus()` and would have silently always used the 8s budget, since that field never contains `"accelerated"`. Fixed to read `GET /setup`'s `profile.tier` instead — stated safe and cheap to poll in `askwell/setup.py`'s own docstring, and it re-probes hardware fresh regardless of the `tier` query value, which only affects the model-download half of that response. Hardware doesn't change mid-session, so `MicControl` fetches it once on mount rather than polling.

An unrecognised or unread tier falls back to the 8s `standard` budget (`voiceLatencyBudgetMs`, `lib/voice.ts`) — the ticket's own unknown-profile edge case — and that fallback is now stated on `/settings`, since nothing there described voice's latency behaviour before. A local, module-level counter (`recordVoiceLatencyBudgetMiss`/`getVoiceLatencyBudgetMissCount`) tallies budget misses for later measurement; C1 means it is never transmitted, so there is nothing to send it to.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (328 tests, 4 new; typecheck; lint — no new warnings; build; `check-tokens`; `contrast`; `check-offline`). Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack (`podman compose up -d`, headless-Chrome screenshots): `/settings` renders the new budget copy; the Ask screen's idle mic control shows no latency text at all; the API's request log confirms `MicControl` calls `GET /setup?tier=standard` once per mount and gets `200`; this machine's own hardware probe reports `tier=standard` (31 GB RAM, no GPU) — the branch this session could actually exercise live. **No live voice turn exercised** — Whisper/Kokoro weights aren't present in this environment, the same known gap every voice ticket so far has carried forward — so the fade-in, the audio-begins clear, and the failure-replaces-indicator transition are verified by `web/lib/voice.test.ts`'s unit tests and the component's own guard logic, not end to end.

**Next**: `M6-STT-FE-129` (low-confidence confirmation) and `M6-VUI-FE-132` (the level meter, blocked on [#454](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/454) — not this ticket's to resolve) remain the next real exercises of `MicControl`'s capture path against real audio in a real browser.

**`0.5.7` — `M6-VUI-FE-128a`**: mic capture, the voice socket client, and the composer's base voice states. **Seventh ticket of M6 — 7 of 13.**

Replaces the Phase 1 stub (`M1-ASK-FE-039a`): `MicControl` (`web/components/ask/ask-screen.tsx`, now `web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx`) had no `getUserMedia`, no transport, permanently `aria-disabled`. It now opens a real `WebSocket` against `askwell.voice_channel`'s `/voice/ws`, captures the mic, and drives the four base states — idle, listening, transcribing, answering — that every dependent ticket reads.

**Push-to-talk, not click-to-toggle** (`docs/ux/voice.md` §7's own settled note): holding the button starts `getUserMedia`, opens the socket, and streams audio; releasing it stops the stream and sends `{"type": "end"}`. This is what makes "transcribing" an honest state rather than a guess — the backend's own VAD (`M6-STT-BE-128`) closes a turn on a pause without telling the client, so waiting for a server signal to leave "listening" would leave nothing to show between "still capturing" and "here is the transcript." Releasing the mic is a real local fact instead, satisfying the ticket's own validation rule ("state is derived from the channel, never optimistic") from the other direction: not by waiting for a signal, but by only ever claiming a state the browser actually just did something to earn.

**The state machine and every wire/PCM conversion are pure, in new `web/lib/voice.ts`** — `nextVoiceStatus` (the reducer), `parseVoiceEvent`, `voiceSocketUrl`, and the capture/playback conversion (`downsampleTo16k`, `floatTo16BitPCM`, `encodeAudioFrame`, `pcm16ToFloat32`), the same split `lib/ask.ts` makes between SSE parsing and the `fetch` that feeds it. 25 new tests (`lib/voice.test.ts`) cover every transition, including the two the acceptance criteria name explicitly: a stray `capture_ended` outside `listening` changes nothing, and a second `capture_started` while already listening does not open a second stream. `web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx` is the browser-API wiring around it — `getUserMedia`, a `ScriptProcessorNode` downsampling the device's native rate to 16 kHz mono PCM16 for the outgoing frames (`askwell.voice.transcribe`'s own fixed input format), and a dedicated 24 kHz `AudioContext` scheduling incoming Kokoro sentences back-to-back for playback.

**Permission denied, no device, and a mid-turn drop are all states, not thrown errors** — `NotAllowedError`/`PermissionDeniedError` → idle with the denial reason stated; `NotFoundError`/`OverconstrainedError` → idle with "no microphone found"; any other `getUserMedia` failure or an unexpected socket close/error while a turn was live → idle with "the connection to Askwell was lost," never a stuck `listening`. Text input is untouched throughout — the mic control is a sibling to the textarea, never disables it.

**Not this ticket's scope, and said so in the code rather than silently done anyway**: rendering the transcript and answer into the actual conversation (`AskProvider`'s `turns`, `ask-state.tsx`) — a voice turn arrives over its own socket with its own `turn_id` and never touches `POST /ask`, and folding the two together is a real design decision (how `conversation_id` threading, issue #156, applies to a voice-originated turn; whether a voice answer should replay through the same streaming path a typed one does) that deserves its own ticket rather than a guess made here. Filed as issue [#460](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/460), with a recommendation. The mic control shows its own transcript/answer text in place, in the tooltip beside it, which is what makes every state transition in this ticket's own acceptance criteria observable without that larger change. The level meter, the stop control, the latency indicator, low-confidence confirmation, and the remaining voice states (non-English, abstention spoken in full) are explicitly out of scope, per the ticket's own text — `M6-STT-FE-129` and `M6-VUI-FE-132` through `135`.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh web-run pnpm test` (324 passed, 25 new in `lib/voice.test.ts`, the existing 299 unchanged). `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean end to end — lint (no new warnings; the 2 pre-existing ones are in unrelated files), typecheck, format, the 324 tests, `next build` (static export unaffected, no new route), `check-version`, `check-tokens` (one flagged literal fixed — a `#156` issue reference read as a hex colour by the regex; reworded to "issue 156"), `contrast` (38 pairs, unaffected — no new colour introduced), `check-offline` (72 built files, no new fetching reference to an external host — the WebSocket is same-origin, loopback, not egress). **Cold-start walkthrough not run against a real browser and microphone in this environment** — this session has no display and no audio input device to grant permission to. Verified instead: the rebuilt static export (`scripts/dev.sh web-build`) is live under the running stack's bind-mounted `./web/out`, and the built JS bundle contains the `/voice/ws` reference, confirming the new code actually ships rather than only compiling. The capture-to-transcript-to-answer path itself, and the two states that need a real socket to prove (a genuine mid-turn drop, a genuine low-confidence transcript once `-129` lands), remain unexercised live — the same shape of gap every voice ticket on the backend side has carried forward for the identical reason (no model weights there; no browser/mic here).

**Next**: `M6-STT-FE-129` (low-confidence confirmation) is now unblocked and depends directly on this ticket. `M6-VUI-FE-132` (the level meter) is the next ticket that can exercise this one's real capture path against real audio in a real browser.

**`0.5.6` — `M6-TTS-BE-131`**: fall back to text when synthesis is unavailable. **Sixth ticket of M6 — 6 of 12.**

Before this ticket, `askwell.voice_tts._speak_answer` let `SynthesisUnavailable`/`SynthesisFailed` propagate straight out of its poll loop. `askwell.voice_channel._run_driver`'s own broad `except Exception` then caught it and marked the whole voice turn `failed` — even though the answer had already generated correctly, the pending `messages` row was left `running` forever (nothing updates it once `_speak_answer` itself raises rather than returning), and the user got nothing at all over one model's own missing weights. That is exactly the failure `docs/ux/voice.md` §5's "TTS unavailable" row and `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §5's identical row rule out.

Now `_speak_answer` catches both exceptions per sentence, inside a new `_speak_sentence` wrapper: the first failure for a turn sets `synthesis_unavailable_this_turn` and calls the new `VoiceTurn.emit_synthesis_unavailable(reason)`, which flips `synthesis_available` off and appends a new `voice` WebSocket event (`{"type": "voice", "available": false, "reason": ...}`, `_Event.kind` on `askwell.voice_channel` gained this kind) — the note a future voice screen renders. No further sentence is sent to `/synthesize` for the rest of that turn: deliberately no per-sentence retry, since a model that failed to load will not load again between two sentences a few hundred milliseconds apart, and "no automatic repair" is the ticket's own stated known gap. Generation and the on-screen text are completely unaffected — `_drain_events` keeps forwarding `token`/`citation`/`fact_citation` exactly as before, so the answer completes as text, and `turn.status` still reflects `ask_turn.status`, never the synthesis outcome. `register_voice_channel`'s reconnect handshake resends the `voice` event alongside `transcript`/`text`/`confidence`/`language` so a client that reconnects mid-turn still learns the note.

**Availability is tracked across turns, not reset every call**: `build_tts_driver` now closes over one `availability: dict[str, bool]` and threads it into every `_speak_answer` call it makes (`_speak_answer` gained an `availability` parameter, `None` — a fresh per-call dict — for callers that don't care, mostly tests). A turn's first failure sets `availability["synthesis"] = False` and logs `voice_synthesis_unavailable` once; the next turn whose first `/synthesize` call succeeds flips it back and logs `voice_synthesis_recovered`. This is what "recovery without a reload" rests on structurally: each turn's first synthesis attempt is a fresh HTTP call regardless of the turn before it, so a later turn simply succeeds once the `voice` container's Kokoro model is back — nothing to reload, restart, or cache-invalidate. The two log lines double as the ticket's own C1 "local counter" analytics requirement and its "availability transitions are logged" requirement, the same shape every other local counter in this codebase already takes (a `log.info`/`log.warning` line, nothing transmitted).

**Out of scope, correctly**: the "both transcription and synthesis unavailable → voice mode disabled, typing unaffected" edge case is a connect-time frontend decision (disable the mic control before a turn ever starts), not reachable from inside a turn already driving `_speak_answer` — deferred to whichever `M6-VUI-FE` ticket reads `voice`'s own `/health` before enabling the mic. Not stubbed here.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (887 passed, 1 skipped, unaffected). `scripts/dev.sh test-db` clean (660 passed — `test_voice_tts.py` now 13, 3 new: synthesis unavailable falls back to text with exactly one `voice` note and the turn still completes; a mid-answer failure after one successfully spoken sentence speaks no more but the rest of the answer is still text, with one note rather than one per remaining sentence; and synthesis recovers on the very next turn, sharing one `availability` mapping across two `_speak_answer` calls the way a real driver's two turns actually would). `scripts/dev.sh lint`/`typecheck`/`format` clean. **Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack, no Whisper/Kokoro weights present** (C1 forbids fetching them here): `voice`'s `/health` still reports both `missing`; a real `websockets` client against `api`'s `/voice/ws`, sending silence and an explicit `end`, produced `TranscriptionUnavailable` → `status: failed` in the `api` log before this ticket's own code is ever reached — Whisper, not Kokoro, is the first model on the path, so this is the same known gap every voice ticket so far has carried forward, one step earlier than `M6-TTS-BE-130`'s own walkthrough hit it. This ticket's new fallback logic is verified only against the real stack's own test run above, not exercised live end to end in this environment.

**Next**: `M6-VUI-FE-132` (the composer's real microphone capture) remains the first ticket that can exercise `128`/`130`/`131` against real audio; `M6-STT-FE-129` (low-confidence confirmation) remains a parallel sibling ticket, not gated on this one.

**`0.5.5` — `M6-TTS-BE-130`**: sentence-streamed speech synthesis — a transcript now drives a real answer, spoken sentence by sentence as it streams. **Fifth ticket of M6 — 5 of 12.**

`api/src/askwell/voice_stt.py`'s `build_stt_driver` gained an `on_transcript` hook: a successfully transcribed turn now continues into generation and speech instead of ending there, unchanged for every other caller (its own tests, and the STT-only path from before this ticket). New `api/src/askwell/voice_tts.py`'s `build_tts_driver` is that hook: it builds an `askwell.ask._Turn` and starts `askwell.ask._generate` on it directly — the same "caller driving `_generate` against a turn it built itself" pattern `test_ask_api.py`'s own stop/disconnection tests already use, not a new one — then polls its `events`/`text` the way `askwell.ask._tail` polls for SSE. Three things happen as new events arrive: every `token` delta forwards verbatim onto the voice turn as a `text` event (screen unchanged, markers and all); every `citation`/`fact_citation` event forwards verbatim too, so a future voice screen can render the same source cards a typed answer's does (`_Event.kind` on `askwell.voice_channel.VoiceTurn` gained both kinds, plus `emit_citation`/`emit_fact_citation`); and once `askwell.agent.claims.segment_sentences` (new — the unmarked-sentence superset `segment_claims` draws its cited subset from) reports a newly completed sentence, that sentence — marker gone, never read aloud — is sent to the `voice` container's new `POST /synthesize` (`askwell.voice.synthesize`, against the already-loaded Kokoro handle, mirroring `/transcribe`'s shape) and the resulting PCM16 audio is queued on `VoiceTurn.audio_out`, so speech starts on the first sentence rather than waiting for the whole answer. Stopping is cooperative: `VoiceTurn.stop_requested` mirrors onto the `_Turn.stop_requested` a `POST /ask/{id}/stop` would set, `_run_generation`'s own token loop already breaks on it promptly, and no further sentence is sent to synthesis once seen, even one already complete in the buffered text — the ticket's own "stops promptly rather than finishing the buffered sentence" edge case. A trailing sentence with no terminating punctuation (the model ran out of tokens mid-answer) is still spoken, as a last best-effort unit, rather than silently dropped.

**Citations are named naturally, resolving issue #447, without touching the shared prompt.** The issue's own recommendation was a voice-mode instruction in `answer_composition.v1.md` asking the model to phrase a source in prose; rejected — that prompt composes the same text the screen renders in every mode, so a model that starts naming sources in prose would leak straight into the typed, on-screen answer too, breaking this ticket's own Validation Rule, and a prompt change needs an eval run `AGENTS.md` §4 has no suite built for. Instead `_natural_reference`/`_spoken_form` (`voice_tts.py`) derive a spoken phrase mechanically from a citation's own `filename` — `"supplier-agreement-2024.pdf"` becomes `"from the supplier agreement 2024"` — added only the first time a given document is cited in an answer, not on every marker, so a repeatedly-cited source is not renamed every sentence. Only document citations get one; a memory-fact or schema-note citation has no filename to build a phrase from and is simply spoken with the marker gone, same as before this ticket. Full reasoning, including what was rejected and why, in `docs/decisions.md`, this date.

New `api/src/askwell/voice/synthesize.py` (`synthesize`, `SynthesisResult`) runs inside the `voice` container against the loaded `kokoro_onnx.Kokoro` handle, converting its float32 output to PCM16 little-endian bytes; `voice/service.py`'s new `/synthesize` returns `503` naming the missing model's path exactly like `/transcribe` when Kokoro is not loaded, and `400` for empty text. `Settings.voice_kokoro_voice` (default `af_heart` — the one voice `hexgrad/Kokoro-82M`'s own `VOICES.md` grades `A` overall and marks as its favourite, verified against the registry this date) is the new, never-hardcoded configuration point for which preset speaks; voice/speed selection beyond this sensible default is out of this ticket's scope.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (`scripts/dev.sh test`: 887 passed, 1 skipped, unaffected; `scripts/dev.sh test-db`: 658 passed, including 8 new in `test_voice_tts.py` covering sentence-by-sentence synthesis while generation is still running, screen text carrying the raw marker unchanged, a source named naturally only once, a very short one-sentence answer, a trailing unterminated sentence, prompt stopping speaking no further sentence, a `/synthesize` failure propagating, and the pending answer row existing before generation starts). `scripts/dev.sh lint`/`typecheck`/`format` clean. **Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack, no Kokoro/Whisper weights present** (C1 forbids fetching them here): `voice`'s `/health` still reports both `missing` with their own paths; a direct `POST /synthesize` against the running `voice` container answered `503` naming Kokoro's missing path rather than crashing; a real `websockets` client against `api`'s `/voice/ws`, sending audio and an explicit `end`, produced the same `status: failed` `M6-STT-BE-127`/`-128` already documented (Whisper absent, so this ticket's own new code — generation and synthesis — is never reached on this machine) with no new traceback in the logs. The generation-and-speech path itself is exercised only against fakes (no real inference server or Kokoro weights in this environment), not live end to end — the same known gap every voice ticket so far has carried forward.

**Next**: `M6-VUI-FE-132` (the composer's real microphone capture) is the first ticket that can exercise any of `M6-STT-BE-128`/`-130`'s pause-detection and sentence-streaming against real audio rather than fakes; `M6-STT-FE-129` (low-confidence confirmation) remains a parallel sibling ticket, not gated on this one.

**`0.5.4` — `M6-STT-BE-128`**: turn detection — a voice turn closes itself on a pause. **Fourth ticket of M6 — 4 of 12.**

`api/src/askwell/voice/vad.py` (new, runs inside the `voice` container against the already-loaded Silero VAD ONNX session) scores each complete 512-sample (32ms at 16kHz) frame for speech probability, resetting the recurrent state to zero every call rather than carrying it across frames — no per-turn state lives in that container, matching `askwell.voice.transcribe`'s own posture. `voice/service.py` exposes this over a new `POST /vad`, `503` with the model's own path when nothing is loaded, same shape as `/transcribe`.

`api/src/askwell/voice_turn_detection.py` (new) is the production `TurnDetector`, wired into `register_voice_channel` from `app.py` alongside the STT driver: one instance per connection, fed every raw audio frame `voice_channel._receive_loop` reads (buffering any remainder short of a full frame into its next call), calling `/vad` over the `internal` network. It tracks whether speech has been heard at all this turn — so a pause before any speech (the "releasing push-to-talk before speaking" edge case, `docs/ux/voice.md` §5) never closes anything, the turn still ends only on the client's own release — and how much trailing silence has piled up since the last frame that looked like speech, closing the turn (same `None` sentinel, same `audio_in_closed` guard as an explicit `end`) once that crosses `Settings.voice_vad_pause_ms` (default 700ms). `voice_channel._receive_loop` now drops any audio frame arriving after the turn has closed, guarding against an already-closed queue quietly growing in continuous hands-free capture. `register_voice_channel` gained a `turn_detector` parameter with the identical injection shape as `driver`; its default (`null_turn_detector_factory`) never fires, so a caller supplying neither gets exactly the pre-ticket behaviour — proven by a dedicated test.

**700ms is a reasoned starting point, not a measured one** — no recorded-speech fixtures exist in this environment (no microphone, no bundled audio, and `M6-VUI-FE-132`'s composer control has not landed to generate real ones) to tune it against. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning for both this and the choice to score VAD statelessly per frame over HTTP rather than keep a per-turn recurrent-state session alive in the `voice` container.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (887 passed, 1 skipped — 35 new: `test_voice_vad.py` covers frame scoring against a fake ONNX session, including the documented input shape/dtype and that a trailing partial frame is dropped not padded; `test_voice_turn_detection.py` covers the pause/no-pause/reset/unreachable-service/error-status cases against a faked `/vad`; `test_voice_service.py` and `test_voice_channel.py` gained cases for the new endpoint and the detector wiring, including that no detector given behaves exactly as before this ticket). `scripts/dev.sh test-db` clean, unaffected — this ticket touches no database code. **Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack, no Silero weights present** (C1 forbids fetching them here): `voice`'s `/health` still reports transcription/synthesis `missing` with their own paths; `POST /vad` on `voice` answers `503` naming the VAD model's path; a real `websockets` client against `api`'s `/voice/ws`, sending one frame of audio and an explicit `end`, produced a logged `POST http://voice:8090/vad` → `503` → `voice_vad_unavailable` warning → the turn still closed on the client's `end` exactly as before this ticket (an unreachable VAD degrades to no-op, never blocks the transport) → `status: failed` (Whisper also absent, the same known gap `M6-STT-BE-127` already carries forward). The pause-closes-a-turn path itself is exercised only against fakes, not live — real Silero weights remain the gap.

**Next**: `M6-STT-FE-129` — low confidence: show the transcript and confirm before answering. Depends on this ticket (done).

**`0.5.3` — `M6-STT-BE-127`**: English transcription behind the voice channel. **Third ticket of M6 — 3 of 12.**

`api/src/askwell/voice/transcribe.py` (new, runs inside the `voice` container against the already-loaded Whisper handle) turns one complete spoken turn's audio into a `TranscriptionResult`: `ok` (a transcript plus a duration-weighted `exp(avg_logprob)` confidence), `no_speech` (Whisper's own thresholds dropped every candidate segment, or the turn closed with no audio at all), or `unsupported_language` (the language-ID pass that `faster-whisper` runs before decoding says the speech isn't English — `info` is ready before the segment generator is ever touched, so "no attempt at another language" holds literally, not just as a stated rule). `voice/service.py` exposes this over a new `POST /transcribe` — `503` with a reason when no model is loaded, `200` with the result otherwise, deliberately not the always-`200` shape `/health` uses, since this is an action a caller has to act on differently depending on the outcome.

`api/src/askwell/voice_stt.py` (new) is the production `TurnDriver`, wired into `register_voice_channel` from `app.py`: it buffers a turn's audio (no partial-utterance streaming ASR — that stays this simple until `M6-STT-BE-128`'s VAD closes turns instead of only the client's own `end`/`stop` signal), calls `voice`'s `/transcribe` over the `internal` network, and stores the outcome — a `messages` row (role `user`), a `conversations` row stamped `mode='voice'` when the client gave none (`VoiceTurn` gained a `conversation_id`, settable via a new `/voice/ws?conversation_id=...` query param), and an `audit_interactions` record (`voice_transcribed`) with confidence and language probability stored as integer percentages, since audit payloads cannot carry a float (`askwell.audit._reject_floats`). A turn with no speech stores nothing at all — `128`'s own stated rule ("no interaction record because nothing was asked") applied here even though `128` hasn't landed. `voice_channel.py`'s `_Event` moved from a fixed `text` field to a generic `fields: dict[str, Any]` so the new `confidence`/`language` WebSocket events could be added alongside `transcript`/`text` without a parallel code path.

**Two calls worth reading in `docs/decisions.md`, this date**: the WebSocket's audio wire format is now fixed at 16 kHz mono 16-bit signed PCM, little-endian — decided here, with no frontend voice code yet to consult, because Silero VAD (`128`) and `faster-whisper` both want exactly this and one format serving capture, VAD and Whisper avoids a resample step at every future boundary; and this ticket persists the transcript itself rather than leaving it to `M6-TTS-BE-130`, because the Acceptance Criteria says so explicitly and C6 makes an unrecorded interaction a bug, not a limitation `130` gets to fix later.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (869 passed, 1 skipped — 15 new: `test_voice_transcribe.py` covers the model logic against a fake Whisper with no real weights needed; `test_voice_service.py` adds `/transcribe`'s 200 and 503 paths; `test_voice_channel.py`'s existing suite is untouched by the `_Event` refactor, confirming the wire shape for `transcript`/`text` didn't change). `scripts/dev.sh test-db` clean (650 passed — `test_voice_stt.py`'s 7 cases against a real Postgres: the `ok`/`no_speech`/`unsupported_language`/no-audio/unavailable paths, and that an existing conversation's `mode` is left untouched). **Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack, no Whisper weights present** (C1 forbids fetching them here — `M6-AUDIO-DEPLOY-125`'s own known gap, still open): `voice`'s `/health` reports transcription `missing` naming the exact path; `POST /transcribe` on `voice` answers `503` with the same reason; a real `websockets` client connected to `api`'s `/voice/ws` from inside the `api` container, sent audio and an `end` signal, and the `api` log showed `voice_turn_started` → a real `POST http://voice:8090/transcribe` → `503` → `TranscriptionUnavailable` → `voice_turn_finished status=failed`, delivered over the socket as `{"type": "status", "status": "failed"}`, with nothing written to `messages` (confirmed against Postgres directly). That request reaching `voice:8090` directly, not `egress-proxy`, is what proves the `NO_PROXY` addition on `api` (now includes `voice`) actually works rather than merely being present in `compose.yaml`. `scripts/verify-localhost-binding.sh` still passes in full. The `ok`/`no_speech`/`unsupported_language` paths and the real confidence numbers are exercised only against fakes, not live — real Whisper weights remain the known gap this milestone carries forward.

**Next**: `M6-STT-BE-128` — turn detection with Silero VAD, closing a turn on a pause instead of only on the client's own `end`/`stop` signal; push-to-talk as the default with a hands-free toggle. Depends on this ticket (done).

**`0.5.2` — `M6-AUDIO-API-126`**: the bidirectional voice WebSocket channel — audio in, audio out, transcript and text on one connection. **Second ticket of M6 — 2 of 12.**

Mounted on `api` (`/voice/ws`, `api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py`), not on the `voice` container — `docs/architecture.md` §2 already locks down that only `api` is reachable from outside the machine, and `voice` has no route to the host at all. `VoiceTurn` is the audio analogue of `askwell.ask`'s `_Turn`/`_turns`: it lives independently of any one connection, keyed by a server-minted `turn_id`, so a dropped connection can reconnect (`?turn_id=...`) and resume rather than restart — leaning on `M1-ASK-BE-040`'s "generation continues server-side," per the ticket's own Assumption. `transcript`/`text` are running strings resent in full on every (re)attach ("the text is complete regardless," `docs/ux/voice.md` §5); `audio_out` is a bounded `asyncio.Queue` with **no replay history at all** — a reconnect just keeps draining the same queue instance from wherever it is, which is what makes "the remaining audio, not from the start" true with no bookkeeping. Backpressure on both `audio_in` and `audio_out` is the same bounded-queue mechanism (`Settings.voice_audio_queue_size`, default 32): a slow client or a slow consumer stalls the producer rather than growing memory, and a long spoken question streams into `audio_in` chunk by chunk rather than buffering whole.

**There is no transcription or synthesis behind this channel yet**, deliberately — `M6-STT-BE-127`/`M6-TTS-BE-130` haven't landed, and `127`'s own acceptance criteria already owns storing the transcript on `messages` and the audit record, so this ticket doesn't duplicate that. `register_voice_channel` takes an injectable `driver`; production runs `_default_driver`, which drains incoming audio and ends the turn on the client's `end` signal without inventing a transcript, text or audio — proving the transport, not the pipeline. Nothing writes to `conversations`/`messages`/the audit log yet; full reasoning for both calls (mounting on `api`, and audio having no replay history) in `docs/decisions.md`, this date.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (861 passed, 1 skipped, 9 new in `test_voice_channel.py`: all four event kinds on one connection, a long input streaming as separate chunks rather than one buffered blob, the stop control, reconnect after completion with no audio replay, reconnect mid-synthesis continuing rather than replaying from the start, and backpressure on both queues asserted directly). **Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack**: a real `websockets` client run from inside the running `api` container connected to `ws://127.0.0.1:8000/voice/ws`, sent audio frames and an `end` control message, and received the `turn` id followed by `status: completed`; reconnecting with that same `turn_id` after the first connection closed resumed the identical turn and completed the same way. `scripts/verify-localhost-binding.sh` still passes in full — the channel adds no new bound port.

**Next**: `M6-STT-BE-127` — English transcription with Whisper `small`, delivered as a transcript before any answer begins. Depends on this ticket (done).

**`0.5.1` — `M6-AUDIO-DEPLOY-125`**: the voice container — Whisper `small`, Silero VAD and Kokoro-82M, all local, all CPU. **First ticket of M6 — 1 of 12.**

A new `voice` service (`api/src/askwell/voice/`) reuses the existing `askwell` image and pyproject rather than getting its own — same shape as `egress-proxy`/`inference-bridge`, a new `askwell-voice` entrypoint. `askwell.voice.models.load_models` opens Whisper `small` (`faster-whisper`, CTranslate2, CPU), Silero VAD (`onnxruntime` directly, not the `silero-vad` PyPI package, to avoid a `torch` dependency for a model this small) and Kokoro-82M (`kokoro-onnx`) from local files named by new `Settings` paths, all defaulting under `/models` — bind-mounted read-only from a new `ASKWELL_MODELS_DIR` (default the same host directory the native inference supervisor already reads its own three models from). `GET /health` reports `transcription` (Whisper folded with VAD, since Whisper cannot usefully run without VAD gating the stream ahead of it) and `synthesis` (Kokoro) separately, always `200` — matching `api/src/askwell/health.py`'s own no-aggregate-boolean rule, not the 200/503 split this ticket's first draft used before that inconsistency was caught. A missing model file never crashes the process; it becomes a `missing` `ModelHealth` naming the exact path, mirroring `deploy/inference/askwell-inference`'s own `MODEL_MISSING` handling. `voice` sits on the `internal` network only — no `egress` membership, unlike `api`/`worker` — since nothing in its scope ever needs a route out (`docs/decisions.md`, "no escalation to the web from voice").

Out of scope, per the ticket: the WebSocket audio transport (`M6-AUDIO-API-126`) and the interface. Nothing can be spoken or transcribed yet — this ticket only loads models and reports on them.

A real, unrelated bug surfaced while verifying this ticket live: `egress-proxy` has been crash-looping since before this session (`RestartCount=25878`) because `compose.yaml` never gives it the three sandbox `Settings` fields (`sandbox_database_url`, `sandbox_owner_password`, `sandbox_readonly_password`), all required with no default. Filed as issue #439 rather than fixed here — out of this ticket's scope, and `docs/decisions.md`'s new entry records why `voice` was given the full set defensively instead of hitting the same failure.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (852 passed, 1 skipped, 8 new — `test_voice_models.py`, `test_voice_service.py`); `scripts/dev.sh lint`/`typecheck`/`fmt-check` clean; `scripts/dev.sh build-api` built clean with the three new dependencies resolved by `scripts/dev.sh lock` with no conflicts. **Cold-start walkthrough against the real, rebuilt, running stack**: `podman compose up -d voice` with no model files present started cleanly, logged `voice_startup` naming both missing models by their exact path; `GET /health` returned `200` with `transcription`/`synthesis` both `"missing"` and a reason naming the path; a socket connect from inside the `voice` container to a public address failed with "Network is unreachable" (`internal` has no route out at all, so nothing reaches the egress proxy to be counted as refused, let alone permitted); stopping `voice` left `GET /health` on the `api` container at `200` throughout, confirming text asking is unaffected. Loading real model weights was not exercised in this session — that needs the actual files, and downloading them as part of verification would itself violate C1.

**`0.5.0` — `M5-TRACE-FE-123`**: the trace panel's remaining states (`docs/ux/trace.md` §5) — normal, abstention, tool ceiling and rotation already existed from `M5-TRACE-FE-119`/`-121`, confirming the note at the end of the `0.4.39` entry below. This ticket's own new work: failed mid-answer, partial, and the online-backend readiness for M8. **This ticket completed M5 — 14 of 14 — `0.4.39` → `0.5.0`.**

`web/lib/trace.ts` gained `isFailedTrace`/`failureReason` reading `trace.status === "failed"`/`trace.reason` — both already written by `_run_generation` on every failure path but previously unread on the frontend — and `isPartialTrace`/`partialUncoveredAspects` reading the same `partial_coverage`/`uncovered_aspects` `M2-PARTIAL-BE-057` already writes onto the trace itself (not just the `compose` step). `trace-panel.tsx`'s `TraceBody` renders a failed trace's steps followed by the error in `--alarm` — `design-system.md`'s own token table reserves `--alarm` for "Failures only", so this deliberately does not copy `ask-screen.tsx`'s existing `--muted` treatment of the same case; filed as issue #436 rather than silently fixed there, since that file is outside this ticket's scope — and a partial trace's uncovered aspects in the same "Not covered by your files" block `ask-screen.tsx`'s own `UncoveredBlock` already renders for the answer body. `isOnlineBackend` plus a new optional `TraceBackend.sent` field make `BackendLine` ready to show "what was sent" once `backend.mode` can read `"online"` — unreachable before M8, since nothing writes that value yet, matching the ticket's own Out of Scope line.

The rotated-away state (`trace_rotated`) needed no new rendering — `M5-TRACE-FE-119` already built it, matching the previous entry's prediction. This ticket instead added the test for the ticket's own named edge case ("an answer whose citations were also lost — impossible by design"): `hitCitation`'s signature takes a hit and a citation list, never a `TraceData`, so a rotated trace structurally has nothing to lose a citation from — citations live in the separate `citations` table (`docs/audit-log.md` §2's three-store split) and are fetched onto `turn.citations` independently of the trace endpoint entirely.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (842 passed, 1 skipped — no backend change, so no new cases); `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (299 tests, 13 new in `web/lib/trace.test.ts` covering all seven states plus the citation/rotation invariant; lint, typecheck, production build, token hygiene, contrast, offline check all clean). **No browser available in this session** — the failed/partial/online-backend branches are verified at the unit level and by reading the render wiring against real backend fields (`ask.py`'s `status`/`reason`/`partial_coverage`/`uncovered_aspects`), not by an interactive click-through; the online-backend state remains unreachable until M8 regardless of environment. Re-verify the failed and partial states visually against a real interrupted/compound turn once a browser is available, per this ticket's own cold-start walkthrough. **Next**: M6 — *I can speak to it* — not started; `M6-AUDIO-DEPLOY-125` is its first ticket.

**`0.4.39` — `M5-TRACE-FE-122`**: threshold adjustment from an abstention trace, with the consequence stated — the last ticket in the `TRACE` epic (`M5-TRACE-FE-119`/`-120`/`-121`/`-BE-125`).

The retrieval threshold becomes a real, runtime-adjustable setting for the first time — `Settings.retrieval_score_threshold` (`0.65`) was deploy-time only before this ticket, and this ticket's own Acceptance Criteria needs a change to take effect on the very next question, which a `.env` value cannot do without a restart on a product with no operator to restart it. New `askwell.retrieve.get_retrieval_threshold`/`set_retrieval_threshold` read and write an override in the generic `settings` table, the same shape `askwell.clarify.get_clarification_cap`/`set_clarification_cap` already established for a per-install preference; `retrieve()` now calls the getter instead of reading `Settings` directly, so `askwell.ask`'s abstention decision (still just `result.threshold`) picks up a change with no code change of its own. `set_retrieval_threshold` is the only way the value changes and always writes an `audit_decisions` record (`retrieval_threshold_changed`) with the old and new value **as strings** — `canonical_payload` refuses a float outright, found while testing (a `jsonb` round trip does not preserve one identically, which would make verification report tampering that never happened). New `GET`/`POST /settings/retrieval-threshold` (`askwell.retrieve.register_retrieval_threshold`) is the one endpoint pair both frontend surfaces below share.

The abstention trace panel offers `RetrievalThresholdControl` (new `web/components/settings/retrieval-threshold.tsx`) only when `web/lib/retrieval-threshold.ts`'s new `nearMiss()` finds one: the trace's own `abstain` step says `below_threshold` **and** its `retrieve` step actually has a hit — never for `empty_corpus`/`source_indexing`, where loosening the threshold would not have helped (the ticket's own named Edge Case). The control states the consequence with the real scores ("The closest passage scored 0.61, just under the 0.65 threshold...") plus a generic sentence covering both directions (lowering answers more, wrong more often; raising abstains more, right more often when it does answer) — a number field and an explicit "Change threshold" button, deliberately not a slider, so a change is a submitted decision rather than a value that drifted while dragging. The identical component, same copy, is now also reachable from a new "Retrieval threshold" section on the settings screen, without the near-miss sentence — `docs/ux/settings.md` §2's own "same warning, reachable here, never frictionless."

The existing guard test (`eval/tests/test_abstain.py::test_retrieval_score_threshold_default_has_not_been_quietly_lowered`, pinning `Settings.model_fields["retrieval_score_threshold"].default == 0.65`) needed no change — it guards the deploy-time default, which this ticket deliberately leaves alone; the runtime override it did not anticipate is structurally forced through `set_retrieval_threshold`, which cannot skip recording a decision, so "a user lowering it repeatedly — each change is recorded" (the ticket's own Edge Case) holds by construction rather than needing its own guard.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (842 passed, 1 skipped); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` clean (644 passed, 4 new in `api/tests/test_retrieve_records.py`: the threshold defaults to the configured value, a stored override is read back and used by `retrieve()`, changing it writes a decisions record with the old and new value, a value outside `[0, 1]` is rejected); `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (290 tests, 5 new in `web/lib/retrieval-threshold.test.ts` for `nearMiss()`'s five cases — found and fixed in the same session that `web/package.json`'s `test` script lists test files explicitly rather than globbing, so the new file had to be added there to actually run). **Cold-start walkthrough run against the real, rebuilt, running stack** (`scripts/dev.sh build-api`, `podman compose up -d --force-recreate api worker`): `GET /settings/retrieval-threshold` returned the default `0.65`; `POST {"threshold": 0.5}` was read back as `0.5` immediately; `POST {"threshold": 1.5}` was rejected `422`; `audit_decisions` recorded `{"previous": "0.65", "new": "0.5"}`; `podman compose exec api askwell-verify` reported the chain intact both with the test record present and after removing it (the tail of the chain — safe, since nothing chains after it) — threshold reset to `0.65` and the two test decision rows removed afterward, leaving no product-data trace, per this repo's established pattern for manual verification. **Not verified**: an interactive browser click-through (no browser tool in this session) — the near-miss gating and the settings-screen section are verified at the unit level and by reading the wiring, not by watching a real abstained turn's trace panel render the control. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning. **Next**: `M5-TRACE-FE-123` — trace states, including the rotated-away trace — is the one remaining ticket in `docs/backlog/M5-it-handles-harder-questions.md`'s `TRACE` section; note that `trace_rotated` rendering already exists (`M5-TRACE-FE-119`/`-121`), so that ticket may be substantially a verification pass rather than new surface, not confirmed here.

**`0.4.38` — `M5-TRACE-FE-121`**: trace interactions — expand, click through, copy. The last ticket in the `TRACE` chain (`M5-TRACE-FE-119`/`-120`/`-BE-125`).

A retrieved passage the answer actually cited now shows full text and clicks through to the source viewer at that position: `web/lib/trace.ts`'s new `hitCitation(hit, citations)` matches a trace hit's `chunk_id` against the turn's own `citations` array — both a `citation` SSE event and a `retrieve` trace step's `hits[].chunk_id` stringify the same `candidate.chunk_id` in `askwell.ask` (lines 453 and 1699), confirmed by reading the backend rather than assumed. A hit with no matching citation (a near-miss, or one outscored for its own claim) still shows only its score — nothing else was ever sent to the browser for it. A cited passage from a since-deleted document renders greyed and not clickable, reusing `provenance-margin.tsx`'s own `useDeletion` (now exported) rather than a second fetch — the ticket's own Edge Case, "consistent with the answer's cards." A memory fact in the trace now renders as the literal same `MemoryChip` an answer's own claim uses, correct-and-delete popover included — "the same popover as in an answer" is only true reusing the component, so it moved out of `ask-screen.tsx` into its own `web/components/ask/memory-chip.tsx` (a plain extraction, not a rewrite — `trace-panel.tsx` importing it back from `ask-screen.tsx` would be a circular import, since `ask-screen.tsx` already imports `TraceToggle`). "Copy trace" (`buildTraceCopyText`, `web/lib/trace.ts`) produces plain text with the question, backend, every step's summary/duration/scores/threshold/query, the tool-ceiling note if any, and a stated truncation past 20,000 characters — the ticket's own edge case for a very long trace.

The panel's open/closed state moved off a local `useState` onto `AskProvider` (`ask-state.tsx`'s new `openTraceTurnId`/`openTrace`/`closeTrace`): clicking a passage navigates to `/documents/`, which unmounts the panel's own page entirely, and only state held above the router (`AskProvider` lives in the root layout) survives that round trip. Landing back on `/` via the viewer's existing "Back to answer" link finds the same turn's `openTraceTurnId` still set, so the trace panel is simply still open — no new query-param plumbing needed for the ticket's own "returning restores the trace panel" assumption.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (lint, typecheck, 285 tests including 6 new ones for `hitCitation`/`buildTraceCopyText`, production build, token hygiene, contrast, offline check). **No browser available in this session, and the development database currently has no ingested documents** (`select id, filename from documents` returned zero rows) — there is no real citation to click through end to end right now. The chunk-id join, the plain-text export, and the deleted/undeleted branches are verified at the unit level and by reading the backend source that produces both sides of the join; the interactive round trip itself (click a passage → viewer → back → still-open panel; click a fact → correct → reflected) was not exercised against a live browser or a real corpus. Re-verify visually once a corpus exists, per this ticket's own cold-start walkthrough. **Next**: `M5-TRACE-FE-122` — threshold adjustment from an abstention trace, with the consequence stated (`docs/backlog/M5-it-handles-harder-questions.md`) — the last ticket in the `TRACE` epic and this ticket's own named Out of Scope item.

**`0.4.37` — `M5-TRACE-FE-120`**: what is inside a trace step's raw detail, formatted per `docs/ux/trace.md` §3 rather than the raw JSON `M5-TRACE-FE-119` left as every expander. `web/lib/trace.ts` gained pure readers — `retrievedHits`/`retrievalThreshold` (sorted highest-first, so a near-miss is the top of the list), `memoryFactRefs`, `sqlStepInfo` (reading a `kind: "sql"` step and a `kind: "tool", tool: "database_query"` step through one shape, since `docs/ux/trace.md` §3 draws no distinction between the single-shot and loop paths), `toolInjectionPatterns`, `toolCeilingPendingCalls` — and `trace-panel.tsx`'s `StepDetail` dispatches on `step.kind` to a matching renderer (`RetrieveStepDetail`, `MemoryRetrieveStepDetail`, `SqlStepDetail`, `ToolStepDetail`), falling back to the raw JSON dump for any kind it does not specifically know, same as `stepSummary` already does for its own fallback. A `memory_retrieve` step's bare ids are resolved to real facts via `GET /memory/facts/{kind}/{id}` (`lib/memory-chips.ts::fetchFactDetail`, the same read a chip's popover already makes) rather than a new endpoint. A database step's query renders through `QueryDisclosure`, reused from `sql-result-table.tsx` — its existing `LIMIT ... /* Added by Askwell */` highlighting is what surfaces the injected limit, so this ticket adds no second field to keep in sync with it. Backend/model are named once per turn from `trace.backend`, above the step list; a tool-ceiling stop renders below it from `trace.loop_stopped_reason`/`loop_pending_calls`. Injection flags (tool-level `injection_flagged`/`injection_patterns`) render as plain `--muted` metadata, never `--alarm` — the ticket's own Scope line. **Issue #428** (`trace-panel.tsx`'s memory-fact marker checking `origin === "user"`, a value no memory fact's own origin ever is) was filed against this ticket by an earlier audit pass before the code existed; `MemoryRetrieveStepDetail`, built fresh here, uses the correct `origin !== "inferred"` check from the start, so the issue is closed by construction rather than needing a separate fix. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (lint, typecheck, 279 tests including 15 new ones for the `trace.ts` readers, production build, token hygiene, contrast, offline check). **No browser available in this session** — verified against the real running stack instead: a synthetic rich trace covering every step kind this ticket renders, plus three real `schema_notes`/`memory` rows spanning `user`/`correction`/`inferred` origin (the `memory` table had no rows to test against, so one was inserted for this and removed after), written directly to an existing message's `trace` column and read back through the cookie-authenticated `GET /ask/{message_id}/trace` and `GET /memory/facts/{kind}/{id}` exactly as the panel's own fetch calls would, confirming the wire shapes this ticket's rendering code assumes match what the API actually returns — including the specific three-origin case #428 named. Synthetic data removed afterward; no product data touched. **Next**: `M5-TRACE-FE-121` (interactions — click a passage, click a memory fact, adjust the threshold from an abstention trace), the last ticket in the `TRACE` chain per `M5-TRACE-FE-119`'s own note.

**`0.4.35` — `M5-TRACE-BE-125`**: `GET /ask/{message_id}/trace` — nothing served `messages.trace` to the browser before this, which is what blocked `M5-TRACE-FE-119` (issue #415, closed by #417's ticket) and closes issue #417 itself. Returns the stored trace verbatim: the step sequence plus `trace_rotated`/`steps_truncated`, never recomputed (C4 — a trace that disagrees with the answer it explains is worse than none). An unknown `message_id` is a 404; a message that exists with `trace IS NULL` (a turn that failed before `_run_generation` ever wrote one) is a valid empty-step trace, not a 404 — the ticket's own named edge case. A turn still `running` has no trustworthy database row yet, since `_run_generation` writes `messages.trace` only once the turn ends, so it is served instead from the new `_Turn.trace_steps` — the exact list `_run_generation` already builds `messages.trace` from, now aliased onto the turn by reference (`trace_steps = turn.trace_steps`) rather than kept as a function-local, so every `.append`/`.extend` along the SQL, retrieve/abstain and document-composition branches is visible to the route the instant it happens, with no change to what `M5-LOOP-BE-117` records (explicitly out of scope). The tool-loop branch's steps are still only known once `run_tool_loop` returns — unchanged from before, and covered live instead by the per-call `step` SSE events `M5-LOOP-BE-117a`/`M5-LOOP-FE-118` already stream. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh lint`/`typecheck`/`fmt-check` clean; `scripts/dev.sh test` clean (842 passed, 1 skipped); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` clean (640 passed), including 6 new cases in `api/tests/test_ask_api.py` — a finished turn's trace round-tripping unchanged, an abstained turn's threshold and near-miss scores round-tripping, a running turn served from `_Turn.trace_steps`, an unknown id 404, a `trace IS NULL` message returning an empty trace rather than 404, and a rotated trace returning `trace_rotated: true`. **Cold-start walkthrough run**: rebuilt the API image (`scripts/dev.sh build-api`), recreated the container, asked a real question over `curl` against the running stack, and confirmed the route serves the finished turn's stored trace and 404s an unknown id — native inference is not running on this machine so a genuinely multi-step live turn was not exercised this way, the same gap `M5-LOOP-BE-117a`'s own entry names (#376); the running-turn path is covered by the DB test instead. **Next**: the trace panel itself, `M5-TRACE-FE-119` — no longer blocked.

**`0.4.34` — `M5-LOOP-FE-118`**: the Ask screen's live tool-call steps get the phase/call-id discriminator `M5-LOOP-BE-117a` left for this ticket, closing issue #420. `AskStepData` (`web/lib/ask.ts`) gains optional `call_id`/`phase`, present only on a tool-call step; `askwell.ask._emit_tool_call_step` now forwards both `"start"` and `"end"` (previously `"end"` only), each built by the new `_tool_call_step`, which names the real operation per tool — `"Searching your files."` / `"Searched your files."` for `document_search`, `"Querying your database."` / `"Queried your database."` for `database_query`, and so on for `schema_lookup`, `document_listing`, `current_date` — rather than the raw `"Called {tool}."` placeholder the last ticket shipped as a stopgap. A failed call's `"end"` reads `"{start label} That didn't work — trying another way."`, naming the edge case the ticket's own Edge Cases line calls for (a retry or a change of approach, not a frozen label) without inventing a specific retry description the tool result doesn't actually carry. `applyAskEvent`'s `"step"` case (`web/lib/ask.ts`) now keys on `call_id` when present: an `"end"` updates its own `"start"`'s entry in place instead of appending a second line for the same call, and — since two calls dispatched together each keep their own `call_id` and their own array entry the whole time they're both live — this is also what renders them as concurrent rather than a queue, with no change needed to `ask-screen.tsx`'s existing `turn.steps.map(s => s.label).join(" · ")`, which already shows every current entry at once. A generic step with no `call_id` (retrieval, SQL) is unaffected and always appends, same as before this ticket. **Deferred, filed as issue #422**: source-scoped wording (the ticket's own `"querying sales-2024"` example) — `ToolCallEvent.arguments` only carries a `source_id` UUID, and resolving it to a name needs a database read the observer's synchronous callback signature cannot make without a scope change `M5-LOOP-BE-117a` deliberately left out; the issue recommends `run_tool_loop` resolving and caching source names once per turn and adding `source_name` to `ToolCallEvent.arguments` instead. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (842 passed, 1 skipped, including 5 new cases in `api/tests/test_ask_tool_step_labels.py`); `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (250 passed, including 3 new cases in `web/lib/ask.test.ts` covering in-place update, concurrent rendering, and the no-`call_id` case staying append-only); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` clean against the running stack. **Not verified**: a live cold-start walkthrough reading labels off a real SSE stream with a real hybrid corpus — same native-inference gap named in `M5-LOOP-BE-117a`'s own entry above (#376). **Next**: the trace panel (`M5-TRACE-FE-119`) is the next FE ticket in this epic; issue #422 (source-name labels) is unblocked but not scheduled.

**`0.4.33` — `M5-LOOP-BE-117a`**: `run_tool_loop` gets a live per-call observer, closing issue #414 (`M5-LOOP-FE-118`'s own filed blocker: no backend event exists that fires when an individual tool call starts or finishes). `on_tool_call` (`askwell.agent.loop.ToolCallObserver`) fires a `"start"` event immediately before a call dispatches and an `"end"` event immediately after it returns, once per call actually run — never for a deduplicated one, which never runs — including inside a concurrent `asyncio.gather` batch, where every `"start"` in the batch fires synchronously before any of that batch's calls are awaited, so they are always seen before any of the batch's `"end"`s (`api/tests/test_loop.py::test_a_batchs_starts_all_arrive_before_any_of_its_ends`, built with two staggered fake tools that finish in the opposite order they were dispatched, so the ordering isn't an accident of dispatch order). A failed call still fires its `"end"`, carrying the real outcome (`"ok"` or the tool's error code) rather than being skipped. An observer that raises is swallowed and logged (`_notify`), the same posture `askwell.traces.TraceRing.write`'s own module docstring already argues for a debugging aid that must never be able to fail the thing it is watching. With no observer passed, `run_tool_loop`'s behaviour is unchanged (its own dedicated test). **`askwell.ask`'s two call sites are wired to it, but issue #420 — filed against this exact ticket before it was built, correctly anticipating the trap — meant the wiring is not the naive "forward both phases" version**: the shipped frontend (`web/lib/ask.ts`'s `applyAskEvent`, `web/components/ask/ask-screen.tsx`'s label join) has no phase/call-id discriminator yet to dedupe on, so `_emit_tool_call_step` only forwards the `"end"` phase into `turn.emit("step", ...)` for now — one live `"Called {tool}."` label per call, landing when that call actually returns, replacing the old post-loop burst, with no doubling reaching `main`. The pre-loop generic label ("Working through this in steps." / "Continuing where it left off.") was kept rather than removed, so there is still a signal between the question landing and the first call finishing. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why `"end"` was chosen over `"start"` and why option 1 (bundling `M5-LOOP-FE-118` into this same change) was rejected. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean — lint, format, typecheck, and `test` (837 passed, 1 skipped, 8 new cases in `test_loop.py`: the staggered-batch interleaving proof, a deduplicated call firing no events, a failed call's `"end"` carrying its real outcome, no-observer-unchanged, and an observer that raises not failing the turn). **Not verified**: a live cold-start walkthrough reading the emitted step events off a real SSE stream — this environment has no running native inference process, the same gap named in nearly every prior loop/eval ticket's own BRAIN entry (#376); the ticket's own cold-start testing note is written for the self-hosted runner or a maintainer's own machine. Issues #414 and #420 are both closed. **Next**: `M5-LOOP-FE-118` is unblocked — the backend event it needed now exists — and should add the phase/call-id discriminator to `AskStepData` as its first order of business; once that lands, `_emit_tool_call_step` should switch to forwarding both phases (no further backend change needed, the observer already supports it).

**`0.4.32` — `M5-EVAL-TEST-124`**: the quality gate's "Tool selection incl. parallel" row (`docs/build-plan.md`, 25 tasks, ≥ 0.85) gets its suite. `eval/tool_selection.py` drives the real `askwell.agent.loop.run_tool_loop` — not a mock — over the fixture document corpus and fixture sandbox database seeded together, so a task can need either, both, or neither of the five registered tools. Every run scores two things kept separate rather than folded into one number: `tool_choice_score` matches the distinct tools actually called against a task's `expected_tool_routes` **exactly** (a subset or a superset both fail — the exact-match choice, and why, is in `docs/decisions.md`, this date), covering "two acceptable tool routes both correct" and "no tool at all is correct" as ordinary route entries; the ordinary `eval.scoring.score` grades the final answer text. **Parallel dispatch is proven, not inferred**: `parallel_achieved` groups non-deduplicated `LoopStep`s by `iteration` and requires two or more sharing one — the one trace shape `asyncio.gather`-based dispatch actually produces, so a task marking `require_parallel` still scores its tool component 0 if the right tools were called one iteration apart rather than together, independent of whether the final answer came out right. Recovering from a genuine tool error (asking the connected database about a table — `warehouses`, `vendors` — that does not exist in the fixture schema) is graded with a new `not_contains_any` scorer: the failure this exists to catch is a fabricated number standing in for an honest "I don't have that," which `contains_all` cannot express. 25 tasks (`eval/suites/tool_selection.v1.json`) span all five tools singly, two ambiguous-route pairs (`schema_lookup`/`database_query`; `document_listing`/`document_search`), four `require_parallel` pairs, two "tempted by an unnecessary call" pairs (a document fact that sounds like a database metric, and vice versa), one no-tool arithmetic task, and two tool-error-recovery tasks — every numeric expected value checked against the fixture data directly (`eval/fixtures/sql/seed.sql`'s actual rows, the three PDF/docx/xlsx fixtures' actual extracted text), not assumed. Wired into `eval/bench.py` (`--suite tool_selection.v1`) and `.github/workflows/eval.yml`'s suite loop (job renamed, timeout raised 35→45 minutes for the loop's extra per-task cost, the trigger-paths comment block extended). **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` clean (832 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged — this ticket adds no code under `api/src/`, which is what `check`'s `test` step covers); `eval`'s own suite (outside `api/pyproject.toml`'s pytest root, run directly against `eval/pyproject.toml`) — 55 passed, including the ticket's own "removing parallel dispatch fails the parallel tasks specifically" case (`test_combined_score_zero_when_parallel_required_but_not_achieved`) and the "tempting" exact-match case (`test_tool_choice_rejects_the_tempting_unnecessary_call`), both pure-logic, no database or model, matching every other eval suite's own test file. **Not verified**: a live run of `eval/bench.py --suite tool_selection.v1` against a running local model and stack — this environment has neither the native inference process nor the sandbox Postgres up, the same gap named in every prior eval-suite ticket's own BRAIN entry (#376); the suite's own testing notes (cold start, index the fixture corpus, import the fixture database, run the suite, disable parallel dispatch and confirm exactly the parallel tasks fail) are written for the self-hosted runner or a maintainer's own machine to carry out, not for this environment. **Next**: nothing named by this ticket blocks. `docs/build-plan.md`'s quality gate now has seven of its eight suites built; only "web escalation discipline" (M6) remains.

**`0.4.31` — `M5-LOOP-BE-117`**: `messages.trace` gets its remaining step kind and actually starts rotating. Auditing every trace-populating ticket before this one found the shape (`docs/architecture.md` §7.1) already mostly built — retrieve/sql/compose steps, `backend`/`stopped_early`/`injection_flagged` — but two real gaps. **The "schema" step never existed**: `askwell.agent.sql_generate.generate_candidate_query` now times its own schema-notes lookup (`GeneratedQuery.schema_lookup_ms`) and `_run_sql_turn` carries it on the new `_SqlAnswer.schema_step`, prepended ahead of the "sql" step in every branch that got as far as selecting a source — matching `trace.md`'s own worked example ("Looked up schema" before "Queried"), kept apart from the sql step's own timing rather than folded in. The executed-query step also gained `limit_injected`, the one shape field nothing wrote. **`messages.trace` never actually rotated with the file `TraceRing`** it is documented to rotate with (§7.1: "`messages.trace` is trimmed with them") — the DB column kept full step detail forever regardless of what aged off disk. `TraceRing.prune()` now returns the message ids it dropped (`TraceRing.write()`'s new `TraceWriteResult`), and `askwell.ask._trim_rotated_traces` clears exactly those rows' `trace.steps` (`trace_rotated: true`), called inline in the same transaction as the triggering write — no separate sweep, no window where the DB and the file store disagree. Every other trace field survives so a reopened old turn still renders; citations and fact usage are untouched, being real tables that never rotate.
