New `TRACE_STEP_BOUND` (50) covers the ticket's other named edge case — a turn whose own trace grows past the per-turn bound is truncated with `steps_truncated: true` stated on the trace rather than cut silently. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why inline trimming was chosen over a periodic sweep. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh lint`/`format`/`typecheck` clean; `scripts/dev.sh test` (832 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged — only existing `test_traces.py` cases were reshaped for the new return type, no new unmarked tests); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` against the real, already-running stack (634 passed, 4 new — the schema step's source/duration on both an executed and a rejected query in `test_ask_sql.py`, `_trim_rotated_traces` actually clearing `steps` while leaving `status`/`backend` intact, and a no-op confirmed when nothing rotated). **Not verified**: a live cold-start round-trip actually forcing a real rotation against a running local model — the same inference-bridge gap several tickets before this one hit (#376); `_trim_rotated_traces` itself is proven directly against a real database instead. **Next**: nothing named by this ticket blocks. `M5-TRACE-FE-119` (rendering, explicitly out of this ticket's scope) can now read a real `"schema"` step and a real `steps_truncated`/`trace_rotated` flag rather than ones that did not exist yet.

**`0.4.30` — `M5-LOOP-BE-116`**: the 8-call ceiling `M5-LOOP-BE-115` deliberately left as a crash guard becomes the real, user-visible one. `askwell.agent.loop.CALL_CEILING` (8, `docs/architecture.md` §10) is now enforced by `run_tool_loop` itself, counting actual fresh tool executions, not iterations — a parallel batch crossing it is truncated at 8, the cut calls recorded on the new `LoopResult.pending_calls` rather than silently dropped (`docs/ux/trace.md` §5's "what it was about to do"). A ceiling reached one call at a time is caught at the next iteration by `_stop_at_ceiling`, which asks the model exactly one more time — told plainly no further tool calls will run — to compose from what is already gathered, appending `_CEILING_NOTE` itself so a stopped-early answer can never omit the note regardless of what the model wrote. The ticket's own named edge case (nothing useful gathered at all) skips that extra call entirely and states so plainly (`_NOTHING_GATHERED_TEXT`) rather than composing from nothing. **`Continue` is a new turn, not a resumed one**: `LoopContinuation` (just the `<tool-result>` history and the next citation index, produced only on a turn's *first* ceiling stop) round-trips through a new `messages.trace.loop_continuation` and a new `AskRequest.continue_from` field — `askwell.ask._run_generation` resolves it before anything else and, when found, skips `_run_sql_turn`/`_has_hybrid_sources` entirely to call `run_tool_loop(resume=...)` directly with a **fresh** 8-call budget (a shared counter would mean "new turn" changes nothing) seeded with the earlier history so the model has no reason to re-fetch it; a stale or non-loop id falls back to answering as an ordinary question rather than failing. Cross-turn tool-call dedup is deliberately not attempted — `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why counting calls rather than iterations was the load-bearing choice. A second ceiling stop in the same `Continue` chain (`continuation_count >= 1`) narrows the answer's text instead of offering a third continuation — no infinite chain, per the ticket's own named edge case. The `done` SSE event gained `tool_ceiling`/`can_continue`; `/ask/counts` gained `tool_ceiling_stops` (the ticket's own local counter, C1); the `ASK_ASKED` interaction-log record gained `tool_ceiling` (the Audit / Logging Requirement, distinct from the trace ring buffer). **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh lint`/`format`/`typecheck` clean; `scripts/dev.sh test` (832 passed, 1 skipped — 13 new cases in `test_loop.py`: a truncated batch, a one-at-a-time ceiling, nothing gathered, a `Continue` resume proving the fresh budget and seeded history, a second stop narrowing instead of offering a third continuation); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` against the real, already-running stack, full suite clean (one existing counts test updated for `tool_ceiling_stops`). **Not verified**: a live cold-start round-trip actually hitting the ceiling against a running local model — the inference bridge was not serving a model in this environment, the same gap `M5-LOOP-BE-115` and several tickets before it hit for the identical reason (#376). **Next**: nothing named by this ticket blocks; the three tracker items `M5-LOOP-BE-115` filed (#407 loop citations, #408 the sql-turn-before-hybrid-gate ordering, #409 full memory/partial/conflict parity for the loop) remain open and unaffected by this change — none of them name `M5-LOOP-BE-116` as their owner.

**`0.4.29` — `M5-LOOP-BE-115`**: the multi-step tool loop, wired into `POST /ask`. New `askwell.agent.loop.run_tool_loop` gives the model the registry `M5-TOOLS-BE-113`/`-114` built: a JSON decision each iteration (`{"type": "tool_calls", "calls": [...]}` or `{"type": "answer", "text": "..."}`), every call the model names in one turn dispatched concurrently via `asyncio.gather` (none of the five tools write anything, so independent lookups never wait on each other), every result fed back as a `<tool-result>` block, until the model says it has enough. A repeated call — same tool, same arguments, this iteration or an earlier one — is never re-executed; its prior result is reused and the step records `deduplicated=True`. A tool error is told to the model as data (`ToolError.message`, not just `ToolStep.detail`, which never carried it) so the turn can try another approach rather than fail — the ticket's own named edge case, covered by a dedicated test. `_SAFETY_MAX_ITERATIONS` (25) is a crash guard only, well above the 8-step ceiling `M5-LOOP-BE-116` will actually enforce and surface to the user; this ticket's own Out of Scope line named that ceiling explicitly. **`askwell.ask._run_generation` only offers the loop to a genuinely hybrid corpus** — `_has_hybrid_sources` requires at least one `ready` document and one `ready` database-backed source, and never fires for a source-scoped question. Every other turn (the overwhelming majority, and every turn `test_ask_api.py`/`test_ask_sql.py` already exercise) falls through unchanged to `_run_sql_turn` and the single-shot document path, with memory, partial-coverage, conflict detection and citations all intact. This narrowing was not in the ticket's own literal reading of "wire ahead of the single-shot path" — it is the fix for a gap an audit found in that literal reading (issue #409): an unconditional loop intercepts *every* document question, not just hybrid ones, and its own answer branch does not yet run memory retrieval, `split_partial_answer` or `split_conflict_answer`, silently dropping shipped features from ordinary traffic with nothing to catch it. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why real parity (#409's own "option 2") is deliberately deferred behind loop citations (#407) rather than attempted here. **Three tracker items filed against this ticket before it closed, all re-owned rather than closed:** #407 (a loop-answered turn's `[index]` markers do not resolve into the `citations` table or a `citation` SSE event — its own follow-up ticket, sized separately), #408 (`_run_sql_turn` is still tried before the hybrid gate, so a hybrid question whose database half alone matches a schema note strongly enough can still short-circuit before the loop runs — left as documented risk, its own recommendation is to revisit once #407 lands, not before), #409 (the hybrid gate above is its own "near-term fix" recommendation, taken; the "real fix", full memory/partial/conflict parity for the loop, remains open). **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent clean (lint/format/typecheck; `test` 827 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged from before this ticket — the loop is pure Python, no I/O of its own); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (630 passed against the real, already-running stack — 624 before this ticket plus 6 new `_has_hybrid_sources` tests against real rows; zero regressions, confirmed both by the full run and by inspection that `test_ask_api.py`/`test_ask_sql.py` never create a database-backed source alongside a document one, the only reason the new gate is safe to add without touching either file). **Not verified**: a live cold-start `POST /ask` round-trip through a running local model — the inference bridge container was not actually serving a model in this environment at the time of this change, the same gap several tickets in the `SQL` epic already hit for the identical reason (#376). The model-interaction protocol is verified by `test_loop.py`'s fakes (9 tests: single call then answer, two independent calls in one turn overlapping the same iteration, a duplicate call within one turn, a duplicate across turns, a tool error recovered from, a malformed response ending the loop with its raw text rather than crashing, the safety limit, an empty `calls` list treated as malformed) and the wiring by `test_ask_hybrid_sources_db.py`'s real rows, not by a live generation. **Next: `M5-LOOP-BE-116`** — the 8-step call ceiling and the "stopped after 8 steps, Continue" state (`docs/ux/ask.md` §5), explicitly out of this ticket's scope and the first ticket with a reason to replace `_SAFETY_MAX_ITERATIONS` with something user-visible.

**`0.4.25` — `M4-RESULT-FE-110`**: the generated SQL is disclosed unconditionally, including for a turn that never executed a query. `QueryDisclosure` (`web/components/ask/sql-result-table.tsx`) — the "Show query" control `M4-RESULT-FE-109` already built — is now exported and shared, enhanced with an injected-`LIMIT` marker (`segmentInjectedLimit`, `web/lib/sql-result.ts`, matching `askwell.sql.limit`'s own trailing `/* Added by Askwell */` comment), a scrolling `<pre>` instead of wrap-and-truncate for a long query, and a **Copy query** button. New `SqlQueryCard` renders that same control, alone, for a database answer that never reached `sql_result` — a rejection, a failed dry run, a timeout, a query-time failure, or the source vanishing mid-turn. **Found while implementing, not a pre-existing gap in a merged ticket**: `M4-SQL-BE-108a` deliberately never populates `sql_result` on those branches (locked in by its own `test_a_write_disguised_as_a_read_is_rejected_with_no_stored_result`), so the query text for them never reached the wire at all — only `messages.trace`, server-side. Fixed with a second `done`-event field, `sql_query: {query, outcome} | None`, populated only on the branches `sql_result` never is, and reconstructed on a reopened turn from the sql step already sitting in the stored `trace` rather than a new column (`askwell.ask._sql_query_disclosure`/`_sql_query_from_trace`; full reasoning, including the two rejected shapes, in `docs/decisions.md`, this date). Filed and closed with this session as [#398](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/398), since no ticket named this gap before now. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (793 passed, 1 skipped, no network); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (609 passed against the real, running stack, including a direct assertion that `_load_finished` reconstructs a rejected query's disclosure from a hand-inserted `trace` row); `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (244 tests, 4 new logic files' worth — `segmentInjectedLimit`, the disclosures-expanded counter — typecheck, build, contrast, offline scan all clean). **Not verified**: a live cold-start-to-rejection browser walkthrough — this environment has no inference model weights on disk at all (not merely a stopped supervisor, as the last several tickets in this epic hit; there is nothing here to load), so `POST /ask` cannot complete a real turn end to end in this environment. Recorded rather than claimed, the same gap `M4-RESULT-FE-109`/`M4-EVAL-TEST-112` (#376) hit for the same underlying reason.

**`0.4.24` — `M4-RESULT-FE-109`**: a database-answered turn renders as a table, plus its own row in the source viewer, now that `M4-SQL-BE-108a`'s `messages.sql_result` gives it a real snapshot to render from rather than a fixture. New `web/lib/sql-result.ts` is the pure logic — `isSingleValue`, `truncationLabel`, `formatCell` (null vs empty string, distinguishable), `columnAlign` (inferred from the values themselves, since `askwell.sql.execute`'s `ExecuteResult.columns` carries names only, never types) and `paginateSqlRows`, all over the client — 15 new tests, no server round trip for paging since the ticket's own assumption is that the snapshot already on screen is the whole answer. `SqlResultTable` (`web/components/ask/sql-result-table.tsx`), wired into `AnsweredContent` for both the live and a reopened turn, covers the ticket's four states: a single value as a number rather than a one-cell table, zero rows as its own honest line distinct from an error or an abstention (`states-and-edge-cases.md` §4's own row), an ordinary result as a paginated, horizontally-scrollable table, and a `truncated` result labelled "first N of possibly more" — never silent about a capped `LIMIT`. The query itself is disclosed unconditionally behind "Show query", per §4's "the query is the citation." "View full result and query" opens the database row of the source viewer (`docs/ux/source-viewer.md` §2), a new `DatabaseResultView` reached via `/documents/?result=<message_id>` — the same static-export query-parameter route the document viewer already owns, extended rather than duplicated. It reads the live turn out of `AskProvider` first (the identical in-memory snapshot the inline table rendered from) and falls back to replaying `GET /ask/{message_id}/stream` — `askwell.ask`'s own reopened-turn endpoint, unchanged, already answering with `sql_result` — only when a reload has dropped that state; no new backend route. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning for that shape, including the two rejected alternatives (a second static page; a new backend endpoint by message id). **Closes issues #375 and #386** — `M4-SQL-BE-108a` (0.4.23, merged before this session) had already done the actual wiring #386 tracked and left #386/#375 open regardless; both are genuinely resolved now, closed with the verification each comment thread had been asking for across roughly a dozen prior sessions that hit the same blocker and could only re-confirm it. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh web-run pnpm test` (240/240, 15 new), `pnpm typecheck` (clean), `pnpm lint` (clean, no new warnings — the 5 pre-existing ones are unrelated files), `pnpm build` (succeeds, static export unaffected). **Not verified**: a live cold-start walkthrough against a real model generating and executing SQL — this environment's native inference supervisor is not running (`GET /health` reports `inference: unreachable`), so the actual model-answers-a-database-question path could not be exercised end to end here; recorded rather than claimed, matching the same gap `M4-EVAL-TEST-112` (0.4.18) hit and filed as #376 for the identical reason.

**`0.4.22` — `M4-DUMP-SEC-091`**: the containment claim demonstrated, not argued. New `api/tests/test_dump_containment.py` runs six hostile fixture dumps — privilege escalation, reading a host file through `COPY ... FROM PROGRAM`, connecting to another database in the sandbox instance, reaching the network the same way, exhausting disk (the size cap), and running indefinitely (the time cap) — through the real `askwell.dump_import.import_dump` against a real sandbox instance, `requires_db`. Every fixture must fail loudly, land its source in `attention` with a reason, record `dump_import_failed` in `audit_decisions`, and leave the sandbox database dropped, checked against a **content hash** of every row's own text (not a row count — an in-place `UPDATE` would pass a count check and still be a breach) for both Askwell's own database (a `documents`/`chunks` canary the import path never touches) and a second, already-loaded sandbox database, before the whole suite and after every single fixture. A whole-suite-in-sequence case then runs all six back to back and confirms the good source and the good sandbox database both come out untouched — "the product stays functional afterwards," not just each attack in isolation.

**Also closes issue #389**, filed against the earlier, closed attempt at this same ticket (PR #335 — built during the 18 September stack outage, marked done while never merged, closed today for conflicting with the twenty-odd M4 tickets landed since and red `test-db` CI; this implementation reuses that PR's fixture set and hashing approach, reapplied against current `main`). #389 named the one acceptance criterion the `requires_db` cases cannot check without a real egress proxy and Redis, neither of which `scripts/dev.sh test-db` brings up: "no network request escapes, confirmed against the proxy's refusal counter." Closed with its own recommended option 3 — `test_sandbox_network_has_no_route_to_the_proxy_or_the_internet`, an **unmarked** test (runs in plain `scripts/dev.sh test`, no stack needed) that parses `compose.yaml` with the same regex approach `test_env_example.py` already established (no YAML library is a dependency here) and asserts the `sandbox` network is `internal: true`, joined by nothing but `api`, `worker` and `sandbox` itself. It cannot watch the proxy's counter move, but it catches the only regression that would make that counter check matter — a future change granting the sandbox network a route out. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why option 2 (a real proxy + Redis in `test-db`) was deferred rather than taken now.

New `docs/manual-tests/M4-DUMP-SEC-091.md` covers the counter check option 3 cannot do — run against the real, cold-started stack in §3, reading `/network` in a browser tab (it needs the session cookie the shell already holds; a bare `curl` gets `"No session."`, `middleware.py`'s `OPEN_PATHS` exempting only `/health`) before and after a hostile `reach_the_network`-shaped import. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent clean (`lint`, `fmt-check`, `typecheck`; `test` 793 passed, 1 skipped, no network, 1 new — the topology assertion); `scripts/dev.sh test-db api/tests/test_dump_containment.py -v` (7 passed against the real, running sandbox instance — all six fixtures plus the sequence case); every command in the new manual-test document run by hand against the real, cold-started stack and corrected where it did not actually work as first written — `/tmp` is read-only in the API/worker image (moved the dump fixture files to `/run/askwell`, the same bind mount `ASKWELL_RUN_DIR` already provides), `$ASKWELL_DATABASE_URL`/`$SANDBOX_POSTGRES_USER` are container-side variables that a host shell does not have unless `.env` is explicitly sourced (wrapped the affected `psql` invocations in `sh -c` so they expand inside the container instead), and `audit_decisions` has no `created_at` column (it is `occurred_at`) — all three privilege-escalation, read-host-file and reach-the-network fixtures reproduced the exact refusal message documented once these were fixed.

**`0.4.21` — `M4-SQL-VAL-106`**: the `EXPLAIN`/`SHOWPLAN` dry run — the last of the four safety layers `docs/data-sources.md` §4 names before a generated query reaches a real database. New `askwell.sql.dry_run.dry_run_sandbox_query`/`dry_run_connection_query` plan an already-validated (`M4-SQL-VAL-104`), already-limited (`M4-SQL-VAL-105`) query under the same read-only role and statement timeout `askwell.sql_execute` uses to actually run it — `EXPLAIN` for Postgres and MySQL/MariaDB, `SET SHOWPLAN_ALL ON` for SQL Server — and never open a row. A query the target's own planner rejects (dropped column, renamed table) comes back `DryRunReason.PLANNING_FAILED` and is recorded to `audit_interactions` under its own kind, `sql_dry_run` — deliberately separate from `validate.SQL_QUERY`, so a planning failure and a validation rejection stay distinguishable in the log, this ticket's own Acceptance Criteria. A planner that itself runs past the statement timeout is `TIMEOUT`; SQL Server's `SHOWPLAN_ALL` itself failing to turn on (a restricted edition, a missing permission) is `UNSUPPORTED`, kept apart from `PLANNING_FAILED` since it is a capability fact about the connection, not the query. **Also closes issue #382**, filed against an earlier, never-committed attempt at this module (no commit, branch or stash for `dry_run.py` exists anywhere in this repository's history — the issue predates the code that now exists): every driver's own connect-time operational-error class (`psycopg.OperationalError`, `pymysql.err.OperationalError`, `pytds.tds_base.LoginError`/`OperationalError`), not just `OSError`, is caught and returned as a `.FAILED` `DryRunResult` rather than propagating unhandled — proven directly by two new `requires_db` tests hitting a real closed port and a real wrong password. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why connect failures are recorded as an unclassified `PLANNING_FAILED` rather than duplicating `sql_execute`'s `ConnectionUnreachable`/`CredentialsRejected` split. **Not wired into `POST /ask`** — issue #375 already tracks that no SQL execution path exists in the turn flow at all; this ticket's own scope is the dry-run step alone, matching `M4-SQL-VAL-104`/`-105` before it, both built and tested standalone ahead of that same wiring gap. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent run clean (`lint`, `fmt-check`, `typecheck` all clean; `test` 792 passed, 1 skipped, no network, 3 new); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (588 passed against the real, running stack, 5 new: a valid query passes and is not recorded, a nonexistent-column query fails planning without running and is recorded with the query and reason, a valid live-connection query passes, and the two issue-#382 crash-reproduction cases — nothing listening on the port, a rejected password — both now return `.FAILED` instead of raising).

**`0.4.19` — `M4-CONN-BE-099`**: connection health and three distinguishable query-time failures. A live connection is now health-checked periodically (`askwell.worker.check_connections_health`, cron, `ASKWELL_CONNECTION_HEALTH_CHECK_SECONDS`, default 60) and on demand (`POST /sources/{source_id}/reconnect`, synchronous) — both reuse `probe_connection`'s own metadata-only catalog queries, never a query over the user's data. New `askwell.connections.check_connection_health` runs one probe and `_record_health_transition` applies it to `sources`: `last_healthy_at`/`last_error` update on every call, but a `connection_health_lost`/`connection_health_recovered` decisions record is written only on a real `ready`↔`attention` transition, never on a repeat of the same state — the exact regression issue #360 was filed to prevent, closed by never letting the health-check path reach `record_introspection` at all rather than by adding the `record_decision` flag that issue recommended (`docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning for why). At query time, `askwell.sql_execute.execute_connection_query` (now taking `source_id`) classifies every engine's connect/execute failure into `ConnectionUnreachable`, `CredentialsRejected` or `QueryRejected` — three different messages, none resembling a zero-row `QueryResult` — and runs the same transition function immediately, so a dead connection discovered mid-question does not wait for the next periodic cycle to show `attention` in the library. New migration `9d4e7a2c1b58` adds `sources.last_healthy_at`, surfaced through the existing ingestion coverage snapshot (`SourceCoverage.last_healthy_at` in `web/lib/ingest.ts`) for the library to read — **no frontend rendering was built**, filed as its own follow-up, [#379](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/379), since a "Connection dead: last check, error, reconnect" row is UI-shaped work this ticket's own `backend` label and 2-3 hour estimate did not cover. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck clean; `test` 769 passed, 1 skipped, no network); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (580 passed against the real, running stack, 28 new: transition-gated audit writes proven with real repeated-failure and repeated-success loops, all three query-time failure types proven against a real Postgres — a bad port, a wrong password, and a role with `SELECT` revoked); `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (229 JS tests, unchanged — only a type widened, no new UI). `scripts/dev.sh db upgrade head` applied the new migration cleanly against the real, running stack. Issue #360 closed with the transition-testing evidence linked; #378 (this ticket) and #379 (the frontend follow-up) filed and tracked. **Out of scope, per the ticket's own Out of Scope line**: any reconnect *retry* policy beyond the periodic check itself — a person or the next cron cycle triggers a reconnect, nothing backs off and retries automatically.

**`0.4.18` — `M4-EVAL-TEST-112`**: the two remaining `docs/build-plan.md` quality-gate categories for this phase — text-to-SQL (40 tasks, execution-matched, `pass_bar: 0.80`) and SQL safety (10 tasks, `pass_bar: 1.00`, no exceptions). New `eval/fixtures/sql/schema.sql`/`seed.sql` — a five-table order-management schema (customers/products/orders/order_items/support_tickets) with one deliberately unguessable column, `orders.stat_cd` (a bare status letter with no self-describing meaning) — loaded into its own sandbox database by new `eval/sql_fixture.py::seed_sql_fixture`, which reuses `askwell.sandbox.create_database` and real schema introspection (`askwell.schema_introspect.write_schema_inventory`) rather than hand-inserting rows, the same "through the real path" posture `eval.grounded.seed_corpus` established for the document fixture. `stat_cd`'s meaning is written as a `user`-origin schema note via `askwell.memory.write_schema_note` — the same shape a real clarification answer produces — and several text-to-SQL tasks (`sql07`–`sql10`, `sql25`–`sql27`, `sql33`) can only be answered correctly with it in place, the ticket's own "result depends on schema notes" edge case made concrete. New `eval/sql_eval.py` drives the real `askwell.agent.sql_generate.generate_candidate_query` → `askwell.sql.validate.validate_query` → `askwell.sql_execute.execute_sandbox_query` path for both suites. **Text-to-SQL is scored by result-set equivalence** (`execution_match_score`, order-insensitive, tolerant of `Decimal`/float display differences) against a gold query run live at eval time — never by comparing query text, and a candidate reaching a different but equally correct query is accepted by construction. **SQL safety is scored on `validate_query`'s own verdict alone, deliberately never on execution** — folding in the read-only sandbox role's own defense-in-depth would let it mask a validator regression forever, since even a validator that accepted every write attempt would still see most of them refused at the database; scoring on the validator alone is the only way the ticket's own "a deliberately weakened validator fails the safety suite" stays testable, proven directly by `eval/tests/test_sql_eval.py::test_safety_a_weakened_validator_that_accepts_a_write_fails_the_suite` (a fabricated accepting `ValidationResult`, no database or model needed). A rejection for the wrong reason (parsing gave up rather than recognising an actual write) still scores 1.0 but is flagged on the run (`error` prefixed `safety_flag:`), per the ticket's own edge case. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning. `eval/suite.py` gained `mode: "sql"`/`"sql_safety"`; `eval/bench.py` dispatches both. `scripts/dev.sh eval` needed a small necessary change — it previously joined only `askwell_internal` with no sandbox credentials, since no suite before this one touched the sandbox; it now also joins `askwell_sandbox` and threads the three `ASKWELL_SANDBOX_*` variables, mirroring `test-db`'s own established pattern for reaching that service from an ad hoc container. `.github/workflows/eval.yml`'s suite list, job name, and commented path-trigger list are updated to include both. 10 new pure-logic tests in `eval/tests/test_sql_eval.py` (result-set matching, all four safety-scoring branches including the weakened-validator case), no database or model needed for the harness's own correctness — matching `eval.grounded`'s own established split between logic tests and by-hand verification. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (763 passed, 1 skipped, lint/format/typecheck clean — api-side untouched by this ticket); `scripts/dev.sh run pytest /app/eval/tests --ignore=.../test_runner.py` (all green; `test_runner.py`'s own failures are pre-existing, reproduced identically on `main` before this change, unrelated `Settings` fixture gap); `bash scripts/gate.test.sh`/`guards.test.sh` (unaffected by the `scripts/dev.sh` edit). Real-stack verification of the fixture path succeeded fully: `seed_sql_fixture` run to completion against the live sandbox Postgres — database created, both SQL files loaded as owner, schema introspected (33 notes, including the one manual `stat_cd` note), and the resulting data spot-checked directly as `askwell_sandbox_readonly` (10 customers; order status counts `C:2 P:2 R:1 S:11`, matching the fixture as designed). **Live model verification (a generated query actually scored end to end) was attempted but blocked**, not by anything in this ticket: the native inference supervisor's `embedding` role crash-loops on this machine and takes the shared bridge socket down with it even for `generation`-only calls — reproduced identically against the already-shipped `smoke.v1` suite, and `state.json`'s `embedding.restarts: 21` predates this session. Filed as [#376](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/376) rather than worked around; re-owned to whoever picks it up, since diagnosing a native-process crash loop is outside this ticket's own dependencies (`M4-SQL-VAL-104`, `M4-SQL-DB-107`, `M2-EVAL-TEST-063`, all already merged and unaffected). The eval gate integration itself (§`docs/build-plan.md` "Integration into the gate with the 1.00 bar enforced as a hard failure") is code-complete and exercised by the harness's own strict-pass-bar machinery (`SuiteRunReport.passed`, unchanged, already enforces this for every `pass_bar == 1.0` suite) — what remains unverified is a live run producing a real score, which needs #376 resolved first.

**`0.4.17` — `M4-SQL-OBS-108`**: the audit requirement for the SQL path — every query `askwell.sql.validate.validate_query` looks at, accepted or rejected, is now one `audit_interactions` record (`kind = "sql_query"`) with the engine, source, the full untruncated query text, `validated`, `rejection_reason`/`detail`, and `limit_injected`/`rows`/`duration_ms` (always `None` today — see below). This corrects `M4-SQL-VAL-104`'s own choice to record only a rejection, only to `audit_decisions`, which `docs/audit-log.md` §7 already assigns to Interactions, not Decisions. New `askwell.sql.observability.sql_rejection_rate` (mirroring `askwell.observability.abstention_rate`'s own shape) reads that `validated` flag back over a bounded recent window, satisfying the ticket's own "rejection rate goes from 2% to 18%, visible before any user complains" acceptance criterion. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning. **Skipped by design, not stubbed**: `limit_injected`/`rows`/`duration_ms` stay `None` because `M4-SQL-VAL-105` (limit injection) exists only as an unmerged, not-accepted WIP commit and no execution wiring exists either — a `None` states honestly that this stage does not know the value yet, rather than inventing one. The trace half of the ticket's Scope ("the same fields in the trace for the trace screen in M5") is **not built** — `messages.trace` is assembled per-message inside `askwell.ask._run_generation`, and nothing in `askwell.ask` calls `sql_generate`/`validate_query` at all (confirmed by grep, and true of every ticket in the SQL epic so far), so there is no live message to attach a `"sql"` step to; inventing one would be scaffolding ahead of the wiring ticket that will actually decide the call shape. Filed and re-owned as [#373](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/373). **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck clean); `scripts/dev.sh test` (763 passed, 1 skipped, no network — unchanged, this ticket is entirely db-backed); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (567 passed, 5 new: full interaction recording on accept/reject, a 20,000-item `IN (...)` query stored at full length, a credential-looking literal stored verbatim, the rejection rate over a mixed window, and the interaction chain verifying after new `sql_query` records); and a real cold-start run against the rebuilt, running stack (`podman compose exec worker python3 -c ...`) submitting two accepted and two rejected queries (including the `RETURNING`-CTE disguise case) — `scripts/dev.sh psql` confirmed all four landed in `audit_interactions` with the right fields, `sql_rejection_rate` reported `2/4 = 0.5`, and `verify(session, Store.INTERACTIONS)` reported the chain intact both before and after manually deleting the four test rows via `psql` (a superuser action, not the app role — C6's "the application never rewrites history" is about the app, not the operator). `M4-SQL-VAL-105` (limit injection, unmerged) is next in the `SQL` epic; issues [#371](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/371) and [#372](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/372) are already filed against its WIP.

**`0.4.16` — `M4-SQL-VAL-104`**: the C2 gate — every model-generated query is parsed with `sqlglot` and rejected unless it is exactly one `SELECT`/`WITH` read, with no regex anywhere in the path. New `askwell.sql` package (`api/src/askwell/sql/`, the path `docs/architecture.md` already named for C2) with `askwell.sql.validate.validate_query`. Rejects more than one statement, any data-modifying or definition statement, a locking read (`FOR UPDATE`/`FOR SHARE`), `SELECT ... INTO` (a write dressed as a read), a short and explicitly incomplete denylist of side-effecting function calls (`pg_terminate_backend`, `xp_cmdshell`, and similar), and anything `sqlglot` cannot parse for the target dialect. The check does not trust the top-level statement type alone: PostgreSQL allows a data-modifying CTE with `RETURNING` — `WITH d AS (DELETE FROM orders RETURNING *) SELECT * FROM d` parses with `SELECT` at the top while deleting every row when run, the ticket's own "nesting to disguise a modification" edge case made concrete and verified against real `sqlglot` 30.18.0 output, not assumed. Every rejection instead walks the whole parsed tree (`Expression.find_all`), which catches this the same way a top-level `DELETE` is caught; a comment hiding a second statement needed no special-casing at all, since `sqlglot`'s tokenizer discards comments before they ever enter the tree. Every rejection is recorded to `audit_decisions` (`kind = "sql_rejected"`) with its reason and the full query text; an acceptance is not recorded, since `askwell.agent.sql_generate` already records a successful generation. Parsing runs under a new, separate bounded wait (`Settings.sql_validation_timeout_seconds`, default 2s) — bounding the parser, not the database, which `sql_statement_timeout_seconds` already does. `sqlglot>=25.0` (resolved 30.18.0) is a new runtime dependency — MIT-licensed, ships its own type stubs, no `mypy` override needed. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning for the whole-tree-scan design and the side-effect denylist's honestly-incomplete scope. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck all clean); `scripts/dev.sh test` (763 passed, 1 skipped, no network, 34 new pure tests covering every hostile shape the ticket names); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (562 passed against the real, rebuilt, running stack, 4 new tests proving the decisions-store recording and the timeout path); and a real cold-start run inside the running `worker` container (`podman compose exec worker python3 -c ...`) submitting seven hostile statement shapes plus one normal read — every hostile shape refused with the right reason, the normal read passed, and `SELECT kind, payload->>'reason', payload->>'query' FROM audit_decisions WHERE kind = 'sql_rejected'` confirmed all six rejections landed with the full offending SQL, then truncated. `grep -rn "re.compile|re.match|re.search|re.fullmatch" api/src/askwell/sql/ api/src/askwell/sql_execute.py api/src/askwell/agent/sql_generate.py` found nothing — no regex-based filtering exists anywhere on the SQL path. **Not wired into `POST /ask`** — the same "do not wire ahead of a dependency that does not exist yet" reasoning `M4-SQL-DB-107` and `M4-SQL-BE-103` both recorded about themselves, now in reverse: `M4-SQL-VAL-105` (limit injection) and `M4-SQL-VAL-106` (the `EXPLAIN` dry run) are the remaining layers before a generated query can safely reach a real turn — `M4-SQL-VAL-105` is next in the `SQL` epic.

**`0.4.15` — `M4-SQL-BE-103`**: schema retrieval and SQL generation — the whole database path's own start, and the first ticket of the `SQL` epic. New `askwell.agent.sql_generate`: `select_database_source` picks which `ready` dump/CSV/connection source a question's SQL should target — automatically when exactly one candidate's schema notes match the question at all (`retrieve_relevant_facts` with `note_limit=1` per candidate, presence not a scored comparison — `ts_rank` is not calibrated across two different sources' worth of text), asking by naming the candidates rather than guessing when more than one matches, which is also the response for a question spanning two databases (this module never sends more than one source's schema to the model in one call, so a real cross-database question cannot produce a wrong-but-plausible query, only one scoped to whichever single source got picked — one mechanism covers both edge cases, no second heuristic to tell them apart). `generate_candidate_query` then retrieves the relevant schema subset — reusing `schema_notes` via `retrieve_relevant_facts` with a higher `note_limit` (40) rather than a second `SchemaInventory`-shaped context, following `M4-DUMP-ING-088`'s own "do not duplicate a dependency's job under a different name" precedent — and asks the model for one candidate query through a new versioned prompt, `agent/prompts/sql_generation.v1.md`, with the schema/notes/memory delimited as data, never instruction (C7). `agent.compose`'s delimiting helpers (`delimit_memory_facts`, `delimit_schema_notes`, `confidence_label`, new `flag_injection_text`) moved from private-to-`agent.conflict` to shared, so a third prompt-composing module reuses rather than reimplements the C7 boundary — `agent.conflict`'s own output and tests are unchanged. A question with no relevant schema anywhere is reported as `NOT_A_DATABASE_QUESTION` rather than forced into a query — the caller's job, not this module's, to fall back to document retrieval. Every successful generation is recorded to `audit_decisions` (query text, source, schema/memory drawn on) whether or not anything downstream ever validates or runs it, per the ticket's own Audit Requirement. **Not wired into `POST /ask`** — the same "do not wire ahead of the validation that has to exist first" call `M4-SQL-DB-107` made about itself, now applying to the ticket that depends on it: showing or executing a candidate query before `M4-SQL-VAL-104`'s `sqlglot` gate exists would be exactly what C2 exists to prevent. **Found and filed rather than fixed here, issue [#368](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/368):** a live-connection source whose credential cannot currently be decrypted (`M4-CONN-SEC-098`'s `CredentialsLocked`) is silently excluded from the candidate list — indistinguishable from a connection that was never added, the same "wrong failure reads like the wrong thing" trap that ticket's own decision log already named and fixed once for introspection; inventing the caller-facing copy for it belongs with whichever ticket first wires this module into a real conversation. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning for all three named choices. **Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck all clean); `scripts/dev.sh test` (729 passed, 1 skipped, no network); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (558 passed) against the real, rebuilt, running stack, including 27 new tests exercising `select_database_source`/`generate_candidate_query` against real `sources`/`schema_notes` rows and real encrypt/decrypt round-trips, not fakes — the model's own completion is the one faked piece, since verifying real model behaviour is `M4-EVAL-TEST-112`'s job, not this ticket's. `M4-SQL-VAL-104` (parse and reject anything that is not a single read) is next in the `SQL` epic, and the first ticket with any real caller for `GeneratedQuery.query`.

**`0.4.14` — `M4-SCHEMA-BE-102`**: closes issue #365 — depends on and tightens `M4-SCHEMA-ING-101`. A stale schema note is now excluded outright from `askwell.memory.retrieve_relevant_facts` (`AND NOT stale`) rather than included with `-101`'s in-prompt caveat: that ticket's own Validation Rule says plainly "a stale note is never used in generation", not "is caveated", and a caveat trusts a small local model to read a footnote correctly every time it matters, which C5's abstention-over-invention reasoning already argues against doing anywhere else. The caveat code (`agent.conflict._delimit_schema_notes`) stays — `retrieve_relevant_facts` is its only real caller, so it is unreachable in production now, but a pure function should not assume every future caller pre-filters the way this one does. `get_active_schema_notes` (the library/memory-screen read) is unchanged — a stale note is still visible to a person, just never fed to generation.

A stale note now carries **why**, not just that: new `schema_notes.stale_reason` (migration `20260918_3f7c1a9e5d20_schema_note_stale_reason.py`) is `'possibly_invisible'` when the vanished table is one a Postgres permission change hid — `SchemaInventory` gained `omitted_tables`, the relation names `_build_postgresql_inventory` already computed via `has_table_privilege` and previously discarded, keeping only the count (`-100`'s own `test_postgresql_inventory_counts_but_never_names_an_invisible_table` read as a deliberate choice but had no recorded reason — reversed, `docs/decisions.md` has why) — and `'dropped'` otherwise, including on every other engine, where the two still cannot be told apart (the same honest `None` gap `omitted_count` already has). A stale **column** note also gets `reattach_suggestion` — the new column name — set only when its table lost exactly one column and gained exactly one in the same introspection run: the one case a rename is unambiguous without a fuzzy guess, which the ticket's own Assumption rules out.

New `askwell.schema_introspect.refresh_schema_attention` moves a source to `attention` when it has any active stale notes, with `sources.last_error` naming the count ("N schema notes refer to a table or column Askwell can no longer find") — `docs/ux/library.md` §5's needs-attention reason for schema drift — and moves it back to `ready` once none remain, without ever overriding an unrelated `attention` reason (a dead connection, locked credentials), tracked via a `Schema drifted:` `last_error` prefix rather than a second column. Called from both places a completed re-introspection already runs `write_schema_inventory`: `connections._run_deep_introspection` (a live connection) and `schema_introspect.reintrospect_sandbox_source` (a dump/CSV source), same session, so `schema_notes` and `sources.status` never disagree.

The fix path's third option: new `POST /memory/facts/schema_note/{id}/reattach` (`askwell.memory.reattach_schema_note`) moves an active, user-supplied note to a new table/column position, reusing `write_schema_note`'s own invariant (a user-origin write retires whatever is active at the new position) and superseding the note it moved from — never automatic, exactly the write a person makes after seeing (and usually accepting) `reattach_suggestion`. Edit and delete already existed (`correct_schema_note`/`delete_schema_note`, `M3-CORRECT-BE-082`) — this ticket only needed the third.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, typecheck all clean, 728 passed/1 skipped. `scripts/dev.sh db upgrade head` against the real, running stack applied the new migration cleanly; `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (531 passed) against it. `curl localhost:8000/health` confirmed the stack (already up) stayed healthy with the new columns in place. No frontend touched — `web/` has no library or memory screen consuming this yet (`docs/ux/library.md` is Phase 1 UI, not built).

**`0.4.13` — `M4-SCHEMA-ING-101`**: schema notes from the clarification loop, the unguessable-column half `M4-SCHEMA-ING-100` deliberately left open (`describe_column`'s own docstring named it). New `askwell.schema_introspect.raise_unguessable_column_clarifications` scans a freshly-introspected `SchemaInventory` for columns whose name alone does not say what it means — `_is_unguessable_column_name` splits the name into letter-only tokens and flags it when any token is three characters or fewer and outside a small hardcoded common-word allowlist (`id`, `at`, `key`, `url`, …), the `st_cd`/`student_status` split the ticket's own worked example asks for — and raises one clarification per flagged column, with a bounded value distribution and row count as evidence (`clarify.column_distribution_evidence`, built by `M3-RAISE-BE-071` and sitting unused until this ticket). A primary key or foreign key column is skipped regardless of its name — `describe_column` already states what it references. Heaviest column (by row count) wins the per-source cap first, so a column touching forty thousand rows is asked about before one touching twelve when both cannot fit. Idempotent per source, the same "already has a clarification row" guard every other trigger uses. Wired into all three call sites that already run `write_schema_inventory` — a live connection's initial introspection (`connections._run_deep_introspection`), a dump import (`dump_import._run_deep_introspection`), and on-demand re-introspection (`schema_introspect.reintrospect_sandbox_source`) — PostgreSQL only for now; MySQL and SQL Server have no bounded value-distribution query wired up (issue #362, named honestly rather than guessed at, the same shape `omitted_count = None` already uses for those two engines elsewhere in this module). Answering a raised clarification needed no new answer path — it flows through the existing `review.answer_clarification` → `reapply` pipeline and promotes the `schema_notes` row to `origin='user'` exactly like every other clarification.

**Also closes issue #355**, part of this ticket's own definition of done: a `user`-origin `schema_notes` row whose table/column position a re-introspection no longer finds used to be left completely untouched — still active, nothing distinguishing it from a note still describing a real column. New `schema_notes.stale` (migration `20260918_1e6f3b9c4a72_schema_note_stale.py`) is set instead, on exactly that note, and cleared again if the position reappears — the note is never superseded or deleted, since the person's explanation is still correct, only the column is gone. `SchemaNote` (the ORM model and `askwell.memory`'s retrieval dataclass), `get_active_schema_notes` and `retrieve_relevant_facts` all carry the flag through; `agent.conflict._delimit_schema_notes` renders it into the composed query-generation prompt as an explicit caveat (`[..., column no longer found in the current schema]`) rather than presenting a vanished column as current fact (C5); `ask.py`'s `fact_citation` event for a `schema_note` now includes `stale` too.

**Two pre-existing gaps found while implementing this, filed rather than folded in**: `table_infer.py`'s own `table_column`/`date_format` clarification `subject`s are a compound string (`"orders: st_cd"`) that never actually matches `reapply.resolve_dependencies`'s exact bare-name lookup, so a CSV-source column clarification appears to have never promoted its schema note to `origin='user'` (issue #361 — this ticket's own `subject` is deliberately a bare `column.name` to avoid repeating it); and the MySQL/SQL Server sampler gap named above (issue #362).

**Verified against the real, rebuilt, running stack** (`scripts/dev.sh build-api`, `podman compose up -d`): wrote a `students` table inventory with `id` (PK), `st_cd`, `full_name` through `write_schema_inventory` + `raise_unguessable_column_clarifications` with a fake sampler — exactly one clarification raised, for `st_cd`, `full_name` and the PK `id` correctly silent; answered it through the real `review.answer_clarification` → `reapply.dispatch` → arq worker path — the `st_cd` note promoted to `origin='user'` with the real answer text; re-introspected with `st_cd` removed from the inventory — the note stayed active with `stale=True`; `retrieve_relevant_facts` + `_delimit_schema_notes` rendered the caveat into the prompt text. `scripts/dev.sh check` (726 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (517 passed) both clean.

**`0.4.12` — `M4-SQL-DB-107`**: the independent read-only role and statement timeout, ahead of `M4-SQL-BE-103`/`VAL-104`–`106` (schema retrieval, SQL generation and `sqlglot` validation) — none of those exist yet, but this ticket's own listed dependencies (`M4-DUMP-DEPLOY-087`, `M4-CONN-SEC-097`, `M0-DATA-DB-014`) are all done and none of them are, so this is the layer those later tickets will call, not the other way round. New `askwell.sql_execute`: `execute_sandbox_query` runs a query as `askwell_sandbox_readonly` (never the owner, C3) with `SET statement_timeout` applied for that session alone; `execute_connection_query` does the same for a live connection's own already-write-probe-verified credential, across all three supported engines — Postgres's `SET statement_timeout`, MySQL's `SET SESSION MAX_EXECUTION_TIME` (milliseconds, `SELECT`-only, which is fine since a read-only role can only ever run one), MariaDB's `SET SESSION max_statement_time` (seconds, all statement types, a different session variable entirely — verified against each driver's real error codes, `pymysql.constants.ER.QUERY_TIMEOUT` vs `ER.STATEMENT_TIMEOUT`), and SQL Server's connect-time `timeout` (`python-tds` has no session-level `SET` for this at all). A cancelled query raises `StatementTimedOut`, naming the query and the configured timeout, and suggesting narrowing it (`docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §4); a decisions record (query, timeout, duration in milliseconds — never a raw float, `audit.canonical_payload` refuses one outright) and a local, never-transmitted Redis counter both follow, mirroring `connections.record_write_probe_refusal`'s own shape. New `Settings.sql_statement_timeout_seconds` (`ASKWELL_SQL_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, default 30, adjustable per the ticket's own edge case). New `askwell.sandbox.verify_readonly_role`, checking `pg_roles` for the four attributes `deploy/sandbox/10-roles.sh` already sets (`rolsuper`/`rolcreatedb`/`rolcreaterole`/`rolbypassrls`) rather than a table-level write attempt — a role attribute is not scoped to one database the way a table grant is, so it is the one category of escalation a per-database grant could never catch. Wired into `askwell.worker.startup` as a second, separate fatal check after the existing `embed.check_dimension`/resume block, deliberately: the sandbox instance being merely not up yet must not skip the main database's own resume, and only an actual misconfiguration (never "not reachable yet") refuses to start, the same "fail loudly on a real defect, not on a laptop that has not finished booting" split `embed.EmbeddingDimensionMismatch` already established. "The role cannot write" itself needed no new test — `test_sandbox.py::test_readonly_cannot_write` already proved it directly against the driver; new tests cover `verify_readonly_role` (real role passes, a nonexistent role and a temporary `CREATEDB` role both refuse) and `sql_execute` (a real sandbox query reads rows, cannot write through the module either, and a `SELECT pg_sleep(5)` against a 1s configured timeout is cancelled and reported, with the decisions row confirmed). `scripts/dev.sh check` (715 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (510 passed) both clean. Verified against the real, rebuilt, cold-started stack rather than only the suite: the worker started cleanly with the real role; `ALTER ROLE askwell_sandbox_readonly CREATEDB` on the running sandbox instance made every worker restart crash-loop with `SandboxRoleMisconfigured` naming the exact privilege, and reverting the role let it resume normally on the next restart. Out of scope, per the ticket's own "Granularity: one role and one timeout": query cost estimation, and wiring this into an actual generated-query path, since `M4-SQL-BE-103`/`VAL-104`–`106` do not exist yet for it to be wired into.

**`0.4.11` — `M4-SCHEMA-ING-100`**: full schema introspection — types, keys, relationships — for a live connection and a loaded sandbox database (dump or CSV) alike, on connect, on import, and now on demand too. See the session log entry, same date, for the full account: new `askwell.schema_introspect` reads columns/PK/FK in bulk per engine (PostgreSQL, MySQL/MariaDB, SQL Server) and writes plain-language table- and column-level `schema_notes` that the existing lexical retrieval already ranks by relevance — no new retrieval path needed. Always runs as a read-only role: the live-connection credential the write-probe already verified, or a new `askwell_sandbox_readonly` DSN (`Settings.sandbox_readonly_password`) for a sandbox database, never the owner that loaded it. PostgreSQL detects and counts (never names) a table the role cannot see; MySQL/SQL Server cannot, honestly (`omitted_count = None`, issue #353). New `POST /sources/{id}/reintrospect` + `reintrospect_source_job` cover "on demand". Re-introspection updates rather than duplicates — an unchanged note is left alone, a changed one is superseded by its replacement, a dropped table/column's note is superseded with nothing to replace it, and a user note is never touched. Two real bugs caught only by testing against a real, restricted Postgres role, both fixed (`docs/decisions.md` has the full account): `information_schema.table_constraints`/`key_column_usage` silently named no primary/foreign key for a plain-`SELECT` role (fixed by reading `pg_catalog.pg_index`/`pg_constraint` directly, which carry no such extra privilege gate), and `askwell.connections.record_introspection` — never previously called more than once per source — wrote a duplicate competing table note on every repeat call rather than leaving an already-noted table alone. Verified live: a real dump import and a real live connection both produced correct column/type/key/FK notes; adding a table and re-introspecting twice in a row showed it appear with zero duplication; a table with `SELECT` revoked was counted as omitted and never named.

**`0.4.10` — `M4-CONN-SEC-098`**: encrypting `sources.config_encrypted` at rest, the last `CONN` ticket and the close of the connection epic. See the session log entry, same date, for the full account: `askwell.crypto`'s HKDF-derived Fernet key over a per-install secret stored outside the database, `create_connection_source`/`run_introspection` wired through it, a new `credentials_locked` reason code that reports a lost or changed key as "cannot decrypt, re-enter" before any socket opens rather than as a misleading network failure, and a passphrase-ready key derivation for M7. Verified live: stored ciphertext unreadable, a working connection end to end, and a deleted install secret producing the locked state rather than a network error.

**`0.4.7` — `M4-DUMP-FE-090`**: the dump route on the add-source screen (`docs/ux/add-source.md` §3), and the first ticket to make `filetypes.detect`'s dump route real end to end. New `POST /sources/dump` (`askwell.sources.add_dump`) resolves one file against the nominated roots the same way `add()` does for a batch, detects it, and either creates a `dump` source and calls the new `askwell.dump_import.dispatch_import` (an `arq` nudge mirroring `ingest.dispatch` exactly — one attempt, swallowed and logged, never able to fail the request) or returns a structured refusal. Engine detection (`filetypes._dump_engine`, mirrored as `dumpEngine` in `add-source.ts`) is best-effort from the dump's own header: PostgreSQL's `pg_dump` preamble, MySQL's `mysqldump` header and `/*!NNNNN` version-comment syntax, SQL Server's `SET ANSI_NULLS ON`/`SET QUOTED_IDENTIFIER ON`/`GO` pragmas — an engine matching none of these refuses asking rather than defaulting to PostgreSQL and letting `psql` fail confusingly two steps later. `ARRIVES[Route.DUMP]` is `None` now in both `filetypes.py` and `add-source.ts` — the only edit `M1-ADD-BE-023`'s own decisions-log entry said this route would ever need once its screen existed. `web/components/add/add-screen.tsx` gained `DumpRoute` (the calm sandbox statement, shown once, never a modal or checkbox; a single-file picker; the same folder-ask/nominate flow the files route uses) and `DumpQueued` (polls `GET /ingest`/its SSE stream for the source's `status`, rendering queued/importing, imported, or failed with `last_error` — a cap abort and an ordinary load failure render identically, both being `status = 'attention'` with a specific reason, which is honest rather than a second failure taxonomy).

**Two pre-existing bugs fixed along the way**, both direct blockers of this ticket's own acceptance criteria: a `.sql`/`.dump`/`.backup` file whose contents do not actually read as a dump is now refused as *unsupported* rather than either silently indexed as plain text or told it is an unidentified dump (issue #341 — a letter renamed `letter.sql` previously suggested resaving it with `pg_dump`, which makes no sense for a document that was never a database export); a compressed dump (`backup.sql.gz`) now gets a dump-specific refusal naming decompression and the two live-data routes out, instead of the generic "unpack it and add what is inside" archive message that talks about documents keeping their own name in citations — nonsense for a single-file export (issue #342). Both fixes are in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-18.

**Issue #340 (no reconcile sweep for a `dump`/`table` source stuck `queued` after a failed Redis enqueue) is deliberately not fixed here** — `dispatch_import`'s own docstring names the gap honestly and the issue stays open, re-owned as its own ticket (a periodic sweep mirroring `askwell.ingest.reconcile`, generalised to cover `table` sources too once one exists) rather than absorbed into a ticket whose own stated granularity is "one route, three messages."

**Verified against the real, rebuilt, running stack** (`scripts/dev.sh build-api`, `scripts/dev.sh web-build`, `podman compose up -d api worker`): a MySQL-headed `.sql` file posted to `/sources/dump` came back refused with both routes out named, no `sources` row written; a real PostgreSQL-preamble dump came back queued, and `GET /ingest` showed it reach `status: "ready"` after the worker picked it up for real (not mocked); a prose file renamed `letter.sql` came back refused as "does not look like a database dump," not as an unidentified dump. `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck, 641 passed/1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (482 passed) both clean; `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (229 JS tests, up from 222; build; version/token/contrast/offline guards).

**`0.4.6` — `M4-CSV-ING-094`**: load a CSV or spreadsheet into its own sandbox database as a real, queryable table with the confirmed types (`docs/data-sources.md` §2). `askwell.table_load.process_table_source` is the one entry point: it runs `askwell.table_infer`'s existing parse-and-raise step (`M4-CSV-ING-092`/`093`, unchanged) and then loads — `_load_inferences` creates the sandbox database if the source does not have one yet (same `askwell.sandbox.create_database`/`generate_name` a dump uses), normalises every header into a valid Postgres identifier (`normalise_identifier`/`normalise_columns`, deduplicating collisions, with the original name recorded as an `inferred` schema note so a question naming it still resolves — the ticket's own edge case), and inserts every row one at a time as the owner role, catching a cast failure per row (`cast_value`) rather than aborting or dropping it, with the 1-indexed row number (header counted as row 1, `table_infer.MalformedTable`'s own convention) collected onto `LoadResult.failed_rows`. A column `table_infer` left `ambiguous` — including a `date_format` verdict of `AMBIGUOUS` — loads as `text`, verbatim, via `sql_type_for`; nothing here coerces a guess `table_infer` was not confident enough to make itself. Same size/time caps as a dump, read straight through `askwell.dump_import.get_dump_size_cap_bytes`/`get_dump_time_cap_seconds` rather than a second setting to discover, checked continuously (elapsed time) or on a poll (`pg_database_size`) inside the per-row insert loop; a breach raises `TableCapExceeded`, drops the sandbox database, and marks the source `attention`, the same shape `dump_import.DumpCapExceeded` uses. An empty file (`EmptyTable`) never reaches the sandbox at all — no database is created for a table with no columns.

**Reload is the ticket's other half.** Answering a `date_format` clarification needs to turn a `text` column that was waiting on an answer into a real `date` — `askwell.reapply._promote_schema_note` now returns the note's `source_id`, and `_process_item`/`run_job` read the clarification's own `trigger` (folded into `evidence` at raise time, not a column of its own) to call the new `askwell.table_load.reload_source` whenever that trigger is `date_format`. `reload_source` re-parses the source file from `sources.root_path`, re-derives every `date_format` clarification answered so far for that source (`_date_format_overrides`, queried fresh from `clarifications` each time rather than kept in a second store, so it is correct no matter how many separate answers led to this point), and rebuilds the whole table rather than `ALTER`ing one column in place — the alternative aborts the entire statement on the first row that fails to parse, exactly the all-or-nothing behaviour the row-level reporting rule exists to avoid, and would need a stable per-row join key this table has no other reason to carry. Rebuilding needs the owner role connectable again after `load_source` already sealed it (`askwell.dump_import`'s `seal_owner` precedent assumes a dump is never touched twice) — new `askwell.sandbox.unseal_owner`, symmetric to `seal_owner`, brackets `reload_source`'s own rebuild and reseals when it finishes.

**Not wired to ingestion**, the same gap `-092` left open and named as this ticket's own job to close — except it is not this ticket's job either: the HTTP endpoint to add a CSV source and the `sources.py` routing change that flips `filetypes.ARRIVES[Route.TABLE]` off `"later"` both belong to `M4-CSV-FE-095`, the next ticket, the same split `M4-DUMP-ING-088`/`M4-DUMP-FE-090` already established for dumps. A worker job (`worker.import_table_job`) exists and is registered, callable once something dispatches it. **Both open items named for this ticket are re-owned, not closed**: issue #325 (no `table_preview` evidence render on the clarifications screen) is `web/`-only and belongs with `-095`, the first ticket that reaches a table source's clarifications from a browser; issue #282 (`clarify.py`/`review.py` writing `memory`/`schema_notes` via inline `INSERT` rather than `askwell.memory`) names those two files specifically and neither was touched here, but every schema note this ticket writes goes through `askwell.memory.write_schema_note`, so this ticket did not add a third inline-`INSERT` site to the pile #282 describes.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck, `test` — 633 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (479 passed) both clean against the real, running stack (Postgres, sandbox Postgres, Redis all up). 27 new pure tests in `api/tests/test_table_load.py` (identifier normalisation, `sql_type_for`'s every branch, `cast_value`, date parsing — no database) and 6 new `requires_db` tests in `api/tests/test_table_load_db.py`, run against the real sandbox instance: a clean two-row CSV loads and reads back correctly; a `Reference #` header normalises to `reference` with the original recorded; nine good integers plus one `oops` loads nine rows and reports the tenth by its real row number (11, header counted as row 1); an empty file raises `EmptyTable` without touching the sandbox; a 1 KB size cap against a 500-row file aborts and drops the database; and the full reload story — a column with no disambiguating value loads as `text`, `information_schema.columns` confirms it, answering the two-option clarification and calling `reload_source` turns it into a real `date` column with the day-first values parsed correctly. No frontend surface touched; there is no HTTP endpoint yet for a browser-based cold-start walkthrough, so the "run it" evidence is `test_table_load_db.py` exercising `process_table_source`/`reload_source` directly against the real sandbox and main database rather than a UI click-through.

**`0.4.5` — `M4-DUMP-VAL-089`**: the size and time caps that abort a runaway dump import and drop the sandbox database, default 5 GB / 10 minutes, both user-adjustable (`docs/data-sources.md` §3). `askwell.dump_import.get_dump_size_cap_bytes`/`set_dump_size_cap_bytes` and the time equivalents are settings-table-backed (same shape as `askwell.clarify`'s clarification cap, not a field on `Settings` — a cap a user adjusts from a screen cannot live in environment configuration read once at process start), and every change is a `dump_cap_changed` decisions record. Enforcement is a `threading.Thread` watchdog inside `_load_blocking`, running for the whole life of the `psql` subprocess and polling the sandbox database's own `pg_database_size` (not `dump_path`'s size on disk — a dump small as a file but large once loaded is exactly the case a file-size check misses) plus the elapsed wall clock, killing `psql` itself the instant either cap is crossed; a check placed only between chunk reads was tried first and rejected because most of a real import's time is spent blocked *inside* a single `write()` or the final `wait()`, exactly where "the statement is terminated rather than waited on" has to fire (`docs/decisions.md` has the full reasoning). A final authoritative check runs right after `psql` exits regardless of outcome, closing the race where a fast load (an empty sandbox database's own catalog overhead can already be over a small cap before one byte of dump content is fed in) finishes before the watchdog's first poll. A breach reuses `import_dump`'s existing failure path — drop `WITH (FORCE)`, mark the source `attention`, record `dump_import_failed` — with a new `cap` field on that payload, so "how many imports were aborted for a cap" is a query over `audit_decisions` rather than a maintained counter (the ticket's own Analytics Events line, C1). `DumpCapExceeded(DumpImportFailed)` carries which cap and the limit that was in force. 11 new tests in `api/tests/test_dump_import.py`: three pure-Python (`_load_blocking` against a fake `psql`, no database — one proving the time cap ends a fake 5s sleep in well under 3s), and `requires_db` tests for both caps' get/set/validate, a size-cap abort, a time-cap abort against a real `pg_sleep(5)` (aborts in ~1.3s, not 5s+), and the both-caps-at-once edge case (size is reported, since it is checked first on the always-run first poll). `scripts/dev.sh check`/`lint`/`typecheck` and `test` (606 passed, 1 skipped) and `test-db` (473 passed) all clean. Verified against the real, rebuilt, cold-started stack via `import_dump` run directly inside the `api` container (not just the suite): a 1 KB size cap against a two-row dump aborted immediately naming "loaded data reached 7.6 MB, over the size cap of 1.0 KB", dropped the sandbox database, and `known_databases` before/after matched exactly; raising the cap to 5 GB then imported the same dump to `ready` normally; a 1s time cap against `SELECT pg_sleep(8);` aborted after 1.31s naming the time cap, not after the full 8s. One bug caught only by `test-db`, not review: `set_dump_time_cap_seconds`'s audit payload carried `previous`/`new` as `float`, which `audit.compute_hash` refuses outright (a float does not round-trip identically through jsonb) — fixed by storing both as strings, `docs/decisions.md` has the reasoning. `M4-DUMP-FE-090` (the add-source screen, wiring `import_dump_job` in for real) is next in the `DUMP` epic; a `.sql`/`.dump`/`.backup` source still shows `Verdict.LATER` at add time until it lands.

**`0.4.4` — `M4-DUMP-ING-088`**: import a PostgreSQL dump into its own sandbox database — create, load as the restricted owner role, introspect the table names, seal the owner off on success, drop and report on any failure. Closed issue #330 (the shared-owner cross-database reach `M4-DUMP-DEPLOY-087` filed) by revoking the owner's `CONNECT` the moment a load succeeds. See the session log entry below for the full account, including the live two-dump isolation proof. `M4-DUMP-VAL-089` (the size/time cap) and `M4-DUMP-FE-090` (the add-source screen) are next in the `DUMP` epic and remain unbuilt; a `.sql`/`.dump`/`.backup` source still shows `Verdict.LATER` at add time until `-090` wires the new `import_dump_job` in.

**`0.4.2` — `M4-CSV-ING-093`**: never infer silently between DD/MM and MM/DD (`docs/data-sources.md` §2). `askwell.table_infer.detect_date_format` runs on every date-typed column `infer_column_types` produces: a numeric `d/d/y` value whose first or second slot exceeds 12 rules one format out, so the column's format is inferred silently and recorded on its `schema_notes` row (`raise_table_inference`'s description now names the disambiguating value and why); a column where no value disambiguates raises a real two-option `clarifications` row (`trigger="date_format"`, `options=["DD/MM/YYYY (day first)", "MM/DD/YYYY (month first)"]`) rather than guessing; a column that disambiguates in *both* directions (one row only valid day-first, another only valid month-first) is reported through the existing generic ambiguous-column path — `inferred_type="string"`, a free-text question — rather than the two-option one, since that would wrongly presume a single format fits every row (`docs/decisions.md` has the reasoning for reusing that path rather than a new "malformed column" shape). ISO (`2026-01-01`) and named-month (`4 January 2026`) dates have nothing to disambiguate and raise nothing, at any sample size. `askwell.clarify._TRIGGER_PRIORITY` gained `date_format` in its documented slot — second, ahead of `document_identity`/`abbreviation`/`unreadable_scan`, second only to `contradiction`. **Not touched, a pre-existing gap `M4-CSV-ING-092` left, filed as issue #327**: `table_column`/`table_header` still have no `_TRIGGER_PRIORITY` entry of their own and fall to the default rank below `unreadable_scan` — including the mixed-date-format case, which is a `table_column` candidate. `scripts/dev.sh check`-equivalent (`lint`, `typecheck`, `test` — 587 passed, 1 skipped — `test-db` with the stack up — 446 passed, including the two new `raise_table_inference` cases for a recorded date-format inference and a real two-option `clarifications` row) all clean; no frontend surface touched. **`docs/manual-tests/M4-CSV-ING-093.md` was run live in full, including Part C against the real running stack.** Found along the way, filed and fixed as its own issue rather than worked around: `podman compose up -d` initially left `api`/`worker`/`inference-bridge` crash-looping on `ASKWELL_SANDBOX_DATABASE_URL is not set` (issue [#328](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/328)) — the running `localhost/askwell-api:dev` image was stale (built before this checkout's own history), since that variable exists nowhere in this branch's `config.py`/`.env.example`; `scripts/dev.sh build-api` rebuilt it from the current source and all three containers started clean. With the stack healthy, Part C's real `schema_notes`/`clarifications` writes matched the doc's claims exactly: a disambiguating column's schema note reads `Date format inferred: DD/MM/YYYY — disambiguated by '25/12/2026' (the first value, 25, cannot be a month).` with zero clarifications raised; an ambiguous column raised exactly one `clarifications` row with `options = ["DD/MM/YYYY (day first)", "MM/DD/YYYY (month first)"]`.

**`0.4.0` — `M3-EVAL-TEST-086`**: the memory-application eval subset — fifteen tasks at the 0.85 bar (`docs/build-plan.md`'s quality gate), and **the last ticket in M3**, so this ticket takes the `MINOR` per `AGENTS.md` §7 (`0.3.20` → `0.4.0`). New `eval/memory_apply.py`, `mode: "memory"` in `eval/suite.py`, dispatched by `eval/bench.py`. Fifteen tasks split five ways: five "apply" tasks (a stored fact resolves which of `conflict_2025.pdf`/`conflict_2026.pdf` — the two genuinely conflicting fixture documents `conflicting_sources.v1.json` already uses — is currently in force, the exact "resolved by memory" path `agent/prompts/conflicting_sources.v1.md` already documents), three "supersede" tasks (the same, correcting the fact once through `askwell.memory.correct_memory_fact` before asking), four "no_invent" tasks (a topically-close but insufficient fact must not license an answer where the near-miss abstention questions from `abstention.v1.json` still abstain), three "irrelevant" tasks (an unrelated fact sitting in memory must not derail an otherwise-grounded `grounded_qa.v1.json` question). Every task also checks whether the memory fact was actually cited (the `fact_citation` SSE event `M3-APPLY-BE-079` already emits); a superseded fact still being applied scores a distinct `superseded_fact_still_applied` rather than a generic `no_application`, per the ticket's own "the cause is legible" edge case.

Fixture facts seed through the real write paths, not a hand-inserted `memory` row: a fixture `clarifications` row is inserted directly (nothing in `askwell.clarify`'s own detectors has a reason to raise a question over invented "which figure is currently in force" content), then answered through the real `askwell.review.answer_clarification` for "apply"/"supersede" tasks, with `askwell.memory.correct_memory_fact` run once more for the supersession step — the same correction a chip or the memory screen would call. "no_invent"/"irrelevant" facts write directly with `askwell.memory.write_memory_fact(origin="manual")`, the same function `add_manual_fact` calls. Every seed is idempotent per subject (skipped if the subject already has any `memory` row), the same guard `eval.grounded.seed_corpus`'s `_ensure_root` uses for the fixture documents. `.github/workflows/eval.yml`'s gate now runs `memory_apply.v1` alongside the other three suites (35-minute timeout, up from 30, for the fourth suite's worth of generations).

**Verified**: 14 new pure-logic tests in `eval/tests/test_memory_apply.py` (no database, no model — same rule `test_abstain.py`/`test_conflict.py` follow), covering every `memory_score` branch (apply pass/no-application/uncited, supersede pass/still-applying-the-old-value/no-application, both no_invent outcomes, both irrelevant outcomes) plus the suite's own shape (fifteen tasks, unique ids, `mode: "memory"`, `pass_bar: 0.85`). `scripts/dev.sh run pytest /app/eval/tests` (75 passed, up from 61) and `scripts/dev.sh lint`/`scripts/dev.sh format` on the new files both clean. **Not run this session**: `eval/bench.py --suite memory_apply.v1` against the real stack — native inference was not running on the host (same precedent as several tickets above); the seeding path (fixture clarification → `answer_clarification` → `correct_memory_fact`/`write_memory_fact`) and the citation-extraction path (`_ask_one`'s `fact_citation` event capture) are new code exercised only by the pure-logic tests above, not yet by a live run. **First real run and the ticket's own cold-start/delete-all-memory walkthrough are still owed** — flagging rather than claiming a live-verification that did not happen.

**`0.3.20` — `M3-MEM-FE-084`**: the memory screen's six interactions — Edit, Confirm, Delete, History, Filter, Add a fact — `docs/ux/memory.md` §4, and issue #288 closed alongside it. `askwell.memory` gains `confirm_memory_fact`/`confirm_schema_note`/`confirm_fact` (promote `origin` in place — `'correction'`/`'user'` — no new row, no re-processing, since the ticket's own "Confirm ... because the content did not change" means there is nothing for `_reprocess_subject` to re-read), `add_manual_fact` (manual entry is a plain `write_memory_fact` with `origin='manual'`, refusing to double-write a subject that already has an active fact — the existing fact comes back so the caller offers a correction instead, this ticket's own edge case), and `delete_all_memory` (loops `delete_memory_fact`/`delete_schema_note` per active row, so every per-row guarantee — the #288 fix, the decisions record, re-processing — applies to a bulk delete exactly as it would to one; refuses with `StaleMemoryCount`/409 when `expected_count` no longer matches what is active, since the count named in the confirmation copy is the one thing this ticket's Validation Rule requires to still be true). Three new routes: `POST /memory/facts/{fact_kind}/{fact_id}/confirm`, `POST /memory/facts` (manual add), `POST /memory/delete-all`. Frontend: `web/lib/memory.ts` gains the three API wrappers plus `applyMemoryFilters`/`memorySources` (inferred-only, by source, unused — narrowing the already-fetched list, no round trip) and `deleteAllConfirmationCopy`; `web/components/memory/memory-screen.tsx` rewritten so every row carries live Edit/Confirm/Delete, a filter bar sits above the list, and an "Add a fact" form and a "Delete all memory" control (named-count confirmation, `AGENTS.md`'s own irreversible-action caution honoured in copy rather than a native `confirm()`) sit below it. Edit reuses `correctFact` and Delete reuses `deleteFact` from `lib/memory-chips.ts` (`M3-CORRECT-FE-081`) rather than duplicating the fetch — "correcting from a chip and from the memory screen produce identical results" holds on the frontend the same way `askwell.memory`'s own module docstring says it holds on the backend.

**Issue #288 fixed, not just filed**: `memory.superseded_by`/`schema_notes.superseded_by` are `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so deleting an active row that is itself a correction previously had Postgres null its predecessor's `superseded_by`, silently reactivating a value the user had already corrected away. `delete_memory_fact`/`delete_schema_note` now re-point any row whose `superseded_by` names the row being deleted at itself, before the delete — the row stays permanently `superseded_by IS NOT NULL` (never active again) without depending on a live target, and stays readable in history since `get_memory_screen`'s history query only ever checks `IS NOT NULL`, never what it points to. This was recommendation option 2 from the issue itself, chosen there specifically for not discarding history rows.
