**Verified against the real stack** (both images rebuilt): `scripts/dev.sh check` (556 passed/1 skipped, up from 550) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (440 passed, up 17 cases in `test_memory.py`) both clean. `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (222 JS tests, up from 215; build; version/token/contrast/offline guards). Live, with a real session cookie: manual add returned a new fact id; a second manual add for the same subject came back `{"duplicate": true, "existing": {...}}` rather than writing a second row; confirming an already-user fact returned `already_confirmed: true`; correcting the manual fact then deleting the correction left `GET /memory` reporting zero rows — not the pre-fix behaviour of resurrecting the manually-entered value — which is the #288 scenario itself, reproduced and then shown fixed on the running stack; `POST /memory/delete-all` with a wrong `expected_count` returned 409, with the right one returned `{"deleted_count": ...}` and left the screen empty.

**Known gaps, unchanged by this ticket**: no bulk confirm (`docs/ux/memory.md` §7, open product question, named Out of Scope in the ticket itself); no memory export/import across machines (`docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §9, not v1); the `GET /memory` / `/memory` page path collision (issue #313, filed against `M3-MEM-FE-083`).

**`0.3.19` — `M3-MEM-FE-083`**: the memory screen — one list, both fact kinds, the confidence marker, source and "used in N answers" per row, inferred sorted first by default. `docs/ux/memory.md` §2/§3/§5. New `askwell.memory.get_memory_screen` reads `memory` and `schema_notes` active rows in one pass and folds "grouped by subject" into "one row per subject/position" (only one row per subject is ever active, by `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note`'s own supersession rule), with the row's superseded history nested inline rather than a separate group header — the "later wins, earlier struck through" state (`docs/ux/memory.md` §5, "Conflicting facts"). History is fetched in two queries total (one per table), not one per subject, so the "hundreds of facts" edge case does not become an N+1. New `GET /memory` (`register_memory`) returns `{rows, inferred_count}`. Frontend: `web/lib/memory.ts` (fetch, wire mapping, pure copy helpers) and `web/components/memory/memory-screen.tsx` replace the `/memory` placeholder — an empty state naming the clarification queue as where to start, and a populated list where every row shows its marker, subject, source tag, value, origin/date/usage sentence, a "learned from a source you deleted" note where it applies, and struck-through history where a row has any. Interactions (Edit/Confirm/Delete/History/Filter/Add a fact) are `M3-MEM-FE-084`, named Out of Scope in the ticket itself and untouched here; bulk confirm remains an open product question per `docs/ux/memory.md` §7.

**Verified against the real stack** (both images rebuilt): `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck, 550 passed/1 skipped, up from 549) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (up 6 cases in `test_memory.py`, 1 new in `test_memory_api.py`) both clean. `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (215 JS tests, up from 208; build; version/token/contrast/offline guards — version discipline confirmed `0.3.19` end to end after the bump). Live: seeded an inferred fact and a user-supplied fact with a named source via `psql`, `GET /memory` with a real session cookie returned them inferred-first (`inferred_count: 1`) with the source tag attached, matching `test_memory_screen_sorts_inferred_first` exactly; rows cleaned up afterward.

**Known gap, filed rather than fixed (issue #313)**: `GET /memory` shares its path with the client-rendered `/memory` page, so a hard reload or a direct request to that path gets the JSON payload, not the page shell — pre-existing in this codebase (`GET /clarifications` has the identical collision against `/clarifications`), not something this ticket introduced or is scoped to redesign; the rail's own links are client-side navigation and never hit this path directly. #313 has the options and a recommendation (an `/api` prefix) for whoever picks it up.

**`0.3.18` — `M3-CORRECT-FE-081`**: the memory chip — a fact used in an answer is now clickable, and correcting or deleting it from there actually sticks. `askwell.memory` had every write-side primitive this needs since `M3-STORE-BE-076`/`M3-CORRECT-BE-082`, but no HTTP surface — `-082`'s own `docs/BRAIN.md` entry said plainly that whichever of the chip or the memory screen started next would need one; this is that. `register_memory` adds `GET /memory/facts/{kind}/{id}` (what a popover needs — the fact, its origin, date, an exact `usage_count` off `fact_usage`, and the current version if the one an answer used has since been superseded), `POST .../correct` and `POST .../delete`, dispatched by two new module functions (`correct_fact`/`delete_fact`) rather than duplicating `correct_memory_fact`/`correct_schema_note`. `correct_fact`'s own edge case: correcting an *inferred* fact from its chip is not `correct_memory_fact` (which raises `CannotCorrectInference` for it by design) but a fresh user-origin `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note` — "assert this instead", which the module's own docstring already named as the only path for a guess nobody asserted. On the frontend, `web/lib/ask.ts` now parses the `fact_citation` SSE event `M3-APPLY-BE-079` already emitted but nothing read; `web/lib/memory-chips.ts` folds it into a per-claim chip (never grouped, unlike a citation card, since `docs/ux/ask.md` §4 wants a *visible* chip in the prose, not a hidden marker); `ask-screen.tsx` renders it right after its claim and a click opens a popover — fact, origin, date, usage count, **Correct** (inline textarea) and **Delete** — reusing the confidence marker CSS class the design system had already specified but nothing had used yet.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck, 549 passed/1 skipped, up from 542) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (up 12 cases in `test_memory.py`) both clean; new `test_memory_api.py` (7 cases, session/validation shape, no database) same pattern as `test_review_api.py`. `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (208 JS tests, up from 204; build; contrast; offline check). Native inference was not running this session (same precedent as `0.3.16`/`0.3.17`), so no click-through in a real browser — the routes were instead exercised live with `curl` against a real fact seeded via `psql`: read, correct (superseded, returned a real reprocessing label), read again (`active: false`, `current` naming the new row), delete, 404 on an unknown id.

**`0.3.17` — `M3-APPLY-BE-079`**: a memory fact or schema note can now be cited the same way a document passage is, attributed to the user and the date it was supplied, and the use is recorded — closing the last uncited path C4 named. `askwell.agent.conflict.compose_conflict`'s `_delimit_memory_facts`/`_delimit_schema_notes` (`M3-APPLY-RET-078`) now number every entry, continuing the citation index straight on from the document candidates' own `1..N` (`facts_start = len(candidates) + 1`, then notes) — one marker convention, `[index]`, rather than a second syntax for memory. `askwell.ask._cite_claim` resolves an index past the document candidates against `fact_usage` (already existing, from `M3-STORE-BE-076`'s own migration — one row per `(message_id, fact_kind, fact_id)`, de-duplicated in Python before the insert) instead of `citations`, and emits a new `fact_citation` SSE event carrying the fact's subject, text, origin, confidence and `created_at` as the attribution date. `prompts/conflicting_sources.v1.md`'s "Memory and schema notes" section now teaches this — cite a fact/note by its index, but a claim asserting content from the user's own material must still cite a document; only a claim that explains what a term *means* may cite the fact or note directly. `askwell.agent.citation_check.check_citations`'s `fact_usage` exclusion (`M3-APPLY-RET-078`, built ahead of this ticket) was dead code until now — nothing populated the table; it does, for real answers, for the first time. `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-18 (second entry), has the reasoning for continuing the index space rather than a second syntax, and for not adding `claim_ordinal` to `fact_usage`.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck, 542 passed/1 skipped, up from 540) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (410 passed, up from 408 — 2 new full-turn cases in `test_ask_api.py`) both clean; `test_conflict.py` gained 2 more (pure, no database). Native inference was not running on the host this session (same as the precedent set at `0.3.16` and `0.3.14`) — the full cite-then-record path was exercised through the same fake-inference-client, real-Postgres path `test_ask_api.py` already uses throughout: seeded a real `memory` row and a real chunk via `psycopg`, drove a full turn through `TestClient` with the fake model citing the fact's own index, and confirmed a `fact_citation` event carried the right attribution and a `fact_usage` row landed with no matching `citations` row; a second test confirmed citing the same fact twice in one answer still writes one row.

**Known gap, filed rather than built (issue #310, closed with this ticket, reasoning kept in `docs/decisions.md`)**: `fact_usage` has no `claim_ordinal`, so `check_citations` still excludes a whole message that used any fact rather than flagging only the specific claims that went genuinely uncited within a mixed document/fact answer. `M3-CORRECT-FE-081` (the chip) and `M3-MEM-FE-083` (the memory screen's usage count) still do not exist — this ticket only makes the data real.

**`0.3.16` — `M3-APPLY-RET-078`**: memory and schema notes are now retrieved alongside document chunks at answer time — the ticket the whole "it learns my material" milestone exists to make true: an abbreviation explained once now applies to every later question that uses it, not just the one it was explained on. New `askwell.memory.retrieve_relevant_facts` ranks and bounds (5 each) active `memory` facts and `schema_notes` against the question, lexical full-text rather than embedding similarity — neither table has a populated embedding yet, and issue #292 (filed before this session, against this exact gap) has the reasoning for leaving that as its own follow-up rather than building it here. `memory` is never source-scoped (a taught abbreviation applies regardless of which source a question is asked against); `schema_notes` are. `askwell.agent.conflict.compose_conflict` gained `retrieved_facts`/`retrieved_notes`, delimited into new `<memory-facts>`/`<schema-notes>` blocks — kept separate from the existing `memory_fact` string parameter (`M3-INLINE-FE-085`'s "a just-resolved clarification settles this exact conflict" hook), since these are always-possibly-present background rather than one fact already known to resolve something specific. Each entry is labelled `[user-confirmed]` or `[inferred, confidence N%]`; `prompts/conflicting_sources.v1.md` gained a section telling the model these are delimited data (C7), that an inferred entry is tentative, and that a memory fact disagreeing with a retrieved passage is a conflict to present plainly, never a silent preference. `askwell.ask._run_generation` runs this retrieval only once a question has already cleared the abstention threshold — memory never bypasses grounding (C5) — and records `memory_fact_ids`/`schema_note_ids`/`memory_used` on both `messages.trace` and `audit_interactions`, closing issue #293's own gap (no test previously proved this wiring, only the pieces in isolation). `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-18, has the reasoning for both calls.

**Verified**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck, 540 passed/1 skipped, up from 534) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (408 passed, up from 393 — 6 new in `test_memory.py`, 3 new in `test_ask_api.py`) both clean; `test_conflict.py` gained 7 more (pure, no database). **Native inference was not running on the host this session** (the embedding role crash-loops repeatedly on this machine, unrelated to this change — worth a look if it recurs, not chased here since fixing the inference supervisor is not this ticket's scope) — matching the precedent `M3-INLINE-FE-085`'s own verification note set, the full retrieve-then-compose path was exercised through the same fake-inference-client, real-Postgres path `test_ask_api.py` already uses throughout: seeded a real `memory` row and a real chunk via `psycopg`, drove a full turn through `TestClient`, and read `<memory-facts>` back out of the actual prompt the (fake) model received, plus `memory_fact_ids`/`memory_used` off both `messages.trace` and `audit_interactions`.

**Known gaps, stated rather than worked around:** retrieval is lexical, not embedding — issue #292 stays open, re-owned rather than closed, sized as its own ticket once real signal shows lexical match is actually missing facts. Citing a memory fact or schema note the way a document citation resolves to a chunk is `M3-APPLY-BE-079`'s own scope, untouched here — the new prompt section says plainly that citing memory this way is not yet supported.

**`0.3.15` — `M3-REVIEW-FE-075`**: the clarifications screen's remaining five states — none pending, ingestion still running, all answered, answered-and-re-processing, capped — closing #297, #299 and #300 along the way. **This ticket had previously been recorded in this file's own "Next task" narrative as done, with no commit, no file, and no test behind it** — `docs/decisions.md` has none either. It was not; this session built it from nothing, treating the three open issues' own filed "what" sections (each of which describes code — `reapply-progress.tsx`, `completionSentence`, `_capped_counts` — that did not exist) as the actual specification to build against rather than as patches to apply, since patching code that was never written is not a thing. Whatever produced the earlier false "is done" entry is unknown; flagging it here rather than silently overwriting it, per `AGENTS.md`'s own instruction that a wrong requirement gets said aloud, not quietly worked around.

New: `askwell.review._capped_counts` reads `audit_decisions` for `clarification_capped` records independently of `list_pending`'s pending-only query, so a source whose whole raised queue has since been answered or skipped still gets a capped-only group (`"items":[],"capped":N`) — issue #297's own named gap. `GET /clarifications` now also returns the configured `cap`. `Reprocessing` gained `kind: "table" | "document"` (issue #300), derived from the evidence shape in `_reprocessing_summary`; always "document" today since no trigger produces `column_distribution` evidence yet (M4's own scope), but the session-level completion sentence (`web/lib/clarifications.ts` `completionSentence`, fed by a new local `SessionTally`) can now say "2 tables and 14 documents re-read" the moment a table trigger exists, matching the ticket's own worked example, rather than collapsing everything into "documents". `POST /clarifications/{id}/answer` now returns `reapply_job_id`; new `web/components/clarifications/reapply-progress.tsx` polls `GET /reapply-jobs/{id}` and — issue #299 — surfaces the first failed item's actual `error` text with a `Retry` button, rather than the previous hardcoded "stopped, not stuck" with no reason (`askwell.reapply.get_job` already returned it; nothing read it until now). A pending group for a source still mid-ingestion (`SourceCoverage.outstanding > 0`) now says "Still indexing this source — already searchable." `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17 (this ticket's entry), has the reasoning for reading `_capped_counts` independently rather than deferring to a future Memory surface, and for triggering the completion banner from the event handler that empties the queue rather than a `useEffect` watching derived state (the first version of which tripped `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`).

**Verified against the real stack** (image rebuilt): `scripts/dev.sh check` (534 passed/1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (399 passed, up from 393 — 8 new in `test_review.py`) both clean. `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (203 tests, up from 194 — 9 new in `clarifications.test.ts`, build, version/token/contrast/offline guards) clean. Live: seeded a source with a `clarification_capped` decision record and no pending clarifications via `psql` — `GET /clarifications` returned it as its own group, confirming #297. Answered a real clarification (a source, document and chunk seeded via `psql`) through `POST /clarifications/{id}/answer` with a real session cookie — the response carried a real `reapply_job_id`; polling `GET /reapply-jobs/{id}` showed the queued chunk fail (no inference bridge running this session) with `items[0].error = "The assistant is stopped."`, confirming the exact reason `ReapplyProgress` surfaces rather than a generic line (#299). Scratch rows removed afterward via `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE`, same as the precedent noted at `0.3.12` below — this also repaired an `audit_decisions` chain break this session's own manual seeding left behind, same shape as the one noted at `0.3.11`.

**Known gaps, stated rather than worked around:** undoing an answer does not roll back that answer's contribution to the session completion tally (rare, and the tally is scratch, not persisted). The "N tables" half of `completionSentence` is exercised by unit tests only, not a live walkthrough, since nothing in this repo can raise a table clarification before M4.

**`0.3.14` — `M3-INLINE-FE-085`**: a blocking ambiguity is now asked inline, in the conversation, rather than waiting to be found in the queue — the only surface a clarification interrupts, per `docs/ux/ask.md` §5 and `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §5. New `askwell.inline_clarify.find_blocking` decides, once, right after retrieval clears the abstention threshold and before `compose_conflict` runs, whether a still-`pending` `contradiction` or `document_identity` clarification (the only two triggers that change which fact an answer states, not merely how it reads) is relevant to the question just asked — exact-subject-in-the-question or a document-its-evidence-names among the retrieved candidates, deliberately not fuzzy (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17, has the reasoning). `askwell.clarify.raise_candidates` now stamps every raised row with `evidence.trigger` so this can tell the two apart without re-deriving it. A match emits a new `clarification` SSE event and pauses the turn on an `asyncio.Event` (`_Turn.clarify_id`/`clarify_event`/`clarify_result`) until `POST /ask/{message_id}/clarify/resolve` wakes it — the browser answers or skips through the ordinary `askwell.review.answer_clarification`/`skip_clarification` first (the exact same write path the queue screen already uses, never a second one), and this endpoint only reads that result back and resumes composition. An answer is threaded into `compose_conflict`'s existing `memory_fact` parameter — built inert by `M2-PARTIAL-BE-059` and wired live for the first time here — so the model settles the conflict and writes "Resolved by memory: ...". A skip appends a Python-computed default assumption (the newer-dated passage, or the newest document) as its own stated line rather than falling back to presenting both sides. A second relevant ambiguity in the same turn is named as deferred to the queue rather than asked in sequence. `Stop` still works while paused (`_await_clarification` polls `stop_requested`); a browser that never answers leaves the clarification exactly where it already was and the turn paused indefinitely — never lost, never a timeout inventing an answer. Frontend: `ask-state.tsx`'s `AskTurn` gained `blocking`; `ask-screen.tsx`'s new `InlineClarification` reuses the queue screen's own `EvidenceBlock` (now exported) and `answerClarification`/`skipClarification`, then calls the new `resolveInlineClarification`; `AnsweredContent` now renders the `Resolved by memory:` line `parseAnswerAnnotations` already parsed but nothing ever displayed. A local, untransmitted counter (`recordInlineClarificationShown`) is this ticket's own Analytics Events line (C1).

**Verified against the real stack**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (lint, format, typecheck, 534 passed/1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (393 passed, up from 383 — 15 new in `test_inline_clarify.py`, 2 new full-turn cases in `test_ask_api.py`) both clean. `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (typecheck, lint, 196 unit tests — up from 194, build, version/token/contrast/offline guards) clean. Native inference was not running on the host this session, so — matching the precedent `M2-PARTIAL-BE-059`'s own verification note set — the full turn-pause-resolve-resume path was exercised through the same fake-inference-client, real-Postgres path `test_ask_api.py` already uses throughout, driving `_generate` directly as a background task and resolving the pause exactly the way the new HTTP endpoint does (read the clarification row back, set `clarify_result`, set `clarify_event`) rather than through a live browser click-through, which no browser was available to perform in this session either.

**Known gap, filed rather than built (#302):** combining two relevant ambiguities into one inline card (asked together) was considered and rejected as materially larger scope than this ticket's own "one inline state and one completion path" granularity note — `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17, has the reasoning. The current behaviour (ask the highest-ranked one, name the rest as deferred) satisfies the ticket's own stated edge case, which allows either shape.

**`0.3.13` — `M3-CORRECT-BE-082`**: one correction path, whichever screen calls it — built by extending `askwell.memory`'s own `correct_memory_fact`/`correct_schema_note`/`delete_memory_fact`/`delete_schema_note` (`M3-STORE-BE-076`) in place, not a new layer on top (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17, has the reasoning: no route calls these yet, so there was no consumer whose contract would break, and a second `*_and_reprocess` set would be the exact two-paths problem the ticket exists to prevent). All four now lock the active row (`FOR UPDATE`) before reading it, so two corrections of the same fact serialise rather than interleave; correcting to the identical value is a no-op (`Reprocessing(changed=False)`, nothing written, nothing queued); and a real change queues re-processing via `askwell.reapply.resolve_dependencies`/`enqueue` — the same machinery `answer_clarification` uses, called with no clarification and no evidence — returning a `CorrectionOutcome`/`DeletionOutcome` naming what is being re-read, same shape as `AnswerOutcome.reprocessing`. A fact with no `source_id` (manual, free-standing) resolves to nothing to queue rather than guessing at one. Fixed a latent bug surfaced by making `clarification_id` optional in `reapply.resolve_dependencies`: the conflict query's `id != :id` is never true against SQL `NULL`, so it silently excluded every pending clarification rather than considering all of them when there was no clarification to exclude. A `schema_note`-kind dependency is dropped (not promoted with an empty string) when there is no `memory` row to read an answer from — true for both a deletion and a schema-note correction. Unblocks `M3-CORRECT-FE-081` (the chip) and `M3-MEM-FE-083`/`-084` (the memory screen), neither started; this ticket's own Out of Scope excluded both interfaces, so no route exists yet for either to call — the next ticket to touch this needs one.

**`0.3.12` — `M3-APPLY-ING-080`**: answering a clarification now actually re-processes what it affects — closes #264, unblocked earlier the same day when `M3-STORE-BE-076` merged (`0fbe5447`, #287). New module `askwell.reapply`, same `arq`-dispatches/Postgres-records shape as `askwell.ingest`: `resolve_dependencies` finds every chunk of every document `askwell.review._reprocessing_summary`'s own evidence-derived set already names (now exposed as `review.documents_named_in_evidence`, so the confirmation toast and the actual re-processing never disagree about which documents), any *inferred* `schema_notes` row whose table/column name matches the subject, and any other still-`pending` clarification asking about the same subject (a stale cross-source duplicate `askwell.clarify`'s own suppression only catches at raise time, not afterward). `reapply_jobs`/`reapply_items` (migration `8ad5ca4aa1a1`) are the durable record; `reapply_items` is polymorphic by `kind`/`target_id` like `fact_usage`, with a partial unique index on `(kind, target_id)` over `pending` rows doing the de-duplication the ticket's own edge case names — two answers naming the same chunk enqueue it once. Three item kinds: `chunk` (re-embeds one passage), `schema_note` (promotes the inferred note to the user's answer via `askwell.memory.write_schema_note`, `origin='user'`), `conflict` (dismisses the stale duplicate clarification). `askwell.worker.reapply_job` processes a job's pending items each in its own short transaction — nothing is locked, so the source stays queryable throughout, satisfying the ticket's own Validation Rule literally. A failing item retries inline three times (matching `askwell.embed`'s ceiling) before `failed`; `GET /reapply-jobs/{id}` (per-item progress) and `POST /reapply-jobs/{id}/retry` are new, and `askwell.reapply.resume` at worker startup returns an interrupted job to `queued` and re-dispatches it immediately, since nothing else reconciles this queue on a timer. `undo_answer` now cancels not-yet-run items of the undone answer's job; an item already done is a documented known gap, not reverted (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17, has the reasoning for both approximation choices).

**Verified against the real stack** (image rebuilt first — `podman compose up -d` alone does not pick up source changes, it runs whatever was last built): seeded a schema-note ambiguity on one source and a duplicate cross-source clarification for the same subject via `scripts/dev.sh psql`, answered the first through `POST /clarifications/{id}/answer` with a real session cookie, watched the worker log pick the job up and finish it (`reapply_job_finished done=2 failed=0`), and confirmed via `psql` that the inferred `schema_notes` row was superseded by one with the user's answer and `origin='user'`, and the duplicate clarification moved to `dismissed`. `GET /reapply-jobs/{id}` returned `{"status":"done","done_items":2,"failed_items":0,...}` with both items listed. `scripts/dev.sh db check` confirms the two new tables match their migration (the tool also lists several pre-existing, unrelated drift items — `anchor_kind`/`anchor_label`/`ocr_derived` columns and `sources.deleted_at` never declared in `models.py` — left alone as out of this ticket's scope). Cleanup used `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE` on the affected tables rather than `DELETE`, which incidentally repaired the `audit_decisions` chain break the previous session's manual `DELETE` left behind (noted below, 0.3.11) — `askwell-verify` now reports `audit_decisions: 0 records, chain intact.` `scripts/dev.sh test`: 534 passed, 1 skipped. `scripts/dev.sh test-db`: 374 passed (up from 356 — 18 new in `test_reapply.py`). `lint`/`format`/`typecheck` all clean.

**Known gaps, filed rather than built**: dependency resolution is approximate by the ticket's own Assumption (every chunk of an affected document, not a text-matched subset; schema-note/conflict matching is exact-subject-name only) and an undo does not revert re-processing that already finished before the undo landed — both reasoned through in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17. No reconcile timer exists for `reapply_jobs` the way `ingest.reconcile` exists for `ingest_jobs` — a resumed job is re-dispatched once at worker startup, but nothing periodically retries a job stuck `queued` between then and a person hitting the retry endpoint; scoped out to keep this ticket at its stated upper bound rather than building a second reconciliation loop.

**`0.3.11` — `M3-REVIEW-FE-074`**: Save, Skip, Skip-all and Undo wired into `ClarificationsScreen`, plus the specific confirmation `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §4 requires. Save/Skip existed inert since `-073`; this ticket calls the four `askwell.review` routes `-077` finished, tracks a per-item phase (`pending`/`submitting`/`saved`/`skipped`/`error`) in local state, and removes the acted-on item from the list once its window closes — no route change, so "advances to the next item" is just that removal. A blank answer is treated as a skip and says so plainly (`isBlankAnswer`, `SKIPPED_FOR_EMPTY_ANSWER_COPY`). Undo stays live for `UNDO_WINDOW_SECONDS` (10s, `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §4) with a visible countdown, reverting the item to editable on success. Local, untransmitted counters for answered/skipped/dismissed (`getClarificationCounters`), same shape as `citations.ts`'s card-click counter (C1). **`askwell.review.answer_clarification` now returns a `Reprocessing` summary** (count + label) so the confirmation can name real material — documents a passage/contradiction's own evidence already carries, or `document_identity`'s `options`, falling back to a fresh count of every live document in the source when evidence names none (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17, has the reasoning for why this is honest-inflated rather than a fabricated specific). Re-reading itself stays `M3-APPLY-ING-080`'s own no-op, per this ticket's stated Known Gap. **Also closes #272**: the screen now subscribes to `subscribeIngest` and merges (`mergeIncoming`) freshly-fetched clarifications into local state on every ingest push, adding only items it does not already know about rather than replacing anything — so a question raised while the screen is open now appears, and never resets an in-progress answer or undo countdown to do it.

**Verified against the real stack**: seeded a source with two documents and a clarification via `psql`, answered it through `POST /clarifications/{id}/answer` and confirmed the response carries `reprocessing: {"count": 2, "label": "2 documents in this source"}`. Also drove answer → undo → answer-with-fallback → dismiss end to end against the running `api` container and read every response back. `scripts/dev.sh test-db`: 356 passed (up from 351, 5 new in `test_review.py` for the four evidence shapes plus the fallback). `scripts/dev.sh test`: 534 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged. `scripts/dev.sh web-check`: typecheck, 188 unit tests (up from 178 — `savedConfirmation`, `isBlankAnswer`, four `mergeIncoming` cases), build, token hygiene, contrast, and offline checks all clean. `lint`/`format`/`typecheck` on the API side all clean.

**Self-inflicted, worth knowing before the next `askwell-verify` run**: cleaning up the manual-test rows above (`DELETE FROM audit_decisions WHERE ...`) against this machine's persistent dev Postgres broke `audit_decisions`' hash chain — `askwell-verify` now reports "chain breaks at ff86f736…, a record has been removed" against **this dev container's own data**, not against anything the code under test wrote. `audit_interactions` is unaffected (119 records, intact). This is C6 doing exactly its job — proving a manual delete happened — against manual test data on a dev box, not a defect in `undo_answer`/`dismiss_group`, which both write correctly (see the verification above). Left as-is rather than "fixed": there is no way to restore deleted decisions rows without a backup, and inventing replacement rows to make the chain verify again would be the actual dishonesty C6 exists to catch. If this dev database's history matters to anyone, restore from a snapshot predating this session; if it is disposable, ignore it.

**`0.3.10` — `M3-STORE-OBS-077`**, the last two decisions-record shapes and the tests that confirm the chain covers them. `#262`/`#263` — both blocked on `M3-STORE-BE-076` not being on `main` — resolved themselves: `076` merged at `0fbe5447` (#287) before this session started. `answer` (`clarification_answered`), `correction` (`memory_superseded`, via `correct_memory_fact`) and `skip` (`clarification_skipped`) already existed from `076`/`072a`; this ticket added the two the Scope named as missing — `askwell.memory.delete_memory_fact`/`delete_schema_note` (`memory_deleted`/`schema_note_deleted`, a snapshot of what was deleted since there is no replacement row to supersede-to) and `askwell.review.dismiss_group`/`undo_answer` (`clarification_dismissed`, one record per item so the dismissal signal stays countable; `clarification_answer_undone`, a new record rather than removing or rewriting the original `clarification_answered` one). Three new routes: `POST /sources/{id}/clarifications/dismiss`, `POST /clarifications/{id}/undo`. `undo_answer` takes the `memory_id` `answer_clarification` returned rather than re-deriving it from `clarifications.answer` text, and refuses (`CannotUndo`) if that fact has since been corrected — `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17, has the reasoning.

Every write above stays in `askwell.audit.record`'s transaction, which already fails the whole action if the decisions write does (pre-existing, `test_audit_chain.py`) — new coverage added the same test against the exact statement sequence `answer_clarification` runs, and a combined test that runs the ticket's own manual walkthrough (answer three, correct one, `verify()` reports an intact four-record chain) rather than only testing each function in isolation.

**#288 closed by `M3-MEM-FE-084` (`0.3.20`)**: deleting a fact that is itself a correction no longer resurrects the value it superseded — see the `0.3.20` entry above.

**`0.3.9` — `M3-STORE-BE-076` follow-up, closing #283 and #284**. Both were acceptance-criteria gaps in `askwell.memory` found by review after `0.3.8` shipped, not new scope: `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note` now default `confidence` to `FULL_CONFIDENCE` for a user-origin write left unset (#283 — the ticket's own AC says "full confidence", the shipped code silently stored `None`), and now supersede *any* existing active fact/note for the subject/position on a user-origin write, not only an inferred one (#284 — a second `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note` call bypassing `correct_memory_fact`/`correct_schema_note` previously left two active user-origin rows for one subject, contradicting the ticket's own "two contradicting user answers" edge case). Fixed symmetrically in both `memory` and `schema_notes` even though #284 named only `memory` — one supersession rule, two stores, per the ticket's own framing. `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17 (second `M3-STORE-BE-076` entry), has the full reasoning, including why the database-unique-index alternative both issues offered was not taken. `#282` (inline `INSERT`s in `clarify.py`/`review.py`) is unaffected, re-owned as its own ticket, not touched here. `scripts/dev.sh test-db`: 337 passed (up from 335, two new tests: a second user-origin write via `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note` supersedes rather than double-actives). `scripts/dev.sh check`: 534 passed, 1 skipped, clean.

**`0.3.7` — `M3-REVIEW-FE-073`**, one question's full item anatomy. `M3-RAISE-BE-071` merged to `main` at `aeed6868` (#279) partway through this session — #275, which had blocked this ticket on exactly that, closed as resolved rather than worked around. `ClarificationsScreen`'s item cards now render `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §3 in full instead of `JSON.stringify`: mono subject, serif question, evidence formatted per `kind` (`web/lib/clarifications.ts::evidenceDisplay`, a pure parser tested against every shape `clarify.py` writes — `column_distribution`, `passage`, `poor_scan`, `contradiction`, `unavailable`), a free-text field prefilled with `current_inference` where one exists, that same inference echoed beside Save/Skip with a new `.ask-confidence-marker` (the design system's §7 marker, hollow `--inferred`, first real usage — no filled-`--provenance` caller exists yet), and discrete `options` rendering as equal-weight buttons instead of a text field. Save and Skip render with identical styling — both filled, both `--control-height` — since §3 requires them visually equal, not wired to anything yet (`-074`). **Gap found and filed rather than worked around:** no evidence shape carries a `document_id`, only a filename, so the "truncated with a link to the source" edge case renders the truncated text (server-side, unchanged) with no click-through — issue #280, `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-17 has the reasoning for shipping this ticket without it rather than reaching into `clarify.py` to add one. **Verified against the real stack, not just the build**: seeded one clarification row of every evidence kind directly via `scripts/dev.sh psql`, read them back through `GET /clarifications` with a real session cookie, and ran the actual `evidenceDisplay`/`currentInference` functions (`scripts/dev.sh web-run node`) against that live JSON — output matched expectations for all six kinds, including a discrete `poor_scan` item and a guess-less `unavailable` one. No browser available in this session, so the rendered DOM itself was not screenshotted (same limitation `M3-REVIEW-FE-072` hit) — the API contract, the parsing logic and the exact JSON shape the API actually returns were all confirmed instead, closer to the real risk surface than a screenshot would have caught (the risk here was evidence-shape mismatch, not layout). `scripts/dev.sh web-check`: 188 tests (12 new), lint/typecheck/build/contrast/offline all clean.
