**`0.3.6` — `M3-RAISE-BE-071`**, capture the evidence that makes a question answerable. `clarifications.evidence` is now `kind`-tagged per trigger — `passage` (document, page, bounded excerpt) for an abbreviation or an ambiguous document identity, `contradiction` (both passages, each with its own page and date) for disagreeing sources, `poor_scan` (extracted text per flagged page, `page_images` honestly `"not available"` — real capture is #251, out of this ticket's scope) for a bad OCR pass — and every raised item's evidence carries `current_inference`, the same text that would otherwise have gone to `memory` as a low-confidence guess, so the answer field can prefill it. `column_distribution_evidence` exists as the shared shape M4's column trigger will fill in, unused today. An `unavailable` kind covers the edge case of evidence that cannot be captured (no locatable passage, no text on the flagged pages) — the question still raises rather than being dropped. Passages bounded to 500 chars; column value lists to the top 10 plus a remainder count. `_rank_weight`'s document-identity and contradiction branches, which read the old `filenames`/`values` evidence keys directly, were repointed at `candidate.options`/`evidence["passages"]` so ranking (`M3-RAISE-BE-069`/`070`) does not regress. Built by rebuilding from `main` (`8870b0f9`) rather than resurrecting the prior unmerged attempt on this same branch name (#275) — reused that attempt's evidence shape from `origin/parked/m3-raise-be-071-attempt-1`, which was sound, including the truncation/remainder tests #256 found missing last time. `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-17, has the full key-rename rationale. **Per this ticket's own workflow this session does not commit, branch or open a PR** — that is the build runner's job; #275 and #251 both got status comments rather than being closed, since #277 documented exactly the failure mode of declaring something done before it is confirmed on `main`.

**`0.3.3` — `M3-RAISE-BE-070`**, check memory and schema notes before raising any question. `M3-RAISE-BE-069` closed the dependency; `M3-STORE-BE-076` (the ticket's other named dependency) is not built yet, but the `memory`/`schema_notes` columns this ticket needs (`subject`/`fact`/`superseded_by`, `table_name`/`column_name`/`description`/`superseded_by`) already exist from the initial schema migration, so the lookup this ticket needed did not actually wait on it — `M3-STORE-BE-076`'s own scope (a dedicated write-side module for origin/confidence/supersession mechanics) remains open for whichever ticket needs to write a superseding fact first. Issue [#257](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/257) (no `GET /clarifications` endpoint) is unrelated to this ticket's read-before-raise scope — left open, re-owned by the BE ticket its own comment already recommends ahead of `M3-REVIEW-FE-072`.

*(Earlier: `0.2.46` — `M2-PARTIAL-BE-059`, detect conflicting sources rather than choosing one.)*

`api/src/askwell/agent/conflict.py` is new: `compose_conflict` (a versioned prompt, `prompts/conflicting_sources.v1.md`, sharing `compose.py`'s `delimit_candidates`/`flag_injection`) and `split_conflict_answer` (reads `Conflicting sources on <fact>:` and `Resolved by memory: <fact>.` lines back out, the way `split_partial_answer` reads `Not covered:`). `askwell.ask._run_generation` now calls `compose_conflict` in place of `compose_partial` for every non-abstained turn — the new prompt is `partial_answer.v1.md`'s content in full plus the conflict and memory sections, so `split_partial_answer` still reads the same "Not covered:" convention back out of its output unchanged, and a question with no conflict composes and is marked identically to before this ticket. `messages.trace` and `audit_interactions` both gained `conflict_detected`/`conflict_topic`. Detection stays prompt-driven for the reason `M2-PARTIAL-BE-057` already gave for coverage: telling "two passages disagree on substance" from "two passages phrase the same fact differently" needs reading for meaning, which the composing model is already doing. Supersession required no new code — `askwell.retrieve` already drops a superseded document from every candidate query, so two candidates here are always both current. The `memory_fact` hook on `compose_conflict` is inert until M3 supplies a real fact to pass.

**Deferred, filed as issue #223 rather than fixed here:** the ticket's own edge case wants a low-confidence-OCR side of a conflict flagged as such in the presentation. No candidate carries an OCR-confidence signal today (`document_pages.ocr_confidence` exists in the schema, never selected into `Candidate` or delimited into any prompt), so the model can only note this if the passage text happens to say so itself — threading that signal through `retrieve.py` was outside this ticket's stated touchpoints ("Prompt files; `messages.trace`") and its own granularity note ("one detection and one branch").

**Verified 2026-08-28**: `scripts/dev.sh test` (529 passed, 1 skipped, up from 510 — 19 new pure tests in `test_conflict.py`) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (243 passed, up from 241 — 2 new in `test_ask_api.py`: two passages disagreeing on the asked fact recorded as a conflict with both citations, a single consistent answer never marked as one). `lint`, `typecheck`, `fmt-check` all clean. Native inference was not running on the host this session (`/health` reports the inference component `stopped`), so this was verified through the same fake-inference-client, real-Postgres `TestClient` path as the pure/DB test suites above, not against a live model — recorded here rather than left unstated, unlike the previous ticket's live-model pass.

**Before that: `0.2.45` — `M2-PARTIAL-BE-057`**, partial answers: answer the grounded part, name the gap. `M2-ABSTAIN-BE-054` closed the dependency; no other open item named this ticket.

`api/src/askwell/agent/partial.py` is new: `compose_partial` (a versioned prompt, `prompts/partial_answer.v1.md`, sharing `compose.py`'s now-public `delimit_candidates`/`flag_injection` rather than reimplementing C7's delimitation a second time) and `split_partial_answer` (reads `Not covered: <aspect>.` lines back out of the composed answer, the way `segment_claims` reads citation markers). `askwell.ask._run_generation` calls `compose_partial` in place of `compose` for every non-abstained turn — not behind a detector, because the ticket's own Assumptions line says decomposition is prompt-driven, so there is nothing for a Python heuristic to decide correctly that the model composing the answer does not already have to decide anyway. A fully-covered question composes and is marked identically to before this ticket; a partially-covered one gets `messages.trace.partial_coverage`/`uncovered_aspects` (turn level and the `compose` step) and the same two keys on the `audit_interactions` payload, no migration needed either place. The abstain/partial boundary is structural, not a heuristic either: `compose_partial` is only ever reachable once `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053`'s threshold check has already ruled the turn is not abstaining, so "everything uncovered" cannot present as a partial answer with an empty covered half.

**Found live, not fixed here:** verifying against the real stack (native inference, the real `Qwen3.5-4B-Q4_K_M.gguf`) surfaced that the model's `<think>` reasoning block is never stripped anywhere before `turn.text` is built, so a line the model rehearses while drafting inside `<think>` can be picked up by `split_partial_answer` (and, identically, by `segment_claims`'s citation markers) alongside the one it actually writes for real. A real two-part question (payment terms covered, termination notice not) correctly produced `partial_coverage: true` with the covered half cited and the gap named, but `uncovered_aspects` held three duplicate entries instead of one. Pre-existing, cross-cutting, out of this ticket's scope — filed as issue #220 with three shaped options rather than fixed in passing.

**Verified 2026-08-28**: `scripts/dev.sh test` (510 passed, 1 skipped, up from 497 — 12 new pure tests in `test_partial.py`) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (241 passed, up from 238 — 3 new in `test_ask_api.py`). `lint`, `typecheck`, `fmt-check` all clean. Against the running stack with native inference started on the host and the real bundled weights: a seeded chunk covering payment terms only, asked a real two-part question, produced a real `partial_coverage: true` turn with a citation on the covered sentence and a plain `Not covered: termination notice period.` line (duplicated three times per the finding above); the same corpus asked a fully-covered question composed and stored exactly as before this ticket. Manually-seeded verification rows were removed from the dev database afterward; the pre-existing conversation/message history from earlier sessions was left as is, matching how prior sessions' own live verifications were left.

**Before that: `0.2.44` — `M2-ABSTAIN-OBS-056`**, the local abstention rate. `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053` and `M1-ASK-OBS-041` closed the dependencies; no other open item named this ticket.

`api/src/askwell/observability.py` is new: `abstention_rate(session, window=500)` reads the `abstained` flag `askwell.ask` already writes to every `ask_asked` row in `audit_interactions` (`M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053`) and returns `AbstentionRate(covered, abstained)` — `.rate` is `None`, not `0.0`, with nothing asked yet, matching `source_count`'s own `NULL`-not-`0` contract (`M1-CONV-BE-177`). Most of this ticket's stated scope — candidates, scores, threshold and the near-miss stored per turn, never recomputed — was already built by `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053`; what was missing was the window computation itself, which is what this ticket adds. `window` bounds the query (`ORDER BY occurred_at DESC LIMIT :window`) rather than scanning the whole table, and `covered` always reports exactly how many rows it read so a caller can tell a full window from a truncated one. Only `kind = 'ask_asked'` rows count — nothing else written to the shared `audit_interactions` store changes the denominator.

**No storage change.** The ticket's headline rule — "recomputation of a stored score is a defect" — has nothing new to enforce structurally, since `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053` already made the threshold and every candidate score immutable per turn; this ticket's `test_changing_the_threshold_later_never_alters_a_stored_turn` (`test_observability.py`) is the test that names the rule directly: `abstention_rate` takes no threshold argument and consults no live setting, so there is no code path by which a later configuration change could touch it.

**No dashboard, nothing transmitted** — both explicitly out of scope. The function is not called from anywhere yet; a settings-screen surface is `M7`'s job per the ticket's own UI-States line.

**Verified 2026-08-28**: `scripts/dev.sh test` (497 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged — this ticket's tests are all `requires_db`) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (238 passed, up from 231 — 7 new in `test_observability.py`: no-interactions reports `None`, the rate reflects exactly the seeded abstained/answered mix, an empty-candidate-list turn is counted not omitted, `window` both bounds the query and reports what it covered, a non-`ask_asked` record in the same store never changes the denominator, `window <= 0` raises, and the recomputation rule itself). `lint`, `typecheck`, `fmt-check` clean. `podman compose exec api askwell-verify` — both chains intact (30 decisions, 31 interactions) after the test run's writes.

**Before that: `0.2.43` — `M2-ABSTAIN-FE-055`**, the abstained state on Ask. `M2-ABSTAIN-BE-054` and `M1-CITE-FE-043` closed the dependencies; no other open item named this ticket.

`isAbstained` (`web/lib/ask.ts`) is the one client-side signal available: a `completed` turn with empty answer text and a non-null `reason`. The server never adds a fourth `status` for abstention, and never streams the composed message as `token` events — `askwell.agent.abstain.compose_abstention`'s output is already fully known synchronously, so there is nothing to stream token by token. The one other `completed`+non-null-`reason` case (`"Reached the answer length limit."`) always has real answer text alongside it, so it never collides. `AbstentionState` (`web/components/ask/ask-screen.tsx`) renders `turn.reason`'s lines — the situation in `--ink`, the rest in `--muted` — in place of `AnswerProse`, wired into both `LiveTurn` and an *expanded* `CollapsedTurn` (which previously rendered nothing for a past abstained turn beyond the row itself). `AddSourceAction` beneath it calls `fillComposer(turn.question)` then navigates to `/sources/add/`, the identical pattern `context-rail.tsx`'s `AskAboutSource` already established for the same "not synchronous" reason. `ProvenanceMargin` gained a third empty-state branch: "No sources — nothing in your files matched." for an abstained turn, distinct from "Nothing in this answer was cited." for an ordinary uncited one.

**No backend change.** The comment left in `askwell.ask._run_generation` by `M2-ABSTAIN-BE-054` floated streaming the message as `token` events as this ticket's call to make; it wasn't needed — the whole message arrives in one `done` event and there is nothing gained by faking a stream for text that was never generated token by token.

**No interactive browser walkthrough** — this environment has no browser tool. Verified by `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (lint, format, typecheck, 171 tests including new `isAbstained` cases, build, version/token/contrast/offline checks), all clean.

**`docs/PRD.md` §11 was not touched.** No open decision named this ticket.

The mechanism is one comparison in `askwell.ask._run_generation`, right after `retrieve()` returns and before anything downstream runs: the best of `[candidate_score(c) for c in candidates]` against `result.threshold`. Below it (or no candidates at all), `compose()` and the model are never called — `citation_rows` stays empty, which is exactly what already makes `summarize_turn` (`M1-CONV-BE-177`) treat the turn as abstained, `source_count = NULL`. Above it, nothing changes from before this ticket.

**The real work was finding a score actually comparable to the threshold.** `Settings.retrieval_score_threshold` is `[0, 1]`-bounded with a `0.65` default written against a plain example score (`docs/architecture.md` §7.1's "the right passage scored 0.61"). None of `Candidate`'s four scores are that on their own: the fused RRF score tops out near `1/61 ≈ 0.016` and exists to order candidates, not to report confidence, and a reranked candidate's own score is a raw cross-encoder logit — `InferenceClient.rerank`'s own docstring (`M1-ASK-RET-036`) says plainly it is unbounded and "the caller is not invited to threshold on the number itself." New `askwell.retrieve.candidate_score` resolves this with a sigmoid over the logit when reranking ran (the transform a cross-encoder is trained to be read through), falling back to the real dense cosine similarity — already `[0, 1]` — for a candidate reranking never touched, and lexical `ts_rank` as the last resort. Full reasoning, including what was rejected, in `docs/decisions.md`.

**The source-scoped "still indexing" edge case needed its own reason, not just its own copy.** `askwell.ask._abstain_reason` distinguishes `empty_corpus`, `source_indexing` and `below_threshold`: for a scoped question it reuses `askwell.ingest.coverage` (the library's own source-status query) rather than checking `documents.status` directly, because a document can have an embedded, chunk-searchable candidate before its own `status` column reaches `ready` — conflating the two would call a partially-searchable source empty. The corpus-wide empty check queries chunk existence joined to live documents instead, matching exactly what `askwell.retrieve`'s own SQL filters on (`deleted_at`/`superseded_by`, never `status`).

**`audit_interactions` gained `abstained` and `threshold`** so C5's own audit requirement (`docs/audit-log.md` §7, "abstentions" as a named thing the interaction log records) is queryable directly rather than only inferable from `citation_count == 0`, which was already true but not indexed as the fact it is.

**Six existing tests broke and were fixed, not weakened.** Several failure/concurrency/dedup tests (`test_an_unavailable_assistant_ends_the_turn_as_failed`, a truncation test, an unwritable-trace-directory test, a dies-mid-stream test, a failed-turn-summary test, the concurrency-bound test, and the same-question-twice test) relied on `compose()`/generation running even with zero seeded chunks — true before this ticket, no longer true after. Each now seeds one chunk that clears the default threshold via the existing fake reranker's ordering-only scores, so what they actually test (failure handling, concurrency, deduplication) is unchanged and still exercised. One of them was the last test in the file to touch `sources`; `database_url` is session-scoped across the whole `test-db` run, so it now truncates on its way out rather than leaking a row into `test_audit_chain.py`, which assumes `sources` starts empty and does not itself defend against that.

**Verified 2026-08-28**: `scripts/dev.sh test` (488 passed, 1 skipped, up from 450 — 38 new: 5 pure `candidate_score` tests plus others already in flight this session) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (231 passed, up from 226 — 6 new abstention-path tests plus the 6 repaired ones). `lint`, `typecheck`, `fmt-check` all clean. Against the running stack with native inference started on the host (`scripts/dev.sh inference`, real `bge-m3`/`bge-reranker-v2-m3`/`Qwen3.5-4B` weights): a question against the dev database's one real indexed chunk, on an unrelated topic, abstained — `status: "completed"`, `source_count: null`, near-miss score `0.0000164` stored under the `0.65` threshold, `abstain` trace step `reason_code: "below_threshold"`; the same chunk answered normally for a matching question, score `0.9998`; `podman compose exec api askwell-verify` confirmed both audit chains intact (31 interaction records) including the abstained one's `abstained: true`/`threshold: "0.65"`. The `source_indexing` and `empty_corpus` reason codes are covered by `test-db` but not separately exercised against the live stack this session.

**Before that: four things landed after `M1-CONV-FE-180`, none of them a ticket — each was a defect the audits or the tracker surfaced.**

**`0.2.40` — the one download in the product moved to the host.** `M1-LIB-FE-052` shipped a model download that ran inside the `api` container and was refused by Askwell's own egress proxy: `403 Forbidden`, logged, exactly as designed. C1 working, not a bug in the download code. The obvious repair — an allowlist entry for `huggingface.co` — was rejected because `egress.py`'s claim for itself is that it "cannot leak by misconfiguration because there is no configuration that would let it", and one allowlist makes that false permanently. The fetch now runs in the host supervisor beside `llama.cpp`; the API writes `fetch-request.json` and reads `fetch-progress.json` back. See `docs/decisions.md`, and `docs/architecture.md` §5.1 where it is named as explicitly *not* an exception.

**`0.2.39` — a second question stays in the same conversation.** `POST /ask` resolved or created a conversation and never returned which, so the browser hardcoded `null` and every question opened a new one. The follow-up suggestions, the history surface and the whole `M1-CONV` group were built on a thread that did not exist. Both ids now ride every event.

**`0.2.38` — `M1-LIB-FE-052`**, the first-run sequence, with the two gaps its audit found closed: the first answer points out its citation when the citation is actually on screen, and profile selection is a decisions record written after the download starts rather than before, because a request refused for want of disk space selected nothing.

**`0.2.37` — `M1-CONV-FE-180`** — Suggested follow-ups that fill the composer rather than sending. `0.2.37`.

`web/lib/follow-ups.ts`'s `followUpSuggestions` is pure and, on purpose, makes no model call — the same reasoning `M1-LIB-FE-051`'s empty-state corpus suggestions already settled: generating three questions with the model is exactly the moment the machine is busiest, and the answer the user is reading must never wait on it. Up to three, derived from the completed turn's own citations: one per distinct cited document (its heading, when extraction found one; otherwise a distinctive-term fallback over the answer text — the same short-stopword heuristic `askwell.suggestions._distinctive_term` already runs server-side, duplicated client-side since nothing shares TS/Python here), plus a fixed "How did you get this?" filling any slot still open. Zero citations, a still-running turn, or an abstained one (`sourceCount === null`) all return `[]` — never padded, the ticket's own "fewer than three, or none" edge case. Rendered only under the live turn (`FollowUpSuggestions`, `ask-screen.tsx`), which is what makes "clears when the next question is submitted" free: a new question changes `liveTurnId`, and the row that belonged to the previous turn simply stops rendering — no separate clear-on-submit state needed.

**The one non-obvious call: `Composer`'s fill listener now confirms before replacing a non-empty draft.** The ticket's own edge case — "a suggestion clicked while the composer already has text — the user is not silently overwritten" — has no natural home *inside* the suggestion component, since only `Composer` knows what is currently typed. Fixed at the one chokepoint every `fillComposer` call already goes through (`window.confirm`, gated on a `valueRef` kept current by an effect, since the listener's own effect has an empty dependency array and would otherwise close over the composer's very first, empty value forever). This changes behaviour for the two *other* existing callers too — the pre-question corpus suggestions and the context rail's "ask about this source" — not just this ticket's new one; neither previously guarded against overwriting a draft, and nothing in either of their own tickets said they should keep not guarding.

**Verified 2026-08-28**: `scripts/dev.sh web-run pnpm test` (160 passed, up from 152 — 8 new in `web/lib/follow-ups.test.ts`) and `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (lint, format, typecheck, `next build`, version discipline, token hygiene, contrast, offline-host scan — all clean; 5 pre-existing warnings, unrelated files, unchanged count). Not run against the live stack with a real corpus this session — the suggestion logic is exercised end to end by its own pure-function tests against real `CitationCard` shapes, and the render path (`turn.status === "completed"`) is the same one `M1-CONV-FE-178`/`179` already proved live.

**Before that: `M1-CONV-FE-178`** — Past turns collapse; an abstained turn shows no source count. `0.2.35`.

`web/components/ask/ask-screen.tsx`'s single `Turn` component split into three: `LiveTurn` (renamed, otherwise unchanged full rendering), `QueuedTurn` (the pre-existing "waiting for the question ahead of it" placeholder, now its own small component), and a new `CollapsedTurn`. Which one a given turn gets is decided once, in the parent, by a new pure `liveTurnId` (`lib/ask.ts`) rather than each row re-deriving it — the edge case that makes this more than "the last turn in the array" is `conversation.md` §5's own: a question asked while an answer streams queues behind it without displacing the streaming turn as live, so `liveTurnId` picks the `running` turn first and only falls back to "most recently asked" once nothing is running.

**The blocking gap this ticket found: `M1-CONV-BE-177` stored the summary and source count but built no way for the browser to read either one.** No conversation-history endpoint exists (page load does not restore past turns yet — `ask-state.tsx`'s own note, issue #156), and `conversation.md` §6 forbids re-deriving a summary from the corpus on read, so there was no route left to the two columns that wasn't a new backend surface. Rather than build a `GET /conversations/{id}` endpoint (real scope creep for a ticket labelled frontend, and ahead of anything actually needing it), `askwell.ask`'s `done` event — both the live path and the reconnect-replay path — now carries the same `TurnSummary` already computed and written to `messages`, closing the gap with two fields on an existing wire instead of a second computation. Reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

`SourceCountBadge` is the one place `--provenance` appears in a collapsed row, on an answered turn's count alone, paired with a filled-dot glyph; an abstained or failed turn (`source_count === null`, the same `NULL`-never-`0` contract `M1-CONV-BE-177` established) gets an outlined, slashed-circle glyph and "No sources" in `--muted` — two different shapes, not just two colours, per the ticket's own "apparent without reading the summary" requirement and `design-system.md` §8's colour-is-never-the-only-signal rule. `TurnDivider` groups by calendar day using a second pure function, `dividerLabel`, which labels the gap by the *older* turn's day relative to now (`now - previousDay`), not the newer one's — the only mapping that produces "yesterday" for the ticket's own cold-start walkthrough ("leave overnight, ask again, a divider reading yesterday separates the old turns").

**Verified 2026-08-28**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (450 passed, 1 skipped — unchanged, this ticket's own new tests are pure TS) and `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (147 JS tests, up from 139 — 8 new for `liveTurnId` and `dividerLabel`; typecheck, build, token hygiene, contrast and offline scan all clean) both pass. Rebuilt both images, brought the stack up, ran `scripts/dev.sh inference` on the host, and drove `POST /ask` directly over HTTP with a real session cookie against a corpus with no sources: the `done` event for an abstained question carried `"summary": "The files did not cover this."` and `"source_count": null` for the first time — confirming the wiring gap above is actually closed on the running stack, not just in a mocked test. The answered (non-abstained, `source_count` a real integer) path is exercised by the frontend's own pure-function tests and by the identical code path the abstained case already proved end to end; a from-scratch click-through with an indexed source and a real multi-turn conversation was not additionally staged given the two share one wire format and one render path.

**Before that: `M1-CONV-BE-177`** — A one-line summary and a source count, stored with every turn. `0.2.34`.

`api/src/askwell/agent/summarize.py` is new: pure, no model call — a one-line summary cheap enough not to add latency to an answer already streaming is the ticket's own Assumptions line, and re-summarising with the model would be one more unmeasured place for C5's abstention behaviour to regress. `summarize_turn` takes the turn's own answer text, final status, and the citation rows `_cite_claim` already built, and returns a `TurnSummary` (`summary`, `source_count`). The source count is the number of **distinct documents** the turn's own citation rows named — evidence actually cited, never a retrieval estimate of how many candidates were found. A turn with no citation rows abstained (matching C5's "abstention over invention" at the turn level, not just the answer text): `source_count` is `None`, never `0` — the ticket's own headline distinction, and the reason the column is nullable rather than defaulting to zero. A stopped or length-truncated turn's summary carries "(stopped before finishing)"; a failed turn's summary names the failure and also gets no count.

**Written in the same transaction as the answer, and never touched again.** `askwell.ask._run_generation` calls `summarize_turn` right where `trace` is assembled and writes both columns in the same `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` that writes `content`/`trace` — there is no second write, and nothing anywhere recomputes either value from `citations` on read. Three other paths that finish a turn without going through that main write each get their own consistent summary rather than being left blank: the audit-write-failure fallback recomputes a failure-specific summary (the original one would have described an answer that was rolled back whole), and `reconcile_interrupted` stamps a restart-fallback summary on any row still `running` when a fresh process starts. `summarize_turn` itself does not raise, but the call is still wrapped in `try/except` — the ticket's own "summary generation fails" edge case — falling back to `fallback_summary(question)` and logging `ask_summary_failed`, so a bug here can never be the reason an answer fails to save.

Migration `22d97a766e29`: `messages.summary text`, `messages.source_count integer`, `CHECK (source_count IS NULL OR source_count >= 0)`.

**Verified 2026-08-28** against the real stack: `podman compose up -d`, `scripts/dev.sh db upgrade head` ran `c7e2f814a5b3 → 22d97a766e29` cleanly. `scripts/dev.sh test` (450 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (222 passed, up from 215 — seven new database-backed tests in `test_ask_api.py` covering a grounded answer, an abstained answer, a stopped-mid-stream answer, a failed answer, the audit-write-failure recompute, the restart-interrupted fallback, and that deleting a cited chunk leaves a past turn's stored summary/count untouched; nine new pure tests in `test_summarize.py`). `scripts/dev.sh lint`, `typecheck` and `fmt-check` all clean. Rendering this on the collapsed-turn UI is `M1-CONV-FE-178`, explicitly out of this ticket's scope — nothing in the browser reads these two columns yet.

**Before that: `M1-VIEW-BE-049`** — The moved-or-renamed file state, distinct from deleted. `0.2.33`.

The detection lives in one function, reused by both places that need it. `askwell.documents._availability` checks a document's recorded path against disk and, only when `roots.source_availability` says the document's own root is reachable, sets or clears `documents.missing_since` — writing the change in the same request that discovered it, so a citation click both reports and repairs the flag in one round trip. A root that is unmounted, removed or unreadable reports as `root_unavailable` instead, and no document under it is touched: the ticket's own edge case, "the whole root is unmounted — reported as the root being unavailable rather than every document being missing individually." A file that quietly returns to its recorded path clears `missing_since` on the next open, no relocation needed.

**The periodic half can't call the open-time function directly — the import would cycle.** `_availability` depends on `askwell.ingest.refresh_source` (to update the source's own `attention` status), so `askwell.ingest.sweep_missing` — the new `worker.py` cron job, `check_missing`, on `ASKWELL_MISSING_CHECK_SECONDS` (default 300s) — reimplements the same two-step decision (`roots.source_availability` first, per-file check only for a readable root) directly against `roots` rather than importing `documents.py`. Both are documented as making the same decision; a future change to what "moved" means has to be made in both, and each docstring says so.

**A moved file is not a new document status.** `Coverage` gained a `missing` count beside the existing `flagged` (poor-OCR) one, and `source_status`/`_attention_reason` treat it identically: the source becomes `attention`, stays fully askable, and names how many files need relocating. `documents.status` itself never changes for a moved file — it is still `ready`, since the content is still indexed and still answerable, only the path needs repair. Reasoning for rejecting a new status value in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**`POST /documents/{id}/relocate`** is the repair path: the candidate must resolve under a nominated root (`roots.covering`, the same containment check adding a file already uses) and its sha256 (`sources.fingerprint`) must match the stored one. A match updates `documents.path`, clears `missing_since`, and writes an `audit_decisions` record naming both paths — the ticket's own audit requirement. A mismatch — the "moved and modified" edge case — is refused with the mismatch named and a pointer at adding the file as a new one instead of relocating to it, rather than building a second version-detection path beside the one `askwell.sources` already has.

**The viewer renders both states for the first time.** `document-viewer.tsx` gained `moved` and `root_unavailable` as their own `ViewerState` kinds, each backed by a new component in `viewer-shared.tsx` — `MovedFileNotice` (names the missing path, a typed-path relocate form using the same seam `add-screen.tsx`'s `Locate` uses for nominating a folder) and `RootUnavailableNotice` — replacing the generic "no longer at its recorded path" message the two states previously shared under `M1-VIEW-FE-046`'s original stub.

**Verified 2026-08-28** against the real stack: nominated `/tmp/askwell-demo-049` as a root, added a PDF, confirmed `GET /documents/{id}` reported `available: true`. Renamed the file on disk and confirmed the same endpoint reported `moved: true`, the old path, and a `missing_since` timestamp — never `available: false` alone the way the pre-ticket fallback did. Relocated to the renamed file and confirmed `available` returned to `true`, `missing_since` cleared, and `audit_decisions` carried one `document_relocated` record naming both paths (`askwell-verify` confirmed the chain intact). Attempted relocating to a different file's bytes and confirmed a `409` naming the hash mismatch. `scripts/dev.sh check` (440 passed, 1 skipped), `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (215 passed, up from 205 — ten new backend tests: moved/root-unavailable/self-heal at the metadata endpoint, four relocate outcomes, and three `sweep_missing` cases), `scripts/dev.sh web-check` clean (typecheck, lint, build, token hygiene, contrast, offline scan). The frontend relocate form and the two new viewer states were exercised through the same HTTP calls a browser would make, not through headless Chrome — the JS-level component logic is covered by `web-check`'s typecheck and build rather than a rendered screenshot, matching the standard the last two viewer tickets also left for their own harder-to-stage cases.

**Before that: `M1-VIEW-FE-048`** — Context rail, back to the answer, and citation stepping. `0.2.32`.

`web/components/documents/context-rail.tsx` is new: the viewer's right-hand rail, reading the originating turn straight out of `AskProvider`'s in-memory state (`useAsk`) rather than fetching anything of its own — the answer that sent someone here is already live in the app, and re-deriving it server-side would risk disagreeing with what is on the Ask screen. It shows the originating question and the claim it supported (`segmentClaims` against the turn's own answer, the same client-side mirror `M1-CITE-FE-043` already built), a "back to answer" control, next/previous stepping across every citation the answer carries (a new pure `stepCitations` in `lib/citations.ts`, tested independently), plain-text search (`window.find`), copy-passage-with-source-and-page, and ask-scoped-to-this-source. Arriving with no turn in scope — no `turn` query parameter, or one that names a turn no longer live — falls back to plain source context with no broken return control, the ticket's own edge case for "arriving from the library."

**Two things had to change underneath the rail for "back to answer" to actually work.** First, `provenance-margin.tsx`'s card link changed from a plain `<a href>` to `next/link`'s `Link` — a full page load drops `AskProvider`'s in-memory turn entirely, and the rail's whole premise depends on that turn surviving the round trip. Second, `documentHref` gained an optional `origin` (`turnId`, `claimOrdinal`) that adds `turn`/`claim`/`chunk` query parameters, and the Ask screen gained a `ReturnToClaim` subcomponent (behind its own `Suspense` boundary, so the rest of the screen keeps prerendering in full under the static export) that reads them and scrolls to the named `ClaimSpan` via a new `useScrollToClaim` in `leader.tsx` — reusing the leader-line registry that already tracks every claim's DOM node rather than a second lookup.

**"Ask about this source" needed `source_id` scoping threaded through the client for the first time** — `POST /ask` already accepted it server-side (`M1-ASK-BE-037`), but nothing in the browser ever sent one. `AskApi.ask` gained an optional `sourceId`, carried on `AskTurn`, and `fillComposer` gained a module-level pending slot so a scope set immediately before `router.push("/")` is not lost to the same non-synchronous-navigation gap `shell.tsx`'s `⌘K` shortcut already lived with — reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**Resolved issue #141 in the same change**, rather than filing a third ticket for it: `GET /documents/{id}` now reports `superseded_by` and `superseded_at` (the superseding document's own `added_at` — `sources.py`'s `supersede()` already sets both in one transaction, so nothing new needed storing), and `SupersededBanner` renders "this version was replaced on `<date>`, with a link to current" above the document pane for every renderer kind, reusing `GET /documents/{id}` again for the new version's filename rather than growing a join.

**Verified 2026-08-28**: `scripts/dev.sh check` (437 passed, 1 skipped), `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (205 passed, up from 203 — two new backend tests for the superseded/live cases), `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (152 JS tests, up from 131 — 21 new for `documentHref`'s origin params and `stepCitations`), typecheck, lint, production build, token hygiene, contrast and offline scan all clean. On the real stack, rebuilt to `0.2.32`: seeded a live document and a superseded one directly via `psql` (inference still unavailable in this environment, same limitation every ticket since `M0-MODEL-BE-019` has recorded, so no real turn could be produced through the actual ask flow), confirmed `GET /documents/{id}` returns `superseded_by`/`superseded_at` over HTTP with a session, and drove headless Chrome against the built frontend: the superseded banner rendered with the correct date and a working link to the current version, the rail's no-turn fallback showed plain source context with no back-to-answer control and no stepping controls (both edge cases named in the ticket), and "Copy passage" appeared only once a `passage` query parameter was present. The Ask screen's own version line — `scripts/check-version.mjs`'s own check — was confirmed still present in the static export after the `ReturnToClaim`/`Suspense` change, which on a first attempt broke it (the whole screen rendered its Suspense fallback in the prerendered HTML) before being isolated to its own small subtree.

**Not demonstrable here, stated plainly**: the actual "click a citation card mid-answer, step through three passages across two documents, return to the exact claim" walkthrough needs a live turn with real citations, which needs working inference — unavailable in this environment. The stepping and return logic itself is covered by pure-function tests (`stepCitations`, `documentHref`'s origin encoding) and by code review of the one effect that calls `useScrollToClaim`, not by a person watching it happen. Worth a real walkthrough the first time this path matters for real, same note `M1-VIEW-FE-047`'s own session left for the OCR panel it also could not walk cold-start.

**Before that: `M1-VIEW-FE-047`** — Non-PDF renderings and OCR text beside scans. `0.2.31`.

`web/lib/document-format.ts` routes a document's `mime` to one of the source viewer's renderers, mirroring `askwell.extract.run`'s own dispatch server-side rather than inferring the routing from `anchor_kind` (a display label that only exists once extraction has already succeeded). `web/components/documents/converted-text-view.tsx` fetches one `document_pages` anchor by ordinal — the same shape `M1-VIEW-FE-046`'s own unrenderable-PDF fallback already used, generalised here into the primary renderer for Word, PowerPoint, plain text, Markdown and HTML — shows the anchor's `anchor_label` as a heading when there is one, and otherwise states plainly that this document has no headings here rather than pretending a position is a heading. `web/components/documents/spreadsheet-view.tsx` fetches the new `GET /documents/{id}/pages` listing once and hand-windows it (fixed row height, one scroll listener, no virtualisation dependency) so a many-thousand-row sheet stays to a few dozen live DOM rows, scrolling to and highlighting the cited row on load. `document-viewer.tsx`'s existing PDF path gained an OCR-alongside panel for a scanned page (the ticket's own "Image" rendering kind — realised as a scanned PDF page, since no ticket has built a standalone-image extractor yet; `askwell.extract.run` has no branch for one, filed as issue [#185](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/185)), fed by `low_confidence`, computed server-side in `documents.py` against `settings.ocr_confidence_threshold` so the browser never carries a second copy of that cut line. `GET /documents/{id}/pages/{n}` gained `anchor_label`, `ocr_confidence` and `low_confidence` in its response; the existing exact-equality test for that endpoint was updated to match, and five new backend tests cover the anchor label, both sides of the confidence threshold, and the new listing endpoint's ordering.

Two shared pieces (`highlightSpan`, `PageNav`, `UnrenderableFallback`) moved out of `document-viewer.tsx` into `web/components/documents/viewer-shared.tsx` rather than being imported from it directly — the new renderers and the viewer that routes to them would otherwise import each other.

**A real cold-start walkthrough, not just the test suite**: a `.docx` and a `.xlsx` were built and extracted for real inside the running `api` container (`extract_docx.run`, `extract_xlsx.run` against real files, not seeded rows), then opened in headless Chrome against the built frontend. Screenshots confirmed the converted-text view landing on a heading-free document with its passage highlighted and the "no headings" note shown, and the spreadsheet view scrolling to and highlighting row 25 of 51 with its own passage marked mid-scroll. The scanned-page OCR panel was not walked cold-start (building a real image-only PDF that both pdfium and pdf.js agree has no text layer was out of proportion to this ticket's remaining budget); its correctness rests on `api/tests/test_documents.py`'s confidence-threshold tests plus code review of the one new branch (`note === "scanned"` in `document-viewer.tsx`) that calls it — worth a real scanned-corpus walkthrough the first time this path matters for real (`M1-VIEW-FE-048`'s context rail, or the ticket's own testing note's cold-start scenario, would both exercise it).

Issue [#126](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/126) named this ticket as one of two possible re-owners for whether per-page OCR language needs a real column; disposition posted there — not needed, this ticket's own UI-States pointer (`source-viewer.md` §2/§4) never asks for language, only text and confidence, and the product's Tamil position is "never claim support," which needs no language surfaced anywhere. Re-owned further to whichever ticket first builds a Tamil-specific surface; none exists yet.

**Verified 2026-08-28**: `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (203 passed), `scripts/dev.sh typecheck` and `lint` (API, clean), `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (131 web tests, typecheck, lint, build, token hygiene, contrast, offline check — all clean) against the real stack; `podman compose up -d`, real extraction, and headless-Chrome screenshots as above.

**Before that: `M1-CITE-TEST-045`** — The uncited-claim query. `0.2.30`.

`api/src/askwell/agent/citation_check.py` is new: `check_citations(session)` reads every `messages` row with `role = 'assistant'`, re-runs `askwell.agent.claims.segment_claims` against its stored `content` (the same segmentation composition uses, per the ticket's own Assumptions line — this measures citation coverage, not a second disagreeing parser), and checks each claim's ordinal against `citations.claim_ordinal` for that message. A message with no claims (an abstention, a transition) is compliant by definition — `segment_claims` finding nothing is not the same as finding an uncited one. A message with any `fact_usage` row is excluded from the check and counted separately as excluded, stated rather than silently dropped: nothing populates `fact_usage` before `M3`, so a memory-backed claim cannot yet be told apart from an actually-uncited one, and the ticket's own edge case says exclude, not flag. `CitationCheckResult.percentage` is the counter-metric `docs/success-metrics.md` §2 needs (bar: 100%); `.violations` names each offending message with the claim's own quoted text, never just a count, because a segmentation disagreement should be inspectable rather than trusted blind.

**Tests, not an eval suite.** `eval/bench.py` does not exist yet (`M2`) and this ticket did not build it — `api/tests/test_citation_check.py` is four database-backed tests standing in for the "eval corpus" the ticket describes: a fully cited five-answer corpus reports 100%; deleting one citation row directly drops the figure and names that exact answer with its quoted claim (the ticket's own acceptance criterion, verified literally); an abstention counts as compliant; a `fact_usage` row excludes rather than flags. Enforcement today is "this pytest module asserts 100% and fails otherwise," which is real but narrower than "checks results are recorded alongside eval runs" — there is nowhere yet to record alongside, since no eval run exists to be alongside. Filed as [#182](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/182) for `M2` to pick up once `eval/bench.py` exists, rather than building a recording surface against a guessed shape.

**Four states, one new endpoint.** Ask with no sources keeps its existing first-run card (`FirstRun`, unchanged — it already matched `ask.md` §5's "not an empty chat box" bar). Ask with sources present but nothing indexed yet gets a new, explicit `IndexingNotice` rather than either a blank composer or an unrelated suggestion state — `useCorpusState` (`web/components/ask/ask-screen.tsx`) now distinguishes three states instead of two (`"none" | "indexing" | "ready"`) by reading the same `SourceCoverage.askable` flag the library already relies on, no new polling. Ask with sources present and at least one askable gets `SuggestedQuestions`: up to three real questions from `GET /suggestions` (new), clicking one fills the composer via a `CustomEvent` rather than sending — the same "lower the cost of asking, do not ask it for them" rule `conversation.md` §3 states for follow-ups after an answer, applied here before the first one. The library's empty state (`EmptyLibrary`, `library-screen.tsx`) now names all four routes from `add-source.md` §1 — Files and Spreadsheet/CSV live, Database dump and Connect a database marked "(arriving later)" rather than omitted — replacing the placeholder copy `M1-LIB-FE-050` deliberately left there. `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §7's "Conversation history" row, previously blank, is filled: there is no separate history screen before past turns collapse (`M1-CONV-FE-178`, not built), so the Ask screen's own empty state stands in for it.

**No ticket anywhere builds a way to read a `ready` document's filename or heading back out**, which the suggestion state needs — `GET /ingest` only ever carries filenames for documents in a transient or failed state, and there is no `GET /documents` or `GET /chunks`. `api/src/askwell/suggestions.py` is new: `GET /suggestions` runs two bounded, hand-written SQL queries (documents `WHERE status = 'ready'`, then their first chunk each) and a small in-Python heuristic — a chunk heading if the document has one, else its most frequent word outside a short stopword list, else just the filename — computed entirely server-side so the browser gets finished question strings, not raw content to duplicate the heuristic against in TypeScript. No model call, no embedding call, cheap enough to sit behind a screen that might render while the machine is still busy indexing something else. Full reasoning for building this here rather than filing it blocked, and for keeping the heuristic server-side rather than mirroring `M1-CITE-FE-043`'s client-side-segmentation pattern, in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**Before that: `M1-LIB-FE-050`** — Library list with status and needs-attention expansion. `0.2.26`. Issue [#176](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/176).

**The library exists as a real screen for the first time**, not the placeholder that has sat at `/library/` since `M0-SHELL-FE-017`. `web/components/library/library-screen.tsx` renders one row per source — no ticket in the whole backlog groups by anything finer, and collections were deliberately removed, so "grouped by source" means the source *is* the row. Name, kind, added date, status (`web/components/library/status-mark.tsx` — a distinct SVG shape per status, not just a colour, per `docs/ux/design-system.md` §8), and open clarification count (always `0`, stubbed until `M3`). Filters by kind, status and has-open-clarifications are pure client-side predicates (`web/lib/library.ts`'s `matchesFilters`) over the same `IngestState` the add screen's `Progress` component already subscribes to — no second connection, no new polling.

A source with status `attention` expands (`Show detail (N)`) to `web/lib/library.ts`'s `attentionCauses`, which names the specific document and reason per cause — a failed extraction (fixable, with a retry wired to the already-existing `POST /ingest/documents/{id}/retry`) and a poorly-scanned document (read-only information, `M1-EXTRACT-ING-029`'s own distinction preserved) both shown, neither hiding the other. A source indexing right now shows its progress inline (`coverageSentence` plus the live `active` entry for that source, if any) without needing to expand anything.

**No backend ticket for any of this existed anywhere in `docs/backlog/`.** `GET /ingest`'s `sources` array — already built for the add screen's own progress panel — gained `kind`, `added_at`, `last_error`, `open_clarifications` (three real columns not previously selected, one literal stub); its `failures` array gained `source_id` so a needs-attention expansion can attribute a failure to its source. `POST /sources/{id}/reindex` is new: `askwell.ingest.reindex_source` resets every *live* document's job back to `queued` regardless of current state — unlike `retry`, which only touches `failed` rows — because a re-index is asked for because the source changed, not because one document errored. It writes `source_reindex_requested` to the decisions store independently of the `source_status_changed` record `refresh_source` already writes for the resulting transition. Full reasoning for building this here rather than filing it as blocked — and for the "size" column the ticket's own Detailed Description names but `docs/ux/library.md` §6 has already settled belongs in Settings, not the library — in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**Before that: `M1-CITE-FE-044`** — Hover pairing and the narrow-window inline fallback. `0.2.25`.

`web/components/ask/leader.tsx`'s store gained a `hovered` field — the currently hovered or focused claim key or card key, either side. It is the same cross-column registry pattern the `claims`/`cards` maps already exist for (a claim span and its card are DOM siblings, not parent and child), so this is one more field on that store rather than a second one. `useHoverHandlers(key)` returns `onHover`/`onUnhover`; `onUnhover` only clears if `key` is still the hovered one, so moving the pointer directly from a claim onto its own card does not flicker the raised state off between the two. `web/lib/pairing.ts`'s `isRaised(key, hovered, pairs)` is the pure matching rule this all comes down to — "this key is the hovered one, or paired with whatever is" — pulled out so it is checkable without a DOM or a React tree, the same reasoning `segmentClaims` and `applyCitation` already follow for their own pure cores; `web/lib/pairing.test.ts` is 5 new tests against it, including the ticket's own "a claim with two cards — both raise" edge case.

`ClaimSpan` (`ask-screen.tsx`) and `SourceCard` (`provenance-margin.tsx`) both wire `onMouseEnter`/`onFocus` to `onHover` and `onMouseLeave`/`onBlur` to `onUnhover`, and both read `useRaised(key)` to toggle a `data-raised` attribute that `.ask-card-raised`/`.ask-claim-raised` (`globals.css`) style — a card lifts and gains a shadow (the existing `--drop` token, the same look `.ask-navigates:hover` already uses elsewhere, just JS-driven instead of a pseudo-class since a card must also raise when its *claim* is hovered, which plain `:hover` cannot express), a claim gets a `--surface` background. `LeaderCanvas` draws the raised pair's line in `--provenance` at double the stroke width and sorts it last so it sits on top of anything it overlaps — the ticket's "overlapping leaders in a dense answer" edge case. `ClaimSpan` gained `tabIndex={0}`, which is what makes "keyboard focus produces the same pairing" true — cards were already focusable via their own anchor tag.

**The inline fallback did not exist before this ticket** — `shell.tsx`'s margin `<aside>` was already `hidden … @5xl:block`, but nothing rendered in its place below that breakpoint; a narrow window silently lost every card. `provenance-margin.tsx`'s new `InlineSourceCards` renders the live turn's cards a second time, inside `Turn` (`ask-screen.tsx`), wrapped `block @5xl:hidden` — the exact mirror of the margin aside's own classes. `SourceCard` gained a `variant: "margin" | "inline"` prop: `margin` keeps the decorative `--provenance` left edge and calls `useCardRef` to register into the leader-line registry; `inline` uses `--rule-strong` — the token the leader itself uses, since below the breakpoint there is no leader and the edge alone must carry which source belongs to which claim — and never calls `useCardRef`, because both variants are mounted simultaneously (CSS toggles which is visible, React does not conditionally render either), and a hidden inline node registering under the same key would clobber the margin card's real position with a collapsed one. `LeaderCanvas`'s own `<svg>` gained `hidden @5xl:block` for the same reason: below the breakpoint the margin card it would draw to is display:none, and without this the canvas would draw lines from a real claim span to a zero-size point.

**Also fixed, found while implementing:** `docs/ux/design-system.md` §7's claim-leader line still said `--rule` at rest — left over from before `M1-CITE-FE-043` moved the leader to `--rule-strong`; corrected in the same change since it directly contradicted this ticket's own instructions and the already-correct §2/§8 token tables.

**Before that: `M1-CITE-FE-043`** — The provenance margin with source cards and leaders. `0.2.24`.

**This ticket's copy has not been human-reviewed.** The ticket carries a "Human review: copy" flag — new user-facing strings, quoted in full so the PR stops on them rather than the reviewer having to dig through the diff:

- Margin, no turn asked yet (unchanged, pre-existing): *"Sources appear here, beside the claims they support."*
- Margin, a turn completed with no factual claims: *"Nothing in this answer was cited."*
- Card long-passage toggle: *"See full passage"* / *"Show less"*.
- Card meta line: `{filename} · {page or anchor label}` — e.g. `contract.pdf · p. 14`, `notes.pptx · Slide 4`, `ledger.xlsx · Row 12`.

`web/components/ask/provenance-margin.tsx`'s `ProvenanceMargin` renders inside `Shell`'s existing reserved `<aside aria-label="Provenance">` — the region itself already existed and was already unconditional; this ticket only changes what fills it. A card (`SourceCard`) shows the filename and `pageLabel`/`anchorLabel` (`web/lib/citations.ts`) in `--provenance`-accented `ask-micro`, and the passage in serif prose, truncated past 220 characters with a toggle rather than a scrollbar. `applyCitation` groups incoming `citation` events by `chunk_id` rather than appending one card per event — the ticket's own edge case, "two claims citing the same passage, one card, two leaders" — and is idempotent against the server resending an identical frame (the same concern the pre-ticket `citationCount`'s `seen` set existed for; that field is gone, replaced by `AskTurn.citations: CitationCard[]`).

The leader is the harder half. A claim's rendered position (centre column) and its card's position (margin rail) are DOM siblings under `ShellFrame`, not parent/child, so `web/components/ask/leader.tsx` is a small mutable store behind `useSyncExternalStore` (not `useState` on a provider — state that only updates on mount/unmount would miss a claim span moving mid-stream as more tokens arrive) that a `ClaimSpan` (`ask-screen.tsx`'s new `AnswerProse`) and a `SourceCard` each register their own DOM node into, keyed `${turnId}:${ordinal}` and `${turnId}:${chunkId}`. `LeaderCanvas` — one `<svg>`, `position: fixed`, rendered once in `ShellFrame` — draws a `getBoundingClientRect`-measured `--rule-strong` line per pair the live turn's citations describe, recomputed on resize, on a capture-phase `scroll` listener (catches either scrollable column, since inner `scroll` events do not bubble but do fire in capture phase up to `window`), and on a 150ms interval while the turn is still `running` — token-driven reflow moves a claim span without any node mounting or unmounting, which the mount-triggered recompute alone would miss. The interval stops once the turn finishes.

`AnswerProse` wraps every cited claim in the streamed answer using `web/lib/claims.ts`'s `segmentClaims` — a client-side mirror of `askwell.agent.claims.segment_claims`, run against the same growing `turn.answer` text on every render (cheap at answer length, the same "recompute the whole prefix" choice the server itself makes) so the two independently-running implementations agree on which sentence is claim ordinal *n* without either side telling the other. `[index]` markers are stripped from what renders; the sentence and its terminating punctuation are what remain, matching the "instrument, not chatbot" direction rather than leaving raw bracket syntax on screen.

**The `citation` SSE event gained `filename`, `anchor_kind`, `heading` and `passage`** — a join the browser had no other route to (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28, "The card's display data rides the citation event; no new endpoint"). `askwell.retrieve.Candidate` gained `filename`/`anchor_kind`, joined from `documents` in the same dense/lexical queries that already join it. The `citations` table itself is unchanged.

**A second, smaller decision, also logged:** a card's click target is `/documents/{document_id}?page={page_from}` — a route `M1-VIEW-FE-048` has not built yet, so it 404s today. Written down as a guess rather than left for two tickets to invent independently (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28).

**Before that: `M1-CITE-BE-042`** — Claim-level citation extraction into the citations table. `0.2.23`.

`api/src/askwell/agent/claims.py` is new: `segment_claims(text) -> list[Claim]` reads a growing answer as sentences, and a sentence is a claim only if it carries one or more `[index]` markers immediately before its own closing punctuation — exactly the convention `answer_composition.v1.md`'s Citing section now states in full, with placement and multi-index examples. A sentence with no marker (a restatement of the question, a transition) is not a claim at all — never counted, never an "uncited claim" — matching the ticket's own edge case directly. `locate_quoted_span(claim_text, chunk_content)` finds the claim's own words verbatim (case-insensitive) inside the chunk, or returns `None` — the citation still resolves to the chunk, just without a span, per the ticket's edge case for a paraphrase that cannot be located exactly.

`askwell.ask._run_generation` now recomputes `segment_claims(turn.text)` after every streamed token — pure and cheap enough at answer length to re-run from scratch rather than track a cursor, the same reasoning `_tail`'s own replay-from-zero already uses. Each newly completed claim becomes one citation row per index it names (`_cite_claim`), sharing one `claim_ordinal` — the "two passages, one claim" edge case is now two rows, not silently collapsed to one the way the flat `[index]`-anywhere-in-text scan before this ticket did. `citations.quoted_span`, left `null` since `M1-ASK-API-038`, is written for the first time. The `citation` SSE event gained `claim_ordinal` and `quoted_span` so a future card (`M1-CITE-FE-043`) can render per claim as it streams, not only once at the end.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `api/tests/test_claims.py` — 11 new tests, no database, no inference (`segment_claims`/`locate_quoted_span` are pure): a marked sentence is one claim; a claim can cite two passages; an unmarked sentence is not a claim (not just uncounted — absent); ordinal only advances on marked sentences; three marked sentences produce three claims in order; duplicate markers in one claim dedupe; a growing prefix reveals only completed sentences (the property `ask.py`'s per-token recompute depends on); recomputing after more text leaves earlier ordinals stable; a verbatim span is found exactly, case-insensitively; an unmatched span resolves to `None`.
- `api/tests/test_ask_api.py` — 4 new tests against real Postgres (`_seed_chunks` is new, several chunks under one document): the ticket's own headline case, three factual claims producing three citation rows against real chunks; a claim citing two passages producing two citation rows at one `claim_ordinal`; a trailing non-factual sentence producing no extra citation; citations resolving via a real join after the message's own `trace` is wiped, standing in for the ticket's "survive trace rotation" criterion since `askwell.traces.TraceRing` (the actual file-backed ring buffer) is still unwired into `ask.py` — the citations table's independence from any trace store is what the schema (no `trace_id` on `citations`) already guarantees, and this proves it holds under an emptied `trace` column. The existing streaming/durability test's citation-event assertion was updated for the two new fields; its `claim_ordinal`/`chunk_id` DB assertion needed no change.
- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 54 modules, **436 passed, 1 skipped** (unrelated, pre-existing), up from 425.
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **173 passed**, up from 169.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** no real model ran anywhere in this environment, so whether a real model actually follows the new "marker immediately before closing punctuation, no marker on a non-factual sentence" convention — as opposed to the prompt asking it to and the parser expecting it — is unverified; the ticket's own Assumptions line says exactly this is prompt-driven and imperfect, and the eval suite that measures its miss rate is `M2`. `M1-CITE-TEST-045`'s uncited-claim query does not exist yet — nothing yet asks "did any factual claim in this answer go uncited," only "did every marked claim get a citation row," which this ticket's own scope stops short of. Nothing renders any of this (`M1-CITE-FE-043`); the margin still shows `Shell`'s permanent empty state. `askwell.traces.TraceRing` remains unwired into `ask.py` — filed already as a gap by `M1-ASK-API-038`'s own session, not reopened here since wiring it is outside this ticket's stated scope.

**Before that: `M1-ASK-BE-040`** — Generation continues server-side when the user navigates away. `0.2.22`. Issue [#163](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/163), closed.

`M1-ASK-API-038` already made generation a background task independent of the HTTP request; this ticket closed the edge cases that ticket's own scope had not reached. `POST /ask` (`api/src/askwell/ask.py`) now inserts the assistant `messages` row as `running`, content empty, in the same request that starts `_generate` — not once generation finishes, as before. `askwell.ask.reconcile_interrupted` runs once, from `app.py`'s `lifespan`, before the first request is served (skipped if Postgres is not reachable yet, matching the existing "starting before your dependencies is normal" stance): it fails every assistant row still `running`, which can only mean the previous process died mid-turn — there is one worker on one machine, and `_turns` is memory, not a table. `Settings.generation_max_concurrent` (default 2, `ASKWELL_GENERATION_MAX_CONCURRENT`) bounds concurrent retrieve-and-generate work through a module-level `asyncio.Semaphore`, the same reasoning `ingest_concurrency` already applies to ingestion. `GET /ask/counts` gained `abandoned`, counting rows `reconcile_interrupted` failed, kept distinct from an ordinary inference failure. The final `messages` write moved from a plain `INSERT` to `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE`, so every existing test that drives `_generate` directly against a `_Turn` it built itself — every stop/disconnect test `M1-ASK-API-038` wrote — still has somewhere to write.

Full reasoning for writing the pending row up front rather than reconciling on a timer, and for rejecting "absence of a row" as the failure signal, in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 52 modules, 425 passed, 1 skipped (pre-existing, unrelated).
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **169 passed**, up from 161: new/updated tests in `test_ask_api.py` covering the pending row existing before generation runs (driven with `conftest.drive_and_disconnect`), `reconcile_interrupted` failing a stale `running` turn while leaving a genuinely completed one alone, idempotence on a clean database, bounded concurrency (with the limit at one, a second turn's fake assistant is provably untouched until the first releases the semaphore), and the same question asked twice producing two independent completed answers — nothing deduplicates by question text.
- On the running stack, rebuilt to `0.2.22`: a real `running` row inserted directly via `psql`, `podman compose restart api`, and the startup log showed `ask_turns_reconciled count=1`; the row read back `failed`, `interrupted: true`, with a reason naming the restart; `GET /ask/counts` reported `abandoned: 1`; `GET /ask/{id}/stream` against that same id returned `done`/`failed` immediately rather than hanging forever, satisfying the ticket's own validation rule live, not just in a test. A real `POST /ask` against the running stack (no native `llama.cpp` process here, the same limitation every ticket since `M0-MODEL-BE-019` has recorded) confirmed the pending-row-then-failed path end to end for the ordinary case too.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** the ticket's own acceptance criteria include closing the browser tab entirely and reopening to see the same completed answer — that needs the frontend to recover a conversation or message id after a hard reload, and nothing does that yet: `conversation_id` threading across turns is still issue #156, open since `M1-ASK-FE-039`. What this ticket proves is the backend half — a known message id reconnects correctly via `GET /ask/{id}/stream` whether the turn is live, finished, or was orphaned by a restart — not the frontend's ability to rediscover that id after losing all client-side state. No browser is available in this environment either, so nothing above was seen in a real tab.

Type for now." — states the situation and the action, no apology, per `design-system.md` §6. No `getUserMedia`, no permission request, no click handler — the button does nothing. Geometry is fixed now so `M6-VUI-FE-132` (level meter, stop control) changes state, not layout.

No component test infra exists in this repo — `web/package.json`'s `test` script is `node --test` over `lib/*.test.ts` only, no DOM/jsdom, the same gap `M1-ASK-FE-039` hit for the composer's own keyboard handling. Not verified: an actual screen reader, an actual keyboard tab-through, an actual narrowed-window resize — no browser is available in this environment, stated plainly rather than claimed.

**Before that: `M1-ASK-FE-039`** — Ask screen: composer, the live turn, streaming and step labels. `0.2.20`. Issue [#157](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/157), closed.

`web/app/page.tsx` now renders `AskScreen` instead of the static first-run placeholder `M1-ADD-FE-022` left there; the first-run copy moved into `AskScreen` itself and still shows when `fetchIngest()` (`M1-ADD-ING-025`) reports no sources. `web/lib/ask.ts` is new: `parseSseFrame`/`readSseEvents` parse `askwell.ask`'s four SSE event kinds (`step`, `token`, `citation`, `done`) out of a `fetch` response body — `EventSource` cannot be used because `POST /ask` is a `POST`, not a `GET` — and `streamAsk`/`stopAsk` wrap the two endpoints. `web/components/ask/ask-state.tsx`'s `AskProvider` is mounted in `Shell`, above the router, so it survives client-side navigation the way `AddProvider` already does; `nextToDispatch` (`lib/ask.ts`, pure) decides which queued turn streams next — never more than one at a time, satisfying `conversation.md` §5's "queued, not interleaved" without a component test. `⌘K`/`Ctrl+K` reaches the screen from anywhere (`useAskShortcut` in `shell.tsx`).

**Two things intentionally not built, both filed rather than guessed at:**

- **`conversation_id` is not threaded across turns** — `askwell.ask` never returns the id it resolved or created, so every question opens its own conversation server-side. Issue [#156](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/156); does not affect this ticket's own acceptance criteria, which only needs a second question to render as a second turn.
- **A non-Latin-script heuristic** (`looksNonEnglish`), not real language detection, decides when to show the English-only statement — reasoning and its known gap (Latin-script non-English languages pass through unflagged) in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

No browser is available in this environment either, so the manual walkthrough above is `curl` against the real session/SSE contract rather than an actual click-through; the composer's keyboard handling (`Enter`/`Shift+Enter`), the `⌘K` shortcut, and the empty-question no-request behaviour are unverified by a real browser and rest on the code and `web-check`'s static/build analysis alone. A hard page reload loses all turn state; `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28, records why reconnect was not wired in and what would be needed.

**Before that: `M1-ASK-API-038`** — server-sent answer streaming with named retrieval steps. `0.2.19`. Issue [#152](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/152).

`api/src/askwell/ask.py` is new: `POST /ask` resolves or opens a conversation, records the question, starts a background `_generate` task, and returns a `text/event-stream` response that tails it. `GET /ask/{message_id}/stream` reconnects to the same turn — live from memory while it exists, from `messages` once it has left memory (retired past `MAX_FINISHED`, or this process having restarted). `POST /ask/{message_id}/stop` sets a flag `_generate` checks between tokens; the stored answer is written with `trace.stopped_early = true` either way it ends early (stopped or failed). Four SSE event kinds, exactly as scoped: `step` (before retrieval, after it with the source count, before generation), `token`, `citation` (resolved from `[index]` matches against the model's own streamed text, mapped back to the candidate `compose()` gave that index), `done`. `InferenceClient.stream_generate` is new — llama.cpp's own SSE `/completion` response, parsed into `StreamChunk`s, raising the same `InferenceUnavailable`/`InferenceFailed` pair every other method raises, including mid-stream if the process dies after tokens were already sent.

**Generation does not live inside the HTTP request.** `asyncio.create_task` starts it from the `POST /ask` handler and nothing awaits or cancels it there; a dropped connection ends the *response generator* (`_tail`), never the task writing to the `_Turn` it was tailing. This is what makes "client disconnects, generation continues, answer is saved" true without a queue, a worker or a second process — the whole product is one machine, one user, and the event loop already running the request is the only concurrency primitive this needed.

**Reconnect replays a turn's own history in full**, rather than resuming exactly where a dropped connection left off — a deliberate departure from the ticket's stated "does not replay tokens already sent." Full reasoning, and the bug in the literal reading that motivated it, in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `api/tests/test_ask_api.py` — 12 new tests against real Postgres with `InferenceClient` stubbed (matching `test_retrieve_records.py`/`test_rerank.py`'s own reasoning): the full acceptance-criteria exchange in one test (steps before the first token, named steps never generic, a citation event and row resolved to the right chunk, the assistant message/citation/audit rows all durable afterward); a missing conversation id and an empty question are client errors; asking without a session is refused; an unavailable assistant ends the turn `failed` with zero tokens sent; a stop flag (exercised against `_generate` directly — `TestClient` blocks its calling thread until a response is fully collected, leaving no window for a concurrent second request) ends generation and marks the stored answer `stopped`/`stopped_early`; `_load_finished` reads a completed turn back correctly and returns `None` for one that never existed; a browser disconnected mid-answer (driven with raw ASGI, `conftest.drive_and_disconnect`) does not stop the background task, which finishes and saves the full answer; reconnecting to an unknown id is `404`.
- `api/tests/conftest.py::drive_and_disconnect` — the raw-ASGI streaming-test helper issue #110 predicted this ticket would need, extracted from `test_ingest_api.py`'s own inline pattern (that file's copy was left as-is; nothing required migrating it, and AGENTS.md's "edit surgically" argues against touching a file outside this ticket's scope to do it).
- `api/tests/test_inference_client.py` — `stream_generate` against the same real-Unix-socket `Stub` the rest of the module uses: tokens arrive in order as separate chunks; the final chunk's `stop`/`truncated` fields are read correctly; a 4xx mid-stream and a non-JSON `data:` line are both `InferenceFailed`; an unavailable assistant is `InferenceUnavailable` before the first byte, matching every other method.
