The Ask screen does not exist (`M1-ASK-FE-039`, not yet in this milestone's completed list), so nothing renders any of this to a user yet. Abstention is still `M2`: this ticket answers from however many candidates retrieval found, including zero, rather than refusing — correct for its own scope, and the exact gap `M2` exists to close. `Citation.quoted_span` is left null; nothing here extracts the exact quoted text a citation refers to, only which chunk it resolves to.

**Before that: `M1-ASK-BE-037`** — answer composition prompt, versioned, retrieved content delimited. `0.2.18`. Issue [#146](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/146).

`api/src/askwell/agent/prompts/answer_composition.v1.md` is the first prompt file in the repository — no system prompt text lives in application logic, satisfying `AGENTS.md` §4's standing rule directly rather than as an aspiration. `api/src/askwell/agent/compose.py` is a new module, `compose(question, candidates) -> ComposedPrompt`, pure and untouched by anything downstream yet: it wraps every retrieved `Candidate` (`askwell.retrieve.Candidate`) in a `<retrieved-content index="…" chunk_id="…">` block ahead of the question, and separately scans each candidate's content against a small, explicitly-named-as-heuristic set of instruction-like regexes, setting `ComposedPrompt.injection_flagged` on a match. The flag is trace-only — C7 says a document cannot give Askwell orders, so flagging never changes what gets composed or sent; a flagged turn is otherwise identical to an unflagged one, matching the ticket's own "flagged, not blocking" instruction. `ComposedPrompt.prompt_version` carries `answer_composition.v1` on every call, ready for a future writer to attribute a turn to the prompt that answered it.

Full reasoning for why delimitation-plus-statement is the actual defence and pattern flagging is scoped to the trace only (not blocking, not a detector) in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**Verified 2026-08-28.**

- `api/tests/test_compose.py` — 12 new tests, no database, no inference (matches `test_retrieve.py`'s own reasoning: `compose()` is pure): the prompt file exists, is versioned, and is not Python source; both C7 mechanisms — the standing statement and the delimiter tag — are present in the real file, with two tests that construct a stand-in file missing one or the other and assert the missing text is actually absent, so a future accidental deletion from the real file is caught by the same assertion; delimitation holds across twenty long candidates, with delimiter open/close counts checked exactly; an ordinary passage is not flagged; an "ignore all previous instructions … reveal your system prompt" passage is flagged, with the composed system prompt byte-identical to the unflagged case and the injected passage's own text still present verbatim in the user content (data, not obeyed, not stripped) — the ticket's own headline scenario; policy-manual-style instructional prose (the ticket's own edge case) is flagged but composes normally; an empty candidate list composes without error.
- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 52 modules, **425 passed, 1 skipped** (unrelated, pre-existing), up from 414: 11 net new (12 added; `test_c7_fails_if_standing_statement_removed`/`_delimiter_removed` construct their own stripped-down prompt files rather than mutating the real one, so the real file's own two guarantees are never actually violated by the test suite — they assert on the stand-in, which is the point: a test that mutated the real file in place could not tell "the file changed" from "the test broke the file").

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** no `ask` endpoint exists (`M1-ASK-API-038`, next), so nothing calls `compose()` with a real question against real retrieved candidates end to end, and `ComposedPrompt.injection_flagged`/`.prompt_version` are captured but written to nothing — `messages.trace.injection_flagged` (`docs/architecture.md` §7.1) has no writer, matching exactly how `M1-ASK-RET-035`/`036` left their own new `RetrievalResult` fields captured and unread until a consumer existed. The model's actual adherence to the standing statement — whether a real model, given an injected passage, actually refuses to follow it — is not measurable in this environment: no native `llama.cpp` process runs here, the same limitation every ticket since `M0-MODEL-BE-019` has recorded, and this ticket's own tests prove the prompt is built correctly, not that a model obeys it. `_INSTRUCTION_PATTERNS` is a first, small, unbenchmarked list — no eval suite exists yet (`M2`) to measure its miss rate or over-flag rate against real documents.

**Before that: `M1-ASK-RET-036`** — reranking pass over the top candidates. `0.2.17`. Issue [#144](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/144).

`api/src/askwell/retrieve.py` gained a fourth step after fusion: `_rerank` sends the top `Settings.rerank_candidate_count` (new, default 10) fused candidates to `InferenceClient.rerank` — the cross-encoder client `M0-MODEL-BE-019` built and `M1-ASK-RET-035`'s own decision log named as unused — and returns them reordered by the reranker's own score, with the untouched remainder of the fused list appended after, unscored. `Candidate.rerank_score` sits alongside the existing fused/dense/lexical scores, never mixed with them; `RetrievalResult` grew `reranked: bool`, `rerank_duration_ms: float | None` and `rerank_skipped_reason: str | None` so a future trace can say plainly whether the returned order is the reranker's or fusion's. `InferenceUnavailable` and `InferenceFailed` — including a call past `Settings.rerank_timeout_seconds` (new, default 10.0s, deliberately shorter than embedding's timeout so a slow rerank degrades rather than doubling the wait) — are both caught inside `_rerank` and never propagate: `retrieve()` cannot fail because reranking did not work, matching the same "assistant absent is not a failed request" distinction `InferenceClient`'s own docstring establishes.

Full reasoning for the window being bounded separately from `retrieval_candidate_count`, and for degrading in-band rather than raising, in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `api/tests/test_rerank.py` — `_rerank` in isolation, against a real Unix socket stub (no database, matching `test_embed.py`'s own reasoning that mocking httpx tests the mock): reordering happens and both scores are retained; the fused score survives reordering untouched; a candidate count smaller than the window sends exactly that many documents, no padding; candidates beyond the window keep their fused order and carry no rerank score; an absent assistant and a request the stub answers with a 500 both degrade to fusion order with a reason naming which; an empty candidate list skips reranking without dialling the socket at all; three tied scores keep the order they arrived in, proving reranking inherits `InferenceClient.rerank`'s own stable sort rather than losing it on reconstruction.
- `api/tests/test_retrieve_records.py`, against real Postgres, extended: `_FakeClient` grew a `rerank` method (identity order by default, so the ten `M1-ASK-RET-035` tests keep asserting on fused order unchanged) plus two new end-to-end tests — the ticket's own Real-World Example Scenario, five near-identical "payment terms" passages from five different suppliers that all score equally under fusion, with the right supplier's passage promoted to first by a fake reranker scoring it explicitly; and the real `InferenceClient` pointed at a socket nothing is listening on, proving `retrieve()` against a real corpus still returns a result with `reranked = False` and a stated reason rather than raising.
- `scripts/dev.sh check` — 414 passed, 1 skipped (unrelated, pre-existing), up from 407: 7 new in `test_rerank.py`. `.env.example` updated with `ASKWELL_RERANK_CANDIDATE_COUNT` and `ASKWELL_RERANK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` in the same change — `test_env_example.py` failed until it was.
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **152 passed**, up from 150: 2 new in `test_retrieve_records.py`.
- On the running stack, rebuilt to `0.2.17`: a real chunk table of five near-identical supplier passages, queried through the real `retrieve()` against real Postgres with a fake client scoring the Meridian passage highest, returned it first with `reranked=True` and `rerank_score=9.0` — the other four kept their (equal, tied) rerank score of `0.1`. The same query against a real `InferenceClient` whose socket nothing answers (the actual state of this environment — no native `llama.cpp` process, the same limitation every ticket since `M0-MODEL-BE-019` has recorded) logged `rerank_skipped reason=unavailable detail='The assistant is stopped.'` and returned the fusion-ordered candidate with `reranked=False`.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** a live walkthrough with a real reranker model actually scoring passages was not run — no native inference process is available in this environment, the same gap `M1-INDEX-ING-032` and every ticket since `M0-MODEL-BE-019` already recorded; this ticket's own wiring is proved with fakes standing in for a model this environment cannot start, exactly as `M1-ASK-RET-035` proved dense search with a fake embedding vector. Nothing reads `RetrievalResult.reranked`/`.rerank_duration_ms`/`.rerank_skipped_reason` yet — no `ask` endpoint or trace writer exists (`M2`/`M4`), so the fields are captured for a consumer that does not exist yet, the same position `M1-ASK-RET-035` left `RetrievalResult.threshold` in. `rerank_candidate_count`/`rerank_timeout_seconds` are one global default each; the deployment-profile config that would set them per profile (`docs/architecture.md` §6) is not built yet — no field in `Settings` varies by profile today.

**Before that: `M1-ASK-RET-035`** — hybrid retrieval with reciprocal rank fusion. `0.2.16`.

`api/src/askwell/retrieve.py` is a new module, not yet called from anywhere: dense search (pgvector `<=>` cosine distance over `chunks.embedding`) and lexical search (`chunks.content_tsv @@ plainto_tsquery(...)`, with the same hyphen-to-space substitution `c7e2f814a5b3` bakes into the column applied to the query text so a reference number matches) run as two independent, parameterised queries, each capped at `Settings.retrieval_candidate_count` (new, default 40) and excluding `deleted_at IS NOT NULL` and `superseded_by IS NOT NULL` documents at the query itself. `_fuse` — pure, synchronous, no database — merges the two ranked lists with Reciprocal Rank Fusion (`RRF_K = 60`) and returns `Candidate` rows carrying the fused score plus the real dense and lexical scores separately, `None` on whichever side missed. `Settings.retrieval_score_threshold` (new, default 0.65) is attached to `RetrievalResult` as configured at the call, never compared against anything — applying it is `M2`'s abstention decision. `source_id` scopes both searches to one source.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `api/tests/test_retrieve.py` — `_fuse` in isolation, no database: a hit in both lists outranks a hit in only one; a candidate missing from one list keeps a null score for it, not a zero; the fused score is the reciprocal-rank sum across both lists; two chunks with identical content from different documents are both kept, never deduplicated; the fused result truncates to the candidate count.
- `api/tests/test_retrieve_records.py`, against real Postgres, chunks and embeddings inserted directly rather than through the full pipeline: a reference number (`INV-2024-0917`) retrieves the chunk containing it via lexical search alone; a paraphrase sharing no wording with the target retrieves it via dense search alone (cosine similarity > 0.9 against a query vector pointed at the target's own embedding); scores and a configured threshold land on the result exactly as configured; a superseded document and a deleted document are both excluded from a query that would otherwise match them, while the live duplicate-content document is returned; an empty corpus returns `candidates == []` with no error; a single-word query does not error; a corpus of one document still fuses to one candidate with both scores populated; identical content in two different documents' chunks returns both chunk IDs; scoping to a source excludes an identically-worded chunk in another source.
- `scripts/dev.sh check` — 407 passed, 1 skipped (unrelated, pre-existing). `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **150 passed**, up from 141: 10 new tests, split 6/4 across the two new files above (`_fuse` in isolation catches a bug before it ever reaches Postgres; the DB suite proves the SQL either side of it against the real generated column and the real vector operator).

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** nothing calls `retrieve()` — no `ask` endpoint exists, so there is no way to submit a question through the API and see a fused result. The ticket's own out-of-scope list names exactly this: reranking (`M1-ASK-RET-036`, next — `InferenceClient.rerank` is already built and unused), the abstention decision (`M2`), and memory/schema notes in retrieval (`M3`/`M4`). No `ivfflat`/`hnsw` index exists on `chunks.embedding`, so dense search is a sequential scan; fine at this milestone's corpus sizes, worth revisiting once real usage makes it slow — not asked for by this ticket and not added speculatively.

**Before that: `M1-INDEX-BE-034`** — supersede a changed document rather than duplicating it. `0.2.15`. Issue [#140](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/140).

`api/src/askwell/sources.py`'s `add` now recognises a third case beyond duplicate and new document: a file at a path that already holds a *live* document, whose content hash differs. It is offered, not decided for the caller — `Outcome.NEW_VERSION`, nothing recorded — until a second call answers via the new `version_decisions: dict[relative_path, "supersede" | "keep_both"]` on `POST /sources`. `"supersede"` inserts the new document at `version + 1` and sets the old row's `superseded_by` to it, in one transaction, plus a `document_superseded` decisions record naming both; `"keep_both"` inserts it as an ordinary independent document with no link — which the schema already permitted, since `uq_documents_live_source_id_sha256` is keyed on content, not path. The match is on exact `path`, so `clients/contract.pdf` and `archive/contract.pdf` stay unrelated; content-hash duplicate recognition (unchanged) runs first, so identical content under a new path is still a plain duplicate, never a version offer.

Repeated re-adding chains correctly rather than orphaning: the candidate lookup is always `superseded_by IS NULL`, so a second edit of the same path finds the version left live by the first supersession, not the original.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — 401 passed, 1 skipped (unmarked suite; unaffected, this ticket's tests are `requires_db`).
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **141 passed**, up from 136: five new tests in `test_sources_records.py` — offered not duplicated with nothing recorded; accepting sets `superseded_by` and bumps `version` without touching `deleted_at`; declining leaves both live; superseding an already-superseded document chains through the current live tip; a new path with identical content is still a plain duplicate ahead of the version check.
- On the running stack, rebuilt to `0.2.15`, `ASKWELL_ROOTS_MOUNT` set to a temporary directory for the session (SELinux labelling needed `chcon -Rt container_file_t`, the same caveat `AGENTS.md` §5 already names): added `contract.txt` through `POST /sources`, edited it on disk, re-added it with no `version_decisions` and received `new_version` with nothing changed in the database (`documents` still one row); re-added with `{"contract.txt": "supersede"}` and confirmed via `psql` that the old row's `superseded_by` now points at the new row (`version` 2), the old row's `deleted_at` is still null, and `audit_decisions` carries one `document_superseded` record naming both document IDs. `askwell-verify` reports both audit chains intact afterward.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** the ticket's own scope names "retrieval excluding superseded versions" and "the superseded banner for the source viewer" — neither is exercisable, because neither exists in the repository yet. No retrieval component (`M1-ASK-RET-035`/`036`) reads `chunks.embedding` or `content_tsv` for a real query, so there is nothing to filter `superseded_by IS NULL` against; the requirement is recorded in `docs/decisions.md` for that ticket to pick up. No document-detail or citation-resolution endpoint exists either, so the banner data has nowhere to attach — filed as issue [#141](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/141) rather than built against a guessed shape.

**Before that: `M1-INDEX-DB-033`** — full-text column population and index. `0.2.14`.

Turned out to be a bug fix, not new plumbing: `chunks.content_tsv` and its GIN index (`ix_chunks_content_tsv`) already existed and already auto-populated every write, since the v1 schema migration (`a8208099ef38`) made `content_tsv` a `GENERATED ALWAYS AS ... STORED` column — no trigger, no application write, nothing for this ticket to add there. What was wrong, found by testing the ticket's own example against the real running database rather than assuming the schema was already right: Postgres's default text search parser reads a hyphen immediately before a digit run as a *sign*, not a separator, so `to_tsvector('english', 'INV-2024-0917')` produced `'inv':1 '-2024':2 '-0917':3` — the minus sign baked into each numeric lexeme — and a query for just `0917`, the realistic case of someone remembering only part of a reference number, never matched. A new migration, `c7e2f814a5b3`, changes the generated column's expression to `to_tsvector('english', regexp_replace(coalesce(content, ''), '-', ' ', 'g'))` — hyphens become spaces before tokenising, so `INV-2024-0917` now indexes as three independent, signless lexemes, each matchable alone. Full reasoning, including the two alternatives rejected (a custom parser extension; a second normalised column) and the cost accepted (a genuine hyphenated English compound like `well-known` loses its compound lexeme, though both halves still index and match), in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

A generated column's expression cannot be altered in place, so the migration drops and re-adds `content_tsv` and its index rather than editing `a8208099ef38` — which had already run against a live database, making a second migration the only honest option.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 47 modules, **401 tests passed, 1 skipped**. No existing test changed; this ticket is additive only.
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **136 passed**, including seven new tests in `test_index_db_records.py`, all against real Postgres with chunks inserted directly (this ticket is about what Postgres does with a `content` value, not about extraction or chunking): a written chunk gets a populated `content_tsv`; a chunk with `content = NULL` gets an empty, non-null vector (`coalesce`, not a dropped row); `INV-2024-0917` matches both in full and by its own trailing group (`0917`) — the ticket's own headline scenario, failing before the migration and passing after; a chunk of pure numbers and codes tokenises without error; a chunk at the hard maximum length (2400 chars, matching `chunk.py`'s own bound) indexes without error; re-chunking a document (delete, then reinsert, matching `chunk.py`'s own replace-not-update behaviour) leaves exactly one row; and, seeded to 300,000 chunks and `ANALYZE`d, `EXPLAIN` on a lexical query shows `ix_chunks_content_tsv` used, not a sequential scan — 20,000 rows was tried first and the planner reasonably preferred a seq scan at that size, which is itself evidence the test needed to actually reach "corpus scale" rather than assume a small table would do.
- On the running stack: `scripts/dev.sh db upgrade head` applied `c7e2f814a5b3` cleanly; `\d chunks` shows the new expression live (`regexp_replace(COALESCE(content, ''::text), '-'::text, ' '::text, 'g'::text)`) and an existing chunk's `content_tsv` recomputed under it with no manual backfill needed — a `STORED` generated column recomputes on `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` automatically. `db downgrade -1` then `db upgrade head` both ran clean, proving the reverse path works.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** nothing queries `content_tsv` from application code — no lexical search exists yet, `M1-ASK-RET-035` builds that. Whichever ticket does must normalise its query the same way the column normalises content (`regexp_replace(query, '-', ' ', 'g')` before `to_tsquery`/`plainto_tsquery`), or the two sides of `@@` tokenise differently and nothing matches; `test_index_db_records.py::_matches` is the reference for that normalisation, and the decision log entry says so explicitly so it is not rediscovered the hard way.

**Before that: `M1-INDEX-ING-032`** — embedding batches with retry and visible failure. `0.2.13`.

`api/src/askwell/embed.py` is the pipeline's third and final declared stage, installed into `ingest.STAGES` in place of the `run=None` placeholder every prior ticket left there. For each un-embedded chunk of a document (`WHERE embedding IS NULL`, so a retried job never re-sends work already done), it batches content in groups of `ASKWELL_EMBEDDING_BATCH_SIZE` (default 16, a new `Settings` field — a machine-sizing knob like `ingest_concurrency`, not a retrieval-quality constant like `chunk.py`'s sizes) and calls `InferenceClient.embed`. A batch that fails retries up to three times with a short linear backoff (`embed._embed_batch`) before the whole document falls through to the pipeline's existing per-document retry and failure surface — nothing new needed there, `GET /ingest` and `POST /ingest/documents/{id}/retry` already render and act on any stage's failure by name.

Three things this ticket decided, in `docs/decisions.md`:

- **`StageFn` grew a fourth argument, `Settings`**, because `embed` is the first stage that needs configuration (the inference socket, the batch size) rather than only a file and a place to write. `extract.run` and `chunk.run` both take and ignore it. The prior ticket's decision log explicitly deferred this exact widening rather than avoiding it.
- **The embedding dimension is checked once, at worker startup** (`embed.check_dimension`, called from `askwell.worker.startup`), against `chunks.embedding`'s actual deployed width — read via `format_type(atttypid, atttypmod)` rather than assumed. A mismatch is the one case in `startup`'s broad "Postgres may not be up yet" exception handler that is deliberately *not* swallowed: it re-raises and crashes the worker, because a configuration/schema mismatch is a fact that will not fix itself on the next reconcile the way a slow database will.
- **An empty chunk is refused as a second line of defence** (`embed.EmptyChunk`) — `askwell.chunk` already guarantees this cannot happen, so this exists purely so a defect upstream surfaces as a named, retryable failure rather than a citation pointing at nothing.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 47 modules, **401 tests passed, 1 skipped**. New: `test_embed.py` (batching and batch-retry logic against a real stub Unix socket, no database). Updated: `test_ingest.py`'s "extraction and chunking installed, embedding is not" assertion (now all three are installed), `test_ingest_api.py`'s `stages[].built` assertion, and every fake stage function across `test_ingest_records.py` that previously matched `StageFn`'s three-argument shape now takes and ignores a fourth.
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **129 passed**, including a new `test_embed_records.py`: chunks receive embeddings of the configured dimension; a document reaches `ready` only once every chunk is embedded; the configured batch size is actually respected (a multi-chunk document sent in batches of one produces one request per chunk); an inference process that never answers leaves the document unindexed with the failure naming `embed`; three exhausted attempts leave the document `attention` with a working retry that, once inference is up, completes to `ready`; a chunk forced empty by direct SQL (bypassing the chunker) is refused rather than embedded, with nothing else in the document embedded either; the dimension check passes at the real deployed width and refuses a mismatched one; `worker.startup` actually re-raises `EmbeddingDimensionMismatch` rather than swallowing it. `test_extract_office_records.py` and `test_chunk_records.py`, which are about extraction and chunking respectively, now freeze `STAGES` at real `extract` + `chunk` with `embed` explicitly unbuilt (their own `unreachable_queue` fixture), matching what they were already asserting before `embed` existed for real.
- `scripts/dev.sh web-check` — unchanged pass; no frontend work this ticket. `web/lib/ingest.ts`'s `retryDocument` and `web/components/add/add-screen.tsx`'s failure list — both built for extraction failures by earlier tickets — are stage-agnostic and already render and act on an `embed` failure with no changes needed.
- On the running stack, rebuilt to `0.2.13` and restarted: a real `.txt` file added through `POST /sources` with no native inference process running failed at `embed` after three batch-level retries (`embed_batch_retrying` logged each attempt) with `InferenceUnavailable: The assistant is stopped.`, the source flipped to `attention`, and `POST /ingest/documents/{id}/retry` re-queued it — logs show `extract` and `chunk` re-running for real (`extract_text_completed`, `chunk_completed`) followed by `embed` retrying again, confirming the whole pipeline, not just embed, replays on retry. `db upgrade head` applied cleanly; `worker_startup`/`worker_resumed` logged with no dimension-mismatch crash, confirming `check_dimension` passes silently when configuration and schema agree. A full cold-start walkthrough with a real embedding model actually answering was not run — no native `llama.cpp` process is available in this environment (no GPU, no bundled weights), the same limitation every ticket since `M0-MODEL-BE-019` has recorded; every acceptance criterion this ticket owns was proved either against the real pipeline with a stub inference socket (`test-db`) or against the real running stack with real failure/retry behaviour (above) — only "vectors that came from a real embedding model" is unverified, and nothing in this ticket's own scope depends on the vectors' content, only on their presence and dimension.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** a document can now reach `ready` and be structurally complete, but nothing yet *reads* `chunks.embedding` — retrieval, hybrid search and reranking are `M1-ASK-RET-035`/`036`, still unbuilt, so "searchable" here means "has a vector," not "can be asked about." `content_tsv`'s full-text index exists in the schema but nothing populates or queries it yet (`M1-INDEX-DB-033`, next). A changed document re-embeds by re-running the whole pipeline from `extract`; there is no cheaper "just the chunks that changed" path, and none was asked for.

**Before that: `M1-INDEX-ING-031`** — structure-aware chunking. `0.2.12`. Issue [#135](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/135).

`api/src/askwell/chunk.py` is a new pipeline stage, installed into `ingest.STAGES` in place of the `None` placeholder `M1-ADD-ING-025` left there. It reads `document_pages.text` exactly as the extractors already left it — Markdown-style `#` headings, `[TABLE]`/`[/TABLE]` markers with `|`-separated rows (`extract_docx`, `extract_pptx`), `-`/`1.` list items — rather than re-parsing bytes a second time, and writes `chunks.document_id`, `.ordinal`, `.page_from`/`.page_to`, `.heading` and `.content`; `.embedding` stays null for `M1-INDEX-ING-032`. A document now parks at `embed` instead of `chunk`.

Three structural rules, one guarantee:

- **A table never splits from its header.** A table under the hard maximum stays one fragment, header and rows together; a table over it is split by row with the header repeated on every part (`_split_table`).
- **A heading is metadata, not duplicated content.** Encountering a `#` line closes whatever chunk was open and becomes every following chunk's `heading` column until the next heading — never a repeated line burned into `content`.
- **Anchors merge freely except slides.** `page`, `heading` and `sheet_row` anchors merge across pages up to the target size, which is what turns a heading-free PDF into sentence-bounded chunks instead of one per page. `documents.anchor_kind = 'slide'` never merges two slides into one chunk — one slide is one chunk unless it alone needs splitting.
- **The hard maximum is guaranteed by construction.** `_enforce_max` is a final whitespace-boundary split applied to every finished chunk regardless of how it was built, so `No chunk may exceed the hard maximum` holds even against a pathological input (a single sentence with no punctuation for ten thousand characters) none of the structural logic anticipated.

Sizes (`CHUNK_TARGET_CHARS = 1600`, `CHUNK_HARD_MAX_CHARS = 2400`, `CHUNK_OVERLAP_CHARS = 200`) are plain module constants rather than `Settings` fields, deliberately — reasoning in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 46 modules, **396 tests passed, 1 skipped**. Two existing tests updated for a stage that is now real rather than a name: `test_ingest.py::test_extraction_and_chunking_are_installed_and_embedding_is_not` (was asserting only `extract` was installed) and eight `awaiting`/`stage` assertions across `test_ingest_records.py` and `test_ingest_api.py` that hard-coded `chunk` as the next unbuilt stage, now `embed`.
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **120 passed**, including six new end-to-end tests through the real `chunk` stage (`test_chunk_records.py`): a rate table's header and rows land in one chunk together, ordinals are sequential, a heading is recorded on the chunk beneath it, every chunk carries a page range and none is empty, a heading-free `.txt` document still chunks by size, and re-running `chunk` on the same document replaces rather than duplicates its rows. `test_chunk.py` carries 17 pure-logic tests against the parsing, splitting and merging functions directly, including the ticket's own edge cases: a table longer than the maximum split with the header repeated, a nested list kept together, a heading-free run split at sentence boundaries, a single paragraph over the maximum split with overlap, and a slide deck producing one chunk per slide.

The chunk stage is proved end to end against the real, network-joined Postgres the stack itself uses (`test-db`), through the real `ingest.process` dispatcher and real `python-docx`/`openpyxl`/`pptx` documents — not a stand-in.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** nothing is searchable — `embed` is `M1-INDEX-ING-032`, out of this ticket's own scope, and a chunk with no embedding cannot be retrieved by anything built so far. The source viewer showing a retrieved passage (this ticket's own "Real-World Example Scenario") has no screen yet; inspecting chunks today means a `psql` query, which is what the verification above actually did.

**One thing found and filed rather than guessed at:** `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/data-sources.md` both say every chunk carries an "ingestion timestamp"; the `chunks` table has no timestamp column, and the ticket's own Scope bullet ("document, ordinal, page range, heading") matches the schema and omits it. Implemented to the ticket and the schema as they stand; the docs/schema mismatch is issue [#134](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/134), not resolved here.

**Also closed as overdue bookkeeping:** issue [#111](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/111) (`M1-ADD-ING-025`) was still open, waiting on a PR (#115) that merged 2026-08-27. Found while resolving this ticket's own definition-of-done list; closed with the outcome recorded, no code change.

**Before that: `M1-EXTRACT-VAL-030`** — extraction failure states: corrupt, encrypted, password-protected. `0.2.11`. Issue [#131](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/131), closed.

Extraction failure went from one grey `except Exception` catch-all to four named exceptions read where they belong. `extract_common.check_readable(work)` — called once, in `extract.run`'s dispatcher, ahead of every format's own parser — opens the file and turns `FileNotFoundError` into `MissingSource` and any other `OSError` into `UnreadableSource`, both naming the file and its recorded path; this is shared across every extractor because a file deleted between add and extraction is the same fact regardless of which library was about to read it. `extract_pdf.py` adds the PDF-specific split: pdfium's own `PdfiumError.err_code` distinguishes `FPDF_ERR_FORMAT`/`FPDF_ERR_UNKNOWN` (`CorruptDocument`) from `FPDF_ERR_PASSWORD`/`FPDF_ERR_SECURITY` (`PasswordProtected` if no password was supplied for this attempt, `WrongPassword` if one was and it did not work).

A password-protected PDF now has a way forward instead of a dead end. `POST /ingest/documents/{id}/password` resets a failed job to `queued` — reusing `retry`'s own reset, attempts forgiven — and dispatches it with a password. `Work` grew a fifth field, `password: str | None = None`, applied by `ingest.process` via `dataclasses.replace` after `_claim`, never read from or written to a database row: the password rides the `arq` job payload alongside `document_id`, the same transport every other job argument already uses, and is never named as a value to `structlog` (only *whether one was supplied* crosses into a log line, in `_classify_open_failure`). Full reasoning, including why a database row was considered and rejected, in `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 44 modules, **380 tests passed, 1 skipped** (the skip is a permission test that needs a non-root user; this container runs as root, matching the documented SELinux/root-bits caveat in `AGENTS.md` §5).
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **112 passed**, including three new end-to-end tests through the real pipeline: a file deleted after `add()` but before extraction reads as `MissingSource`, not `CorruptDocument`; a real truncated PDF (a valid `%PDF-1.7` signature, garbage after it) is classified `CorruptDocument` by real pdfium, not mocked; and a password-protected PDF (pdfium's own `PdfDocument` constructor faked at the one call it makes the password decision on, everything else — page count, text — read by real pdfium against a real document) walks through no-password → prompted, wrong password → reported wrong and left retryable, right password → completes extraction and parks awaiting `chunk`.
- On the running stack, with `ASKWELL_ROOTS_MOUNT` set to a temporary directory for this session and reverted afterward: a real corrupt PDF and a file deleted immediately after being added were both read through `POST /sources` and `GET /ingest`, confirming `CorruptDocument: broken.pdf could not be read — its contents look corrupted.` and `MissingSource: vanishing.pdf is no longer at <path>. It may have been moved or deleted since it was added.` appear verbatim in `failures[].error`, each naming its file, after three real retries. The password endpoint's 404 (unknown document), 409 (document not failed) and 422 (empty password) responses were also exercised live via `curl`. A real password-protected PDF was not built live — no encryption library is available in this image and adding one is out of this ticket's scope — so that path is proved by the `test-db` case above, which exercises the real pipeline around a faked decision at the one point only pdfium can make it.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** the "remember this password" opt-in the ticket's own assumption defers to `M4` does not exist — there is nowhere to click to keep a password, on purpose, matching "storage is not offered" rather than "offered but broken". No screen renders the password prompt yet; `web/` has no UI for this endpoint, which is expected — `docs/ux/add-source.md` §5 describes the surface and building it is frontend work not scoped here. Legacy binary Office (`.doc`/`.xls`/`.ppt`) still fails with `UnsupportedForExtraction`'s own message rather than one of this ticket's four kinds — unchanged, out of scope, issue #121.

**Before that: `M1-EXTRACT-ING-029`** — flag low-confidence OCR and surface it as needs attention. `0.2.10`. Issue [#128](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/128).

Every OCR'd page now carries a confidence figure — Tesseract's own mean word confidence, from a second `image_to_data` pass alongside the existing `image_to_string` recognition (`api/src/askwell/extract_ocr.py`). `extract_pdf.run` stores it two places: `document_pages.ocr_confidence` (new column, this ticket's migration) per page, and `documents.ocr_confidence` (already existed, unread until now) as their mean. A text-layer page or document carries neither — confidence is a fact about a page Tesseract was actually asked to read, and a document that never used OCR has nothing to be false about.

Below `ASKWELL_OCR_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` (default `0.60`, `api/src/askwell/config.py`), a document is flagged. Flagging is never a failure and never touches `documents.status` — the document is exactly as indexed as any other. What changes is the *source*: `askwell.ingest.source_status` now takes a `flagged` count alongside `failed`, and returns `attention` the moment one exists, independent of `outstanding` — unlike a failure, a flag never retries and never waits for the rest of an import to finish. `sources.last_error` names both causes when both are true ("2 of 60 files could not be indexed. 1 file scanned poorly and may be hard to search."), and the `/ingest` snapshot grew a `flagged` list — mirroring the existing `failures` list — naming the specific pages that read worst per document, plus a local `documents_flagged` counter (C1: nothing transmitted). `web/lib/ingest.ts` and `add-screen.tsx` render it next to the existing failure list, with no retry control — there is nothing to click yet, only something to read (the re-scan clarification is `M3`, explicitly out of scope).

One decision worth reading in full in `docs/decisions.md`: the flagged count is deliberately **not** gated on `documents.status = 'ready'`, unlike every other `Coverage` figure. `chunk` and `embed` are not built yet, so today every document parks at `queued` once `extract` finishes — none ever reaches `ready`. Gating the flag the same way `ready`/`failed` are gated would have shipped something inert until two tickets this one does not depend on land.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 44 modules, **370 tests passed** (8 new pure-logic tests for `source_status`'s `flagged` behaviour and `_attention_reason`).
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **109 passed**, including: a clean scan reading with a real Tesseract-measured confidence and no flag; a text-layer document carrying no confidence at all; a flagged document reaching `ready` with its source `attention` and a readable `last_error`; and — the case the "not gated on `ready`" decision exists for — a flagged document recognised while still `queued`, parked awaiting `chunk`.
- `scripts/dev.sh web-check` — typecheck, lint, **52 JS tests passed** (4 new for `flaggedSentence`), production build, contrast and offline-bundle checks all clean.
- `scripts/dev.sh db upgrade head` applied the `page_ocr_confidence` migration cleanly against the running stack's Postgres. `curl` against a live `/ingest` (session cookie established via a page request) confirmed the new `documents_flagged`, `flagged` and per-source `flagged` fields are present and shaped as expected; a live cold-start walkthrough with a real poor scan through a nominated folder was not run this session — `ASKWELL_ROOTS_MOUNT` is unset in this environment, so no folder can be nominated here at all. The OCR-confidence-to-flag path itself is proved end to end against the same real Tesseract binary and real Postgres the running stack uses, via `test-db`.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** issue #126 (Tamil-script OCR path untested) is untouched by this ticket — it names `M1-EXTRACT-ING-029` only as one place `document_pages` could grow a language column if a later screen needs to show OCR language, and this ticket's own scope is confidence, not language; re-owning that decision to whichever ticket first needs to show OCR language in the UI, not decided here. Chunking, embedding and the source viewer's side-by-side rendering remain unbuilt, so nothing is searchable and no screen shows the scanned image beside the text — unchanged by this ticket, and explicitly out of its scope.

**Before that: `M1-EXTRACT-ING-028`** — OCR fallback with orientation detection. `0.2.9`. Issue [#125](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/125).

A page whose text layer fails `extract_pdf`'s usability check is now rendered to an image, oriented and recognised by a new module, `api/src/askwell/extract_ocr.py` — Tesseract via `pytesseract`, both Apache-2.0, the binary bundled in `api/Dockerfile` (`tesseract-ocr`, `tesseract-ocr-eng`, `tesseract-ocr-osd`, `tesseract-ocr-tam`; package names verified against packages.debian.org/bookworm). This runs inline, per page, inside `extract_pdf.run`'s existing loop — not as a separate wait for a fourth pipeline stage.

Two things this ticket decided, both in `docs/decisions.md`:

- **OCR moved from "a stage a document parks awaiting" to "something `extract` just does".** `M1-EXTRACT-ING-026` built `NeedsOCR` and `ingest.OCR_STAGE` because nothing existed yet to hand a textless PDF to; now something does, so parking forever naming a ticket that has landed would itself be dishonest. Both are removed. The ticket's own edge case — "a mixed document: OCR runs only on the pages that need it" — also could not have been satisfied by the old whole-document-only `NeedsOCR` trigger, so this is not just tidying, it is what made the edge case possible to satisfy at all.
- **A document that gets no text from OCR either is `EmptyDocument`, the same C5 failure every other extractor reports**, not a park with nowhere left to go. Before this ticket, "nobody has read it yet" was the honest excuse for parking; OCR has now been given every page a fair try, so an all-blank or all-photograph scan is reported the same way a `.docx` with nothing extractable is.

Orientation and script are detected with one call, `pytesseract.image_to_osd` (`--psm 0`), before recognition — the ticket's own title. Script decides the language pack: `Tamil` reads via the bundled `tam` traineddata (the v1 hedge, `AGENTS.md` §1) and is logged `supported=False`; every other script still reads as `eng`, matching v1's English-only scope. A page OSD cannot score confidently (a blank page, a photograph) reads at zero rotation as English rather than failing the page — `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §3's "photograph with no text" row.

**Issue #118 resolved as obsolete, not implemented.** It reported the add-source progress sentence naming `chunk` instead of `ocr` for a document parked `awaiting: ocr`. Since this ticket removes the `awaiting: ocr` state entirely — every PDF now parks at `chunk` once `extract` finishes, scanned or not — the specific condition the issue described can no longer occur. The underlying frontend gap it found — `queueSentence` never reads `state.awaiting` — is still real for the next stage that parks separately, so it is re-filed as a fresh, general issue: [#124](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/124).

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 44 modules, **362 tests passed**.
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **105 passed**, including 4 new end-to-end OCR tests built against real JPEG-embedded PDFs (no text layer at all, upside-down, mixed text-layer-plus-scan, and the all-blank-even-after-OCR failure path) and the existing rotated-text-layer and mixed-blank-page tests carried forward unchanged.
- Ran the OCR path directly against the real Tesseract binary inside the built API image (rendering, orientation detection on a page rotated 180°, and recognition), confirming the exact text came back correctly oriented, before wiring it into the pipeline. `scripts/dev.sh db upgrade head` applied the `ocr_derived` migration cleanly against the running stack's Postgres.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** no screen shows the scanned image beside the text — `documents.ocr_derived` is written but nothing in `web/` reads it yet, matching how `ocr_confidence` already sits unread; that is `M1-EXTRACT-ING-029`'s and the source viewer's job. Low-confidence flagging is explicitly out of this ticket's scope (`M1-EXTRACT-ING-029`). Chunking and embedding remain unbuilt, so nothing is searchable — unchanged by this ticket.

**Before that: `M1-EXTRACT-ING-027`** — Word, PowerPoint, spreadsheet, text, Markdown and HTML extraction. `0.2.8`. Issue [#120](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/120).

`extract` stopped being one PDF-only function wired straight into `askwell.ingest.STAGES` and became a dispatcher (`api/src/askwell/extract.py`) choosing among six format-specific modules by `Work.mime` — the value `askwell.filetypes.detect` already wrote at add time. Six formats, one shared write path (`api/src/askwell/extract_common.py`): every extractor produces a list of `Anchor` (an ordinal, an optional human-facing label, text, and whether it has any) and hands it to `write_anchors`, which is the only place `document_pages` gets written now, PDF included in spirit though `extract_pdf.py` itself was touched by exactly one line.

Four things this ticket decided, all in `docs/decisions.md`:

- **`document_pages` grew two nullable columns rather than a table per format.** `documents.anchor_kind` (`page` / `slide` / `sheet_row` / `heading`) says once per document what the existing `page_number` ordinal means; `document_pages.anchor_label` carries the pointer a bare ordinal cannot — "Sheet1, row 45", a heading's own text. `extract_pdf.py` now sets `anchor_kind = 'page'` too, so every document format has one recorded, not just the six this ticket added.
- **Word's page anchors are honest about being approximate.** Nothing renders a `.docx`, so there is no true page break — only the ones an author inserted explicitly. A document with none of those is one page; the label says "approximate" rather than claiming a page number Word itself would only produce by opening the file.
- **Legacy binary Office (`.doc`, `.xls`, `.ppt`) is a named, retryable failure, not a crash or a silent skip.** `askwell.filetypes` already routed these to `files` as `supported` before this ticket existed to read them, and none of `python-docx`, `python-pptx` or `openpyxl` opens the pre-2007 binary container. `askwell.extract.UnsupportedForExtraction` gives a person a reason to read rather than an `AttributeError`. Filed as a follow-up: issue [#121](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/121) — not attempted, on purpose, until it proves to matter for a real archive.
- **A `<title>` tag is not a page's content**, found by running a real saved HTML page through the pipeline rather than designed for up front: `_html_to_headed_text` was reading the whole document, and a page's `<title>` was landing as an unlabelled first section ahead of the real one. Fixed by reading `<body>` alone; a regression test guards it in both `test_extract_text.py` and the end-to-end HTML test.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 41 modules, **362 tests passed** (25 new pure-logic tests: heading/front-matter/chrome-stripping logic for text/Markdown/HTML, and Word/PowerPoint/Excel helper functions against in-memory documents built with the real libraries).
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **102 passed**, including 9 new end-to-end tests through the real `extract` dispatcher: one per new format, plus the empty-document failure path and the legacy-`.doc` failure path.
- On the running stack: one `.docx`, one `.pptx`, one `.xlsx`, one `.md`, one `.html` and one `.txt` added through `POST /sources`, all reaching `parked` awaiting `chunk` with zero failures. `psql` confirmed `anchor_kind` per format (`page` / `slide` / `sheet_row` / `heading` / `heading` / `heading`) and `document_pages` content matching the source: the Word file's heading and table markers intact, the deck's speaker notes present and labelled, the workbook's rows anchored `"Budget, row 1"` / `"Budget, row 2"`, the Markdown file's front matter absent from its text, and — after the `<title>` fix — the HTML file's extracted text holding only "Terms" and its paragraph, no "Home", no "Contract".

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** nothing is chunked or searchable — `chunk` and `embed` are `M1-INDEX-ING-031` and `M1-INDEX-ING-032`, out of this ticket's own scope. Multi-sheet and merged-cell spreadsheets are read crudely (every sheet, every row; a merged range's value survives only in its top-left cell) exactly as the ticket allows, with the real fix — asking rather than guessing — deferred to `docs/data-sources.md` §8 and out of scope for M1. Legacy binary Office is issue #121, not attempted.

**Before that: `M1-EXTRACT-ING-026`** — a text-layer PDF is actually read. `0.2.7`. Issue [#117](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/117), closed.

The ingestion pipeline's first real stage. `extract_pdf.py` reads a PDF page by page with `pypdfium2` (BSD-3/Apache-2.0, chosen over the AGPL PyMuPDF — `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-27) and writes one `document_pages` row per page, whether or not the page has text, plus a real `documents.page_count`. `askwell.ingest.STAGES` installs `extract` for real; a document with a text layer proceeds and parks at `chunk` (`M1-INDEX-ING-031`, still unbuilt), and a document with no usable text layer anywhere parks naming `M1-EXTRACT-ING-028` instead, via a `NeedsOCR` exception rather than a fourth pipeline stage.

Three things this ticket decided, all in `docs/decisions.md`:

- **A stage now gets a session factory of its own.** `StageFn` grew a third argument because `extract` is the first stage that actually writes to Postgres — the pipeline built by `M1-ADD-ING-025` gave a stage a file and a progress callback and nothing to write with.
- **OCR is inside `extract`'s scope, not a fourth `STAGES` entry.** A whole-document OCR need raises `NeedsOCR`, caught next to the branch that parks on a missing `Stage.run`, so both routes to `parked` share one mechanism. A *partly* usable document (some pages have text, some don't) is not this case — it proceeds normally with its blank pages recorded, per the ticket's own edge case.
- **#109 is actually fixed now, not just described as fixed.** That issue's prior closing comment (on `M1-ADD-ING-025`) said `resume()` revives a `parked` document once its stage lands; the code never did that. Found while building this ticket, by reading `resume()` rather than trusting the comment. `resume()` now really does return a `parked` job to `queued` when its `awaiting` stage has a `run` installed — reopened, fixed, tested, closed again with real evidence.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack and against a real database.**

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint, format, `mypy --strict` over 35 modules, **337 tests passed**.
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **94 passed**, including the two new #109 tests and coverage for a mixed text/blank-page document, a document with no usable text anywhere, and a rotated page.
- On the running stack: a real 3-page PDF added through `POST /sources` extracted for real — `documents.page_count = 3` matching the file, three `document_pages` rows each holding the right page's text, worker log `extract_pdf_completed pages=3 pages_with_text=3` then `ingest_parked awaiting=chunk`. A second file with no text on any page parked `awaiting=ocr` instead, confirming the OCR-routing path outside of tests too.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** nothing is chunked or searchable — `chunk` and `embed` are `M1-INDEX-ING-031` and `M1-INDEX-ING-032`, this ticket's own out-of-scope list. Corrupt and password-protected PDFs fail with pdfium's raw error text rather than the classified, retryable messages `M1-EXTRACT-VAL-030` adds later. Multi-column reading order is best-effort, stated in the module docstring rather than solved.

**Before that: `M1-ADD-ING-025`** — indexing stops belonging to the page you are looking at. `0.2.6`. Issue [#111](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/111).

Recording a drop now writes a queue row per document in the same transaction as the document, and a worker drains it. The add request ends; the work does not.

Four things this ticket decided rather than assumed, two of them in `decisions.md`:

- **Postgres is the queue's record; Redis is only its transport.** `arq` wakes a worker and nothing more. A job that exists only in Redis is lost by `podman compose down -v`, by a worker killed mid-job, by a laptop suspended for two days and by an enqueue that failed after the documents were committed — and none of those produces an error anywhere. `resume()` at worker startup returns interrupted jobs; a reconcile cron every thirty seconds repairs the rest. A failed dispatch is a delay, never a lost import, and never a failed request.
- **A stage that has not been built is `parked`, not finished and not failed.** The pipeline declares `extract`, `chunk`, `embed` with their tickets and installs none. A job runs, reaches extraction, finds nothing there and stops, saying which stage and which ticket. `ready` would tell retrieval a document has passages it has not got; `failed` would turn a fresh install's whole library red for no reason. Parked is what `states-and-edge-cases.md` §3 already asked for.
- **A source's status is derived from its documents, not from a job being claimed.** The first version read `indexing` off a claimed job, and with no stage installed a job is claimed and parked inside a millisecond — so three files flicked the source `queued` → `indexing` → `queued` and wrote six decisions records about work that never happened. Caught by running it, not by a test; there is a test now.
- **Askability is a coverage figure, not a status.** A source with fifty-eight of sixty files readable and two failed is `attention` — that is the only place the library has to offer the retry — *and* askable, because fifty-eight papers' worth of answers should not wait for two corrupt ones.

**Verified on the stack**, not just in tests: three files added with the worker stopped, `GET /ingest` reporting them queued at positions 1, 2, 3 with the honest "no estimate yet" basis; one job forced to `running` and Redis then flushed entirely; the worker started, logging `ingest_resumed_interrupted documents=1` and `worker_resumed dispatched=3 interrupted=1`, and all three completing. `curl -N /ingest/stream` held open across a fourth file being added received two events — the snapshot and the change — and nothing in between. The migration's backfill was observed picking up a document recorded by `M1-ADD-BE-023` before the queue existed.

**What is not demonstrable yet, stated plainly:** no document reaches `ready`, because nothing extracts. Progress advancing per file, progress inside a file, failure with a retry, and partial coverage are proved by tests that install a stage of their own; a walkthrough today ends at "recorded and waiting" with the missing stage named. That is the ticket working as scoped, not a gap in it — but it is why the cold-start walkthrough in the ticket cannot be run end to end until `M1-EXTRACT-ING-026`.

**`M1-ADD-BE-023`** — Askwell remembers what it was given, and notices when it has been given it before. `0.2.5`.

A queued batch stopped being a list in a browser tab. `POST /sources` creates one source for the folder and one document per file — path, filename, media type, SHA-256, arrival time — and the add screen's *Queued* now describes rows rather than an intention.

Four things this ticket decided rather than assumed:

- **Recognition is global; the index is per source.** The user's problem is the same contract in three folders, and three folders are three sources — so `add()` looks the hash up across every live document. The partial unique index underneath is narrower on purpose — `uq_documents_live_source_id_sha256`, which the v1 migration already created and which no model declared, so an autogenerate run would have proposed dropping it. The application check produces the sentence; the index makes a second live row impossible when a later code path forgets to ask. Reasoning in `decisions.md`, 2026-08-28.
- **`queued` is a status, added to both vocabularies and made the default.** None of `indexing`, `ready`, `attention` or `deleted` was true of a row this ticket creates, and storing `indexing` is what a progress bar that never moves gets rendered from.
- **The server re-detects.** `api/src/askwell/filetypes.py` is a deliberate second copy of the browser's signature table, reading the file itself. Nothing the client says about a type reaches a row. One divergence is deliberate and recorded: the server will not take a `.md`/`.txt`/`.html` file away from the files route on a comma or a `CREATE` keyword. The client still will, which is an open item below.
- **The hash is over content and nothing else** — never the name, never the mtime. The file's identity (device, inode, size, mtime) is checked before and after the read, and a file that keeps changing is re-read up to three times and then reported rather than recorded with a hash of bytes nobody will read again.

Two requirements carried forward from earlier tickets are now met: **adding is a decisions record** (one for the source, one per document, each naming the path) and **a refusal reaches the operational log**. A duplicate and a refusal are *not* decisions records — nothing changed.

**Verified 2026-08-28, on the stack.** Everything owed by the session that wrote this has now been run, and it all passes.

- `scripts/dev.sh check` — lint clean, 62 files formatted, `mypy --strict` clean over 30 source modules, **314 tests passed**.
- `scripts/dev.sh test-db` — **67 tests passed**, the first time any of them has been executed. The `c3d9e1a45b76` migration (the `queued` status) had never been applied to a database and applied cleanly.
- `scripts/dev.sh web-check` and `web-build` — pass, including the contrast audit and the offline-hosts check.
- `podman compose exec api askwell-verify` — `audit_decisions: 4 records, chain intact.`

**The cold-start walkthrough, end to end.** The running container turned out to be a stale image — it carried a `documents.py` that does not exist in this tree and answered `405` to `POST /sources` — so `build-api` and `compose up -d api worker` came first. That is worth remembering: a green test run says nothing about what the stack is actually serving.

One request with three files in `contracts/` — `contract.pdf`, a byte-identical `duplicate-in-batch.pdf`, and a zero-byte `empty.pdf` — returned `added=1 duplicates=1 refused=1`, the duplicate naming `contract.pdf` as where those contents already are, and the empty file refused with *"There is nothing in this file to index. Nothing was changed on disk."* A second request adding the identical file under a different name from a different folder (`backup/contract copy.pdf`) returned `duplicates=1` and **`source: null`** — no second source row was created — naming both paths. The database holds **one** document, `status = queued`, with its path and hash; `audit_decisions` holds one `source_added` and one `document_added`, each naming the path, and nothing for the duplicate or the refusal. Both indexes are present, including `uq_documents_live_source_id_sha256`. Every acceptance criterion in the ticket is met against a real database.

**The walkthrough also settled a risk that had been open since `M1-ADD-ING-021`: SELinux does refuse the roots mount.** The first add refused all three files, and the reason it gave was the right one. `podman exec askwell-api-1 ls -ln` on the mounted folder returns `-????????? ? ? ? ?` for every entry; one `chcon -R -t container_file_t` made them readable and the walkthrough then passed. So on the maintainer's own distribution Askwell reads zero files until a folder is relabelled, and every ingestion ticket after this one was standing on that. [#107](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/107), reasoning in `decisions.md`, 2026-08-28. `--security-opt label=disable` was considered and rejected — it removes confinement from the two containers holding the whole corpus to fix a problem scoped to one directory.

Three items were filed now that `gh` is reachable: [#105](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/105) (hashing a whole drop inside one request), [#106](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/106) (a file under two nominated folders appears under one source), [#107](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/107) (SELinux). The rest of **Open** below is still unfiled.

**No version bump.** This session ran commands, filed issues and wrote documents; no application behaviour changed (`AGENTS.md` §7).

**Before that: `M1-ADD-VAL-024`** — an unsupported file is refused by name, and a CSV is told when its turn comes. `0.2.4`.

Detection answered two ways and needed to answer three. `supported: boolean` became `verdict: "supported" | "later" | "refused"`, with the milestone carried alongside — so a dropped CSV is recognised, named, dated **M4 and not queued**, where the previous screen queued it and counted it as added while a panel three centimetres below said its route arrives in M4. The verdict is derived from `ROUTES[].arrives`, not hardcoded: when M4 lands, `arrives: null` on two routes flips detection and the panel together.

Three other things this ticket changed:

- **Markdown and HTML are read.** `docs/data-sources.md` §1 has listed both since it was written and detection had neither. HTML is judged on its opening and checked *before* the delimiter test — a saved page is full of rows and was being routed to the table route, which under the change above would have told someone their web page arrives in M4. Markdown is named from the extension, the one case where the name is better evidence than the bytes.
- **A refusal names the file, the detected type and the way out**, per file, with the supported list once beneath the block rather than after each of five. One archive among sixty contracts refuses the archive and queues the contracts.
- **A drop that expands to nothing says so.** An empty folder is a gesture that deserves an answer; a cancelled file dialog produces the same empty list and stays silent, and the folder count is what tells them apart.

Two new terminal phases, `later` and `empty`, exist so that neither is rendered as *Refused*. Rejections are counted locally in `localStorage` beside the added counter — no wire, none being built (C1). Reasoning in `decisions.md`, 2026-08-27.

**Not verified in this session.** The environment refused `node`, `podman`, `scripts/dev.sh` and `gh` alike. Owed: `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (nine new assertions in `web/lib/add-source.test.ts` have never been executed), `scripts/dev.sh web-build`, the cold-start walkthrough — drop a zip and a PDF together, confirm the zip is named and refused and the PDF proceeds; rename a zip to `.pdf` and confirm it is still refused on content — and the issue, which could not be created.

**Before that: `M1-ADD-FE-022`** — material can be handed to Askwell. `0.2.3`.

The add-source screen at `/sources/add/`, its files route, and a drop target that is the **whole window** rather than that screen. A folder of contracts dropped onto Ask is taken and the flow follows it; the queue lives in the shell, above the router, because a queue owned by the add screen would be created by the navigation the drop causes and would therefore always be empty.

Three things this ticket decided rather than assumed:

- **A file is what its contents say it is.** The first 4 KB decide; the extension is consulted only to tell the four zipped Office formats apart (they share their first bytes) and to report the disagreement in the user's own words — *named `.pdf`, contents are a PNG image*. A program is refused **by name**, with the sentence that nothing was run.
- **The ticket's stated assumption is false.** "The browser's drop event gives usable paths under every supported platform" — no browser gives them, on any platform, and no flag changes that. So a drop is expanded and counted first, then asked *one* question for the whole drop: which folder these came from, typed. A root is a permission over a tree, so one `GET /roots/covering` settles all of them. `M7-TAURI-FE-182` deletes the question; `web/lib/selection.ts` is the seam it replaces.
- **The estimate is a count and a size, not a duration.** Nothing here has measured CPU embedding throughput, and a fabricated number printed beside a real count reads as measured. The duration arrives with `M1-ADD-ING-025`, which is the first thing that can observe it.

A second drop while one is being read is **queued, never rejected**; detection runs one batch at a time in chunks of 25 that hand the window back. Reasoning in `decisions.md`, 2026-08-27.

**Where it stops:** a batch ends at `queued`. Nothing is extracted, embedded or searchable — records are `M1-ADD-BE-023`, background ingestion is `M1-ADD-ING-025` — and the screen says exactly that rather than showing progress that will never move.

**Not verified in this session.** No command could be run: the environment refused `node`, `podman` and `scripts/dev.sh` alike. Owed before this can be called done: `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (which now runs `pnpm test` — the new step, `node --test` over `web/lib/add-source.test.ts`, has never been executed), `scripts/dev.sh web-build` (**`web/out/` is stale, so the API is still serving an interface with no `/sources/add/` route**), the cold-start walkthrough in the ticket, and the closing issue comment.

**Before that: `M1-ADD-ING-021`** — Askwell can be told which folders it may read. `0.2.2`.

Indexing in place means Askwell reads the user's own directories, so it needs a route to them that is narrow and explicit. That route is now two things that must not be confused:

- **The registry** — `roots`, its own table, tombstoned on removal. It is the *permission*: `askwell.roots.covering()` is the check, and a path no row covers is never read. It resolves symlinks, because one link inside a nominated folder would otherwise stand in for the whole disk.
- **The mount** — `ASKWELL_ROOTS_MOUNT`, one host directory bind-mounted read-only into the API and worker **at the same absolute path it has on the host**. It is the *route*. Identity mounting is deliberate: `sources.root_path` holds host paths, and any other mount point would need host↔container translation everywhere a path is stored, shown or reopened.

Mount state is **probed on every read and never stored**. A stored value reports an unplugged USB drive as available an hour later, and nothing would ever correct it.

Four unreadable states, kept apart because they have four different fixes: `not_mounted` (a line in `.env` and bring the stack up again), `unavailable` (a drive or share not connected — **never** rendered as deleted or as moved), `unreadable` (permissions, or SELinux on the mount), `available`.

A folder outside the window is **registered anyway**, reporting what to set and that the stack must come up again. Everything else unreadable is refused at registration, because it is true now and will not fix itself; a missing mount is not.

Reachable at `GET /roots`, `POST /roots`, `GET /roots/covering`, `GET /roots/{id}/removal`, `DELETE /roots/{id}`, and in the interface under Settings → Folders Askwell may read. Registering and removing are decisions records. Full reasoning in `decisions.md`, 2026-08-27; the flow is specified in `ux/add-source.md` §7, which is new — no screen covered path registration before.

**Not verified in this session.** The change was written but the toolchain could not be run: `podman`, `scripts/dev.sh` and `gh` are all unavailable in the environment it was written in. `scripts/dev.sh check`, `test-db` and a cold-start walkthrough are still owed, and so is the closing issue comment.

**Before that: [#89](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/89)** — inference is three processes. This blocked M1 retrieval and is now cleared.

```
generation  ready   Qwen3.5-4B-Q4_K_M.gguf
embedding   ready   bge-m3-FP16.gguf              1024 dimensions
reranking   ready   bge-reranker-v2-m3-FP16.gguf
```

Through the client, from inside the API container, over one socket:

```
generate: 8 tokens
embed:    2 vectors of 1024 dimensions
rerank:   "how much notice to end the lease?"
  -2.419  Either party may terminate on ninety days written notice.
  -7.846  The tenant shall pay rent monthly in advance.
 -11.028  The premises shall be used for office purposes only.
```

The reranker puts the right passage first by a wide margin, which is the signal retrieval needs. **The scores are raw logits, not probabilities** — negative and unbounded — so only their order means anything, and the client returns them sorted rather than inviting a threshold on the number.

**Why this was necessary rather than tidy.** Embeddings from the generation model came back at 2560 dimensions where `chunks.embedding` is `vector(1024)`. They would not have been merely poor for retrieval — the database would have refused the insert. And `--reranking` needs its own model and is mutually exclusive with generation.

Each role is supervised independently: one model missing does not stop the others, because a user with no reranker can still ask questions and reporting the assistant down for it would be false. Three supervisors share one state file, written under a lock — without it they read-modify-write over each other and a role's entry vanishes, which the API would read as that role never having reported.

Both models verified against the registry before their names were written down (C9): `bge-m3` is MIT with 36M downloads, `bge-reranker-v2-m3` is Apache-2.0 with 18M. Both ungated.

## The build runner is live, and its first ticket

`M1-ADD-ING-021` was built by `scripts/build-runner.sh` end to end: gate proved on a clean tree, build, gate (**failed on lint**), one fix attempt with the failing output, gate again (passed), audit, manual-test document.

**The audit failed the work, and it was right to.** Four defects a passing gate cannot see. Two were serious:

- **A symlinked root broke the ticket's primary acceptance criterion.** `covering()` compared a file's *resolved* path against the root's *unresolved* one, so `/home/anna/work -> /mnt/big/work` registered happily and then covered nothing — and the refusal offered to nominate the same folder again. Fixed by resolving both sides, with a test for the shape and a second test proving a symlink *inside* a root still cannot escape.
- **`probe()` blocked the event loop.** A synchronous `os.scandir` reached from three request handlers, with no thread and no timeout. `AGENTS.md` §6 forbids exactly this, and the consequence is concrete: one hard-mounted share whose server has gone away would hang every request including `/health` — the surface that exists to say what is wrong would be the first thing to stop answering. Now off the loop with a 3s ceiling, and `listing` probes concurrently so one dead share costs the wait once rather than once per root ahead of it.

The other two: the network-share warning never fires in the ordinary first-run order, and there were no HTTP-level tests for any of the five endpoints. Eight now exist, including one for the route-ordering hazard the code warns about itself — `/roots/covering` must be declared before `/roots/{root_id}` or it becomes a 422 about a malformed UUID.

**Six runner bugs found by running it**, all mine: the gate was a stub returning success; the ledger was empty so it would have rebuilt M0; the agent call was never implemented; `run_agent` read `$4` under `set -u`; `guard_record_spend` was called with one argument of two; and the agent had no write permission, so its first working run produced a design document and zero files.

**And a seventh, which the audit exposed rather than the code:** the runner asked for a verdict and never read it. `AUDIT: FAIL` was advisory. It is binding now — a rejected ticket is not recorded as done, and nothing is pushed.

Plus a ruff gap that was mine, not the agent's: `B008` rejected FastAPI's own `Depends()` idiom, so 844 lines of sound work failed the gate for using the framework as documented.

## Next task

**M1 and M2 are both complete.** M2 — *it says when it doesn't know* — closed with `M2-EVAL-DEPLOY-067`: all 15 tickets done, `0.3.0`. `M2-FAIL-FE-060`'s ingestion bullet was left unimplemented, blocked on issue #227 — a decision is needed there before it can close, but it does not block the milestone. **M3 — *it learns my material*, `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md`, is under way**: `M3-RAISE-BE-068`, `M3-RAISE-BE-069` and `M3-RAISE-BE-070` are done (see above). **`M3-REVIEW-BE-072a` is also done, `0.3.4`**, built out of sequence: `M3-REVIEW-FE-072`'s own dependency on it, discovered and filed twice (issues #257, #268, both now closed) by build agents that stopped rather than invent the contract. `askwell.review` adds `GET /clarifications` (pending items grouped by source, newest source first, with counts, a total and each item's evidence), `POST /clarifications/{id}/answer` (a `memory` row with `origin = 'clarification'` and a `clarification_answered` decisions record, one transaction — refused by name for an already-answered item, allowed for a skipped one) and `POST /clarifications/{id}/skip` (status only, no `memory` row). a merged header cell is flagged, never guessed), infers a type and confidence per column, votes on header presence from whether the first row's cells match the typed majority beneath them, and raises everything it cannot resolve — a missing or ambiguous header, a blank header cell, a column mixing `1,200.00` and `1200.5` — as real `clarifications` rows via `raise_table_inference`, capped and idempotent per source the same way `askwell.clarify.raise_candidates` is for documents.
