Issue #340 (no reconcile sweep for a stuck `queued` dump/table source) remains open, re-owned rather than fixed here. **`M4-CONN-FE-096` is now done too, `0.4.8`** — the connection wizard is real: `POST /sources/connection` validates before any socket opens, then connects for real against PostgreSQL (`psycopg`), MySQL/MariaDB (`pymysql`, a new dependency) or SQL Server (`python-tds`, a new dependency), runs a minimal read check, and on success creates a `connection` source and dispatches `introspect_connection_job` (table names into `schema_notes`, `status` to `ready`) — the same "backend job a frontend ticket dispatches" shape `M4-DUMP-FE-090` used. `askwell.connections` classifies every failure into `host_unresolved`/`connection_refused`/`network_blocked`/`timeout`/`auth_failed`/`permission_denied`, matched against each driver's real exception types rather than guessed. **What this ticket does *not* do, found while building it and filed as [#345](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/345) rather than built informally:** the egress proxy still permits no destination at all (`docs/architecture.md` §5.1) and never has, for any of the three exceptions the architecture names — online AI and web search wait on M8, and a live connection needs the same shape of mechanism, not a config line, so it is out of scope for a `frontend`-labelled 4-6 hour ticket. Proven live rather than assumed: connecting to the sandbox Postgres (reachable because `api` already shares that network for C3) round-trips end to end; the identical request against a real external IP comes back `network_blocked`, and against a real external host name comes back `host_unresolved` — DNS resolution outside the container network fails exactly like a typo, so that second case is genuinely misleading until #345 lands, which is the concrete finding #345 documents rather than a hand-wave. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why the 2026-08-28 model-download precedent (no informal allowlist entries) applies here too. The wizard, its five-reason classifier, and the connect→introspect→ready path are all tested (`api/tests/test_connections.py`, `test_connections_db.py`, `test_sources_api.py`) and were exercised against the real stack, not just unit-tested. Settings gained a "Connected databases" count, deliberately separate from the network-activity zero (`docs/ux/settings.md` §4, updated in this change). The write probe (`M4-CONN-SEC-097`) and credential encryption (`M4-CONN-SEC-098`) remain this ticket's own named out-of-scope items, unstarted. **`M4-CONN-SEC-097` is now done too, `0.4.9`** — `probe_connection` refuses a write-capable credential before either read check runs, on all three engines, naming the permission and object detected (superuser/`sysadmin`, database-level `CREATE`, or a table-level grant) and offering copyable, engine-specific SQL for a read-only user (`read_only_user_sql`) — never run by Askwell itself, per this ticket's own "Out of Scope". A new `probe_unreliable` reason code refuses the connection outright whenever the privilege-introspection query itself fails, on any engine, rather than falling back to the SELECT-check's older, more permissive assumption. A refusal writes a `connection_write_refused` decisions row (engine/host/permission, never the credential) and increments a local, never-transmitted Redis counter, best-effort like `askwell.egress._record`. Tested against a real Postgres for the table-grant, database-`CREATE`, and read-only-passes cases (`api/tests/test_connections_write_probe_db.py`); MySQL grant parsing and SQL Server permission/role classification are pure functions tested without a database, since neither engine exists in this stack (`api/tests/test_connections.py`). `web/components/add/add-screen.tsx`'s stale "not wired up yet" copy and `web/components/settings/connections.tsx`'s "write permissions not yet checked" line are both corrected in the same change, and the refusal's SQL renders as a copyable `<textarea>`. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why the write probe fails closed where the pre-existing SQL Server SELECT check does not — filed as its own new issue, **#348**, rather than fixed under this ticket, since it touches a check `M4-CONN-FE-096` built and this ticket only restructured to share a query. Both DoD-listed open items are re-owned: **issue #345** (egress permits no live-connection destination at all) is unchanged by this ticket — the write probe is testable against a locally-reachable Postgres regardless of whether #345 has landed, so it does not block on it, but a cold-start walkthrough against a real external database still cannot reach the write probe at all until #345 does. **Issue #346** (the UX doc undercounting failure modes) is partly resolved: its own recommended "not yet wired" caveat for `docs/ux/add-source.md` §4 is now moot, since the write probe is real; the six-reason-code counting gap in §5's "Connection unreachable" row is untouched, since this ticket named `write_capable`/`probe_unreliable` as two more reason codes rather than touching that row, so #346 stays open for that part. **`M4-CONN-SEC-098` is now done too, `0.4.10`, and the connection epic closes.** `sources.config_encrypted` is genuinely encrypted: a new `askwell.crypto` module derives a Fernet key (HKDF-SHA256) from a per-install secret (32 random bytes, generated on first use, owner-only permissions, at `Settings.install_secret_path` — the same host bind mount `api` and `worker` already share for the inference socket, outside `postgres-data`). `create_connection_source` encrypts before insert; `run_introspection` decrypts before ever opening a socket, so a lost or changed secret is caught as `credentials_locked` and the source moves to `attention` with a message that explicitly rules out a network cause — never misreported as `host_unresolved`, the same "wrong failure reads like a wrong host" trap #345 already named for a different case. `derive_key` takes an optional passphrase and folds it into the same HKDF call, so M7's passphrase extends the key without re-encrypting or re-prompting for stored credentials, which is this ticket's own forward-compatibility acceptance criterion. Verified live against the running stack, not just unit-tested: a real connection's `config_encrypted` read back as opaque Fernet ciphertext with no plaintext substring; the connection worked normally end to end (`queued` → `ready`); deleting `.run/install.key` and re-running `run_introspection` directly against the worker container produced `attention` with the locked message, and the database was never contacted. `cryptography>=44.0` (resolved to 50.0.1) is a new runtime dependency. `docs/decisions.md`, this date, has the full reasoning, including why the secret lives on the existing bind mount rather than a new volume or the `settings` table, and why a missing secret is filled in silently but every other read failure is not. Issue #345 (egress permits no live-connection destination at all) is unchanged by this ticket and stays open, re-owned again: encryption and the write probe are both testable and real against a locally-reachable database regardless of whether #345 has landed, but a cold-start walkthrough against a real *external* database still cannot reach any of this until #345 does — that remains the one item genuinely blocking the connection epic from working end-to-end on a real install. **`M4-SCHEMA-ING-100` is now done too, `0.4.11`** (see Last completed and the session log) — full schema introspection for a live connection and a loaded sandbox database alike, always as a read-only role, plus `POST /sources/{id}/reintrospect` for on demand. `M4-CSV-FE-095` (the CSV route's own add-source screen, still blocked per issue #339) is the one open `M4` item nothing in this session touched. Issue #368 (a locked connection credential silently vanishes from source selection) filed and re-owned, not fixed. `M4-SQL-VAL-104` (parse generated SQL and reject anything that is not a single read) is next in the `SQL` epic. **`M4-SQL-VAL-104` is now done too, `0.4.16`** (see Last completed) — the C2 gate is real: `askwell.sql.validate.validate_query` parses with `sqlglot` and rejects anything that is not exactly one `SELECT`/`WITH` read, walking the whole parsed tree rather than trusting the top-level statement type alone (a PostgreSQL data-modifying CTE with `RETURNING` parses with `SELECT` at the top but deletes every row when run — caught the same way a top-level `DELETE` is). issues #375 and #386 are both closed. **`M4-RESULT-FE-110` is now done too, `0.4.25`, and it is the last built ticket in the `SQL` epic** (see Last completed) — the query is now disclosed unconditionally, including for a rejected/dry-run-failed/timed-out/query-time-failed/source-gone turn, which needed a new `sql_query` wire field alongside `sql_result` (`docs/decisions.md`, this date); issue #398 (filed and closed with this session) tracks the gap found while building it. **`M4-RESULT-FE-111` is now done too, `0.4.26`, and it is the `SQL` epic's last ticket** (see Last completed) — the fifth database state, "no connections configured", closing issue #400 by construction (the override only ever fires after document retrieval has already abstained). `M4-CSV-FE-095` (issues #339/#344) remains the one long-standing blocked item nothing in this session touched. **M4 is complete in every ticket this session touched; M5 — *it handles harder questions*, `docs/backlog/M5-it-handles-harder-questions.md`, is now under way, starting with `M5-TOOLS-BE-113`, now done, `0.4.27`** — the tool registry: `askwell.agent.tools.TOOLS` exposes exactly five tools (`document_search`, `database_query`, `schema_lookup`, `document_listing`, `current_date`) behind one shared contract, each with a `pydantic` argument model, a bounded result that states its own truncation, and a `call_tool` wrapper that turns every failure — an unknown tool name, invalid arguments, or the handler itself raising — into a recoverable `ToolResult`/`ToolStep` pair rather than an exception. `database_query` re-runs the same checked chain `askwell.ask._run_sql_turn` already assembles (generation → C2 validation → limit injection → dry run → execution) as a second caller of those same stages, not a second implementation of any of them. **Not wired into `POST /ask`** — deliberately: `M5-LOOP-BE-115` (multi-step loop with parallel calls) is the next `M5` ticket and the first thing with a reason to call this registry more than once per turn; wiring ahead of it would be the same mistake `M4-SQL-DB-107`/`M4-SQL-BE-103` both avoided about themselves. **`M5-TOOLS-BE-114` is now done too, `0.4.28`** — C7 extended from retrieved document passages to tool results. `askwell.agent.compose.delimit_tool_result` wraps a tool call's result in its own `<tool-result index="…" tool="…">` block, with a new `_escape_forged_delimiter` neutralising a literal `<tool-result`/`</tool-result>` found inside the data itself first — deliberately stricter than `delimit_candidates` (`M1-ASK-BE-037`), which does not escape, because a tool result can carry text an attacker fully controls (an imported dump's own row, C3) in a way ordinary document text does not; `docs/decisions.md`, this date, records why the two delimiters are allowed to diverge. The prompt file's standing C7 statement now names `<tool-result>` explicitly, with its own worked example, alongside `<retrieved-content>`. `askwell.agent.tools.ToolStep` gained `injection_flagged`/`injection_patterns`, computed unconditionally in `_step` by flattening a result's content to its string leaves and reusing `askwell.agent.compose.flag_injection_text` rather than a second copy of the same heuristic — every tool call is flagged on the trace the moment `call_tool` runs, without waiting for `M5-LOOP-BE-115` to exist first. Flagging never blocks, per this ticket's own out-of-scope line: a flagged tool result is returned and would be answered exactly like an unflagged one. **`M5-LOOP-BE-115` is now done too, `0.4.29`** (see Last completed) — the multi-step tool loop, wired into `POST /ask` for a genuinely hybrid corpus, with parallel dispatch, dedup and per-iteration step recording. Issues #407, #408 and #409 are filed and re-owned, not closed. **`M5-LOOP-BE-116` is now done too, `0.4.30`** (see Last completed) — the 8-call ceiling is real and enforced, with a composed stopped-early answer, "what it was about to do" recorded as `pending_calls`, and a `Continue` action that starts a genuinely new turn (fresh budget, seeded history) rather than repeating. Issues #407, #408 and #409 remain open and unaffected — none name this ticket as their owner. **`M5-LOOP-BE-117` is now done too, `0.4.31`** (see Last completed) — the trace's last missing step kind (`"schema"`) and the DB-side rotation `docs/architecture.md` §7.1 always promised but nothing ever did. Issues #407, #408 and #409 remain open and unaffected — none name this ticket as their owner either. **`M5-EVAL-TEST-124` is now done too, `0.4.32`** (see Last completed) — the quality gate's "Tool selection incl. parallel" suite. **`M5-LOOP-BE-117a` is now done too, `0.4.33`, closing issues #414 and #420** (see Last completed) — `run_tool_loop` gets a live per-call `on_tool_call` observer, and `askwell.ask` emits its tool-call `step` events live from it rather than in a post-loop burst; only the `"end"` phase currently reaches the browser, per #420's own recommended mitigation, until `M5-LOOP-FE-118` adds a phase discriminator. **Next: `M5-LOOP-FE-118`** — step labels for a multi-step turn, now genuinely unblocked (see #414's closing comment); it should start by adding the phase/call-id discriminator to `AskStepData` so `_emit_tool_call_step` can switch to forwarding both `"start"` and `"end"`. `M5-TRACE-FE-119` (rendering the trace itself) follows it. **`M5-TRACE-FE-119` is now done too, `0.4.36`** (see session log) — the trace panel itself: a toggle under any answer (streaming, completed, abstained, stopped or failed) opens a numbered step sequence over Ask, each step with a plain-language summary and a duration, raw JSON expandable underneath. Issue #425 (a tool-loop turn's mid-turn poll returns no steps until the loop finishes) is confirmed live now that a real client polls mid-turn, and re-owned rather than fixed — it is backend-only work `M5-LOOP-BE-117a`'s own `ToolCallObserver` already supports, out of this ticket's own scope. **`M5-TRACE-FE-120` is now done too, `0.4.37`** (see Last completed) — the trace step detail is formatted per kind (scores against the threshold, resolved memory facts, the query and rejection reason for a database turn, injection flags as information) rather than the raw JSON `-119` left. **Next: `M5-TRACE-FE-121`** (interactions — click a passage, click a memory fact, adjust the threshold from an abstention trace), the last ticket in the `TRACE` chain.

**This section stops tracking individual tickets past `M5-TRACE-FE-121`; work has since moved into Phase 6.5, `docs/backlog/M6.5-it-can-look-outside.md`.** For the current state of that milestone, read the top of **Last completed** rather than this paragraph. As of `0.6.15`: `M6.5-WEB-BE-185`, `-187`, `-188`, `-189` (the provider, egress authorisation, capped fetching, and stored citation records), `M6.5-WEB-FE-190` (the web results region component) and `M6.5-WEB-FE-186` (the escalation offer, with its own `POST /ask/{message_id}/escalate/web`) are all done. The escalation is now reachable end to end from the browser — what is still missing is a live turn actually citing a web result: `record_web_citations` and `fetch_pages` both still have no caller. **Next: `M6.5-WEB-FE-191`** (mixed answers — the compose-loop wiring that gives a web result a claim ordinal to cite) or `M6.5-WEB-BE-195` (the real `ddgs` provider, replacing `FixtureWebSearchProvider`).

Read that file directly rather than trusting this one for scope. Things about M2 worth knowing before starting:

- **It is the milestone where C5 becomes measurable.** Until now abstention is a sentence in a prompt. M2 builds the eval suite that puts a number on it, with a pass bar of 0.90 — and `AGENTS.md` §3 and `docs/success-metrics.md` §2 both warn about the same shortcut: the abstention rate can be driven to zero by lowering the retrieval threshold, one config change, dashboard looks excellent, product silently starts answering from general knowledge. Do not weaken those tests to make a change pass, and do not touch `retrieval_score_threshold` to improve the number.
- **The threshold is now applied, not just captured.** `M1-ASK-RET-035` deliberately left `RetrievalResult.threshold` carried but not enforced; `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053` is where deciding what clears it landed — `askwell.ask._run_generation` abstains before `compose()` runs when nothing does.
- **The copy exists now.** `M2-ABSTAIN-BE-054` built `askwell.agent.abstain.compose_abstention` and wrote the composed message into `messages.content` and the audit record's `answer` for an abstained turn — three variants keyed off `reason_code` (`empty_corpus`/`source_indexing`/`below_threshold`), per `docs/ux/ask.md` §6. What is still missing, and is `-FE-055`'s whole job: the message is not streamed to the browser as a `token` SSE event at all today, so nothing renders. `docs/ux/ask.md` §6 has the required layout (full measure, `--ink`/`--muted`, never an error colour) and the list of things it must never do (apologise, hedge, offer general knowledge).

**Running the queue:** `SPEND_CEILING=<n> BUILD_MODEL=sonnet AUDIT_MODEL=sonnet DOC_MODEL=sonnet .build-runner/supervise.sh --milestone M2`. It builds, gates, audits, documents, opens a PR and merges on green, one ticket at a time. Start only from a clean `main` with nothing else running — see **Build procedure**.

## Open

Checked against the tracker on 2026-08-28; #249 added 2026-08-30 during `M3-RAISE-BE-069`. Anything closed since has been removed rather than left to mislead.

- **[#249](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/249), filed 2026-08-30 — a late-arriving high-ranking clarification candidate cannot displace an already-raised, unanswered one.** `M3-RAISE-BE-069`'s own edge case: the cap applies to a source overall, not per batch, so a contradiction discovered in a second import into an already-scanned source should be able to bump a first-batch abbreviation still sitting unanswered. `askwell.clarify.raise_candidates` scans a source exactly once — guarded by "does it already have any clarification row" — and never re-runs. **Why it matters:** a source added to incrementally (the common case — a folder someone keeps dropping files into) locks in whichever candidates existed at the first "nothing outstanding" moment, regardless of what shows up later, silently defeating the point of ranking. **Recommendation:** deferred rather than built now — real design work (what happens to a pending clarification's evidence when the underlying data changes between scans) that risks scope creep beyond a single ticket; revisit if a real multi-batch source is observed hitting it, the same bar `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §9.2 sets for the cap of 5 itself. Labels: `phase:2`.
- **[#230](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/230), filed 2026-08-28 — a `chunk`/`embed` stage write racing a document deletion is not guarded the way `M2-DELETE-BE-061`'s other four ingestion writes are.** The ticket guarded `_park`/`_finish`/`_fail`/`process`'s `indexing` write with `deleted_at IS NULL`, but `chunk.run`/`embed.run` themselves write `chunks` directly and unguarded — a write landing in the few milliseconds after a deletion clears a document's chunks would leave it with live content again, invisible to the library but not to retrieval. **Why it matters:** it is the one remaining gap in an otherwise-closed deletion path, and the failure mode is exactly what C5 exists to prevent. **Recommendation:** a database-level gate on `chunks` writes for a tombstoned document, the same shape as `ck_chunks_cleared_content_has_no_embedding`. Labels: `phase:2`, `constraint:audit`.
- **[#211](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/211), filed 2026-08-28 — M1's landing did not take the `MINOR` version bump `AGENTS.md` §7 requires.** `VERSION` walked `PATCH` from `0.2.0` straight through M1 to `0.2.41` (this session's ticket) with no `MINOR` step marking M1 as done, even though `docs/BRAIN.md` has said "M1 complete" for several sessions. **Why it matters:** §7's own stated reason for the rule is that a version, a `BRAIN.md` entry and a closing comment should line up — right now the version cannot be read as "which phase is this", and the same gap will repeat at M2's own landing unless something starts checking for it. **Recommendation:** bump to `0.3.0` at the start of a session (not mid-ticket, to keep that ticket's own delta legible), with a `CHANGELOG.md` note marking it as M1's retroactive landing, and add a checklist step so a milestone close is a checked action rather than something to remember unprompted. Labels: `documentation`.
- **[#185](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/185), filed 2026-08-28 — no extractor reads a standalone image file; `askwell.extract.run` has no branch for one.** `filetypes.py` detects PNG/JPEG/GIF/TIFF/BMP/WebP correctly and routes them to `files`, but `extract.run` falls into its generic `else` and raises `UnsupportedForExtraction` for every one of them — a source that is just a scanned page saved as an image cannot be added at all today. **Why it matters:** `docs/ux/source-viewer.md` §2 names "Image" as its own citation-rendering kind, and `M1-VIEW-FE-047` (this session) had nothing to build it against — realised the ticket's own "Image" row as a scanned PDF page instead, the only OCR-derived image case the pipeline currently produces. **Recommendation:** a thin extractor reusing `extract_ocr.ocr_page`'s existing machinery (orientation, script, confidence) against the image's own bytes rather than a rendered PDF page, one `document_pages` row, `anchor_kind = "image"`. Found auditing `M1-VIEW-FE-047`. Labels: `phase:1`, `constraint:grounding`.
- **[#105](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/105), re-owned 2026-08-28 by `M1-ADD-ING-025` — hashing a whole drop happens inside one HTTP request, so a large add is a long request with nothing to show for it.** `POST /sources` reads every file end to end before it answers: five thousand files, or one 4 GB scan, is minutes of work in a single request with no progress, no cancellation and no partial result if the browser or anything in front of it gives up. **Why it matters:** the ticket's own assumption is "hashing a large file is fast enough to happen before indexing rather than during it", and that is true per file and false per drop. The failure shape is bad: a request that times out client-side has still written every row it got to, so retrying re-hashes everything and reports most of it as a duplicate — which is correct and reads like a bug. The add screen shows *Recording* with no count while it happens. **Where it surfaced:** `api/src/askwell/sources.py` `add()`; `web/components/add/add-state.tsx` `locate()`, the `recordSource` call. **Options:** (a) chunk the request client-side — record in batches of, say, 200 and accumulate the outcomes, which needs no new infrastructure and gives the screen a real running count; (b) make recording a job on the `arq` queue that `M1-ADD-ING-025` introduces anyway, and have the screen poll — the right long-term shape and the wrong thing to build before the queue exists; (c) leave it and cap the request lower. **Recommendation, updated now the queue exists:** (b), as its own ticket covering the endpoint *and* the add screen's result flow — the per-file outcomes it returns synchronously are what the screen renders, so moving recording onto the queue means they arrive over the progress stream instead. (a), client-side chunking, is no longer worth doing: (b) would throw it away and two code paths would record the same drop in the meantime. Labels: `phase:1`, `bug`.
- **[#106](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/106), filed 2026-08-28 — a file present under two nominated folders is recorded once and appears under only one source.** Duplicate recognition is global across live documents, deliberately (`decisions.md`, 2026-08-28), so adding `/clients/contract.pdf` and then `/archive/contract.pdf` produces one document under the first source; the second source lists nothing for it. **Why it matters:** it is the right answer for retrieval and citations, which is why it was chosen, and it will look like a bug from the library: someone opens the archive source, sees a file they know they added missing, and has no explanation. The duplicate sentence is shown once, at add time, on a screen they then close. **Where it surfaced:** `api/src/askwell/sources.py` `_duplicate_of()`. **Options:** (a) show the duplicate relationship in the library — the source that recognised it lists the file with "indexed from <other path>", which needs a link table or a repeat of the row; (b) record a second document row and let retrieval deduplicate by hash at query time, which moves the cost to every question asked; (c) leave it and make sure the library's empty state says what it means. **Recommendation:** (a), sized as part of the library work rather than retrofitted here. Labels: `phase:1`, `question`.
- **Resolved before it was filed (checked 2026-08-28) — the successful path through `POST /sources` does have an HTTP-level test.** `api/tests/test_sources_api.py::test_a_successful_add_is_committed_and_survives_the_request` is `requires_db`, drives a real add through the app, and reads the row back **on a separate `psycopg` connection after the request has closed** — which is the only way to catch a lost commit, since querying the request's own session would pass whether or not it ever committed. It also asserts the decisions records are exactly `[source_added, document_added]`. It passed on its first-ever run, 2026-08-28. The item was written from an earlier state of the file; nothing needs filing.
- **Needs an issue filing — ordinary prose is misread as a CSV or a SQL dump, and since `M1-ADD-VAL-024` that means a supported file is silently withheld until M4.** In `detect()` the textual branch runs `SQL_MARKERS` and `looksDelimited()` *before* falling back to Markdown or plain text, and neither consults the extension. A `.md` or `.txt` file whose first line carries two commas — `# Contracts, leases and addenda, 2026`, or `Dear Anna, thank you, and regards` — goes to the `table` route; one containing a line beginning `CREATE`, `INSERT INTO`, `COPY`, `SET `, `ALTER TABLE` or `DROP TABLE` within the first 4 KB, which is any note documenting a schema, goes to the `dump` route. **Why it matters:** before this ticket both mistakes ended in the queue, so the file was still added and the wrong label was cosmetic. Now a non-`files` route means `verdict: "later"` and the file is **not queued at all** — the user is told their plain-text note "arrives in M4", which is false about a format M1 reads today. That is the exact failure the ticket exists to prevent, inverted: not a bare rejection, but a confident wrong answer that costs the user material they believe they added. **Where it surfaced:** `web/lib/add-source.ts` `detect()`, the `looksTextual` branch — the SQL and delimiter tests at the `extension === "sql" …` and `extension === "csv" …` lines. The covering test `prose is plain text, not a CSV, because one comma is not a column` uses a first line with **one** comma, so it passes over the boundary rather than on it, and no test drops a `.md` or `.txt` file with two. **Options:** (a) let a known `files`-route extension (`md`, `markdown`, `txt`, `html`, `htm`) win over the delimiter and SQL heuristics, keeping content-first for every case where the extension claims nothing — one condition, and it matches the module's own stated rule that the extension is better evidence for Markdown; (b) strengthen `looksDelimited()` to require a consistent separator count across several lines, and `SQL_MARKERS` to require a dump preamble rather than any DDL keyword, which is more faithful to "detection by content" and more work to get right; (c) keep detection and treat only a `later` verdict on a `files`-extension file as suspect at the queue boundary, which scatters the knowledge again. **Recommendation:** (a) now, with tests for both misreads, and (b) when M4 makes the table route real and the cost of a wrong CSV verdict changes. Labels: `phase:1`, `bug`. Found auditing `M1-ADD-VAL-024`. **Updated 2026-08-28:** `M1-ADD-BE-023` took option (a) *server-side* — `api/src/askwell/filetypes.py` will not take a `.md`, `.txt`, `.html` or `.htm` file away from the files route on a delimiter or SQL-keyword heuristic, with tests for both misreads. That does not fix the user's experience, because the client decides what is sent at all and a `later` verdict means the file never reaches the server. The remaining work is the same three lines in `web/lib/add-source.ts`, plus the two tests, and the two implementations disagree until it is done.
- **Needs an issue filing — the cap stops files being kept but does not stop the walk, which is the thing it exists to prevent.** `flatten()` in `web/lib/add-source.ts` checks `files.length >= limit` only on the *file* branch; the directory branch calls `children()` unconditionally, so every folder in the dropped tree is still enumerated after the 5,000th file. **Why it matters:** the function's own comment says the limit "exists because a folder dropped by mistake can be a home directory, and walking one entry at a time through the browser's directory reader is not free", and the ticket names "a very large number of files dropped — accepted with a count and an honest estimate, not a frozen window" as an edge case. Dropping a home directory shows a settled count of 5,000 while the browser keeps reading directory entries for minutes, and `next` accumulates the whole level in memory. The screen looks finished while the work is not. **Where it surfaced:** `web/lib/add-source.ts` `flatten()`, the `folders += 1` branch; the covering test `the cap stops the walk and says so rather than truncating quietly` uses a flat list only, so it passes without exercising this. **Options:** (a) break out of the walk entirely once `files.length >= limit`, reporting `truncated` — folders below the cap are never visited and the count is what was taken; (b) keep walking but cap the *queue* as well, which bounds memory and not time; (c) leave it and correct the comment. **Recommendation:** (a), plus a test with a nested tree wider than the limit — the current test cannot distinguish (a) from today's behaviour. Labels: `phase:1`, `bug`.
- **Needs an issue filing — one covering check per drop is only sound for a drop with one top-level folder.** `locate()` in `web/components/add/add-state.tsx` builds the path from `supportedIn(batch)[0]` alone and asks `GET /roots/covering` once. A drop can carry several top-level entries — two folders dragged together, or a folder plus loose files — and they need not live under the same tree. **Why it matters:** the batch is marked `queued` and counted as added on the strength of a check that covered one file. The screen tells the user Askwell may read material it may not, and the correction arrives much later, from the ingestion ticket, as a failure per file with no memory of what was promised. The reasoning recorded in `decisions.md` ("a root is a permission over a tree, so one answer settles all of them") is true per tree and is being applied per drop. **Where it surfaced:** `web/components/add/add-state.tsx` `locate()`; `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-08-27, "One covering check per drop, not one per file". **Options:** (a) group the batch by the first path segment and ask once per distinct top-level entry, which is one request for the common drop and a handful for the awkward one; (b) ask once per drop but check the answer against every distinct top-level entry server-side, adding a batch endpoint; (c) refuse a drop with more than one top-level entry, which turns an ordinary gesture into an error. **Recommendation:** (a). It keeps the request count proportional to what the user actually dropped and needs no API change. Labels: `phase:1`, `bug`, `constraint:local-first`.
- **Resolved by `M1-ADD-BE-023` (2026-08-28) — a rejection is counted but never logged.** Option (a) was taken: the server re-detects and writes a `file_refused` log line naming the path, the detected format and the reason, for every file it turns away. The browser's `localStorage` counter stays as the local tally. What is still true is that a file the *client* refuses never reaches the server and so is still counted and not logged — which is the same gap for a `.zip` dropped among contracts, narrowed to the files the client decides about alone. Left as-is deliberately: the alternative is an endpoint whose only caller is a log line. Surfaced in `M1-ADD-VAL-024`, closed in `api/src/askwell/sources.py`.
- **Filed as issue #585** (confirmed still true of `fromNativeFiles` too, once `M7-TAURI-FE-182` actually landed) **— the browse alternative applies no cap, so the two selection paths disagree about what a large selection is.** `fromDrop()` runs through `flatten()` and is bounded by `MAX_FILES`; `fromFiles()` maps the whole `FileList` and hard-codes `truncated: false`. Choosing a 40,000-file folder with **Choose a folder** produces a batch the drop path would have capped at 5,000 and reported. **Why it matters:** the cap is a stated behaviour on this screen — `docs/ux/add-source.md` §5, "More files than one drop will take" — and it is now true of one route in and not the other, which is the kind of divergence that gets discovered by whoever hits it. It also matters for the seam: `M7-TAURI-FE-182` replaces both entry points, and a rule enforced in only one of them will be reimplemented in only one of them. **Where it surfaced:** `web/lib/selection.ts` `fromFiles()`. **Options:** (a) apply `MAX_FILES` in `fromFiles()` and set `truncated` accordingly, three lines and no new concept; (b) move the cap up into `accept()` in `add-state.tsx` so every path through the queue is bounded once, wherever selection came from; (c) leave it, on the grounds that a browse of that size is rare. **Recommendation:** (b) — it is the one place both routes and the future Tauri route must pass through. Labels: `phase:1`, `bug`.
- **Resolved by `M1-ADD-BE-023` (2026-08-28) — the client's type detection is a courtesy, not a boundary.** Option (a) was taken: `api/src/askwell/filetypes.py` re-detects from the file on disk and nothing the client says about a type reaches a row. The client sends only a folder and relative paths.
- **Resolved by `M1-ADD-BE-023` (2026-08-28) — nothing records that a source was added.** Option (a) was taken: `source_added` and one `document_added` per file, both in the decisions store, both naming the path, both written inside the transaction that creates the rows — so a record that cannot be written takes the insert with it.
- **Needs an issue filing — the browser halves of the add flow have no automated test at all.** `web/lib/add-source.ts` is unit-tested (24 assertions over detection, expansion and formatting) and `web/lib/selection.ts`, `drop-target.tsx` and `add-state.tsx` are not: there is no browser-level test runner in this repository, so the directory walk, the drop handler and the queue are covered only by the manual walkthrough. **Why it matters:** two of the bugs most likely to exist here are invisible to a passing build and silent when they happen — `readEntries` returning a batch rather than the whole directory (a folder of 200 contracts quietly becoming 100, a count that looks plausible) and the `DataTransferItemList` being emptied when the drop handler returns (a drop that does nothing at all). Both are guarded by comments and by construction; neither is guarded by a test. **Where it surfaced:** `web/lib/selection.ts` `readAll()` and `fromDrop()`. **Options:** (a) add Playwright, which is a browser download and a real dependency, and drive one drop end to end; (b) fake `DataTransfer` and `FileSystemDirectoryReader` in `node --test`, which tests the walk but not the browser's actual behaviour — the part that is uncertain; (c) leave it to the manual walkthrough and say so. **Recommendation:** (b) now, since it catches the batching bug cheaply, and (a) when M7 makes a real end-to-end run worth its weight. Labels: `phase:1`, `bug`.
- **[#107](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/107), filed 2026-08-28, and no longer untested — SELinux *does* refuse the mount; option (a) below was run and option (c) is now the plan (`decisions.md`, 2026-08-28). the roots mount is untested against SELinux.** `compose.yaml` bind-mounts `ASKWELL_ROOTS_MOUNT` with `:ro` and deliberately **without** `z`, because `z` relabels recursively and would rewrite SELinux labels across a user's whole 40 GB material tree — slow, and a modification Askwell has no business making to files it only reads. The consequence is that on an SELinux host (this development machine is Fedora) `container_t` may be refused traversal of the mount, and every nominated folder would report `unreadable`. **Why it matters:** if SELinux does refuse, M1 cannot read a single file on the maintainer's own machine and every ingestion ticket after this one is blocked, with a message that names SELinux but no supported way to resolve it. **Where it surfaced:** `compose.yaml` api and worker `volumes`; `askwell.roots.probe()`, the `UNREADABLE` branch; `decisions.md`, 2026-08-27. **It is unverified because `podman` could not be run in the session that wrote this.** **Options:** (a) verify with `podman run --rm -v $PWD/x:$PWD/x:ro <image> ls $PWD/x` on a Fedora host and record the result — do this first, it is one command and may close the item; (b) if refused, add `--security-opt label=disable` to the api and worker services, which disables SELinux confinement for those containers entirely and is a real weakening; (c) if refused, document a one-off `chcon -Rt container_file_t` the user runs on their own tree, which is the same relabel as `z` but explicit and consented to. **Recommendation:** (a), then (c) if needed — (b) trades a narrow problem for a broad one. Labels: `phase:1`, `deploy`.
- **Needs an issue filing — a folder reached through a symlink can be nominated but nothing under it can ever be added.** `covering()` requires the candidate's *real* path to be contained by the root's *stored* path, and `normalise()` deliberately does not resolve the stored path. So a root nominated as `/home/anna/work` where that is a link to `/mnt/big/work` registers fine and then refuses every file inside it: the literal path is covered, the real path is not. **Why it matters:** it breaks the ticket's first acceptance criterion — "a user can nominate a root directory and then add files from anywhere under it" — and it fails in the worst possible shape, because the refusal offers to nominate the same folder again, which changes nothing. Symlinked home and data directories are ordinary, and `/tmp` is a symlink on macOS. **Where it surfaced:** `api/src/askwell/roots.py`, `covering()` and `normalise()`; `M1-ADD-ING-021` audit, 2026-08-27. The symlink tests cover escape only (`api/tests/test_roots.py::test_a_symlink_out_of_a_root_is_resolved_before_it_is_judged`), never a symlinked root. **Options:** (a) resolve the root at check time and require containment of *literal under literal* **or** *real under real* — escape is still refused because a link inside a root has a real path under neither; (b) resolve at registration and store the real path, which loses the path the user typed and that the picker will return in M7; (c) store both forms. **Recommendation:** (a). It keeps the stored path the user's own, which is the reason `normalise()` does not resolve, and it fixes the false negative without weakening the escape check. Labels: `phase:1`, `constraint:local-first`.
- **Needs an issue filing — the network-share warning never fires in the ordinary first-run sequence.** `register()` calls `detect_filesystem()` for every accepted root, including one outside `ASKWELL_ROOTS_MOUNT`. For that root the container cannot see the path, so the longest matching entry in the container's `/proc/self/mounts` is `/` and the stored `filesystem` becomes `overlay`. It is never re-detected, so `network_share` stays false forever and `warning_for()` returns nothing. **Why it matters:** the ticket names this exact edge case — "a network share — permitted, with a stated warning that indexing will be slow and the share must be present at query time for the viewer" — and the sequence that breaks it is the *normal* one: nominate, be told to widen the mount, restart, index. The user gets no warning at the only moment it would have changed what they did. It also contradicts the module's own stated rule that `filesystem` is null when unknown and never invented. **Where it surfaced:** `api/src/askwell/roots.py`, `register()` and `detect_filesystem()`. **Options:** (a) skip detection when the probe returned `not_mounted`, storing null, and re-detect lazily on the first read where the state is `available`; (b) detect on every read and stop storing the column, matching how mount state is already handled; (c) leave it and accept a silently wrong warning. **Recommendation:** (b) — it is the decision already taken for mount state, for the same reason (a value nothing would ever correct), and it removes a column rather than adding a repair path. Labels: `phase:1`, `bug`.
- **Needs an issue filing — `probe()` blocks the event loop, and a disconnected network share blocks it for minutes.** `probe()` calls `os.scandir()` synchronously and is reached from `GET /roots`, `POST /roots` and `GET /roots/covering` without `asyncio.to_thread`. On a hard-mounted NFS or CIFS share that has gone away, that syscall does not return quickly and is not interruptible. **Why it matters:** it stops being one slow request and becomes the whole API — `/health` included — not answering, which is the failure the health surface exists to report. `AGENTS.md` §6 states the rule directly: no blocking calls in request handlers. Network shares are permitted by this ticket, so this is a supported configuration, not an exotic one. **Where it surfaced:** `api/src/askwell/roots.py`, `probe()`, `view()`, `listing()` and the three handlers in `register_roots()`. **Options:** (a) wrap each probe in `asyncio.to_thread` and bound it with `asyncio.wait_for`, reporting a timeout as `unavailable` — the existing `health_probe_timeout_seconds` is the precedent and the same shape; (b) probe off the request path on a timer and cache, which reintroduces the stale state the decision log rejects; (c) leave it. **Recommendation:** (a). Labels: `phase:1`, `bug`.
- **Needs an issue filing — none of the five `/roots` endpoints has an HTTP-level test.** `api/tests/test_roots.py` and `test_roots_registry.py` are thorough about the pure functions and the registry, and neither goes through the app. `test_app.py`, `test_interface.py` and `test_session.py` already establish the `httpx` + `ASGITransport` pattern, so this is untested rather than untestable. **Why it matters:** three things with no coverage are exactly the things most likely to be wrong. The route-ordering hazard the code comments on — `/roots/covering` must be registered before `/roots/{root_id}` or a literal segment is parsed as a UUID — is enforced only by the order of two decorators and would regress silently on a reorder. The registration `prompt` payload is what satisfies "a file outside every root prompts registration rather than failing obscurely", and nothing asserts its shape. And the deliberate 400-not-422 choice for a refusal is a decision with no test holding it. **Where it surfaced:** `api/src/askwell/roots.py`, `register_roots()`; `M1-ADD-ING-021` audit, 2026-08-27. **Options:** (a) add a `requires_db` module driving all five endpoints through `ASGITransport`, asserting 201/200/400/404 and the `prompt` body; (b) assert route order alone as a unit test, which is cheap and covers only one of the three. **Recommendation:** (a). Labels: `phase:1`, `bug`.
- [#49](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/49)–[#52](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/52) — contributor issues. #49 (does an 8 GB machine actually work?) is the riskiest unverified number in the project and needs hardware nobody here has.
- The build runner still refuses live runs by design. It needs a product gate to check its own work against, and M0 is what builds that gate. Unblock it when `AGENTS.md` §7.3 can be filled with commands that exist.

## The repositioning — read this before anything else

On **2026-08-10** the product was redefined. The previous documentation described a materially different product and much of it was wrong, not merely outdated.

| Was | Is |
| --- | --- |
| Sold to government ministries, hospitals, banks | Free download for one individual professional |
| Organisations, four roles, RBAC, seat tiers, LKR pricing | **Single user, single machine.** No roles, no tenancy, no licence |
| Quantum Plus product with a "Deployer" persona | Standalone venture by Suseenthiran Arulraj Rumeasiyan; users install it themselves |
| Air-gapped, no network calls, ever | Local by default; **online AI is an explicit per-conversation opt-in**, and it is the only revenue line |
| Documents + live database connections | Also **CSV and SQL dump import**, with a sandbox for dumps |
| Ingest silently | **Clarification loop and memory** — the differentiator |
| Multi-node HA for the top tier | Single machine only, permanently |

If you find text about ministries, organisations, roles, licences or seats anywhere, it is leftover and wrong. Fix it.

Note that "Quantum Plus" and the ministry framing were in the original `PRD.md` (commit `dcd12cf`), not introduced later.

## Documentation map

`PRD.md` is now **business only** — shareable with users and investors. Technical content moved out:

| Doc | Holds |
| --- | ----- |
| `PRD.md` | Business case, positioning, pricing, roadmap |
| `architecture.md` | Stack, topology, auth, data model, retrieval, security |
| `data-sources.md` | Files, CSV, dumps + sandbox, live connections |
| `memory-and-clarification.md` | The clarification loop and memory |
| `audit-log.md` | Three stores, retention, hash chain |
| `build-plan.md` | Phases, acceptance criteria, quality gate, repo layout |

## Open blockers

| # | Question | Blocks |
| - | -------- | ------ |
| [#9](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/9) | Stack versions stale — Next 15→16, Tailwind 4, Postgres 18 | **Phase 0 `web/`** |
| [#6](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/6) | Pin Python 3.12; dev machine has 3.14, no `podman-compose` | Phase 0 |

**All product decisions are answered.** Every decision issue #1–#5 and #10–#15 is closed. The three open issues are engineering tasks, not questions.

Questions raised by the rewrite were settled as defaults rather than handed back, since none needed a product call:

| Settled | Value | Where |
| ------- | ----- | ----- |
| Clarification cap per source | **5**, adjustable, with a documented ranking for what makes the cut | `memory-and-clarification.md` §8 |
| Log storage budget | **2 GB or 5% of free disk**, whichever smaller; 12-month interaction retention | `audit-log.md` §8 |
| Dump engines in v1 | **PostgreSQL only.** MySQL/SQL Server via live connection or CSV | `data-sources.md` §7 |
| Sandbox caps | 5 GB, 10 minutes per import | `data-sources.md` §7 |
| Telemetry | **None, not even opt-in**, through Phase 6 | `success-metrics.md` §6 |

**Everything open is now a tracked issue.** Nothing lives only in a document — a doc section with no owner is the same failure as a chat message with no owner, which `AGENTS.md` §8 exists to prevent.

| Waiting on you | Issue |
| -------------- | ----- |
| Code signing certificates — Apple and Windows, with lead times | [#42](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/42) |
| Update delivery | [#44](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/44) |
| What online mode transmits | [#45](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/45) |
| Credit pricing | [#46](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/46) |
| Trademark, and agreeing the support boundary | [#47](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/47) |
| The copy-review marker | [#40](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/40) |

Everything else that was open in a document has been **decided and recorded where it belongs** — STT placement, PDF rendering, scan highlighting, passphrase-and-backup, trace retention and score presentation, audio retention, folder watching, Excel sheets and merged headers, clarification ranking and bulk patterns, bulk confirm, manual model install, suggested questions, margin scrolling, conversation paging, per-source storage. Four items are deferred with a reason rather than left open: voice escalation, re-asking an escalated question locally, editing a past question, and memory import/export.

Three need real data and cannot be answered by thinking: the clarification cap of 5, the retention targets, and the abstention band.

## Build procedure

Running the concept-to-build procedure over the existing docs. **Its phase numbers are not `build-plan.md` phase numbers.**

| Step | State |
| ---- | ----- |
| P0/P1 — spec, metrics, states | **done**, then redone after the repositioning |
| P2 — design the screens, in `docs/ux/` | **done** — design system + 10 screens |
| Review the data model against what the screens need | **done** — #20 closed |
| P6 — full ticket backlog | **done** — 177 tickets in `docs/backlog/`, superseding the `docs/stories/` sample format |
| Build M0 | **unblocked** — docs, specs, designs and backlog all locked to current decisions |

Screens before schema is deliberate: drawing a screen surfaces the missing button and the number with nowhere to come from.
