## Session log

**2026-09-20 (`M5-TRACE-FE-119`)** — The trace panel: "How did you get this?" over Ask, not a page, from a toggle under any answer (streaming, completed, abstained, stopped or failed). New `web/lib/trace.ts` (`fetchTrace`, `stepSummary`, `formatDuration`, `hasExpandableDetail`, `traceRows`) turns `GET /ask/{message_id}/trace`'s raw, per-`kind` step objects — a shape that differs across `retrieve`/`abstain`/`memory_retrieve`/`inline_clarification`/`compose`/`schema`/`sql`/`tool` and was never designed to be read by a browser before `M5-TRACE-BE-125` — into a numbered sequence with a plain-language summary and a duration per step; raw detail (the step's own JSON, verbatim, per C4: never recomputed or reformatted) expands underneath rather than in a separate mode, and a step with nothing beyond `kind`/timing/`outcome` (e.g. `{"kind": "sql", "outcome": "no_connections"}`) gets no expander at all, the ticket's own stated edge case. New `web/components/ask/trace-panel.tsx` (`TraceToggle`/`TracePanel`) follows `rail-drawer.tsx`'s existing overlay pattern (a backdrop plus a focus-managed `role="dialog"` panel, closed by Escape or the backdrop, focus returned to the toggle) rather than inventing a second one; wired under both `LiveTurn` and `CollapsedTurn`'s expanded view in `ask-screen.tsx`, so a live-streaming and a past, re-expanded turn share one component. A panel opened on a still-running turn polls the endpoint every second and stops the moment the fetched trace's own `status` is no longer `"running"` — verified against a real, completed trace read back through the running stack (`GET /ask/{id}/trace`, session-cookie-authenticated via `curl -H "Accept: text/html"` first) rather than only fabricated fixtures, which is also what caught that the server's own `ms` field is a float (`12544.67...`) needing rounding, not the clean integer a hand-written fixture would have used. A rotated trace (`docs/architecture.md` §7.1's ring buffer, `trace_rotated: true`) shows the spec's own "cleared" copy instead of an empty step list, which would otherwise read identically. A local, untransmitted open-count (C1) matches `lib/ask.ts`'s existing `inlineClarificationsShownCount` shape. 17 new tests in `web/lib/trace.test.ts` (added to `web/package.json`'s explicit test list, not a glob); `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (267 lib tests, lint/typecheck/build/contrast/offline) and `scripts/dev.sh check` (842 passed, 1 skipped, no backend files touched) both clean.

Issue #425 (a tool-loop turn's `GET /ask/{id}/trace` returns no steps for the whole duration of the loop, since `askwell.ask` only extends `trace_steps` once `run_tool_loop` returns) is confirmed live rather than theoretical now that a real client — this panel — actually polls mid-turn, and re-owned with that confirmation rather than fixed: the fix is backend-only (`M5-LOOP-BE-117a`'s existing `ToolCallObserver` appending incrementally instead), explicitly out of this ticket's own scope ("Changing what `M5-LOOP-BE-117` records" per issue #425's own body). Not built, per the ticket's own Out of Scope: contents detail — formatting what is *inside* a step's raw JSON (scores against the threshold, a clickable passage, syntax-highlighted SQL) — is `M5-TRACE-FE-120`; clicking a passage, clicking a memory fact, and the threshold-adjustment control are `M5-TRACE-FE-121`. No browser available in this session, so the panel was not visually screenshotted — the summarization logic was verified directly against a real trace payload from the running stack instead. `0.4.35` → `0.4.36`.

**2026-09-19 (`M4-RESULT-FE-111`)** — The fifth and last database state, "no connections configured", closing the `SQL` epic and M4's own `RESULT` chain (`-109`, `-110`, `-111`). Four of the ticket's five states already had distinct copy from earlier tickets — `unreachable` (`askwell.sql_execute.UNREACHABLE_MESSAGE`), `timeout` (`StatementTimedOut`), `rejected` (`validate_query`'s refusal text), `zero_rows` (`_sql_result_text`, `M4-RESULT-FE-109`) — so this ticket's own work was the fifth, plus the routing that decides when it fires.

New `askwell.ask._no_database_answer`, called only from `_run_generation`'s document-abstention branch (never from `_run_sql_turn`, and never for a source-scoped question) — this is issue #400's own recommended fix (option 3), and the reason it closes that issue rather than merely working around it: a database-shaped question is answered from the user's documents whenever they actually cover it, and is only ever reported as unconnected once document retrieval has genuinely abstained. The keyword short-circuit #400 described (a wide word list intercepting a question ahead of document retrieval) turned out not to exist in the code as it stands — `_run_sql_turn`'s `NO_DATABASES`/`NOT_A_DATABASE_QUESTION` branches already fall through unconditionally, most likely because `M4-SQL-BE-103`'s schema-note-based source selection replaced whatever the issue's original snapshot did — but the "no connections configured" state the surrounding ticket needed had never actually been built, and this closes both at once: the state now exists, and it exists in the one shape that cannot reproduce #400's failure mode.

`_looks_database_shaped` is deliberately narrow — `"database"`/`"sql"` literal mentions only, not the wide list (`"table"`, `"records"`, `"total"`, `"count of"`) #400 flagged as the root cause. Recall is sacrificed for precision on two counts at once: a wide list matches ordinary prose about the user's own documents, and it matches the abstention eval's own near-miss questions ("What was Meridian Loom's total revenue in Q2?", "...headcount in the Support department?") — firing on either of those would either mislabel a real "not in your documents" case or corrupt `eval.abstain.abstain_score`'s exact-prefix scoring for a real, unrelated abstention. `api/tests/test_ask_looks_database_shaped.py` asserts directly against `eval/suites/abstention.v1.json`'s own prompts (copied verbatim, not paraphrased) so a future widening of the list is caught before it reaches the eval.

Two edge cases from the same helper, both new: `_non_ready_sql_sources` (name/status/last_error for every `dump`/`csv`/`connection` source that is not `ready`) lets a relevant-looking question distinguish "still importing" from "needs attention" from "nothing at all" — three different fixes, three different messages — and names which source when several exist. A `ready` database source that simply did not match the question's schema returns `None` from `_no_database_answer` (a genuine "not about your data" case, not a connection problem) rather than being folded into any of the three.

`db_state` is a new field on the `done` event (both branches — always `None` from a turn `_run_sql_turn` itself answered) and on `AskTurn`, read by a new pure decision, `web/lib/ask.ts`'s `addSourceActionLabel`: `AbstentionState`'s one add-source control (`ask.md`'s own "exactly one control until `M6.5-WEB-FE-186`" constraint, which ruled out a second button) is relabelled "Connect a database" for `no_connections`, and dropped entirely for `source_importing`/`source_attention` — adding a new source fixes neither of those. `web/components/settings/connections.tsx`'s empty state gained the `states-and-edge-cases.md` §7 copy it was missing (what connecting enables, that credentials must be read-only), alongside the existing network-activity-count clarification rather than replacing it.

18 new tests: 5 pure (`test_ask_looks_database_shaped.py`, including the near-miss-eval-prompt assertions), 6 `requires_db` (`test_ask_sql.py` — the three `_no_database_answer` branches, the "ready source exists but does not match" `None` case, and the five-messages-never-collide validation rule), 2 end-to-end through `POST /ask` (`test_ask_api.py` — a real empty corpus plus a database-shaped question gets the new message and `db_state`; the same question against a real, clearly-matching seeded document is answered from it, `db_state: null`, proving the override never intercepts a real document answer), and 5 web (`ask.test.ts`, `addSourceActionLabel`'s three branches). `scripts/dev.sh check` (798 passed, 1 skipped), `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (617 passed against the real, running stack), and `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (247 tests, lint/typecheck/build/contrast/offline all clean) all green.

**Not verified**: a live cold-start-to-browser walkthrough — this environment's native inference supervisor is stopped (`GET /health` reports `inference: unreachable`, embedding role `restarts: 142`), the same gap every `RESULT`/`SQL`-epic ticket this session has hit for the identical reason (`M4-RESULT-FE-109`/`-110`, issue #376). Recorded rather than claimed: the two `test_ask_api.py` tests above are the substitute this codebase's own convention uses for that gap — a real `TestClient` against a real, running Postgres, faking only the one step nothing here can run (the model call itself), which is stronger than a curl walkthrough would have been for the retrieval half of this ticket in particular, since `compose_abstention`/`_no_database_answer` need no model at all. Closed issue #400 with the account above. `0.4.25` → `0.4.26`.

**2026-09-18 (`M4-SQL-DB-107`)** — Independent read-only role and statement timeout, the sixth `SQL` epic ticket by number but the first built — `M4-SQL-BE-103`/`VAL-104`–`106` do not exist yet, and this ticket's own dependencies (`M4-DUMP-DEPLOY-087`, `M4-CONN-SEC-097`, `M0-DATA-DB-014`) named none of them. New `askwell.sql_execute.execute_sandbox_query`/`execute_connection_query`: a query runs as `askwell_sandbox_readonly` for a sandbox source (never `askwell_sandbox_owner`, C3) or as a live connection's own credential — the one `M4-CONN-SEC-097`'s write-permission probe already refused if it could write — with a `Settings.sql_statement_timeout_seconds`-second (default 30, new `ASKWELL_SQL_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) statement timeout applied for that session alone, on every one of the three engines `askwell.connections` already supports: Postgres's `SET statement_timeout` (milliseconds), MySQL's `SET SESSION MAX_EXECUTION_TIME` (milliseconds, `SELECT`-only — the only statement shape a read-only role runs anyway), MariaDB's `SET SESSION max_statement_time` (a distinct session variable, seconds, every statement type), and SQL Server's connect-time `timeout` (`python-tds` has no per-statement `SET` for this at all). A cancellation raises `StatementTimedOut`, carrying the query, the configured timeout and how long it actually ran, with a message naming both and suggesting narrowing the query; each engine's own cancellation error was checked against the installed driver rather than assumed — `psycopg.errors.QueryCanceled` (SQLSTATE `57014`), `pymysql`'s `ER.QUERY_TIMEOUT` (3024) vs `ER.STATEMENT_TIMEOUT` (1969), `pytds.TimeoutError`. A timeout writes a decisions record (query, timeout, duration — duration stored in milliseconds as an integer, never the raw float, since `audit.canonical_payload` refuses a float outright: `jsonb` does not round-trip one identically, and a hash computed before that round trip would disagree with one recomputed after it) and increments a local, never-transmitted Redis counter, best-effort, the same shape `connections.record_write_probe_refusal` already established.

New `askwell.sandbox.verify_readonly_role`/`SandboxRoleMisconfigured`: checks `pg_roles` for `rolsuper`/`rolcreatedb`/`rolcreaterole`/`rolbypassrls` on `askwell_sandbox_readonly` (or a role name passed in, for testing) rather than attempting a write — a role *attribute* is not scoped to one database the way `create_database`'s existing `SELECT`-only default-privilege grant is, so it is the one category of escalation no per-database check could ever catch, and it is exactly what `deploy/sandbox/10-roles.sh` already sets the role's four `NO*` attributes to guard. Wired into `askwell.worker.startup` as a *second*, separate try/except placed after the existing `embed.check_dimension`/`ingest.resume`/`reconcile` block, not folded into it: the sandbox instance being merely not up yet — the ordinary case on a laptop, same as the main database — must not skip the main database's own resume and dimension check, which do not depend on the sandbox at all; only `SandboxRoleMisconfigured` itself is fatal, propagated out of `startup` the same way `embed.EmbeddingDimensionMismatch` already is, so arq's own crash-loop-on-`on_startup`-failure is what actually refuses to start. Getting this ordering wrong the first time broke an existing test (`test_embed_records.py::test_worker_startup_refuses_to_start_on_a_dimension_mismatch`) whose fixture's `sandbox_database_url` is deliberately unroutable — the sandbox-role check, if run first inside the same try, swallowed the dimension mismatch's own fatal exception into the ordinary "database not up yet" path before `check_dimension` ever ran; moving it to its own try after resolved it without weakening either check.

"The role cannot write" itself needed no new test against the driver — `test_sandbox.py::test_readonly_cannot_write` (predates this ticket) already proves that directly. New tests: `test_sandbox.py` gained three for `verify_readonly_role` (the real role passes; a role that does not exist refuses; a temporary role granted `CREATEDB` — never the real `askwell_sandbox_readonly`, so a run's own misconfiguration test cannot break the next test to run — refuses, dropped in a `finally`). New `test_sql_execute.py` (pure — `StatementTimedOut`'s message) and `test_sql_execute_db.py` (`requires_db` — reads rows correctly, cannot write through the module either, a `SELECT pg_sleep(5)` against a 1s configured timeout is cancelled and reported as `StatementTimedOut` naming the query, and the resulting decisions row is confirmed with the right `source_kind`/`timeout_seconds`). The `settings` fixture in the new `requires_db` file needed the *real* `TEST_SANDBOX_READONLY_PASSWORD`, not the placeholder `test_sandbox.py`'s own `session` fixture uses — that placeholder works there only because `create_database`/`drop_database` never read it, while `execute_sandbox_query` genuinely connects as the role and authenticates with it. `scripts/dev.sh check` (715 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (510 passed) both clean.

Verified against the real, rebuilt, cold-started stack rather than only the suite: the worker started cleanly logging `worker_resumed` with the real, correctly-configured role. `ALTER ROLE askwell_sandbox_readonly CREATEDB` against the running sandbox instance, followed by `podman compose restart worker`, produced a crash loop — the traceback ends in `askwell.sandbox.SandboxRoleMisconfigured: 'askwell_sandbox_readonly' carries a privilege a read-only execution role must never have (superuser=False, createdb=True, createrole=False, bypassrls=False)`, and arq restarts the process into the same failure rather than ever reaching `worker_resumed`; `ALTER ROLE askwell_sandbox_readonly NOCREATEDB` followed by another restart resumed normally. Out of scope, per the ticket's own "Granularity: one role and one timeout": query cost estimation, and any wiring into an actual model-generated-query path, since `M4-SQL-BE-103`/`VAL-104`–`106` do not exist yet for this to be wired into — `execute_sandbox_query`/`execute_connection_query` are built for those tickets to call, not called by anything yet. `0.4.11` → `0.4.12`.

**2026-09-18 (`M4-SCHEMA-ING-100`)** — Full schema introspection: types, keys and relationships, for a live connection and a loaded sandbox database (dump or CSV) alike, on connect, on import, and now on demand. New `askwell.schema_introspect`: `SchemaInventory`/`Table`/`Column`/`ForeignKey`, `describe_table`/`describe_column` (plain-language, the `st_cd` shape `docs/data-sources.md` §5 asks for), per-engine bulk introspection (`_introspect_postgresql_blocking`/`_introspect_mysql_blocking`/`_introspect_sqlserver_blocking` — one query per shape per engine, not one per table, for the "thousands of tables" edge case), and `write_schema_inventory`, which writes/updates `schema_notes` idempotently: an unchanged inferred note is left alone, a changed one is superseded by its actual replacement (not a self-supersede — that is reserved for a table or column no longer present at all), and a user-supplied note is never touched. No new retrieval path — the existing `askwell.memory.retrieve_relevant_facts` (lexical, bounded to 5 notes per question) already ranks `schema_notes` by relevance to a question.

Introspection always runs as a read-only role. A live connection reuses the credential `M4-CONN-SEC-097`'s write-permission probe already verified; a sandbox database is read as a new `askwell_sandbox_readonly` DSN (`askwell.sandbox.readonly_url`, new required `Settings.sandbox_readonly_password`/`ASKWELL_SANDBOX_READONLY_PASSWORD`, propagated through `compose.yaml` and `.env.example` the same way `M4-DUMP-ING-088`'s owner password was) rather than the owner role that loaded it. For PostgreSQL, `pg_class` joined to `has_table_privilege` finds every table/view/matview regardless of privilege and says which are actually selectable, so a table the readonly role cannot see is counted (`omitted_count`) without ever being named. MySQL and SQL Server expose no privilege-independent catalog to compare against, so `omitted_count` is honestly `None` for those two engines rather than a fabricated zero — filed as issue #353 (no live test coverage either, since no MySQL/SQL Server container exists in this stack) rather than guessed at.

`askwell.connections.run_introspection` and `askwell.dump_import.import_dump` each gained a second pass, `_run_deep_introspection`, right after their existing shallow one — a failure there is logged and recorded (`schema_introspection_failed` decisions row) but never flips a working source back to `attention`, since the shallow pass already proved the connection or the load itself works. New `POST /sources/{id}/reintrospect` (`connection`/`dump`/`csv` only — a `file` source uses the existing `/reindex`) and worker job `reintrospect_source_job` cover "on demand": it dispatches to `connections.run_introspection` (already reconnect-safe, whether the source was just added or has been `ready` for months) or the new `schema_introspect.reintrospect_sandbox_source` depending on the source's own `kind`.

Two real bugs, caught only by running this against a real, restricted Postgres role (`AGENTS.md` §4's own "run it" rule) rather than by review or a mocked cursor — `docs/decisions.md` has the full account of both. First: `information_schema.table_constraints`/`key_column_usage`, the standard-SQL way to ask "what is this table's primary key", silently returned zero rows for `askwell_sandbox_readonly` even though `has_table_privilege(..., 'SELECT')` was `true` and `information_schema.columns` worked fine for the same table — Postgres's own constraint views gate on more than table-level `SELECT`. Fixed by reading `pg_catalog.pg_index`/`pg_constraint` directly (table and constraint *metadata* carries no such extra privilege gate; only `has_table_privilege` is used to decide *visibility*, which is correct and stays as designed). Second: `askwell.connections.record_introspection` — real, tested, but never exercised more than once per source before this ticket's own on-demand re-introspection made a second call possible — wrote a second, competing active "Table X." placeholder note on every repeat call instead of leaving an already-noted table alone, discovered live by re-introspecting a real connection twice and finding two active notes for the same table. Fixed: idempotent on repeat calls now, with a new regression test (`test_recording_introspection_twice_never_duplicates_the_table_note`) locking it in.

12 new/extended tests: `test_schema_introspect.py` (pure — description text, all three engines' row-grouping from fabricated rows, including the omitted-object edge case's own grouping logic; `requires_db` — `write_schema_inventory`'s insert/no-op/supersede/stale-removal/user-note-preserved behaviour against Askwell's own database, and `_introspect_postgresql_blocking`/`reintrospect_sandbox_source` against a real sandbox database with a table the readonly role cannot see), a new assertion in `test_dump_import.py`'s existing full-import test (real column/type/key notes appear after a real dump loads), a new regression test in `test_connections_db.py`, and a session-boundary test in `test_sources_api.py`. `scripts/dev.sh check` (710 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (503 passed) both clean.

Verified against the real, rebuilt, cold-started stack, not just the suite: imported a real PostgreSQL dump (two related tables, a foreign key) through `POST /sources/dump` and confirmed correct table/column/PK/FK `schema_notes` after; connected to a second real Postgres database through `POST /sources/connection` and confirmed the same; added a table to that live database's schema, called the new `/reintrospect` endpoint twice in a row, and confirmed the new table appeared with exactly one active note per position both times (the duplication bug above was caught and fixed during exactly this walkthrough); revoked `SELECT` on a table from `askwell_sandbox_readonly` directly and confirmed it was counted as omitted and never named. `0.4.10` → `0.4.11`.

**2026-09-18 (`M4-CONN-SEC-098`)** — Encrypting stored connection credentials at rest, the last `CONN` ticket. New `askwell.crypto`: `load_or_create_install_secret` (32 random bytes, generated once, owner-only permissions, at `Settings.install_secret_path` — default `/run/askwell/install.key`, the same host bind mount `api`/`worker` already share for the inference socket, deliberately outside `postgres-data`); `derive_key` (HKDF-SHA256, extensible to an optional passphrase without re-deriving anything stored — M7's own acceptance requirement); `encrypt`/`decrypt` (Fernet); `CredentialsLocked`, raised on a wrong key or a malformed secret file rather than exposing a raw crypto error. `create_connection_source` (now takes `settings`) encrypts before insert; `run_introspection` decrypts before ever calling `probe_connection`, so a locked credential is caught before any socket opens and reported as a new `credentials_locked` reason code — the source moves to `attention` with a message that explicitly says the database was never contacted, never misreported as `host_unresolved` (the same "wrong failure reads like a wrong host" trap #345 already documents for a different case). `cryptography>=44.0` (resolved 50.0.1, verified against PyPI) is a new runtime dependency; `.env.example` gained `ASKWELL_INSTALL_SECRET_PATH`. 7 new tests in `test_crypto.py` (round-trip, wrong-key failure, passphrase changes the key, malformed secret locks, owner-only permissions) and 3 new `requires_db` tests in `test_connections_db.py` (stored configuration is unreadable without the key, a different install secret cannot decrypt it, a lost secret locks `run_introspection` rather than attempting a doomed connection); all existing `create_connection_source` call sites and tests updated to thread `settings` through. `scripts/dev.sh check` and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (495 passed) both clean. Verified against the real, cold-started stack rather than only the suite: created a live connection through `POST /sources/connection` against the sandbox Postgres (a scratch read-only role granted for this walkthrough, dropped after), read `config_encrypted` back with `psql` as opaque Fernet ciphertext with no plaintext password or host substring, confirmed the source reached `ready` normally; deleted `.run/install.key` and re-ran `run_introspection` directly inside the `worker` container — the source moved to `attention` with the locked message, and no connection attempt was made. Issue #345 (egress permits no live-connection destination at all) is unchanged and stays open, re-owned again — encryption is real and testable regardless of whether #345 has landed, but a cold-start walkthrough against a real *external* database still cannot reach any of this until #345 does. `0.4.9` → `0.4.10`.

**2026-09-18 (`M4-DUMP-VAL-089`)** — Size and time caps that abort a dump import and drop the sandbox, the third `DUMP` ticket. `api/src/askwell/dump_import.py` gained `get_dump_size_cap_bytes`/`set_dump_size_cap_bytes` and `get_dump_time_cap_seconds`/`set_dump_time_cap_seconds` (settings-table-backed, defaults 5 GB / 600s, every change a `dump_cap_changed` decisions record — the same shape `askwell.clarify.set_clarification_cap` already established) and `DumpCapExceeded(DumpImportFailed)`. Enforcement lives in `_load_blocking`: a daemon `threading.Thread` watchdog runs for the whole life of the `psql` subprocess, polling `pg_database_size` on the sandbox database itself (never `dump_path`'s size on disk, so a dump that expands once loaded is still caught) on a `CAP_POLL_SECONDS` cadence and the elapsed wall clock on every tick, killing `psql` outright the moment either is crossed. This replaced an earlier, simpler design that only checked between chunks read from `dump_path` — rejected once it became clear most of a real import's time is spent blocked *inside* `proc.stdin.write` (pipe full, `psql` busy on a statement) or the final `proc.wait()` once every byte is already fed, neither of which a between-chunks check ever runs during; a watchdog thread has no such blind spot (`docs/decisions.md` has the full account). A final, unconditional check runs right after `psql` exits regardless of outcome, closing the race where a load finishes faster than the watchdog's first poll — which is not a contrived case: a freshly created, empty sandbox database's own catalog overhead is already several MB, so a small size cap can be exceeded before a single byte of the dump is fed in. A breach reuses `import_dump`'s existing failure path unchanged (drop `WITH (FORCE)` — which is also what actually terminates a statement still running server-side, since killing the client only stops feeding it more — mark `attention`, record `dump_import_failed`), with a new `cap` field on that payload standing in for the ticket's own "local counter of aborted imports" (C1): a count over `audit_decisions` rather than a maintained integer, the same shape every other local counter in this codebase already takes. 11 new tests in `test_dump_import.py`, three pure-Python against a fake `psql` (no database, no network) including one proving a fake 5s sleep is killed in well under 3s by a 0.1s time cap, and eight `requires_db`: both caps' get/set/validate, a real size-cap abort, a real time-cap abort against `SELECT pg_sleep(5);`, and the both-caps-at-once edge case (size wins, since it is checked first and the first poll always runs regardless of the poll interval). `scripts/dev.sh check` (606 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (473 passed) both clean. Verified against the real, rebuilt, cold-started stack rather than only the suite, running `import_dump` directly inside the `api` container: a 1 KB size cap against a real two-row dump aborted immediately, named "loaded data reached 7.6 MB, over the size cap of 1.0 KB", dropped the sandbox database, and `known_databases` matched exactly before and after; raising the cap to 5 GB then imported the identical dump to `ready`; a 1s time cap against a real `SELECT pg_sleep(8);` aborted after 1.31s naming the time cap, not after the full 8 seconds. One bug `test-db` caught that review did not: `set_dump_time_cap_seconds`'s audit payload carried `previous`/`new` as `float`, which `audit.compute_hash` refuses outright because a float does not round-trip identically through jsonb (a later chain verification would report tampering that never happened) — fixed by storing both as strings; `docs/decisions.md` has the account. `0.4.4` → `0.4.5`.

**2026-09-18 (`M4-DUMP-ING-088`)** — Import a PostgreSQL dump into its own sandbox database, the second `DUMP` ticket. `api/src/askwell/dump_import.py`: `create_dump_source` registers a `dump` source; `import_dump` generates a sandbox database (`askwell.sandbox.create_database`), streams the dump into `psql` as `askwell_sandbox_owner` (`--set ON_ERROR_STOP=1`, chunked so progress has something to measure — `psql` gives none of its own, `stderr` spooled to a temp file rather than a pipe to avoid the classic deadlock a broken dump's `NOTICE` volume can cause), introspects `information_schema.tables` for the loaded table names, then seals the owner role off the database and marks the source `ready`. Any failure — bad SQL, a `CREATE ROLE` the restricted owner cannot run, anything `ON_ERROR_STOP=1` catches — drops the sandbox database outright and records the reason on `sources.last_error`; nothing is ever left half-loaded. Closed **issue #330** the way its own recommendation named (option 1): new `askwell.sandbox.seal_owner` revokes `askwell_sandbox_owner`'s `CONNECT` on a database the instant its load succeeds, so the role that runs the *next* dump's content can never simultaneously reach an earlier one — verified live by importing two dumps back to back and confirming the owner role refuses to connect to the first once the second has started. An import interrupted by a stack restart (`sources.status = 'indexing'`, `sandbox_db` still set, nothing left to finish it) is reclaimed at worker startup by the new `dump_import.reclaim_interrupted`, wired into the same `_reclaim_sandbox_orphans` helper `askwell.sandbox.reclaim_orphans` already used — a different case from an orphan (a database no source references at all), not the same one twice. Full schema introspection (types, keys, relationships, retrieval-indexing) stays `M4-SCHEMA-ING-100`, which depends on this ticket; recorded as a decision so the boundary is deliberate rather than a stub. New required `Settings.sandbox_owner_password` (`ASKWELL_SANDBOX_OWNER_PASSWORD`) — the owner role's own credential, distinct from the sandbox superuser `sandbox_database_url` already carried — propagated through `compose.yaml` (api and worker), `.env`/`.env.example`, and every test fixture that constructs `Settings` directly; `conftest_db.py`'s migration harness needed it too, since `load_settings()` now requires it before `alembic upgrade head` can even run under `test-db`. `postgresql-client` added to the API image for `psql` (package name and 15.19/bookworm version checked against packages.debian.org first, `AGENTS.md` §4 — the sandbox server itself is pg18, an ordinary older-client-to-newer-server combination). 3 new tests in `test_sandbox.py` (`owner_url`, `seal_owner` × 2), a new `test_dump_import.py` (pure `_load_blocking` tests against a fake `psql` on `PATH`, no network; `requires_db` tests for a full successful import, a failed one, the two-dumps-cannot-reach-each-other proof, and `reclaim_interrupted`). `scripts/dev.sh check` (605 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (467 passed) both clean. Verified against the real, cold-started stack rather than only the suite: enqueued a real two-row dump through the running `worker` container, watched it create the sandbox database, load it, introspect `['widgets']`, seal the owner and mark the source `ready`; confirmed `askwell_sandbox_readonly` could read the loaded rows and `askwell_sandbox_owner` could no longer connect at all; cleaned up the scratch source and database afterwards. Not built, per the ticket's own scope: the caps (`M4-DUMP-VAL-089`), the add-source screen wiring and copy (`M4-DUMP-FE-090`) — a `.sql`/`.dump`/`.backup` source still shows `Verdict.LATER` at add time until `-090` lands and calls the new `import_dump_job` worker function. Closed issue #321 (`M4-DUMP-DEPLOY-087`'s own tracking issue, fully built and verified in the prior session but never closed) and #330 (this ticket's fix) with comments naming what resolved each. `0.4.3` → `0.4.4`.

**2026-09-18 (`M4-DUMP-DEPLOY-087`)** — First M4-`DUMP` ticket: the sandbox Postgres instance C3 requires before anything can import. `compose.yaml` gained a `sandbox` service (same `pgvector/pgvector:pg18` image), its own `sandbox-data` volume, and a `sandbox` network joined only by `api`/`worker` — not `postgres`, `redis`, or `egress-proxy`, so C3 is a network boundary rather than a promise. `deploy/sandbox/10-roles.sh` creates two fixed roles, `askwell_sandbox_owner`/`askwell_sandbox_readonly`, and locks `PUBLIC`'s `CONNECT` on `template1`/`postgres` plus both large-object APIs. `api/src/askwell/sandbox.py`: `generate_name`/`InvalidSandboxName` (names are generated, never trusted from a caller — C2's own reasoning), `create_database`/`drop_database` (each now `async`, take Askwell's own `AsyncSession`, and write an `audit_decisions` row — a gap a same-session code review, filed as #322, caught before this closed), `reclaim_orphans` (runs from `worker.startup`, best-effort, drops any sandbox database no live `sources.sandbox_db` claims). New `sandbox` health component; `Settings.sandbox_database_url` required. Two things cost real time and are in `docs/decisions.md`: `CREATE DATABASE` does **not** inherit `template1`'s `pg_database.datacl` (a shared catalog, not part of a template's file copy) — `create_database` revokes `PUBLIC`'s `CONNECT` on its own database explicitly rather than relying on the instance-wide revoke — while `pg_proc` (function privileges, what makes the large-object revokes work) **does** propagate; and the two fixed roles accumulate `CONNECT` across every sandbox database they have been granted onto, an accepted limit for this ticket's scope (no importer exists yet) filed as #330 for `M4-DUMP-ING-088` to close. `scripts/dev.sh test-db`/CI's `database` job now bring up a second, separate Postgres service so `test_sandbox.py`'s `requires_db` tests run against a real instance rather than a mock. 16 new tests in `test_sandbox.py`, plus fixture/config-required-field updates across `test_config.py`/`test_health.py`/`test_app.py`/`test_roots.py`/`test_reapply.py`/`test_env_example.py`. `scripts/dev.sh check` (600 passed, 1 skipped) and `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (459 passed) both clean. Verified against the real, cold-started stack rather than only the suite: `GET /health` reports `sandbox` independently; from inside the `sandbox` container neither `postgres` nor `egress-proxy` resolve at all (no route, confirmed with `getent hosts`), while `api` resolves `sandbox` and `postgres` cannot resolve `sandbox`; `scripts/verify-localhost-binding.sh` confirms nothing beyond `api` is published. Live-exercised inside the running `api` container: `askwell_sandbox_owner` refused creating a superuser, creating a database, `COPY ... TO PROGRAM`, `lo_import`, and `lo_creat`; refused connecting to `postgres`; `askwell_sandbox_readonly` could read what the owner created and was refused writing. Killed the worker mid-"import" (a sandbox database with no `sources` row committed) and confirmed the next worker startup dropped it and wrote a `sandbox_database_dropped` (`reason: orphaned_at_startup`) decisions record. Out of scope, per the ticket: importing anything (`M4-DUMP-ING-088`) and the size/time cap (`M4-DUMP-VAL-089`). Issue #321's own body claimed this was already built and verified before this session started; it was not (no matching file, no matching commit) — corrected in a comment on that issue rather than trusted. `0.4.2` → `0.4.3`.

**2026-09-17 (`M3-REVIEW-FE-072`)** — The clarifications screen as a single reviewable list. `web/lib/clarifications.ts` reads `GET /clarifications` (`review.py`, `M3-REVIEW-BE-072a`) unchanged — grouping stays server-side, this only carries it. `ClarificationsScreen` at `/clarifications`: groups by source with a per-group count and a total at the top, the `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §5 "None pending" teaching copy when the queue is empty, no navigation between items. Two entry points added: a `Clarifications` destination in `Rail` with a plain count badge (`useClarificationsTotal`, polled every 10s, hidden at zero — never a red dot), and `ClarificationsPrompt` in the shell's banner slot, which watches `subscribeIngest` for the queue going idle and shows a one-line dismissible line once per idle transition rather than repeating. `evidence` came back from the API as a JSON object (`{"occurrences": 3}`), not a string as first assumed — the type was wrong until a manual `psql` insert against the real endpoint caught it; item anatomy (formatting `evidence` properly) is `M3-REVIEW-FE-073`'s own scope, so it renders via `JSON.stringify` for now rather than inventing formatting this ticket does not own. Answering and skipping are deliberately absent — `-073`/`-074`, Out of Scope here. 4 new lib tests (`clarifications.test.ts`, added to `web/package.json`'s explicit test list — it is not a glob); `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (181 tests, lint/typecheck/build/contrast/offline) and `scripts/dev.sh test` (534 passed, 1 skipped) both clean. Verified against the real stack: `GET /clarifications` returns `{"groups":[],"total":0}` cold, and correctly grouped output after a manual `psql` insert against a live, non-deleted source (cleaned up after). No browser available in this session, so the hydrated list/badge/prompt were not visually screenshotted — the API contracts each reads, the SSR shell markup (rail entry present, badge absent at zero), and the component logic were confirmed correct instead. `0.3.4` → `0.3.5`.

**2026-08-28 (`M2-EVAL-TEST-063`)** — Ported the eval harness, offline. `eval/bench.py` loads a suite from `eval/suites/*.json`, runs each task through `InferenceClient.generate` (the product's own Unix-socket path — no separate network story needed), a fixed three times per task (`RUNS_PER_TASK`, not a flag), and reports mean/worst-of-3 per category or strict pass/fail for a `pass_bar == 1.0` suite. `InferenceUnavailable` aborts the whole run with a named reason and writes nothing; a per-run `InferenceFailed` is recorded with its reason and the task continues — the two edge cases the ticket named by name. Results carry the model actually loaded (from the supervisor's `state.json`), the profile, and every prompt file's version. New `scripts/dev.sh eval` command, mounting `.run/` at `/run/askwell` with `--network=none`; runs from `/app/api` rather than `/app/api`'s sibling repo root, because `Settings` reading `.env` from the repo root picks up raw `POSTGRES_*` variables Compose interpolates for other services and refuses to start. `eval/suites/smoke.v1.json` is a two-task fixture proving the harness works, not one of the eight real categories — those (`M2-EVAL-TEST-064` onward) and the CI gate (`M2-EVAL-DEPLOY-067`) are out of scope, per the ticket. 28 tests in `eval/tests/` (own `eval/pyproject.toml` — `eval/` sits outside `api/pyproject.toml`'s `testpaths`; run via `scripts/dev.sh run pytest /app/eval/tests`, not yet part of `scripts/dev.sh check`), all against a fake `InferenceClient`. Verified against the real stack twice: against the stopped native supervisor, `scripts/dev.sh eval --suite smoke.v1` failed with `model unavailable: The assistant is stopped.` and wrote no file; started `scripts/dev.sh inference` natively, re-ran — `Qwen3.5-4B-Q4_K_M.gguf`, mean 1.00, worst-of-3 1.00 on both tasks, ~2m20s for six generations — then stopped the supervisor again. `scripts/dev.sh check` (529 passed, 1 skipped) stayed clean. Filed as issue [#236](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/236). `0.2.49` → `0.2.50`.

**2026-08-28 (`M2-DELETE-FE-062`)** — Deletion confirmation and the deleted-source citation card. `DeleteControl` on each library row confirms against a source (the library is grouped by source, `docs/ux/library.md` §2 — not by document, despite the ticket's own confirmation-copy example naming a file; recorded as a decision), states the three facts, calls `DELETE /sources/{id}`. Two backend gaps this ticket's own dependencies had not closed: `sources.deleted_at` did not exist (migration `5f3a7c1e9d42`) and `askwell.ingest.coverage` was filtering deleted sources out of the snapshot the library reads — both fixed, both recorded as a decision. `LibraryFilters.showDeleted` (default off) brings them back. The provenance margin's cards now live-check `GET /documents/{id}` per citation and render a greyed "Deleted on `<date>`" card, unclickable, when it answers deleted; the viewer does the same via `DeletedSourceNotice` and subscribes to `subscribeIngest` so a document open in one tab catches a deletion made in another. Found and fixed in the same change: issue #231 — `document_metadata` 404'd a tombstoned document indistinguishably from a bad id; it now resolves `deleted`/`deleted_at`/`deleted_reason` before the availability checks that assume a live document. 4 new backend tests (`test_documents.py` ×2 for #231, `test_ingest_records.py` ×1 for the snapshot, `test_sources_records.py` extended for `sources.deleted_at`); `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (263), `scripts/dev.sh check` (529, 1 skipped), `scripts/dev.sh web-check` (177 lib tests, lint/typecheck/build/contrast/offline) all clean. Exercised against the real stack — the compose `api` service has no source bind mount, so this needed `scripts/dev.sh build-api` before the fix was actually live; confirmed `GET /documents/{id}` resolving a pre-existing tombstone from an earlier manual walkthrough, `DELETE /sources/{id}` setting `deleted_at` and staying in `/ingest`'s snapshot, and the file route still 404ing. No browser available in this session, so the greyed row/card/notice were not visually checked — the API contracts each reads were confirmed correct instead. `0.2.48` → `0.2.49`.

**2026-08-28 (`M2-DELETE-BE-061`)** — Tombstoned deletion. `askwell.sources.delete_document`/`delete_source`, `DELETE /documents/{id}?reason=`/`DELETE /sources/{id}`. Both already-built pieces the ticket depended on were verified rather than reimplemented: retrieval's exclusion of tombstoned/superseded documents, and the `ck_chunks_cleared_content_has_no_embedding` check constraint. Added a guard to `askwell.ingest`'s stage-transition writes (`_park`, `_finish`, `_fail`, `process`'s `indexing` write) so a job claimed before a deletion runs cannot write a tombstoned document's status back to something live. 15 new tests in `test_sources_records.py`; `scripts/dev.sh test-db` (261) and `scripts/dev.sh check` (529, 1 skipped) both clean. Exercised against the real stack with `chcon` applied to a fresh `/tmp` root (this machine's own SELinux-relabel requirement, per the 2026-08-28 entry below) — `psql` confirmed the tombstone, the cleared chunk, and the untouched file on disk; both routes appear in `audit_decisions`. `0.2.47` → `0.2.48`.

**2026-08-26 (decisions locked)** — Designs approved. **Tauri desktop shell** and **web search** both decided yes, and everything reworked to match rather than left drifting. Web search is constraint **C10**: an escalation the user performs, never a fallback when retrieval is thin — because an automatic one destroys abstention, which is the behaviour the whole product protects. C1 now names both egress paths. New `docs/web-search.md`, new specs `docs/ux/conversation.md` and `docs/ux/web-search.md`, three new lab screens (40 total, swept clean across three widths and both themes). Backlog reworked to **198 tickets** across 10 milestones including a new Phase 6.5, 573–821 hours raw. Quality gate at 165 tasks with a web-escalation category at 1.00, no exceptions. Closed #34, #35, #37, #38.

**2026-08-26 (issues closed)** — Resolved every open issue before any build work. **C9 added** — a bundled model must be redistributable, commercial-use permitted and ungated; Gemma is permanently excluded on that basis. **Model swapping is permitted and marked** rather than restricted, with a persistent marker on answers from an unvalidated model, following the retrieval-threshold precedent. v2 language findings recorded in `architecture.md` §6.1 — the Tamil TTS plan rests on a non-commercial model and Sinhala ASR has no shippable option, which strengthens the deferral that was originally argued on schedule alone. Closed #6, #7, #9 as superseded by M0 tickets, and #24–#28 as resolved. Backlog updated: new ticket M7-SET-FE-146a, C9 added to the constraint coverage map.

**2026-08-26 (backlog)** — MODE B produced the full execution package in `docs/backlog/`: 9 milestones, 31 epics, **176 tickets**, 514–737 hours before the rework multiplier. Verified: IDs 001–176 with no gaps or duplicates, no ticket over 6 hours, zero code or SQL or YAML, 187 references to the existing screen specs and 65 to the state document, six tickets correctly left `blocked:decision` rather than guessed. Applied #24 and #25 in the same change — Piper reverted to Kokoro-82M, profile models corrected to the real Qwen3.5/3.6 line, and `AGENTS.md` §4 gained a registry-verification rule for model names after that mistake was made twice. #26 and #28 remain open decisions.

**2026-08-26** — Ran the backlog agent's MODE A over all documentation. It surfaced four things worth the pass on its own: **target platforms were never documented anywhere**, the C1 egress mechanism was one sentence with no way to produce the outbound counter that settings promises, containerised inference silently excluded macOS, and PyMuPDF's AGPL licence conflicts with Apache-2.0 distribution. Confirmed the stack: all three platforms with **native inference**, **egress proxy** enforcing C1, **no web container** (static assets served by the API), **pypdfium2**. Seven containers plus one native process. Also fixed documentation bugs it found — PRD §11 had lost two items and was numbered 1,3; architecture claimed seven containers and diagrammed eight; `states-and-edge-cases.md` still said "for admins" and cited the renamed `edge` profile and two stale constraint numbers. Next: MODE B, the full ticket backlog.

**2026-08-10 (P6 format)** — Story format and milestones in `docs/stories/`. Five M1 stories written in full as samples rather than generating the whole backlog against an unagreed shape. Notable: **M2 "It says when it doesn't know" is its own milestone** — abstention and the failure states normally get folded into "the chat feature" and quietly dropped when time runs short, and they are the product's central claim. M1.3 ships an ungrounded answer path and says so explicitly, with M1.4 following immediately to satisfy C4; that is a deliberate temporary state, not an oversight. Manual tests walk from a cold start every time, so an upstream regression surfaces on the next story rather than in someone's install.

**2026-08-10 (data model)** — Reviewed `architecture.md` §7 against all ten screens and rewrote it. Four changes: `documents.path`/`missing_since` so a moved file is distinguishable from a deleted one (indexing in place makes stale paths normal, not exceptional); **`citations` promoted to a real table** because C4 cannot be enforced or measured while citations live in a jsonb blob — `success-metrics.md` tracks uncited claims at 100% and that query has to be possible; `fact_usage` join table for "used in N answers"; `clarifications.rank`/`evidence` for the cap and the value distributions. Dropped `collections` — a flat list is right until someone needs grouping, and documents hang off `sources` now. Specified the shape of `messages.trace`, with scores and the threshold **stored not recomputed**, since recomputing gives a different number after any threshold change and breaks the explanation exactly when someone is investigating an old answer. Listed the constraints the ORM will not express, to go in the same migration that creates the tables. Closed #20.

**2026-08-10 (P2 complete)** — Remaining 8 screens written: first-run, add-source, library, source-viewer, memory, trace, voice, settings. Decisions taken while specifying, each recorded in its screen doc: PDFs render **in-app** rather than handing off to the OS viewer, because handing off loses the highlight and the way back and the citation loop has to be cheap or people stop checking; the file-moved state is distinct from deleted, since indexing in place makes stale paths inevitable; the retrieval threshold is adjustable only **from an abstention trace, with the consequence stated**, never as a frictionless slider; memory sorts inferred facts first and shows "used in N answers", which is the number that makes a wrong belief noticeable; update checking is **off by default** with the payload stated, because a silent check contradicts C1. Next: review `architecture.md` §7 data model against what the screens actually need.

**2026-08-10 (rename)** — VaultQ → **Askwell**, repo renamed to `Rumeasiyan/askwell`. Apache-2.0 for the application, proprietary credit service. Marginalis was more coherent with the design signature but lost on four syllables that need spelling aloud; Gleanly lost on brand adjacency to Glean. Every dictionary word was taken on npm and PyPI. Surveyed the field before choosing a discovery strategy: open-webui 148k stars, AnythingLLM 64k, private-gpt 57k, Quivr 39k, Khoj 36k, Onyx 31k — **the name will not win search against these and should not try**; none of them asks about your data or remembers the answers, and that phrase is unclaimed.

**2026-08-10 (P2 start)** — Design system and the two highest-value screens. Direction: **instrument, not chatbot** — the templated centred-chat-column with sources behind a toggle was rejected because it makes citations a disclosure you click, contradicting the product's central claim. Signature is the **permanent provenance margin**: source cards aligned to the claim they support, joined by a hairline leader, never collapsible. An uncited claim is visibly wrong because nothing sits beside it — the layout enforces C4 rather than trusting the model to. Palette encodes epistemics: green means traceable and is spent on nothing else, ochre means Askwell guessed. Serif for language, mono for machinery. Wrote `design-system.md`, `ask.md`, `clarifications.md`, and an HTML visual reference.

**2026-08-10 (rewrite)** — Product repositioned; see the table above. Rewrote `PRD.md` as a business-only document and split all technical content into `architecture.md`, `data-sources.md`, `memory-and-clarification.md`, `audit-log.md` and `build-plan.md`. Rewrote `success-metrics.md` (no pilot exists, so every number was re-derived) and `states-and-edge-cases.md` (licence, seat, RBAC and permission states deleted). `AGENTS.md` §1–§3 rewritten: constraints renumbered, old C7 (column access control) removed, new C3 (dump sandbox) added, C1 now allows explicit online opt-in, C6 restated as tamper-evident rather than immutable. Closed #3, #4, #5, #10, #11, #12, #13, #14, #15.

**2026-08-10 (earlier)** — Closed the P0/P1 gaps: added `success-metrics.md` and `states-and-edge-cases.md`. Filed #9 after verifying stack versions against registries.

**2026-08-10** — Set up agent working documentation: `AGENTS.md`, decision log, versioning, tracker conventions, issue templates, labels.

## Notes for the next session

**Read these first — each cost real time to learn.**

- **A model name is never asserted from memory, including from a decision entry.** `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-08-10 entry claimed Qwen3.5 was not a real release; it is, and that entry downgraded the generation model by a family version. It now carries a superseded banner, because newest-first ordering protects a person reading top-down and not a `grep` — and a `grep` is what an agent uses. Verify against the registry every time (`AGENTS.md` §4).
- **The build runner's failures have almost all been in the harness, not the agents.** Seven were fixed in one session and every one had the same shape: something reporting a failure the work did not have, or a success it had not earned — agents that could not run the toolchain, a gate row whose pass line was a substring of its failure line, model knobs declared and read by nothing, a killed queue leaving its agents alive, an orphaned runner editing the tree for seven hours, a bare `git pull` refusing a diverged branch, and a rejected ticket's work following the checkout onto `main`. When a ticket fails, suspect the machinery before the code.
- **An audit rejection is usually right.** Of the rejections in that session, every one named a real defect the six-row gate had passed: a merged-cell counter that could only ever report zero, an auto-retry that overwrote "wrong password" with "password protected", an acceptance criterion with no implementation, a stale ref dropping streamed tokens, `except AuditError` not catching the database failure it existed for, and follow-up padding that three tests asserted *as correct*. That last one is the pattern to fear: a test encoding the wrong requirement passes the gate and makes the defect look deliberate.
- **Prove a fix with a mutation.** Break the fix, run the suite, confirm the new test fails and nothing else does. It caught two tests this session that would otherwise have proved nothing — and once produced a hang rather than a failure, which turned out to be exactly the user-visible symptom.


- Dev machine runs Python **3.14.6**; the project targets **3.12**. `podman-compose` is not installed — use `podman compose`. Tracked in [#6](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/6).
- `bench.py` exists in draft form outside this repo — port it to `eval/bench.py` in Phase 1 rather than rewriting it.
- `decisions.md` is append-only. Entries written before 2026-08-10 describe the organisation-era product and are historically accurate for that time; the repositioning entry supersedes their framing rather than editing them.
